\chapter{Quantum Groups: Foundations}
\label{ch:quantum-groups}

\providecommand{\Meff}{\mathcal{M}^{\mathfrak A}_{\sigma,S}}
\providecommand{\PBPS}{\mathfrak{P}^{\mathrm{BPS}}}
\providecommand{\Gammaeff}{\Gamma^{+}_{\sigma,S,o}}
\providecommand{\Gammass}{\Gamma^{\mathrm{ss}}_{\sigma,S}}
\providecommand{\GammaBPS}{\Gamma^{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\sigma,S,o}}
\providecommand{\Teq}{T_{\mathrm{eq}}}
\providecommand{\DKS}{\mathfrak{D}^{\mathrm{KS}}_{\sigma,S,o}}
\providecommand{\ThetaBPS}{\Theta^{\mathrm{BPS}}_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}}

The toric fan supplies a positive cone, a chamber atlas, a PBW order,
and a positivity structure in one combinatorial object. General BPS
spectra separate these four roles. The object below records the
separation before any toric degeneration is imposed: the positive stack
is indexed by semistable support, the completion is indexed by BPS
support, the wall-crossing lives in a completed quantum torus, and the
theta basis is an enhancement only where it is constructed.

\begin{definition}[Relative BPS Hall-scattering datum]
\label{def:universal-positive-geometry-grammar}
Let $\mathcal{C}$ be a CY$_3$ category with geometric support $X$; in
the sheaf-theoretic case $\mathcal{C}=D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)$. Write its
numerical charge lattice as
\[
 \Gamma_X := K^{\mathrm{num}}_0(\mathcal{C})
\]
and antisymmetric Euler form $\langle-,-\rangle$. Fix:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item a Bridgeland stability condition $\sigma=(Z,\mathcal{P})$ on the
component under consideration;
\item a strict sector $S\subset\mathbb{C}^*$, with no active BPS ray on
its boundary;
\item orientation data $o$, giving the determinant-line square root and
the sign cocycle $\varepsilon_o$ used in the quantum torus;
\item a support-property quadratic form $Q$ which is negative definite
on $\ker Z$ and non-negative on every active charge;
\item a geometry-specific equivariance group $\Teq$;
\item an oriented derived critical atlas $\mathfrak A$ on the
semistable moduli stacks;
\item an ambient integration theory
$\bullet\in\{\mathrm{mot},\mathrm{cl}\}$.
\end{enumerate}
If any of these inputs is missing, the datum below is not constructed at
that chamber. Put
\[
 \Gamma_X^{\mathrm{or}}
 :=
 \bigl(\Gamma_X,\langle-,-\rangle,\varepsilon_o\bigr)
\]
and define the semistable sector support by
\[
 \Gammass
 :=
 \{0\}\cup
 \bigl\{\gamma\in\Gamma_X:
 Z(\gamma)\in S,\
 \mathcal M_\sigma(\gamma)\neq\varnothing,\
 Q(\gamma)\geq0
 \bigr\}.
\]
The oriented critical stack is
\[
 \Meff(X)
 :=
 \coprod_{\gamma\in\Gammass}
 \mathcal M^{\mathfrak A}_\sigma(\gamma),
\]
where, on a quiver chart $U$, the summand is of the form
$[\mathrm{Crit}(\mathrm{Tr}\,W_{U,\mathbf d})/G_{\mathbf d}]$ and the
coefficient system is
$\phi_{\mathfrak A,o}=\phi_{\mathrm{Tr}\,W_{U,\mathbf d}}\otimes
\mathscr L_{o_U}$, with the orientation local system included.
The BPS cohomology and its integrations are
\[
 \operatorname{BPS}_{\gamma,\sigma,o,\Teq}
 :=
 H^\bullet_{c,\Teq}
 \bigl(\mathcal M^{\mathfrak A}_\sigma(\gamma),
 \phi_{\mathfrak A,o}\bigr),
\]
\[
 \Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}_{\sigma,o}(\gamma)
 :=
 [\operatorname{BPS}_{\gamma,\sigma,o,\Teq}]_{\mathrm{vir}},
 \qquad
 \Omega^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\sigma,o}(\gamma)
 :=
 \chi\bigl(\operatorname{BPS}_{\gamma,\sigma,o,\Teq}\bigr).
\]
The BPS support and its effective completion monoid are
\[
 {\GammaBPS}^{\bullet}
 :=
 \bigl\{\gamma\in\Gammass:
 \Omega^{\bullet}_{\sigma,o}(\gamma)\neq0\bigr\},
 \qquad
 \Gammaeff
 :=
 \mathbb N\langle{\GammaBPS}^{\bullet}\rangle.
\]
The \emph{relative BPS Hall-scattering datum} is
\[
 {\PBPS}^{\bullet}_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)
 :=
 \bigl(
 \Gamma_X^{\mathrm{or}},\,
 Q,\,
 \Gammass,\,
 {\GammaBPS}^{\bullet},\,
 \Gammaeff,\,
 \Meff(X),\,
 \phi_{\mathfrak A,o},\,
 \operatorname{BPS}_{\sigma,o,\Teq},\,
 \Omega^{\bullet}_{\sigma,o},\,
 \widehat{\mathbb T}^{\bullet}_{\Gamma,S},\,
 {\DKS}^{\bullet}
 \bigr),
\]
where the motivic completed torus is generated by symbols $x_\gamma$
with
\[
 x_\alpha x_\beta
 =
 \mathbb L^{\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle/2}
 \varepsilon_o(\alpha,\beta)x_{\alpha+\beta},
\]
completed along $\Gammaeff$, and the classical ambient is its Euler
specialisation with Poisson bracket
$\{e_\alpha,e_\beta\}=\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle e_{\alpha+\beta}$.
For a strict subsector $V\subset S$, the motivic KS factor is the
phase-ordered product
\[
 A_V(\sigma)
 =
 \prod_{\ell\subset V}^{\curvearrowright}
 \prod_{Z(\gamma)\in\ell}
 \mathbb E(x_\gamma)^{\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}_{\sigma,o}(\gamma)}
 \in\widehat{\mathbb T}^{\mathrm{mot}}_{\Gamma,V}.
\]
Its classical wall automorphism is the Euler-specialised shadow
\[
 \mathcal K^{\mathrm{cl}}_\gamma(e_\eta)
 =
 e_\eta
 \bigl(1-\varepsilon_o(\gamma)e_\gamma\bigr)^{
 \Omega^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\sigma,o}(\gamma)\langle\gamma,\eta\rangle}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Positive half of the relative BPS datum]
\label{def:positive-half-effective-bps}
The chamber-sector positive half is the completed equivariant
vanishing-cycle cohomology
\[
 Y^+_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)
 :=
 \widehat{\bigoplus}_{\gamma\in\Gammass}
 H^\bullet_{\Teq}
 \bigl(\mathcal M^{\mathfrak A}_\sigma(\gamma),
 \phi_{\mathfrak A,o}\bigr),
\]
where the completion is along $\Gammaeff$. It is an $E_1$ Hall algebra
when the critical Hall correspondence is constructed and compatible with
orientation data, Thom--Sebastiani, and equivariant descent. The stack
is indexed by the semistable support $\Gammass$; the topology and the
wall-crossing completion are indexed by the BPS-generated monoid
$\Gammaeff$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[PBW shadow of the positive half]
\label{thm:effective-bps-pbw-shadow}
On a ray or slope $\mu$ for which the oriented critical CoHA is
constructed and Davison--Meinhardt integrality applies, the perverse
PBW filtration on the positive half has associated graded
\[
 \operatorname{gr}_F
 \mathcal H^{\mathrm{crit}}_{\sigma,\mu,o,\Teq}
 \cong
 \operatorname{Sym}_{\mathrm{super}}
 \bigl(
 \operatorname{BPS}_{\sigma,\mu,o,\Teq}
 \otimes H^\bullet(B\mathbb C^*)
 \bigr).
\]
This is a theorem about $Y^+_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)$ as a positive half.
It does not construct a Drinfeld double, a theta basis, or a modular
tensor category.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The statement is the Davison--Meinhardt PBW theorem for critical CoHAs
with orientation data, read in the fixed chamber and sector. The
super-symmetric algebra records the parity of the BPS cohomology. In
the toric quiver-with-potential cases this is part of the standard
critical CoHA construction; for a compact non-toric CY$_3$ it is conditional
on the existence of the oriented critical atlas and on the hypotheses of
the integrality theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional Hall--Drinfeld double]
\label{thm:quantum-group-as-positive-geometry-double}
Assume that $Y^+_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)$ is a complete topological Hall
bialgebra, or Hopf algebra after the required antipode is constructed;
that it satisfies the PBW theorem above; that a Serre-dual negative half
$Y^-_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)$ and a Cartan half
\[
 Y^0_\sigma(X)=\mathbb Q((\hbar))[[\Gamma_X]]_\chi,
 \qquad
 K_\gamma y_\delta K_\gamma^{-1}
 =
 q^{\langle\gamma,\delta\rangle}y_\delta ,
\]
are fixed; and that the continuous Hall--Serre pairing between the
positive and negative halves is non-degenerate after quotienting its
radical. Then the chamber-sector Hall quantum group is the completed
Drinfeld double
\[
 G^{\mathrm{Hall}}_{\sigma,S}(X)
 :=
 D_{\sigma,S}
 \bigl(
 \widehat{Y^+_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}}(X),
 Y^0_\sigma(X),
 \langle-,-\rangle_{\sigma,S}
 \bigr),
\]
equivalently
\[
 G^{\mathrm{Hall}}_{\sigma,S}(X)
 =
 \widehat{Y^-_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}}(X)
 \,\widehat{\bowtie}\,
 Y^0_\sigma(X)
 \,\widehat{\bowtie}\,
 \widehat{Y^+_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}}(X).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Scope]
The Hall product and wall-crossing automorphisms are Kontsevich--
Soibelman; the numerical Hall integration and no-pole statements are
Joyce--Song and Bridgeland in their stated ambients; PBW integrality is
Davison--Meinhardt. The non-degenerate pairing and completed Drinfeld
double are proved in the explicit toric Hall loci beginning with
Schiffmann--Vasserot for $\mathbb{C}^3$ and in the named no-compact-$4$-
cycle toric regimes under the Rap\v{c}ak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao
hypotheses. Outside those loci the displayed double is not an extra
axiom in ${\PBPS}^{\bullet}_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)$; it is the object
obtained only after the coproduct, Cartan, negative half, completion, and
pairing have been built.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Theta-enhanced BPS positive geometry]
\label{def:theta-enhanced-effective-bps}
When broken-line, GMN spectral-network, cluster, or Hall-factorisation
methods construct a positive basis for the completed Hall algebra, the
theta-enhanced geometry is
\[
 {\PBPS}^{\bullet,+\theta}_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)
 :=
 \bigl(
 {\PBPS}^{\bullet}_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X),
 {\ThetaBPS}^{\bullet}
 \bigr).
\]
The enhancement is proved in the standard toric, cluster, and
class-S/Hitchin settings where such bases are constructed. For a
general compact CY$_3$ chamber it is conjectural and is not part of the
minimal Hall-scattering datum.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Toric effective geometry as terminal degeneration]
\label{prop:toric-effective-geometry-terminal-degeneration}
Let $X_{\Sigma}$ be a toric CY$_3$ with quiver-with-potential
$(Q_{\Sigma},W_{\Sigma})$ in a toric chamber for which the standard
critical CoHA construction and orientation data are available. Then the
relative BPS Hall-scattering datum degenerates to the toric effective
positive geometry:
\[
 \Gammass=\Gammaeff=\mathbb{Z}^{Q_0}_{\geq 0},
 \qquad
 \Meff(X_{\Sigma})
 =
 \coprod_{\mathbf d\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_0}_{\geq 0}}
 [\mathrm{Crit}(\mathrm{Tr}\,W_{\mathbf d})/G_{\mathbf d}],
\]
where $G_{\mathbf d}=\prod_{i\in Q_0}GL(d_i)$. The scattering diagram
is the quiver/cluster KS scattering diagram and
\[
 Y^+_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X_{\Sigma})
 =
 \CoHA(Q_{\Sigma},W_{\Sigma}).
\]
For $X=\mathbb{C}^3$ this is
$Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$, the positive half of the affine
Yangian; it is not $\mathcal W_{1+\infty}$ before the double and
representation-theoretic passage. For the conifold and the standard
no-compact-$4$-cycle toric threefolds it is the positive half of the
affine super-Yangian attached to the toric quiver.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In a toric Hall chamber the moduli problem is presented by the
dimension-vector monoid of the quiver. Hence the Hall indexing monoid is
$\mathbb{Z}^{Q_0}_{\geq0}$, and in the standard chambers the active BPS
charges generate this same completion monoid. The derived critical stack
is the quotient of the critical locus of the trace potential on
representation space by $G_{\mathbf d}=\prod_i GL(d_i)$. The Hall
product is the usual extension correspondence for quiver
representations, with vanishing-cycle coefficients and orientation data,
hence the critical CoHA\@. Schiffmann--Vasserot identify the
$\mathbb{C}^3$ CoHA with $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$; the
Rap\v{c}ak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao toric construction identifies the
no-compact-$4$-cycle toric CoHA with the corresponding affine-super-
Yangian positive half under its hypotheses. Kontsevich--Soibelman
wall-crossing on the same quiver is the cluster scattering diagram. Thus
the fan/quiver diagram is the rational-polyhedral collapse of
${\PBPS}^{\bullet}_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Strict-sector effective cone and theta enhancement]
\label{conj:effective-bps-cone-positive-basis}
For every CY$_3$ category admitting a Bridgeland chamber with support
property, orientation data, and oriented critical semistable moduli, the
strict-sector datum ${\PBPS}^{\bullet}_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)$ is locally
finite after completion along $\Gammaeff$. Its motivic KS products are
Harder--Narasimhan invariant under chamber deformation, and whenever the
Hall broken-line or spectral-network construction exists it produces the
theta enhancement
${\PBPS}^{\bullet,+\theta}_{\sigma,S,o,\Teq}(X)$. The toric effective
positive geometry of
Proposition~\ref{prop:toric-effective-geometry-terminal-degeneration}
is the most degenerate fibre: the chamber complex becomes rational
polyhedral, the completion monoid is generated by quiver vertices, and
the theta basis becomes the shuffle, cluster, or plane-partition basis in
the constructed toric charts.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Equivariance type stratifies by geometric class]
\label{rem:equivariance-stratifies-by-class}
The equivariance group $\Teq$ in
Definition~\ref{def:universal-positive-geometry-grammar} is part of the
input, not a universal constant. Toric CY$_d$ carries the full torus
$T^d$ and degenerates to the rational-polyhedral cone above; reduced
affine classes such as the resolved conifold and local $\mathbb{P}^2$
carry residual loop-rotation equivariance; orbifolds $[X/\Gamma]$ carry
inertia-stack equivariance encoding twisted sectors; lattice-polarised
K3 and $K3\times E$ families carry period-domain equivariance from the
Mukai lattice automorphism group. The uniform object is the relative
Hall-scattering datum. Its equivariant cohomology, scattering diagram,
and theta enhancement are stratified by the geometry.
\end{remark}

Conjecture~CY-C asks when the CY-to-chiral correspondence $\{\Phi_d\}$ produces a quantum group at the representation-theoretic level. To answer that question one needs the standard quantum-group structure in a form compatible with the ordered bar and the Drinfeld center. The quantized enveloping algebra $U_q(\frakg)$ and its $R$-matrix are the targets of $\Phi_d$ on that side: Conjecture~CY-C predicts that the braided monoidal category $\Rep_q(\frakg)$ arises as $\Rep^{\Etwo}(A_\cC)$ for a CY category $\cC(\frakg, q)$. At $d \geq 3$, the algebra $A_\cC = \Phi_d(\cC)$ is natively $\Eone$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level}); the $\Etwo$-braided structure on $\Rep^{\Etwo}(A_\cC)$ arises via the Drinfeld-centre half-braiding on $\Rep(A_\cC)$, not from an intrinsic $\Etwo$-structure on $A_\cC$ itself (Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}). The Drinfeld--Jimbo presentation and the FRT presentation give two access points, corresponding respectively to the Koszul dual presentation by generators and relations and the ordered-bar RTT formalism.

The CY-to-chiral direction treats $U_q(\frakg)$ as an \emph{output} of
$\{\Phi_d\}$ applied to a CY category whose Drinfeld center recovers
the modular tensor category of conformal blocks. Everything below is
classical and due to Drinfeld, Jimbo, Lusztig,
Reshetikhin--Turaev, and Kazhdan--Lusztig; the new datum is the
comparison with $\{\Phi_d\}$.

% ============================================================
\section{Quantized enveloping algebras}
\label{sec:qgf-quantized-enveloping}
% ============================================================

The Drinfeld--Jimbo presentation realises $U_q(\frakg)$ as a deformation of $U(\frakg)$ in the Hopf-algebra category: generators and Serre relations deform with $q$-corrections, and the coproduct acquires an asymmetry that at $q = 1$ reduces to the symmetric classical coproduct. The data entering the definition is the Cartan matrix and its symmetrisers; everything else is forced.

Let $\frakg$ be a finite-dimensional simple Lie algebra over $\C$ of rank $\ell$ with Cartan matrix $(a_{ij})_{i,j=1}^{\ell}$ and symmetrizers $d_1, \ldots, d_\ell \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ such that $d_i a_{ij} = d_j a_{ji}$. Let $q \in \C^*$ be a formal parameter (or complex number, not a root of unity) and write $q_i = q^{d_i}$, $[n]_q = (q^n - q^{-n})/(q - q^{-1})$, $[n]_q! = [1]_q [2]_q \cdots [n]_q$.

\begin{definition}[Drinfeld--Jimbo quantized enveloping algebra]
\label{def:qgf-quantum-group}
\label{def:qgf-drinfeld-jimbo}
The \emph{quantized enveloping algebra} $U_q(\frakg)$ is the unital associative $\C(q)$-algebra generated by $E_i, F_i, K_i^{\pm 1}$ for $i = 1, \ldots, \ell$ subject to the relations
\begin{align}
 K_i K_j &= K_j K_i, \qquad K_i K_i^{-1} = K_i^{-1} K_i = 1, \\
 K_i E_j K_i^{-1} &= q_i^{a_{ij}} E_j, \qquad K_i F_j K_i^{-1} = q_i^{-a_{ij}} F_j, \\
 [E_i, F_j] &= \delta_{ij} \, \frac{K_i - K_i^{-1}}{q_i - q_i^{-1}},
\end{align}
together with the quantum Serre relations, for $i \ne j$,
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{k=0}^{1 - a_{ij}} (-1)^k \binom{1 - a_{ij}}{k}_{q_i} E_i^{1 - a_{ij} - k} E_j E_i^k = 0,
 \label{eq:qgf-quantum-serre-E}
\end{equation}
and the analogous identity for the $F_i$, where $\binom{n}{k}_{q_i} = [n]_{q_i}!/([k]_{q_i}! [n-k]_{q_i}!)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Classical limit]
\label{rem:qgf-classical-limit}
Writing $q = e^{\hbar/2}$ and $K_i = q_i^{H_i} = e^{d_i \hbar H_i / 2}$ and formally expanding in $\hbar$, the relation $[E_i, F_j] = \delta_{ij} (K_i - K_i^{-1})/(q_i - q_i^{-1})$ becomes $[E_i, F_j] = \delta_{ij} H_i + O(\hbar)$, and the quantum Serre relations reduce to the classical Serre relations at $\hbar = 0$. Thus $U_q(\frakg)/(q - 1) \cong U(\frakg)$ as Hopf algebras (Drinfeld 1986, Jimbo 1985).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Hopf algebra structure]
\label{prop:qgf-hopf}
$U_q(\frakg)$ carries a Hopf algebra structure with coproduct $\Delta$, counit $\varepsilon$, and antipode $S$ given on generators by
\begin{align}
 \Delta(K_i) &= K_i \otimes K_i, \\
 \Delta(E_i) &= E_i \otimes 1 + K_i \otimes E_i, \\
 \Delta(F_i) &= F_i \otimes K_i^{-1} + 1 \otimes F_i, \\
 \varepsilon(K_i) &= 1, \qquad \varepsilon(E_i) = \varepsilon(F_i) = 0, \\
 S(K_i) &= K_i^{-1}, \qquad S(E_i) = -K_i^{-1} E_i, \qquad S(F_i) = -F_i K_i.
\end{align}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Drinfeld, ``Quantum groups'' (ICM Berkeley 1986); Jimbo, ``A $q$-difference analogue of $U(\frakg)$ and the Yang--Baxter equation'' (Lett.\ Math.\ Phys.\ 1985). The Hopf axioms are verified by direct computation on generators; the Serre relations are compatible with the coproduct by a standard argument using the $q$-binomial identity.
\end{proof}

The algebra $U_q(\frakg)$ admits a triangular decomposition $U_q(\frakg) \cong U_q^-(\frakg) \otimes U_q^0(\frakg) \otimes U_q^+(\frakg)$, with $U_q^{\pm}(\frakg)$ generated by the $E_i$ respectively $F_i$, and $U_q^0(\frakg) \cong \C(q)[K_1^{\pm 1}, \ldots, K_\ell^{\pm 1}]$ the Cartan subalgebra. On the CY-to-chiral side, this decomposition matches the bar filtration of $B(A_\cC)$: weight zero is $U_q^0$, positive weights are $U_q^+$, negative weights are $U_q^-$.

\begin{remark}[Universal \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix, formal form]
\label{rem:qgf-universal-R-formal}
Drinfeld (1986) and independently Rosso, Kirillov--Reshetikhin show that $U_q(\frakg)$ is a \emph{quasi-triangular} Hopf algebra: there exists a formal element
\begin{equation}
 \cR = \sum_{\mu \in Q^+} \cR_\mu \in U_q(\frakg) \mathbin{\hat{\otimes}} U_q(\frakg),
 \label{eq:qgf-universal-R}
\end{equation}
where the sum ranges over the positive root lattice $Q^+$ and each $\cR_\mu \in U_q^+ \otimes U_q^-$ is a finite product of quantum exponentials. The element $\cR$ intertwines $\Delta$ with its opposite: $\cR \Delta(x) \cR^{-1} = \Delta^{\mathrm{op}}(x)$ for all $x \in U_q(\frakg)$. Closed expressions exist only for $\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$ and low-rank cases.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\section{The universal \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix and Yang--Baxter equation}
\label{sec:qgf-r-matrix-ybe}
% ============================================================

\begin{proposition}[Quantum Yang--Baxter equation]
\label{prop:qgf-qybe}
The universal $R$-matrix $\cR \in U_q(\frakg)^{\hat{\otimes} 2}$ of Remark~\ref{rem:qgf-universal-R-formal} satisfies the quantum Yang--Baxter equation
\begin{equation}
 \cR_{12} \, \cR_{13} \, \cR_{23} = \cR_{23} \, \cR_{13} \, \cR_{12}
 \qquad \text{in } U_q(\frakg)^{\hat{\otimes} 3},
 \label{eq:qgf-qybe}
\end{equation}
where $\cR_{ij}$ denotes the embedding of $\cR$ into the $(i, j)$-tensor factors (with identity on the remaining factor).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Drinfeld, ``Quantum groups'' (ICM Berkeley 1986), Theorem~2; see also Chari--Pressley, \emph{A Guide to Quantum Groups}, \S4.2. The proof is a direct consequence of quasi-triangularity and the coassociativity of $\Delta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Classical limit and cross-volume compatibility]
\label{prop:qgf-classical-limit-r}
Write $q = e^{\hbar/2}$ and expand the universal $R$-matrix as
\begin{equation}
 \cR = 1 + \hbar \, r + O(\hbar^2), \qquad r \in \frakg \otimes \frakg.
 \label{eq:qgf-R-expansion}
\end{equation}
Then $r$ is the \emph{classical $r$-matrix} of $\frakg$, satisfying the symmetry identity $r + r^{21} = \Omega_{\frakg}$ where $\Omega_{\frakg} \in (\frakg \otimes \frakg)^{\frakg}$ is the quadratic Casimir tensor (normalised by the invariant form). Explicitly, $r$ decomposes as $r = \tfrac{1}{2}\Omega_{\frakg} + r_{\mathrm{sk}}$ with $r_{\mathrm{sk}} = \sum_{\alpha > 0} E_\alpha \wedge F_\alpha$ the skew-symmetric Drinfeld--Sklyanin part; the skew part carries the Lie-bialgebra cobracket data and the symmetric part is forced by quasi-triangularity. The quantum Yang--Baxter equation~\eqref{eq:qgf-qybe} expands to the classical Yang--Baxter equation
\begin{equation}
 [r_{12}, r_{13}] + [r_{12}, r_{23}] + [r_{13}, r_{23}] = 0
 \label{eq:qgf-cybe}
\end{equation}
at order $\hbar^2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Drinfeld, ``Hamiltonian structures on Lie groups, Lie bialgebras and the geometric meaning of classical Yang--Baxter equations'' (Soviet Math.\ Dokl.\ 1983); Drinfeld (1986), Theorem~3.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Level-stripped affine \texorpdfstring{$r$}{r}-matrix and the two sanity points]
\label{rem:qgf-level-stripped-r}
Passing from $U_q(\frakg)$ (finite type) to the affine quantum group $U_q(\hat{\frakg})$ at level $k$, Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-classical-limit-r} acquires a level prefix: the classical limit produces
\begin{equation}
 r(z) = k \cdot \frac{\Omega_{\frakg}}{z} + O(\hbar, z^0),
 \label{eq:qgf-r-level-k}
\end{equation}
matching the convention used here. The affine formula has two distinguished values of $k$ at which the algebraic structure collapses for different reasons; both must be verified whenever an affine $r$-matrix is written.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
 \item \emph{Level zero} ($k = 0$). The level prefix in~\eqref{eq:qgf-r-level-k} vanishes, so $r(z) = 0$ and $\cR(z) = 1$: the affine algebra collapses to the loop algebra, whose invariant form is identically zero. The Sugawara central charge $c_{\mathrm{Sug}} = k\dim(\frakg)/(k + h^\vee)$ vanishes as well at $k = 0$, while the Kac--Moody modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{KM}} = \dim(\frakg)(k + h^\vee)/(2 h^\vee)$ takes the value $\dim(\frakg)/2$ (the free-field value, governed by the $h^\vee$ shift of the Sugawara construction, not the level prefix of the $r$-matrix).
 \item \emph{Critical level} ($k = -h^\vee$). The prefix $(k + h^\vee)$ vanishes, so $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{KM}} = 0$; the $R$-matrix degenerates and the quantum group collapses to the classical enveloping algebra of the loop algebra. This is the Feigin--Frenkel regime.
\end{enumerate}
The two limits give independent consistency conditions: the $r$-matrix vanishes as $k \to 0$ by the level prefix, and degenerates at $k = -h^\vee$ by the critical-level prefactor.
\end{remark}

The spectral-parameter version extends Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-qybe} to a family.

\begin{proposition}[Parametric Yang--Baxter equation for affine quantum groups]
\label{prop:qgf-parametric-ybe}
For the affine quantum group $U_q(\hat{\frakg})$ at generic $q$, there is a universal $R$-matrix $\cR(z/w) \in U_q(\hat{\frakg})^{\hat{\otimes} 2}[[z^{\pm 1}, w^{\pm 1}]]$ depending on a multiplicative spectral parameter, which satisfies the parametric Yang--Baxter equation
\begin{equation}
 \cR_{12}(z_1/z_2) \, \cR_{13}(z_1/z_3) \, \cR_{23}(z_2/z_3)
 = \cR_{23}(z_2/z_3) \, \cR_{13}(z_1/z_3) \, \cR_{12}(z_1/z_2).
 \label{eq:qgf-parametric-ybe}
\end{equation}
In the classical limit, the trigonometric solution reduces to the Baxterised $r(z)$ of the Drinfeld--Kohno bridge.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Drinfeld, ``A new realization of Yangians and quantized affine algebras'' (Soviet Math.\ Dokl.\ 1988); Frenkel--Reshetikhin, ``Quantum affine algebras and holonomic difference equations'' (Comm.\ Math.\ Phys.\ 1992).
\end{proof}

% ============================================================
\section{Representation categories}
\label{sec:qgf-rep-categories}
% ============================================================

Let $\Rep_q(\frakg) := \Rep^{\mathrm{fd}}(U_q(\frakg))$ denote the category of finite-dimensional type-$1$ representations of $U_q(\frakg)$, equipped with the tensor product provided by the coproduct. The universal $R$-matrix promotes $\Rep_q(\frakg)$ to a braided monoidal category: the braiding on $V \otimes W$ is given by $c_{V, W} = \tau \circ \cR|_{V \otimes W}$ where $\tau$ is the flip.

\begin{proposition}[Ribbon structure]
\label{prop:qgf-ribbon}
For generic $q$, the category $\Rep_q(\frakg)$ is a ribbon category in the sense of Reshetikhin--Turaev, with twist $\theta_V$ acting on an irreducible $V$ of highest weight $\lambda$ by $q^{(\lambda, \lambda + 2\rho)}$, where $\rho$ is the half-sum of positive roots.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Reshetikhin--Turaev, ``Ribbon graphs and their invariants derived from quantum groups'' (Comm.\ Math.\ Phys.\ 1990); Turaev, \emph{Quantum Invariants of Knots and $3$-Manifolds}, Chapter~XI.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Drinfeld--Kohno, generic \texorpdfstring{$q$}{q}]
\label{prop:qgf-drinfeld-kohno}
At generic $q$, the category $\Rep_q(\frakg)$ is equivalent as a braided monoidal category to the category $\Rep(\frakg)$ of finite-dimensional $\frakg$-modules equipped with the Drinfeld associator (built from the KZ connection) and the braiding induced by the classical $r$-matrix of Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-classical-limit-r}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Drinfeld, ``On almost cocommutative Hopf algebras'' (Leningrad Math.\ J.\ 1990) and ``Quasi-Hopf algebras'' (Leningrad Math.\ J.\ 1990); Kohno, ``Monodromy representations of braid groups and Yang--Baxter equations'' (Ann.\ Inst.\ Fourier 1987).
\end{proof}

The root-of-unity case is the setting relevant to conformal blocks.

\begin{proposition}[Modular tensor category at a root of unity]
\label{prop:qgf-mtc-root-of-unity}
Let $q = \exp(\pi i / (d \cdot (k + h^\vee)))$ with $k$ a positive integer, $h^\vee$ the dual Coxeter number, and $d$ the lacing number. The category $\Rep_q(\frakg)$ is \emph{not} semisimple, but the semisimplification (quotient by the tensor ideal of negligible morphisms, equivalently by modules of quantum dimension zero) is a modular tensor category $\mathcal{MTC}_q(\frakg)$ in the sense of Turaev.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Andersen, ``Tensor products of quantized tilting modules'' (Comm.\ Math.\ Phys.\ 1992); Andersen--Paradowski, ``Fusion categories arising from semisimple Lie algebras'' (Comm.\ Math.\ Phys.\ 1995). The Lusztig divided-power integral form is used implicitly (Lusztig, \emph{Introduction to Quantum Groups}, 1993).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Kazhdan--Lusztig equivalence;
\ClaimStatusProvedElsewhere]
\label{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig}
For $k$ a positive integer and $q = \exp(\pi i / (d(k + h^\vee)))$, there is an equivalence of modular tensor categories
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal{MTC}_q(\frakg) \ \simeq \ \mathcal{O}_k^{\mathrm{int}}(\hat{\frakg}),
 \label{eq:qgf-KL}
\end{equation}
between the semisimplified quantum group category and the category $\mathcal{O}_k^{\mathrm{int}}(\hat{\frakg})$ of integrable highest-weight modules over the affine Kac--Moody algebra $\hat{\frakg}$ at level $k$, with its Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov braiding.
\end{theorem}

The equivalence \eqref{eq:qgf-KL} is a theorem of Kazhdan--Lusztig, \emph{Tensor structures arising from affine Lie algebras} I--IV \cite{KazhdanLusztig1993,KazhdanLusztig1994}, with the positive-level fusion case closed by Finkelberg, \emph{An equivalence of fusion categories} (GAFA 1996). It requires $q$ at a root of unity; at generic $q$ the two sides are no longer equivalent (the affine side does not even make sense as a finite modular category). The statement proved there is a braided (indeed modular) tensor equivalence of representation categories attached to the formal disk. Its promotion to the curve-level chiral setting used elsewhere in this volume is a separate, open step:

\begin{conjecture}[Chiral upgrade of the Kazhdan--Lusztig
equivalence; \ClaimStatusConjectured]
\label{conj:qgf-kl-chiral-upgrade}
Fix a smooth algebraic curve $X$, a positive integer $k$, and $q = \exp(\pi i / (d(k + h^\vee)))$. The Kazhdan--Lusztig equivalence \eqref{eq:qgf-KL} lifts to an equivalence of factorisation module categories over $\Ran(X)$: the factorisation category of $V_k(\frakg)$-modules (conformal blocks with their sewing/factorisation structure) is equivalent to a factorisation category built from $\mathcal{MTC}_q(\frakg)$, compatibly with the Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov connection at all insertion points, and restricting at a single point to \eqref{eq:qgf-KL}. The open content is the construction of the quantum-group side as a factorisation category together with the comparison map; the pointwise equivalence \eqref{eq:qgf-KL} does not by itself determine the multi-point gluing data. No case of this upgrade is proved here.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Two-parameter Kazhdan--Lusztig equivalence, toroidal \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{sl}_2$}{sl-2}]
\label{conj:kazhdan-lusztig-toroidal-sl2}
Let $\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}}$ denote the toroidal (double-affine) Lie algebra associated with $\mathfrak{sl}_2$, and let $U_{q,t}(\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}})$ be the two-parameter quantum toroidal algebra of Ding--Iohara--Miki type at rank one. For parameters $(q, t) \in (\C^*)^2$ of the form
\[
 (q, t) \;=\; \bigl(e^{i\pi k/(k+2)},\ e^{i\pi k'/(k'+2)}\bigr),
 \qquad k, k' \in \Q \setminus \Z_{\le -2},
\]
restricted to the off-resonance locus (excluding $k, k' \in \Z_{\le -2}$, where the specialisation is ill-defined), there exists a braided monoidal equivalence
\begin{equation}
 \Rep_{q,t}\bigl(U_{q,t}(\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}})\bigr)
 \ \simeq\ \cO_{k,k'}\bigl(\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}}\bigr),
 \label{eq:kl-toroidal-sl2}
\end{equation}
between (i) the category of finite-dimensional (admissible) representations of $U_{q,t}(\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}})$ with its universal $R$-matrix braiding, and (ii) a suitably-defined double-level category $\cO_{k,k'}(\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}})$ of admissible highest-weight $\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}}$-modules at horizontal level $k$ and vertical level $k'$, equipped with the two-parameter Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov--Bernard braiding on the torus. The equivalence restricts, along either degeneration $t \to 1$ or $q \to 1$, to the one-parameter affine Kazhdan--Lusztig equivalence of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig} at $\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Algebraic reductions and categorical obstruction]
\label{rem:kl-toroidal-sl2-evidence}
The evidence for Conjecture~\ref{conj:kazhdan-lusztig-toroidal-sl2} splits across algebra and category sides.

\emph{Algebra side (reducible).} The quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}})$ admits three isomorphic presentations whose equivalences are established: (a)~a Drinfeld-type generator-and-relation presentation (Ding--Iohara 1997, $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ specialisation); (b)~a shuffle-algebra presentation on $\Q(q, t)[z^{\pm 1}]$ with the Feigin--Hashizume--Hoshino--Shiraishi--Yanagida symmetric wheel conditions (FHHSY, J.\ Math.\ Phys.\ 2009); (c)~an elliptic-Hall / Ding--Iohara--Miki Fock presentation (Schiffmann--Vasserot, Duke 2013, at $\mathfrak{gl}_1$, extended to $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ via Feigin--Tsymbaliuk). Miki (J.\ Math.\ Phys.\ 2007) supplies the cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-automorphism on the positive half (permuting the three axes of the two-parameter spectral torus under $\sum\epsilon_i = 0$), extending to an $S_3$-action on the Drinfeld double via the $\mathbb{Z}/2$-involution of the double. On the Drinfeld-double level, the positive half $U^+_{q,t}$ coincides with the shuffle algebra and, with its natural coproduct, lifts to a bialgebra with a universal $R$-matrix; this produces the braided monoidal structure on $\Rep_{q,t}$ in \eqref{eq:kl-toroidal-sl2}. The $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ case descends from the Feigin--Jimbo--Miwa--Mukhin treatment of higher-rank quantum toroidal $\mathfrak{gl}_n$ (J.\ Phys.\ A 2015) by specialisation, with Soibelman's cohomological Hall algebra picture and Negu\c{t}'s shuffle presentation (IMRN 2014) providing further structural reductions.

\emph{Category side (genuinely open).} The right-hand side $\cO_{k,k'}(\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}})$ of \eqref{eq:kl-toroidal-sl2} has no construction comparable to Finkelberg's affine fusion at one level. Two candidate constructions are (i)~admissible modules over the toroidal Kac--Moody vertex algebra with fusion via surface operators on a torus, and (ii)~a modular functor for the double-affine Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov--Bernard equation on a $T^2$-worldsheet. Neither has been shown to produce a modular-tensor-category structure; the associativity constraint for fusion of admissible modules at double level is the explicit gap, and this is why \eqref{eq:kl-toroidal-sl2} cannot presently be stated as a theorem.

\emph{One-parameter degeneration check.} Under $t \to 1$, the Ding--Iohara--Miki shuffle algebra degenerates to the quantum affine shuffle algebra of Feigin--Odesskii type, $\Rep_{q,t}$ degenerates to $\Rep_q(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}_2})$ at level $k$, and the right-hand side degenerates to $\cO_k^{\mathrm{int}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}_2})$. On this degeneration, Equation~\eqref{eq:kl-toroidal-sl2} becomes the $\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$ case of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig}, which provides a codimension-one consistency check.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Algebra side of toroidal Kazhdan--Lusztig, \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{sl}_2$}{sl-2}, formal disk]
\label{prop:kl-toroidal-sl2-algebra-side}
At formal-disk level, the algebra $U_{q,t}(\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\hat{\hat{\ }}})$ is isomorphic to the Ding--Iohara--Miki shuffle algebra $\mathrm{Sh}_{q,t}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ of Feigin--Hashizume--Hoshino--Shiraishi--Yanagida, and carries a natural bialgebra structure with a universal $R$-matrix acting on Fock representations, compatible with the cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-automorphism of Miki (2007) on the positive half (extending to $S_3$ on the double) that permutes the three spectral axes of the two-parameter torus.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Ding--Iohara 1997 (Drinfeld-type presentation); Feigin--Hashizume--Hoshino--Shiraishi--Yanagida 2009 (shuffle presentation and bialgebra structure for $\mathfrak{sl}_2$, Theorem~3.1 and Section~5); Miki 2007 (the cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-automorphism on $Y^+$, extending to $S_3$ on the double); Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013 (elliptic Hall / Fock realisation and universal $R$-matrix at $\mathfrak{gl}_1$, extended to $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ via Feigin--Tsymbaliuk); Negu\c{t} 2014 (shuffle presentation in the higher-rank framework, specialising at rank one). The composition $\text{Ding--Iohara (Drinfeld)} \cong \text{FHHSY (shuffle)} \cong \text{Schiffmann--Vasserot / Feigin--Tsymbaliuk (elliptic-Hall Fock)}$, glued structurally by the Miki cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-automorphism (extending to $S_3$ on the Drinfeld double), assembles the three presentations into a single bialgebra with universal $R$-matrix.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[CY-to-chiral standpoint]
\label{rem:qgf-vol3-standpoint}
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig} is the prototype for Conjecture CY-C. The modular tensor category $\mathcal{MTC}_q(\frakg)$ is the \emph{output} of a geometric functor: it is $\Rep^{\Etwo}(A_{\hat{\frakg}, k})$ where $A_{\hat{\frakg}, k}$ is the affine chiral algebra at level $k$. Here $\Rep^{\Etwo}$ denotes the braided monoidal structure arising from the Drinfeld-centre half-braiding on the representation category of the natively $\Eone$ algebra $A_{\hat{\frakg},k}$ (Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}). The CY correspondence generalises this by replacing the affine Kac--Moody input with a CY-category datum $\cC$ and the output $U_q(\frakg)$ with the quantum-group-like object $C(\cC, q)$ extracted from $\Phi(\cC)$.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\section{The four regimes}
\label{sec:qgf-four-regimes}
% ============================================================

The quantum group associated to a simple Lie algebra $\frakg$ admits four incarnations, indexed by the geometry of the spectral-parameter curve. Each regime produces a different algebra with a different $R$-matrix; the bar-cobar formalism applies uniformly to all four, and the CY correspondence recovers each regime from a geometric input.

\begin{definition}[The four quantum group regimes]
\label{def:qgf-four-regimes}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Rational: the Yangian $Y_\hbar(\frakg)$.} Spectral parameter $u = z_1 - z_2 \in \C$ (additive). The $R$-matrix $R(u) = 1 - \hbar\,P/u + O(\hbar^2)$ solves the additive Yang--Baxter equation. The underlying curve is $\bP^1$ (or $\C$). In the classical limit, $r(u) = P/u$ is the rational $r$-matrix. The Yangian is the $\Eone$-ordered bar construction of the affine chiral algebra at generic level; as an algebra it is natively $\Eone$ in the sense of Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level}.

 \item \emph{Trigonometric: the quantum loop algebra $U_q(L\frakg) \cong U_q(\hat{\frakg})$ at level zero.} Spectral parameter $z = e^{2\pi i u / \beta} \in \C^*$ (multiplicative). The $R$-matrix is the image of the universal $\cR$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-parametric-ybe} under evaluation representations. The underlying curve is $\C^*$. Sections~\ref{sec:qgf-quantized-enveloping}--\ref{sec:qgf-rep-categories} treat this regime.

 \item \emph{Elliptic: the elliptic quantum group $E_{q,p}(\frakg)$.} Spectral parameter $u \in E_\tau$ (elliptic curve). The Belavin $R$-matrix is the unique (up to gauge) solution of the QYBE with doubly-periodic meromorphic dependence on the spectral parameter. The propagator becomes $d\!\log\theta_1(z \mid \tau)$, and the Arnold relation lifts to the Fay trisecant identity. See Chapter~\ref{chap:toroidal-elliptic} for the bar-cobar treatment.

 \item \emph{Toroidal: the quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\hat{\hat{\frakg}})$.} Two spectral parameters $(z_1, z_2) \in \C^* \times \C^*$ (double loop). This regime arises from the Omega-background geometry $\R_t \times \C^2$ (Costello's 5d noncommutative Chern--Simons theory) and is the habitat of the affine Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ central to toric CY$_3$ computations. The Koszul dual is conjectured to be $U_{q^{-1}, t^{-1}}(\hat{\hat{\frakg}})$ (parameter inversion; Table~\ref{tab:rat-trig-ell}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Regime vs.\ Lie type]
\label{rem:qgf-regime-vs-type}
The four regimes are orthogonal to the choice of $\frakg$: each simple Lie algebra $\frakg$ has a rational, trigonometric, elliptic, and toroidal quantum group. The CY correspondence predicts that the regime is determined by the geometry of the ambient space of line operators: a CY$_3$ with line operators supported on a rational curve produces a Yangian; on a cylinder, a quantum loop algebra; on an elliptic curve, an elliptic quantum group; on a torus fibration, a toroidal algebra.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\section{Connection to Conjecture CY-C}
\label{sec:qgf-cy-c-connection}
% ============================================================

Conjecture CY-C upgrades Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig} from affine Kac--Moody data to CY-category data. We recall the precise statement and scope.

\begin{conjecture}[CY-C: quantum group realization]
\label{conj:qgf-cy-c}
Let $\cC$ be a smooth proper CY-$d$ category ($d = 2$ or $d = 3$), with
$A_\cC=\Phi_2(\cC)$ when $d=2$, and
$A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}=\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)
=\SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))$ on the framed $d=3$ locus of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}. There exists a parameter $q \in \C^*$
(determined by the tier-(iii) invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ of the
chosen chiral output) and a Hopf algebra in a braided category,
$C(\cC, q)$, such that there is an equivalence of modular tensor
categories
\begin{equation}
 \Rep^{\mathrm{fd}}(C(\cC, q)) \ \simeq \ \mathcal{MTC}(A_\cC),
 \label{eq:qgf-cy-c}
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal{MTC}(A_\cC) := \Rep^{\Etwo}(A_\cC)^{\mathrm{ss}}$ is the
(semisimplified) conformal-block category of $A_\cC$; at $d=3$ this
means the corresponding category of the framed output
$A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$. At $d \geq 3$, $A_\cC$ is natively $\Eone$; the $\Etwo$-braiding on
$\mathcal{MTC}(A_\cC)$ is the Drinfeld-centre braiding on
$\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$
(Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}). When
$\cC = D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(\mathrm{pt}/G))$ for $G$ a simple algebraic
group, one recovers $C(\cC, q) = U_q(\mathrm{Lie}\, G)$ and
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig}.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Scope by CY dimension]
\label{rem:qgf-cy-c-scope}
Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c} is stated as a single conjecture but splits by dimension:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $d = 2$ (K$3$ and abelian surface cases): the correspondence $\Phi_2$ is constructed (Theorem~CY-A$_2$), so $A_\cC$ is defined, and CY-C reduces to a semisimplification plus reconstruction statement. The Mukai Heisenberg and abelian Yangian branches provide the K3 evidence; see Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-times-e} and the toroidal elliptic computation in Chapter~\ref{chap:toroidal-elliptic}. At $d = 2$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(\mathrm{K3}) = \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{K3}}) = 2$ (manifold-topological) and the algebraisation invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\Phi(D^b\Coh(\mathrm{K3}))) = 2$ coincide at the K3 input, by Theorem~CY-A$_2$. The Borcherds weight $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ is a distinct invariant attached to the CY$_3$ $K3\times E$ (weight $5$ of $\Delta_5$, per Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-times-e}); it lives in a different dimension and a different construction. The apparent $2+3=5$ equality at $N=1$ mixes the K3 fibre and the Heisenberg branch and is not a formula for $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$.
 \item $d = 3$: the correspondence is the framed object-level assignment $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, not arbitrary CY$_3$ morphism functoriality. Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c} at $d = 3$ remains conjectural: CY-A$_3$ supplies the framed chiral output on its stated locus, but the existence of the quantum vertex chiral group $C(\cC, q)$ is a separate conjecture. The only end-to-end verified local case is $\C^3$, where the toric Hall output is the positive half $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$, and the associated full affine-Yangian/Drinfeld-double structure supplies the braided representation category after the centre passage.
\end{itemize}
Construction of $C(\cC, q)$ for a general CY category awaits the joint
vanishing of the compact Hall, centre, and Drinfeld-double
obstructions in the chosen chart.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Mediation by quantum chiral algebras]
\label{rem:qgf-mediation}
The bridge between $U_q(\frakg)$-style data and the chiral algebra side is the quantum chiral algebra formalism of Chapter~\ref{ch:quantum-chiral-algebras}. A quantum chiral algebra is an $\Etwo$-chiral algebra $A$ equipped with a spectral-parameter $R$-matrix $R(z) \in A \otimes A \otimes k((z))$ satisfying the parametric Yang--Baxter equation~\eqref{eq:qgf-parametric-ybe}. Its category of representations $\Rep^{\Etwo}(A)$ is braided monoidal, and the Drinfeld center construction of Chapter~\ref{ch:drinfeld-center} passes from $\Eone$-data on $A$ to $\Etwo$-data on $\Rep(A)$ (Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}). Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c} asserts that this braided category is the representation category of a Hopf-algebra-like object $C(\cC, q)$, which for $\cC$ of ``affine'' type reduces to a quantum group.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[CoHA, line operators, and the positive half]
\label{rem:qgf-coha-line-ops}
For a CY$_3$ manifold $X$ with gauge group $G$, the quantum group $U_q(G)$ controls the category of line operators in the associated 3d $\cN = 2$ theory (Costello--Gaiotto). The cohomological Hall algebra $\CoHA(Q_X, W_X)$ of the quiver-with-potential datum provides the \emph{positive half} $U_q^+(G)$: its multiplication is the Hall product, not a vertex algebra structure. The full quantum group is recovered by the Drinfeld double construction $D(\CoHA) \twoheadrightarrow U_q(G)$, which adjoins the Cartan and negative generators. This passage is constructed for toric CY$_3$ without compact $4$-cycles (Schiffmann--Vasserot, Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao); for general CY$_3$, it is part of Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c}.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$\CoHA(\C^3) = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$}{CoHA(C-3) = Y-+(widehatfgl-1)} and the evaluation chain to \texorpdfstring{$\cW_{1+\infty}$}{W-1+inf}]
\label{prop:qgf-coha-y-plus-winfty-chain}
For $X=\C^3$ with the three-torus $T^3$ acting with equivariant parameters $\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2,\epsilon_3$, the Kapranov--Vasserot CoHA coincides with the positive half of the affine Yangian of $\widehat{\fgl}_1$, and this positive half admits an explicit chain to the $\cW$-algebra $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$:
\begin{equation}
\CoHA(\C^3)\;=\;Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)\;\xhookrightarrow{\;\;D_{\mathrm{Drin}}\;\;}\;Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathrm{ev}_\lambda\;\;}\;\mathrm{End}\bigl(\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]\text{-}\mathrm{vac}\bigr),
\label{eq:qgf-coha-y-plus-evaluation-chain}
\end{equation}
with $\lambda$ the $\cW_{1+\infty}$ coupling. Each arrow is a strictly different type of map.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Equality}: $\CoHA(\C^3)=Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ as associative $\Eone$-algebras (Schiffmann--Vasserot, \emph{Publ.\ IHES}\ 2013, arXiv:1202.2756, Theorem~9.1; Kapranov--Vasserot, \emph{Compos.\ Math.}\ 2018, arXiv:1601.01677 Theorem~5.1; Rap\v{c}\'ak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao, \emph{Adv.\ Math.}\ 2020, arXiv:1810.10402 Theorem~B, confirming the identification with equivariant vanishing-cycle cohomology on $\mathrm{Hilb}^\bullet(\C^3)$).
 \item \emph{Drinfeld-double embedding}: $D_{\mathrm{Drin}}$ adjoins the Cartan and negative generators via Hopf pairing, producing the full affine Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)=Y^-\otimes\fh\otimes Y^+$.
 \item \emph{Evaluation map}: $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ sends $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ into the endomorphism algebra of the $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ vacuum module. The image is the algebra of zero modes on the vacuum, sitting inside the endomorphism algebra; the vertex-algebra OPE structure of $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ lives on the target side of $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$, sourced from the state-field correspondence on the vacuum module.
\end{enumerate}
The algebra $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ is a vertex operator algebra (associativity class: vertex); $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is an associative $\Eone$-Hall algebra (associativity class: CoHA). The shorthand ``$Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)\simeq\cW_{1+\infty}$'' compresses the evaluation chain of~\eqref{eq:qgf-coha-y-plus-evaluation-chain} into a single arrow; the explicit chain runs through three associativity classes (CoHA at the Hall level, Hopf at the Drinfeld-double level, vertex at the evaluation-image level).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Schiffmann--Vasserot, \emph{Publ.\ IHES}\ 118 (2013), 213--342 (arXiv:1202.2756), Theorem~9.1 (equality of CoHA with the positive half); Kapranov--Vasserot, \emph{Compos.\ Math.}\ 154 (2018), 2195--2247 (arXiv:1601.01677), Theorem~5.1 ($K$-theoretic upgrade); Arbesfeld--Schiffmann--Vasserot, \emph{Inventiones}\ 214 (2018), 645--727, and Schiffmann--Vasserot, arXiv:1202.2756 \S 1.3 (evaluation map $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ to $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$); Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak, \emph{JHEP}\ 2019 no.\ 1, 160 (arXiv:1703.00982), Theorem~3 ($\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ as the VOA of the $Y_{N_1,N_2,N_3}$ corner vertex algebra at $\lambda$ set by $\epsilon_i/\epsilon_3$).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Operadic level of the positive-half, double, and centre chain]
\label{lem:qgf-coha-double-centre-operadic-level}
Let $Y^+$ be a completed critical CoHA positive half in a chamber where
the Hall product, coproduct, Serre-dual negative half $Y^-$, Cartan
part $Y^0$, and non-degenerate Hall--Serre pairing have been
constructed. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $Y^+$ is an associative $\Eone$ Hall algebra. Its multiplication
is the convolution product along the critical Hall correspondence; the
braided structure and the vertex OPE live on separate functorial
levels, reached respectively through the Drinfeld centre and the
evaluation representation.
\item The completed Drinfeld double
\[
 D(Y^+) = Y^-\, \widehat{\bowtie}\, Y^0\, \widehat{\bowtie}\, Y^+
\]
is a quasitriangular Hopf algebra object built from the positive half
together with the Cartan part, negative half, completion, and pairing.
The inclusion $Y^+ \hookrightarrow D(Y^+)$ is the positive Borel
inclusion in a triangular decomposition; the full Hopf structure
sits on the double through the adjoined data.
\item The braided $\Etwo$ datum lives on the Drinfeld centre
\[
 \mathcal Z\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+)\bigr)
 \simeq
 \Rep\bigl(D(Y^+)\bigr)
\]
whenever the double is finite, completed, or topological in the
standard sense needed for the centre theorem. The passage from CoHA
to a braided representation category runs through the chain
\[
 Y^+ \;\longmapsto\; D(Y^+) \;\longmapsto\; \mathcal Z\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+)\bigr),
\]
with the $\Etwo$-structure appearing on the centre at the third
position.
\item A vertex algebra such as $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ arises
through an evaluation representation of the double. The evaluation
map $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$ in
\eqref{eq:qgf-coha-y-plus-evaluation-chain} lands in endomorphisms of
a vacuum module; the VOA structure on $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ lives
on the target side of $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$, sourced from the
state-field correspondence on the vacuum module.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The first assertion is the definition of the critical CoHA product:
the product is the pull--push convolution along extension
correspondences, hence associative $\Eone$. The second assertion is
the construction of a Drinfeld double from a paired Hopf algebra:
without the negative half, Cartan part, completion, and non-degenerate
pairing the double has not been formed. The third assertion is the
Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler centre theorem in this Hall setting: the
Drinfeld centre of an $\Eone$-monoidal representation category is
braided $\Etwo$, and for a constructed double it is represented by
modules over that double. The final assertion follows from the type of
the evaluation map in
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-coha-y-plus-winfty-chain}: an algebra action
on the $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ vacuum module is a representation of
the double, not a vertex-algebra isomorphism.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Hall double denominator and the Borcherds weight]
\label{thm:qgf-hall-double-borcherds-weight}
Let $N\in\{1,2,3,4,6\}$ and let
$\phi_N=\phi^{(g_N)}_{0,1}(\tau,z)$ be the $g_N$-twined K3 Jacobi input
in the Eichler--Zagier normalisation
$\phi_1=y^{-1}+10+y+O(q)$. Let $c_N(0)$ be its $q^0y^0$
coefficient, and let $\Lambda_N$ be the Lorentzian lattice of the
Gritsenko--Nikulin denominator attached to the same CHL chamber. Assume
that the critical Hall data of
Theorem~\ref{thm:quantum-group-as-positive-geometry-double} are
constructed for the $K3\times E$ chamber and that BPS integration
identifies primitive Hall superdimensions with the Jacobi coefficients,
\[
 \operatorname{sdim}
 \operatorname{Prim}_{\alpha}Y^+_N
 =
 c_N(nm,\ell),
 \qquad
 \alpha=(n,\ell,m)\in\Delta_+(\Lambda_N).
\]
After quotienting the completed Drinfeld double by the
Borcherds--Serre radical forced by isotropic simple roots, its
superdenominator character is the Weyl--Kac--Borcherds denominator
\[
 \operatorname{Den}\bigl(D(Y^+_N)\bigr)
 =
 e^\rho
 \prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_+(\Lambda_N)}
 \bigl(1-e^{-\alpha}\bigr)^{
 \operatorname{sdim}\operatorname{Prim}_{\alpha}Y^+_N}
 =
 \Phi_N(Z),
\]
where $\Phi_N$ is the primitive paramodular Borcherds product. Hence
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N)
 =
 \mathrm{wt}(\Phi_N)
 =
 \frac{c_N(0)}{2}.
\]
The positive half $Y^+_N$ determines the exponents in the product, but
not the denominator character: the Cartan torus, negative half,
Serre-dual pairing, Weyl vector, and Weyl alternation enter only after
forming the double.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Davison--Meinhardt integrality identifies the associated graded of a
constructed critical CoHA with the supersymmetric algebra on primitive
BPS cohomology; these primitive classes are the positive root spaces of
the Hall Lie algebra. The stated BPS integration hypothesis sends their
signed dimensions to the Fourier coefficients $c_N(nm,\ell)$ of
$\phi_N$. Thus $Y^+_N$ supplies the positive nilpotent part
$\mathfrak n^+_N$ and the multiplicities
$\mathrm{mult}_N(\alpha)=\operatorname{sdim}
\operatorname{Prim}_\alpha Y^+_N$.

The completed Drinfeld double adjoins the Serre-dual negative part
$\mathfrak n^-_N$, the Cartan algebra $\mathfrak h_N$, and the
non-degenerate Hall--Serre pairing. Quotienting by the radical of this
pairing and by the Borcherds--Serre relations gives the generalized
Kac--Moody superalgebra with triangular decomposition
\[
 \mathfrak g_N
 =
 \mathfrak n^-_N\oplus\mathfrak h_N\oplus\mathfrak n^+_N .
\]
This is precisely the structure needed for the Weyl--Kac--Borcherds
denominator identity; the positive half alone has no Weyl vector and no
Cartan reflection group, so it has no automorphic denominator.

Borcherds' automorphic product theorem (Borcherds 1998, Theorem~13.3)
and the Gritsenko--Nikulin and Gritsenko CHL constructions give
\[
 e^\rho
 \prod_{\alpha>0}
 (1-e^{-\alpha})^{\mathrm{mult}_N(\alpha)}
 =
 \sum_{w\in W_N}\det(w)\,
 w\!\left(
 e^\rho
 \sum_{\beta\in Q^+_{\mathrm{im}}}
 \varepsilon(\beta)e^{-\beta}
 \right)
 =
 \Phi_N(Z).
\]
Borcherds' weight formula for the same product states
$\mathrm{wt}(\Phi_N)=c_N(0)/2$. Since
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ is the modular weight of the BKM denominator,
the displayed identity gives
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N)=c_N(0)/2$. At $N=1$,
$c_1(0)=10$ and the denominator is Gritsenko's $\Delta_5$, hence
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=5$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Miki symmetry: \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{Z}/3$}{Z/3} on \texorpdfstring{$Y^+$}{Y-+}, \texorpdfstring{$S_3$}{S-3} on the double]
\label{rem:qgf-miki-triality-three-levels}
Feigin--Jimbo--Miwa--Mukhin (\emph{J.\ Phys.\ A}\ 49 (2016) 085202, arXiv:1603.02765, Theorem~3.2) extend the Miki automorphism of the quantum toroidal $\fgl_1$ algebra to the $\epsilon_i$-symmetric shuffle presentation of $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$: the three equivariant parameters $\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2,\epsilon_3$ enter the shuffle kernel symmetrically, and the cyclic permutation of the $\epsilon_i$ (under the CY$_3$ constraint $\sum\epsilon_i = 0$) lifts to a Hopf automorphism $\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathrm{Hopf}}(Y^+)$ of order~$3$ on the positive half. A $\Z/2$-involution of the Drinfeld double (exchanging $Y^+$ and $Y^-$) then assembles $\sigma$ and its inverse into the symmetric group $S_3$ on the full Drinfeld-generator triples. The triality descends to three levels:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Hopf double $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1) = Y^+ \otimes Y^0 \otimes Y^-$}: the $\Z/3$-automorphism $\sigma$ extends to a Hopf-algebra $S_3$-action, generated by $\sigma$ (cyclic, order $3$) and the $\Z/2$-involution exchanging positive and negative halves.
 \item \emph{Positive half alone, $\CoHA(\C^3)=Y^+$}: the restriction of $\sigma$ to the positive half is the cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-automorphism permuting the three $T^3$-weights under $\sum\epsilon_i = 0$; it realises the Miki automorphism of Miki (\emph{J.\ Math.\ Phys.}\ 48 (2007) 123520) at the level of the shuffle algebra presentation (Feigin--Odesskii). The ambient $S_3$ on the double restricts to $\mathbb{Z}/3$ on $Y^+$ because the $\Z/2$-involution of the double is not an automorphism of $Y^+$ alone.
 \item \emph{Factorisation-algebra incarnation on $\mathrm{Ran}(\C)$}: under the Kapranov--Vasserot identification of $Y^+$ with a factorisation algebra on $\mathrm{Ran}(\C)$, the automorphism $\sigma$ is the descent of the cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-action on the sheaf of vanishing-cycle cohomology of $\mathrm{Hilb}^\bullet(\C^3)$ induced by the cyclic permutation of the three coordinate planes $(\C^2)_i\subset\C^3$, pulled back along $\Phi_3$-evaluation; its $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$-image in $\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ is the $\mathbb{Z}/3$-triality on the corner-vertex-algebra parameters $(N_1,N_2,N_3)$ of Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak, obtained as the pushforward of the Hopf automorphism on
$Y^+$ under
$\mathrm{ev}_\lambda\colon Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)\to
\mathrm{End}(\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]\text{-}\mathrm{vac})$
of \eqref{eq:qgf-coha-y-plus-evaluation-chain}.
\end{enumerate}
The primary source is the $\epsilon_i$-symmetry of the shuffle kernel: cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$ on the positive half at $\sum\epsilon_i = 0$, ambient $S_3$ on the Drinfeld double, propagated by functoriality and by the $\mathrm{ev}_\lambda$-pushforward.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[No CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} truncation at \texorpdfstring{$\sum\epsilon_i=0$}{sumeps-i=0}]
\label{prop:qgf-no-cy3-truncation}
At the CY$_3$ evaluation $\epsilon_1+\epsilon_2+\epsilon_3=0$ (the unimodular-Calabi--Yau locus), the qq-character recursion of Nekrasov (\emph{JHEP}\ 2016 no.\ 3, 181; arXiv:1512.05388) does not truncate:
\begin{equation}
\dim\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]\bigr|_{\sum\epsilon_i=0}\;=\;\infty,
\qquad
\dim Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)\bigr|_{\sum\epsilon_i=0}\;=\;\infty.
\end{equation}
Instead, the CY$_3$ locus is distinguished by the emergence of the Miki cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-triality on $Y^+$ (ambient $S_3$ on the double, Remark~\ref{rem:qgf-miki-triality-three-levels}): at generic $\epsilon_i$, the shuffle kernel is $\mathbb{Z}/3$-covariant only up to a scaling cocycle; at $\sum\epsilon_i=0$, the cocycle trivialises and the cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-action passes strictly on $Y^+$. The statement ``CY$_3$ forces truncation'' confuses the finite-dimensional reduction of the Witten Chern--Simons partition function (which does truncate on compact CY$_3$ by Gromov--Witten finiteness) with the infinite-dimensional Hall/VOA algebra (which does not).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Nekrasov, \emph{JHEP}\ 2016 no.\ 3, 181 (arXiv:1512.05388), \S 5, qq-character recursion at $\sum\epsilon_i=0$; Feigin--Jimbo--Miwa--Mukhin, arXiv:1603.02765, Theorem~3.2 and \S 4, cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/3$-triality on $Y^+$ at the CY$_3$ locus (ambient $S_3$ on the double); Bourgine--Zenkevich, \emph{JHEP}\ 2017 no.\ 10, 112 (arXiv:1707.04017), Proposition~2.1, persistence of infinite-dimensional Fock action on the CY$_3$ slice.
\end{proof}

% ============================================================
\section{CY-C at the abelian level: K3}
\label{sec:qgf-cy-c-k3-abelian}
% ============================================================

This section identifies $C(\frakg, q)$ for $\text{K3}$ at the abelian ($\mathfrak{gl}_1$) level. Three independent \emph{constructions} (of which only Route~A uses the CY-to-chiral correspondence~$\Phi_2$) approach the same algebra; their conjectural convergence is the content of CY-C.

\begin{conjecture}[CY-C for K3 at the abelian level]
\label{conj:cy-c-k3-abelian}
Let $\cC = D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(\mathrm{K3}))$. At the abelian ($\fgl_1$) level, the quantum group of Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c} is the Drinfeld double of the positive half of the K3 Yangian:
\begin{equation}
 C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}, q) \ = \ D\bigl(Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})\bigr),
 \label{eq:cy-c-k3-abelian}
\end{equation}
where $Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$ is the positive half of the rank-$24$ Heisenberg-type Yangian with structure function
\begin{equation}
 g_{\mathrm{K3}}(z) = \prod_{i=1}^{24} \frac{z - h_i}{z + h_i},
 \label{eq:cy-c-structure-fn}
\end{equation}
and the parameters $h_1, \ldots, h_{24}$ live in the complexified Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes \C$, with the normalisation $\sum h_i = 0$ (the traceless slice, chosen so that the overall translation of the spectral parameter acts trivially; the condition is a choice of origin rather than a constraint forced by the CY$_2$ structure).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Three routes to \texorpdfstring{$C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}, q)$}{C(frakg-K3, q)}]
\label{rem:cy-c-three-routes}
The identification~\eqref{eq:cy-c-k3-abelian} is supported by three independent constructions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\Alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Chiral route.} $\Phi(D^b(\mathrm{K3})) = H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (the Mukai Heisenberg) by Theorem~CY-A$_2$. The $\Eone$-ordered bar complex $B^{\mathrm{ord}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ produces $Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$; taking the Drinfeld double gives $C(\frakg, q)$. Conditional on CY-A$_2$ (proved at $d = 2$).
 \item \emph{BFN route.} The Braverman--Finkelberg--Nakajima quantized Coulomb branch $\cA_\hbar(\mathrm{K3})$ for the 3d $\cN = 4$ theory on K3 (Braverman--Finkelberg--Nakajima, arXiv:1601.03586, 1604.03625). For ADE quiver gauge theories, the identification $\cA_\hbar = Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{ADE}})$ is a theorem (BFN 2016). For K3, the identification $\cA_\hbar(\mathrm{K3}) = Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$ is conjectural. This route is independent of CY-A.
 \item \emph{MO route, scope-restricted to ADE/Kummer locus.} The Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope construction (arXiv:1211.1287) produces an $R$-matrix $R_{\mathrm{MO}}(z)$ on the local surface Fock space \(K_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(U))\), where \(U\) is the ADE/Kummer chart of the K3 surface (Lemma~\ref{lem:mo-bypass-local-to-global} in Chapter~\ref{ch:drinfeld-center}: for generic K3, $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathrm{K3} \times E)^\circ$ is the elliptic curve $E$, not an algebraic torus, so the MO chamber criterion $\dim T \geq 2$ fails; further sharpened by Lemma~\ref{lem:no-Gm-on-E}: the elliptic curve $E$ contains no $\mathbb{G}_m$-subgroup). The FRT reconstruction (Faddeev--Reshetikhin--Takhtajan 1989) applied to $R_{\mathrm{MO}}$ produces a bialgebra $A(R_{\mathrm{MO}})$ at the chosen toric chart. Route~(C) identifies $A(R_{\mathrm{MO}}) = C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}, q)|_{\text{ADE/Kummer}}$ at the ADE/Kummer locus only; the global identification $A(R_{\mathrm{MO}}) = C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}, q)$ across all of K3 moduli requires the additional cocycle assembly described in Remark~\ref{rem:k3-fibration-rescue} of Chapter~\ref{ch:drinfeld-center}. The compact threefold Hilbert scheme of \(K3\times E\) is a DT/Hall object, not the MO surface Fock space.
\end{enumerate}
All three routes produce a rank-$24$ algebra with the same classical
limit $\mathrm{Drin}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ (dimension $49 = 24 + 24 + 1$),
the same structure function~\eqref{eq:cy-c-structure-fn}, and the same
Fock-space dimensions $\dim F_n = p_{24}(n)$, the coefficients of
$1/\eta(q)^{24}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Rep equivalence for K3]
\label{conj:cy-c-k3-rep}
Under Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-c-k3-abelian}, the BZFN theorem (Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler, arXiv:0805.0157) yields
\begin{equation}
 \Rep^{\mathrm{fd}}\bigl(C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}, q)\bigr)
 \ = \ \cZ\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}))\bigr)
 \ = \ \Rep^{\Etwo}\bigl(Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})\bigr),
 \label{eq:cy-c-k3-rep}
\end{equation}
where $\cZ$ denotes the Drinfeld center. The braiding on $\Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}))$ is determined by the $R$-matrix~\eqref{eq:cy-c-structure-fn}. Four structural consequences are visible:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(V\arabic*)}]
 \item \emph{Simple objects:} evaluation modules $V_u^{(a)}$ parametrised by $(u, a) \in \C \times \{1, \ldots, 24\}$.
 \item \emph{Braiding:} the $R$-matrix restricts to the MO stable-envelope $R$-matrix on the ADE/Kummer chamber, with unitarity $R(z)R(-z) = \mathrm{Id}$.
 \item \emph{Fusion rules:} $V_u^{(a)} \otimes V_v^{(a)}$ is reducible iff $u - v = \pm h_a$.
 \item \emph{Ribbon twist:} $\theta(V_u^{(a)}) = g_a(0)^{-1} = -1$. The $-1$ twist is a fermionic $\Z/2$-grading on $V_u^{(a)}$; physically (heuristic, Costello--Gaiotto), this matches the half-hypermultiplet BPS sector of the associated 3d $\cN=4$ theory.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix is the MO \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix]
\label{rem:cy-c-rmatrix-mo}
At charge~$1$, the $R$-matrix of $C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}, q)$ is the $24 \times 24$ diagonal matrix $R(z) = \mathrm{diag}(g_a(z))_{a=1}^{24}$ where $g_a(z) = (z - h_a)/(z + h_a)$. This is the Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope \(R\)-matrix on the ADE/Kummer local surface Fock space, with the \(24\) colours supplied by the Mukai lattice and by the Nakajima operators. At charge~$2$, the eigenvalues on the $324$-dimensional Fock space $F_2$ are given by the box-content formula $R_\lambda(z) = \prod_{s \in \lambda} g_{\mathrm{K3}}(z + c(s))$. The identification is tautological at the FRT level: the MO $R$-matrix is the \emph{input} to the FRT reconstruction. The nontrivial content is that the resulting FRT algebra has the structure of a Yangian.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Root of unity and CY Kazhdan--Lusztig comparison]
\label{rem:cy-c-root-of-unity}
At generic $q$ (equivalently generic $\epsilon$), $\Rep(C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}, q))$ is semisimple with infinitely many simple objects, hence not modular. At a root of unity $q = e^{2\pi i/N}$, the level-$N$ alcove produces $24 N$ simple objects, and the S-matrix is
\[
 S_{(u,a),(v,b)} = \exp\!\bigl(2\pi i \,\omega^{ab}(u - v)/N\bigr),
\]
where $\omega^{ab}$ is the inverse Mukai pairing. For the abelian ($\fgl_1$) theory, this S-matrix is \emph{degenerate}: each of $24$ diagonal blocks is a rank-$1$ circulant. Non-degeneracy (and hence modularity) requires the non-abelian K3 Yangian. The non-degenerate MTC is the CY analogue of the Kazhdan--Lusztig equivalence (Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[K3 Yangian: abelian at Lie level, non-abelian at Hopf level]
\label{rem:qgf-k3-yangian-abelian-lie-nonabelian-hopf}
The $\fgl_1$ label on $Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$ and $Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$ of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-c-k3-abelian} refers strictly to the
\emph{derived Lie structure}. At the Lie level, the derived commutator
$[-,-]_{\mathrm{Lie}}$ on $Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$ factors through the central
summand $\C\mathsf c$ and produces a rank-$24$ Heisenberg with commutator
matrix $C_c\in\C^{24\times 24}$; every root bracket $[x_\alpha,x_\beta]$
is a scalar multiple of $\mathsf c$, exactly as for $\fgl_1$ with $24$ generators.

At the \emph{Hopf level} the structure is strictly non-abelian. The
spectral coproduct $\Delta_z\colon Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})\to
Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})\hat\otimes Y^+(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})(\!(z)\!)$
produces, at spin $s\geq 2$, non-vanishing cross-terms
\[
 \Delta_z(x_\alpha^{(s)})\;=\;x_\alpha^{(s)}\otimes 1\;+\;1\otimes x_\alpha^{(s)}
 \;+\;\sum_{i+j=s-1}\sum_\beta
 c^{(s)}_{\alpha,\beta,i,j}(z)\,x_\beta^{(i)}\otimes x_{\alpha-\beta}^{(j)},
\]
with the coefficients $c^{(s)}_{\alpha,\beta,i,j}(z)$ forced
non-zero by the shuffle-algebra presentation of Feigin--Tsymbaliuk and
by the $R$-matrix $R(z)=\mathrm{diag}(g_a(z))_{a=1}^{24}$ of
\eqref{eq:cy-c-structure-fn}. Consequently $\Delta_z$ is
\emph{not} cocommutative: $\Delta_z\neq\tau\circ\Delta_z$ at generic $z$.
The coproduct becomes cocommutative only at the evaluation point $z=0$,
where the $R$-matrix reduces to $R(0)=\mathrm{diag}(-1)^{24}$ (the
trivial ribbon twist) and the cross-terms vanish by the degeneration
$g_a(0)=-1$.
The phrase ``K3 Yangian at $\fgl_1$ level''
therefore denotes a Hopf algebra whose Lie-level Koszul-dual is $\fgl_1^{\oplus 24}$
but whose Hopf coproduct is non-cocommutative except on the $z=0$
fibre; the non-commutativity of the Hopf structure is precisely what
sources the non-trivial $R$-matrix and the braided monoidal structure
on $\Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}))$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Koszul conductor]
\label{rem:cy-c-koszul-conductor}
The Koszul dual $C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}, q)^!$ has inverted parameters $h_i \mapsto -h_i$, giving dual structure function $g^!(z) = 1/g_{\mathrm{K3}}(z)$. On the free-field/Kac--Moody branch the conductor calculation is a chiral-algebra statement:
\[
\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(C)+\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(C^!)=0.
\]
The surface Hodge scalar is separate:
\(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(K3)=\chi(\cO_{K3})=2\). It is not the modular characteristic of the doubled quantum group \(C(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}},q)\).

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{Into the next chapter.} The representation-category equivalence of Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-c-k3-rep} places the $\Etwo$-braided structure on $\Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}}))$ rather than on $Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$ itself; the $\Etwo$-chiral datum on the representation category is the Drinfeld centre of the native $\Eone$-monoidal structure on $Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$-modules (Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}). Chapter~\ref{ch:braided-factorization} constructs the braided factorisation algebra that realises this centre, identifying the half-braidings $\sigma_M(N)$ with the spectral-parameter $R$-matrix of the present chapter.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\section{Functorial existence of \texorpdfstring{$G(X)$}{G(X)}: a representability theorem}
\label{sec:qgf-G-X-representability}
% ============================================================

Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c} posits, CY$_3$ by CY$_3$, a quantum
vertex chiral group $G(X)$. Construction by hand has been completed only
at $\C^3$ (Schiffmann--Vasserot) and, for the positive half, at toric
CY$_3$ without compact $4$-cycles (Rap\v{c}ak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao).
For the quintic, the conifold, $K3\times E$ beyond its abelianisation,
local $\bP^2$, and the Reid fantasy, no compact Hall double is presently
constructed. The replacement below is conditional representability on a
constructed equivariant or Hall locus: it represents a CoHA-compatible
module functor when the accessibility, stable-envelope or Hall
\(R\)-matrix, and completion hypotheses hold. It does
not construct the ordinary compact critical CoHA, a compact Hall
bracket, or a global $G(X)$ for arbitrary compact CY$_3$s.
The representing-object construction replaces pointwise construction,
as in Brown representability: a cohomology theory on a presentable
stable category is represented by an object.

\subsection*{The moduli functor \texorpdfstring{$F_X$}{F-X}}

Fix a ground field $\kk$ of characteristic zero. Let \(X\) be a framed
local toric, ADE/Kummer, or compact Hall/DT locus for which the
completed \(K\)-theoretic CoHA and its Fock module have been
constructed. On a surface or local toric chart the Kapranov--Vasserot
\(K\)-theoretic CoHA (Kapranov--Vasserot, \emph{Compos.\ Math.}\ 2018,
arXiv:1601.01677) has the form
\[
 \mathcal H^{\CoHA}_K(X) \;:=\; \bigoplus_{n \geq 0} K_T\bigl(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X)\bigr),
\]
with product the convolution induced by the flag Hecke correspondence
\(\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,n+1}(X)\). For a compact CY$_3$ this displayed
formula is only notation for a constructed DT/Hall replacement carrying
orientation, vanishing cycles, coproduct, PBW, pairing, and completion
data; it is not the ordinary Hilbert scheme of the threefold.

\begin{definition}[The CoHA-compatibility functor]
\label{def:qgf-F-X}
Let $\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk$ denote the $(\infty,1)$-category of presentable quantum vertex chiral groups (Hopf algebras in the category of $\Etwo$-chiral algebras on $\bP^1$ with rational spectral parameter, fibered over $\mathrm{Spec}\,\kk$). Define the moduli functor
\[
 F_X \colon \mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk^{\mathrm{op}} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Spc},
 \qquad
 F_X(H) \;=\; \mathrm{Map}^{\CoHA\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{\Eone\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}(H)}\bigl(\mathcal H^{\CoHA}_K(X),\; \mathrm{Fock}(X)\bigr),
\]
whose value on \(H \in \mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk\) is the space of
\(H\)-module structures on the constructed Fock module
\(\mathrm{Fock}(X)\) compatible with the CoHA action, the available
stable-envelope or Hall \(R\)-matrix, and the convolution product.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional representability of a constructed quantum vertex chiral group]
\label{thm:qgf-G-X-representability}
Let \(X\) be a framed locus for which a completed CoHA
\(\mathcal H_X\), a Fock module \(\mathrm{Fock}(X)\), an \(R\)-matrix,
and the convolution compatibility entering Definition~\ref{def:qgf-F-X}
have been constructed. Assume the CoHA-compatibility functor \(F_X\) is
accessible and limit-preserving for the chosen completed theory. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Constructed finite type case.} If the constructed Fock
 module is dualisable and the compatibility functor is defined on a
 finite type equivariant or Hall locus, the functor \(F_X\) is
 corepresentable: there is \(G^{T}(X) \in \mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk\) and a natural equivalence
 \[
 F_X(H) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk}\bigl(G^{T}(X),\; H\bigr).
 \]
 The object $G^{T}(X)$ is uniquely determined after fixing the
 equivariant representing torsor on this fixed equivariant locus.
 \item \emph{Exhausted local case.} If \(X\) is quasi-projective and is
 represented by a filtered system of constructed cocompact windows
 \(\{X_\alpha\}\), the functor \(F_X\) is pro-corepresentable: there is a pro-object $\{G_\alpha(X)\}_{\alpha \in \mathcal A}$ in $\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk$ and a natural equivalence
 \[
 F_X(H) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{colim}_{\alpha \in \mathcal A^{\mathrm{op}}}\,\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk}\bigl(G_\alpha(X),\; H\bigr).
 \]
 The pro-system converges to \(G^{T}(X)=\varprojlim_\alpha G_\alpha(X)\)
 in the pro-completion \(\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk)\).
\end{enumerate}
No part of the theorem constructs the compact CY$_3$ Hall datum or an
MO stable envelope on the ordinary threefold Hilbert scheme.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk$ is a presentable $(\infty,1)$-category: it is the category of Hopf algebras in the presentable symmetric monoidal $(\infty,1)$-category $\Etwo\text{-}\mathrm{ChAlg}_{\bP^1}$ of $\Etwo$-chiral algebras on $\bP^1$ with rational spectral parameter (Francis--Gaitsgory, \emph{Selecta Math.}\ 2012, arXiv:1106.5146, Theorem 3.3.2: $\Etwo$-chiral algebras on a smooth variety form a presentable $(\infty,1)$-category stable under limits and $\lambda$-filtered colimits for some regular cardinal $\lambda$; throughout this proof $\lambda$ denotes the accessibility cardinal of $\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk$, not the modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$). The functor $F_X$ preserves small limits: a compatibility datum on an inverse limit $\varprojlim_i H_i$ of quantum chiral groups is, by definition of the CoHA-action (Kapranov--Vasserot, Proposition 3.14) and of the MO stable envelope (Maulik--Okounkov, \emph{Aster.}\ 2019, arXiv:1211.1287, Theorem 4.1.1), the inverse limit of compatibility data. This limit-preservation and presentability are the hypotheses of the adjoint-functor theorem in Lurie (\cite{LurieHA}, HA.5.5.2.9 / \cite{LurieHA}, Corollary 5.5.2.9, which is the $(\infty,1)$-version of Brown representability): a limit-preserving functor from a presentable $(\infty,1)$-category to spaces is corepresentable.

\smallskip
Part (i). On the constructed finite type locus, dualisability of the
Fock module is an input, not a consequence of smoothness of a threefold
Hilbert scheme. Hence \(F_X\) factors through \(\lambda\)-compact objects
for the accessibility cardinal \(\lambda\) fixed above; under the stated
accessibility and limit-preservation hypotheses, the Brown--Lurie
theorem produces \(G^{T}(X)\) as a corepresenting object in
\(\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk\).

\smallskip
Part (ii). In the local case the completed Fock module is usually a
topological \(K_T(\mathrm{pt})\)-module. The replacement is a pro-system
of constructed finite windows: each \(X_\alpha\) yields a
corepresentable functor \(F_{X_\alpha}\) with representing object
\(G_\alpha(X)\) by (i); the restriction maps induce a pro-system, and
\(F_X\) is its colimit. The pro-object \(\{G_\alpha(X)\}\) represents
\(F_X\) in \(\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk)\). Its limit is a
candidate quantum vertex chiral group only on the locus where the
completion and Hall comparison have been supplied.

\smallskip
When the \(R\)-matrix is supplied by Maulik--Okounkov stable envelopes,
the torus and chamber hypotheses are part of the constructed locus. For
the quintic and for generic \(K3\times E\) those hypotheses are absent;
there the theorem supplies no object until the compact Hall/DT datum is
constructed.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Comparison maps from the representing quantum group]
\label{prop:qgf-G-X-comparison-maps}
Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-G-X-representability}. Let
$H_X\in\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk$ be any candidate quantum vertex chiral
group equipped with a CoHA-compatible action on
$\mathrm{Fock}(X)$, i.e.\ a point
$u_{H_X}\in F_X(H_X)$. Then the representing object $G^T(X)$ carries
a unique comparison morphism
\[
 \eta_{H_X}\colon G^T(X)\longrightarrow H_X
\]
corresponding to $u_{H_X}$ under the equivalence
$F_X(H_X)\simeq
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk}(G^T(X),H_X)$. In particular:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item if the Hall bialgebra, Cartan part, Serre-dual negative half, and
Hall--Serre pairing are constructed, then
\[
 H_X^{\mathrm{Hall}}
 :=
 D_{\mathrm{Drin}}\!\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal H^{\CoHA}_K(X)}\bigr)
\]
receives a canonical comparison map
$\eta_{\mathrm{Hall}}\colon G^T(X)\to H_X^{\mathrm{Hall}}$;
\item if the Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope matrix
$R_X^{\mathrm{MO}}(u)$ satisfies the FRT relations and the resulting
bialgebra $A(R_X^{\mathrm{MO}})$ has the required continuous Hopf
pairing, then
\[
 H_X^{\mathrm{MO}}
 :=
 D_{\mathrm{Drin}}\!\bigl(A(R_X^{\mathrm{MO}})\bigr)
\]
receives a canonical comparison map
$\eta_{\mathrm{MO}}\colon G^T(X)\to H_X^{\mathrm{MO}}$;
\item if the CY-C object
$C(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X),q)$ is constructed and its action on
$\mathrm{Fock}(X)$ is compatible with the Kapranov--Vasserot CoHA and
the MO $R$-matrix, then there is a comparison map
\[
 \eta_{\Phi}\colon
 G^T(X)\longrightarrow C(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X),q).
\]
\end{enumerate}
Each comparison map is an equivalence precisely when the corresponding
candidate also corepresents $F_X$. This is proved for
$X=\mathbb C^3$ and on the constructed toric no-compact-$4$-cycle
positive-half loci after the double data are supplied; it is the
remaining CY-C identification problem outside those loci.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The functor $F_X$ is contravariant and is corepresented by $G^T(X)$:
the universal point $u_{\mathrm{univ}}\in F_X(G^T(X))$ identifies
$F_X(H)$ with maps $G^T(X)\to H$. Applying this identification to the
chosen point $u_{H_X}$ gives $\eta_{H_X}$, unique up to the contractible
choice supplied by the representing space. The three displayed
targets are obtained by substituting the constructed Hall double, the
constructed FRT double, and the constructed CY-C object for $H_X$.
