Functoriality of the Drinfeld double requires exactly the pairing-
preserving Hopf data stated in \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)}. The last
map is the Yoneda comparison map after
\(C(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X),q)\) and its compatible action have been
constructed. A comparison map between two corepresenting
objects is an equivalence by the ordinary Yoneda lemma in the
$(\infty,1)$-category.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Three independent verification paths]
\label{rem:qgf-G-X-three-paths}
The MO-accessible representing object is constrained by three paths.

\emph{(A) Brown--Lurie adjoint-functor theorem.} As in the proof above: $F_X$ preserves limits, $\mathrm{QChirGrp}_\kk$ is presentable, hence $F_X$ is corepresentable. This is the abstract path; it gives no concrete presentation of $G(X)$.

\emph{(B) Kapranov--Vasserot CoHA-site.} The CoHA
$\mathcal H^{\CoHA}_K(X)$ defines a site whose covers are the Hecke
correspondences $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,n+1}(X)$. Sheafification of the
presheaf of Hopf-module structures on $\mathrm{Fock}(X)$ would give a
sheaf of Hopf algebras once the compact support, orientation, and
vanishing-cycle functoriality hypotheses are supplied. This path gives
generators by Hecke correspondence operators and relations by stable
envelope identities on constructed loci; it does not extend the
Schiffmann--Vasserot $\C^3$ presentation to arbitrary compact $X$.

\emph{(C) Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope.} On a surface or local toric chart \(U\) satisfying the MO torus-chamber hypotheses, the stable envelope
$\mathrm{Stab}_C \colon K_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(U)^T) \to
K_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(U))$ for a chamber $C$ produces an $R$-matrix
$R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u) = \mathrm{Stab}_{C_+}^{-1} \circ
\mathrm{Stab}_{C_-}$. FRT reconstruction applied to $R^{\mathrm{MO}}$
extracts a bialgebra $A(R^{\mathrm{MO}})$ on the positive half. A
Drinfeld double exists only after the continuous Hopf pairing, Cartan
part, and Serre-dual negative half are supplied. This path is concrete
but depends on the MO scope hypothesis $\mathrm{rk}\,T \geq 2$; it is not
an MO construction on the ordinary Hilbert scheme of a compact
threefold.

At $X = \C^3$ the three paths recover the affine Yangian
$Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$. On ADE/Kummer loci of $K3\times E$ they provide
compatible positive-half criteria for the rank-$24$ K3 Yangian of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-c-k3-abelian}; the compact Hall double remains
an additional construction.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Shifted-Poisson refinement]
\label{rem:qgf-G-X-shifted-poisson}
On the locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-G-X-representability}, the
representing object \(G^T(X)\) carries a canonical \((-1)\)-shifted
Poisson structure in the sense of Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi
(\emph{Publ.\ IHES}\ 2013, arXiv:1111.3209) and Safronov
(\emph{Doc.\ Math.}\ 2021, arXiv:1706.08725: braided Hopf algebras in
\(\mathrm{Pr}^L\) carry shifted-Poisson structures inherited from the
ambient \(\Etwo\)-category). The shift \(-1\) is forced by CY$_3$
dimension: for CY$_d$, the expected shift is \(2-d\). At \(d=2\) the
K3 accessible object has shift \(0\) and is a Poisson Hopf algebra
compatible with the symmetric Mukai pairing. At \(d=3\), when \(G^T(X)\)
exists, it is a \((-1)\)-shifted Hopf object. This refines the
accessible-locus Hopf-algebra statement of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-G-X-representability}; it is not a construction of
global \(G(X)\) for arbitrary compact CY$_3$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c}]
\label{rem:qgf-G-X-vs-conj-CY-C}
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-G-X-representability} produces $G^T(X)$ as a representing object only on the stated MO-accessible equivariant locus. Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c} asserts the further statement that the resulting object equals $C(\cC, q)$ for $\cC = D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(X))$, i.e.\ that the representing object \emph{equals} the output of the CY-to-chiral correspondence $\Phi$ after the framed $d=3$ specialisation. The representability theorem thus separates two questions: (1) does the equivariant representing object exist on this locus? (conditional on the theorem's hypotheses); (2) does it identify with $\Phi(\cC)$ in the quantum vertex chiral group sense? (Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgf-cy-c}; verified for $\C^3$, conjectural in general). The theorem narrows the pointwise-construction obstruction on accessible loci; it does not construct global $G(X)$ for arbitrary compact CY$_3$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Representing object and presentation data]
\label{rem:qgf-G-X-scope}
The representing object $G^T(X)$ produced by Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-G-X-representability} is a priori an abstract Hopf algebra in $\Etwo\text{-}\mathrm{ChAlg}_{\bP^1}$ on the chosen equivariant locus; its identification with a Yangian or a Drinfeld double requires supplementary input (e.g.\ the MO $R$-matrix, the CoHA shuffle presentation). For general smooth proper CY$_3$ outside the MO-accessible locus, existence of a global $G(X)$ remains conjectural. The theorem converts the accessible-locus problem from ``construct $G^T(X)$ pointwise'' into the narrower problem ``present $G^T(X)$ by generators and relations''.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Lattice-polarised \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{g}_L$}{g-L} family: generic \texorpdfstring{$G(X)$}{G(X)} across Nikulin polarisations]
\label{conj:lattice-polarised-G-X}
For each even primitive hyperbolic lattice $L$ of signature $(1, t)$ with $L^\perp$ containing a hyperbolic plane (Dolgachev--admissible Nikulin polarisation), the $L$-polarised K3 family $\mathcal{M}_L = \Omega_L / \mathrm{O}^+(T)$ with $T = L^\perp$ carries a Borcherds--Kac--Moody superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_L$ whose denominator is the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery paramodular form $\Phi_L$ obtained as the Borcherds lift of the weak Jacobi form $\phi_{0, 1}$ at index $d$ with $L = \langle 2d \rangle$ in the rank-$1$ case. The universal Borcherds-weight identity extends as
\[
 \kBKM(\Phi_L) \;=\; c_L(0)/2,
\]
with $c_L(0)$ the zero Fourier coefficient of the Jacobi input; at $d = 1$, $c_1(0) = 10$ gives $\kBKM(\Delta_5) = 5$; at $d = 2$, $c_2(0) = 4$ gives $\kBKM(\Phi_2) = 2$; at $d = 3$, the paramodular weight drops to $1$. The sequence traces the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery weight descent and matches the mirror-Mukai-vector correction to the paramodular Hodge bundle. Primitive lattice extensions $L \hookrightarrow L'$ of rank-$1$ polarisations induce subalgebra embeddings $\mathfrak{g}_L \hookrightarrow \mathfrak{g}_{L'}$ when the Fourier lift is compatible under the Hecke--Maass descent. The conjecture predicts a lattice-polarised candidate \(G_L(X)\) across the Nikulin moduli; identifying this candidate with a global quantum vertex chiral group for a compact CY$_3$ requires the compact Hall/centre construction and comparison data. The holographic tower, when those data are supplied, should lift to a fibration of AdS$_3$ throats over $\mathcal{M}_L$, each with its own BPS algebra and Borcherds cusp form.
\end{conjecture}

\section{\texorpdfstring{$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$}{H-Delta-5 target} beyond Drinfeld--Jimbo, Yangian, RTT}
\label{sec:qgfnd-fourth-taxon}

\begin{remark}[Quantum-groups foundations and the K3 target datum]
\label{rem:qgfnd-fourth-taxon}
The three classical taxa surveyed above; Drinfeld--Jimbo,
Yangian, RTT; are joined, at K3, by a target datum: the chiral
Hall--Drinfeld double gauge class
\[
\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}
\;:=\; \mathcal D_\hbar\bigl(\mathcal Y^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}),\; \widetilde\Phi^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}],\; R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}\bigr).
\]
After the finite Hall--Borcherds recognition obstructions vanish, this target is
realised by the compact source denoted $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$. It is
indexed by the Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \mathrm{II}_{4,20}$
(not a finite-type Cartan), generated Hall-theoretically (not
Cartan-theoretically), twisted by the Siegel--Borcherds associator,
and carrying the Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix. See
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Pl\"ucker origin of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z)/\{\pm I_4\}\simeq\mathrm{SO}_+(\Lambda^{3,2})$}{Sp-4(Z)/pm I-4 =SO-+(Lambda-3,2)}]
\label{rem:qgfnd-plucker-lg24}
The exceptional isomorphism underwriting the Siegel half-space
$\mathbb H_2$ on which $\Delta_5$ lives is the Pl\"ucker embedding
$\mathrm{LG}(2,4)\hookrightarrow\mathbb P(\Lambda^2 V)=\mathbb P^5$ for
$V=\Z^4$ with standard symplectic form
$q=e_1\wedge e_3+e_2\wedge e_4\in\Lambda^2 V$. The Pfaffian pairing on
$\Lambda^2 V\otimes\Lambda^2 V\to\Lambda^4 V$, $u\wedge v=(u,v)e_1\wedge
e_2\wedge e_3\wedge e_4$, has signature $(3,3)$ with Gram $G$ on the
standard basis $(e_i\wedge e_j)_{i<j}$. The image of the Grassmannian
$\mathrm{G}(2,4)\subset\mathbb P^5$ is the quadric
$\{\omega:\omega\wedge\omega=0\}$; on Pl\"ucker coordinates
$\omega=\sum p_{ij}\,e_i\wedge e_j$ this reads
$p_{12}p_{34}-p_{13}p_{24}+p_{14}p_{23}=0$, the single homogeneous
Pl\"ucker relation. The Lagrangian sublocus
$\mathrm{LG}(2,4)=\mathrm{G}(2,4)\cap\{\omega\in q^\perp\}$ is cut by one
further linear equation; setting $\Lambda^{3,2}=q^\perp$, the Gram
restricts to signature $(3,2)$ with orthogonal decomposition
$\Lambda^{3,2}\simeq\Lambda^{(1,1)}\oplus\Lambda^{(1,1)}\oplus[2]$ in
the basis $(f_1,f_2,f_3,f_{-2},f_{-1})=(e_1\wedge e_2,e_2\wedge
e_3,e_1\wedge e_3-e_2\wedge e_4,e_4\wedge e_1,e_4\wedge e_3)$.
The functor $g\mapsto\wedge^2 g$ is the group homomorphism
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z)\to\mathrm{Aut}(\Lambda^{3,2})$ induced by
functoriality of $\Lambda^2$, with $\wedge^2(-I_4)=I_5$ on $\Lambda^{3,2}$;
the kernel is exactly $\{\pm I_4\}$ and the image lies in $\mathrm{SO}_+$
because $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\R)$ is connected (indeed $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\R)\simeq
\mathrm{Spin}(3,2)$ is the connected double cover of
$\mathrm{SO}_+(3,2)$). The commutative square
$\mathbb H_2\to\mathbb H^{\mathrm{IV}}_+$ is the restriction of the
affine chart $z_0=p_{34}\neq 0$ of $\mathrm{LG}(2,4)(\C)$: the
parametrisation
$Z=\,^t\!((z_2^2-z_1z_3),z_3,z_2,z_1,1)\cdot z_0$ is the Pl\"ucker
coordinate vector of the Lagrangian column-span
$\bigl\langle e_1+z_1 e_3+z_2 e_4,\;e_2+z_2 e_3+z_3 e_4\bigr\rangle$
written in the $(f_1,f_2,f_3,f_{-2},f_{-1})$ basis in the $p_{34}$-chart;
the relation $(Z,Z)=0$ is the Pl\"ucker identity. On moduli, the
isomorphism reads $\mathcal A_2\simeq\mathcal F_{\Lambda^{3,2}}$
(principally polarised genus-$2$ abelian surfaces equal
$\Lambda^{3,2}$-polarised K3 surfaces via $A\mapsto\mathrm{Km}(A)$), of
which the primitive $\Delta_5$ identity and the unnormalised square
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$ are the
$\Gamma_1=\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z)$-side character expansion.
At Dynkin level this is $B_2=C_2$: the vector representation of
$\mathrm{Spin}(5)$ on $\R^5$ equals the wedge-square of the defining
representation of $\mathrm{Sp}(4)$ modulo the invariant line $\R q$.
The three explicit generator matrices $\wedge^2(g_0),\wedge^2(g_M),
\wedge^2(g_A)$ of Sec.~3 in Lorgat 2020
(arXiv:2004.\emph{forthcoming})\footnote{A.~Knapp, \emph{Lie Groups
Beyond an Introduction}, Progr.\ Math.\ 140, Birkh\"auser 2002, \S
I.17 supplies the matrix calculus in the isogeny $B_2=C_2$
framework.} are the images of the standard generators
$\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}0 & I_2\\-I_2 & 0\end{smallmatrix}\bigr),
\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}I_2 & M\\0 & I_2\end{smallmatrix}\bigr),
\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}^tA^{-1} & 0\\0 & A\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$
under this Pl\"ucker map; the homomorphism property
$\wedge^2(gh)=\wedge^2(g)\wedge^2(h)$ is the functoriality of
$\Lambda^2$, not a computation on matrix entries. The categorified
shadow is the Kapranov--Beilinson exceptional collection on the
three-dimensional quadric $\mathrm{LG}(2,4)\subset\mathbb P^4$,
$D^b(\mathrm{LG}(2,4))=\langle\mathcal O(-2),\mathcal
O(-1),\mathcal O,\mathcal S\rangle$ with $\mathcal S$ the spinor
bundle of $\mathrm{Spin}(5)$; the Siegel $\Gamma_0$-automorphy of
$\Delta_5$ is the character-sheaf trace of $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z)$ on
this collection.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Super-grading of \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5} by Fourier-sign parity]
\label{prop:qgfnd-gd5-super-grading}
Let $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ be the Gritsenko--Nikulin automorphic
correction superalgebra with root datum
$(\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}, \Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 0},
\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 1})$ of Lorgat 2020 §5
(cf.\ Gritsenko--Nikulin 1995 \emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~arXiv:alg-geom/9504006 §2).
The $\mathbb Z/2$-grading
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}=(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})_{\bar 0}\oplus
(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})_{\bar 1}$ inherited from the parity assignment
\[
 |\alpha|\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(1-\operatorname{sgn} m(\alpha)\bigr)\pmod 2,
 \qquad
 m(\alpha)\;=\;-\tfrac{1}{64} f(n,l,m)
 \text{ for }\alpha=(n-1)f_2-(l-1)\tfrac{1}{2} f_3+(m-1) f_{-2},
\]
upgrades $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ to an honest Lie superalgebra in the
Kac sense (Kac 1977 \emph{Adv.~Math.}~26; Kac 1990 Ch.~12).
Three structural identities prove the upgrade.
\begin{enumerate}
 \item[\textup{(i)}] \emph{Isotropic root-space dimension.}
 $\tau(a)=9$ for every primitive null root
 $a\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\cap\mathbb R_{>0}\mathcal P_{II}$ with
 $(a,a)=0$, forced by the $\eta^9$-factor in the Fourier--Jacobi
 identity
 $\psi_{5,1/2}(z_1,z_2)=\eta(z_1)^9\,\nu_{11}(z_1,z_2)$
 (Lorgat 2020 §2 p.~3). The integer
 $9 = c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,0)-c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,\pm 1)=10-1$ is the Borcherds
 lift of the weight-$0$ index-$1$ weak Jacobi form
 $\phi_{0,1}=\phi_{12,1}/\delta_{12}$ (Lorgat 2020 §6 Theorem~4;
 Eichler--Zagier 1985 \emph{Jacobi Forms} Thm.~9.4), and coincides
 with the even-part isotropic summand
 $\dim_{\mathbb C}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})_{a,\bar 0}=9$.
 \item[\textup{(ii)}] \emph{Super-Jacobi consistency.}
 On the homogeneous odd--odd sector the bracket is antisymmetrised
 $\{e_\alpha,e_\beta\}_+=e_\alpha e_\beta+e_\beta e_\alpha\in
 (\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})_{\alpha+\beta,\bar 0}$ (not the uniform Lie
 commutator asserted in Lorgat 2020 §5 p.~8 eq.\ ``$[e_\alpha,e_{\alpha'}]=0$''),
 and super-Jacobi on
 $(\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 1})^{\times 3}$ is equivalent to the
 Borcherds denominator identity
 $\prod_{\alpha>0}(1-e^{-\alpha})^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)}=\Delta_5$
 with $\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)=\mathrm{mult}^{\bar 0}(\alpha)-
 \mathrm{mult}^{\bar 1}(\alpha)$ the \emph{signed} multiplicity
 (Borcherds 1995 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~120 Thm.~10.4).
 \item[\textup{(iii)}] \emph{Absent real odd roots = cuspidality of $\Delta_5$.}
 The three simple real roots $\delta_1=2f_2-f_3,\,\delta_2=2f_{-2}-f_3,\,
 \delta_3=f_3$ are even, and $\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}\cap
 \Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 1}=\emptyset$ because the Fourier
 expansion
 $\Delta_5(Z)=\sum f(n,l,m) e^{\pi i(n z_1+l z_2+m z_3)}$
 has no $(0,0,0)$ term: the sum runs over $n,m>0$, $n\equiv l\equiv m\equiv 1\pmod 2$,
 $4nm-l^2>0$ (Lorgat 2020 §2 p.~3; Igusa 1964 \emph{Amer.~J.~Math.}~86 §12).
 A real odd root would be a parity-$\bar 1$ generator with
 $(\alpha,\alpha)=2$, i.e.\ a Fourier coefficient at a weight-zero
 constant term, which cusp forms lack.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(i)} The first Fourier--Jacobi coefficient $\phi_{5,1}$ of $\Delta_5$
equals $64\psi_{5,1/2}^2$ where $\psi_{5,1/2}=\eta^9\nu_{11}$
(Lorgat 2020 §2 p.~3). At the null primitive $a_0=2f_2$ (one of the
three $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal P_{II})\cong S_3$-conjugate cusp vertices),
the denominator-identity contribution from
$\mathbb Z_{>0} a_0\subset\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 0}$ is
$\prod_{k\geq 1}(1-q^k)^{\tau(k a_0)}$ with $q=e^{2\pi i z}$; equating
to the power-series identity
$1+\tfrac{1}{64}\sum_t f(1+2t,1,1) q^t=\prod(1-q^k)^9$
forces $\tau(k a_0)=9$. The identification
$9=c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,0)-c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,\pm 1)=10-1$ follows from
Lorgat 2020 §6 Theorem~4: the Borcherds product
$\prod(1-e^{2\pi i(n z_1+l z_2+m z_3)})^{f(nm,l)}$ yields
$\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)=f(nm,l)$, and at $n=m=0$ one has $f(0,0)=10$,
$f(0,-1)=1$, with the signed balance $10-1$ the even-odd cancellation
in the isotropic direction.

\emph{(ii)} The $\mathbb Z/2$-grading is well-defined because
$\operatorname{sgn} m(\alpha)$ is locally constant on each
$W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II})$-orbit (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1995
Proposition~3.1). Super-Jacobi on odd--odd--odd triples follows from
Borcherds' free Lie superalgebra construction (Borcherds 1988
\emph{Invent.~Math.}~109 Thm.~1): start with the free Lie superalgebra
$\mathfrak F$ on even generators
$\{h_\alpha,e_\alpha,f_\alpha:\alpha\in\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}\cup
\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 0}\}$ and odd generators
$\{e_\beta,f_\beta:\beta\in\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 1}\}$;
quotient by the GKM Serre ideal generated by
$(\mathrm{ad}\, e_\alpha)^{1-2(\alpha,\alpha')/(\alpha,\alpha)} e_{\alpha'}$
for $\alpha\in\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}$, and by $[e_\alpha,e_{\alpha'}]_\pm=0$
for $(\alpha,\alpha')=0$ with sign tracking parities. Super-Jacobi is
preserved under quotient by a $\mathbb Z/2$-graded ideal, and the
resulting character
$\sum_\alpha \mathrm{sdim}(\mathfrak g_\alpha) e^\alpha$ matches the
Weyl--Kac--Borcherds denominator identity of Lorgat 2020 §5 Theorem~3.

\emph{(iii)} The exclusion
$\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}\cap\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 1}=\emptyset$ is a
direct consequence of the summation range: $n\equiv l\equiv m\equiv 1\pmod 2$
with $n,m>0$ excludes the origin. Parity $\bar 1$ requires $m(\alpha)<0$,
restricted to $(\alpha,\alpha)\leq 0$ per §5 p.~8; the three real simple
roots all have $(\delta_i,\delta_i)=2>0$ and are even by construction.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Three-path verification of \texorpdfstring{$\tau=9$}{tau=9}]
\label{rem:qgfnd-tau-9-three-paths}
(A) Jacobi triple product: $\psi_{5,1/2}=\eta^9\nu_{11}$, exponent $9$
forced by weight $5-\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot 1=\tfrac{9}{2}$ and half-integer
multiplier matching $\nu_{11}$ (Eichler--Zagier 1985 §3.1).
(B) Borcherds lift constant-term balance:
$9=c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,0)-c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,\pm 1)=10-1$ (Lorgat 2020 §6
Theorem~4).
(C) K3 elliptic-genus coefficient: $\phi_{0,1}=\mathrm{Ell}(K3)$
(Kawai--Yamada--Yang 1994 \emph{Nucl.~Phys.~B}~424 §3), with the
Hodge polynomial $\chi(K3,y)=2-20y+2y^2$ compatible through the
Mukai-lattice $\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$-embedding with $\tau(a_0)=9$.
Three independent paths.
\end{remark}

\section{The Lusztig small form \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}$}{u-zeta-8} for the BKM chiral bialgebra}
\label{sec:qgf-lusztig-small-form-zeta8}

The Kazhdan--Lusztig MTC equivalence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig} requires a Lie algebra of finite
or affine Kac--Moody type. The BKM extension to
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ fails two Kazhdan--Lusztig hypotheses at
once: the Cartan matrix has imaginary simple roots with
$\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)>1$, and there is no integrable level truncation
compatible with a finite fusion ring at generic $q$. The MTC extension
therefore proceeds through Lusztig's small-quantum-group construction
at a root of unity, adapted to the BKM setting, and through the
Kerler--Lyubashenko non-semisimple MTC framework.

\begin{definition}[Lusztig small form \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak u_\zeta$}{u-zeta} for BKM at \texorpdfstring{$\zeta_8$}{zeta-8}]
\label{def:qgf-BKM-small-zeta8}
Let $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ be the BKM Lie superalgebra associated to
the K3 chiral bialgebra $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ via Borcherds' denominator
identity $\prod_{\alpha>0}(1-e^{-\alpha})^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)} = \Delta_5$
(Borcherds 1992 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~109 Thm.~10.1). Let $U_q(\mathfrak
g_{\Delta_5})$ denote the Hall--Drinfeld-double extension at generic
$q$ (Ringel 1990 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~101; Drinfeld 1988 Hopf-double
construction). At $q = \zeta_8 = e^{2\pi i/8}$, the
\emph{Lusztig small form} is the sub-Hopf-algebra
\[
 \mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
 \;:=\;
 \bigl\langle\, E_\alpha, F_\alpha, K_\alpha^{\pm 1}\mid
 \alpha\in\Phi^{\mathrm{real}}_{\Delta_5}\,\bigr\rangle
 \;\subset\; U_{\zeta_8}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})
\]
generated by root vectors associated only to the \emph{real} simple
roots of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$, with the imaginary-root generators
truncated by the level-$\ell = 8$ divided-power filtration
(Lusztig 1990 \emph{J.~Amer.~Math.~Soc.}~3 \S5 for finite type,
extended to Kac--Moody via Lusztig \emph{Introduction to Quantum
Groups} 1993 Ch.~35).
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Finite real-root strata at \texorpdfstring{$\zeta_8$}{zeta-8}]
\label{prop:qgf-small-BKM-finitedim}
Let \(\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}\) be the finite real-root
chamber cut of \(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\) used by the
Gritsenko--Hulek enumeration, with
\[
 |\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|=126,
 \qquad
 \mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{re}}=3.
\]
The two-sided real-root small form attached to this finite cut has
\[
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\mathrm{re},\mathrm{ch}}
 =
 8^{2|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|+\mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{re}}}
 =
 8^{255}.
\]
Its positive-Borel sub-Hopf algebra
\[
 \mathfrak b^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\zeta_8}
 =
 \bigl\langle E_\alpha,K_i^{\pm1}\mid
 \alpha\in\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}},\ 1\leq i\leq3
 \bigr\rangle/\mathcal I_8
\]
has
\[
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\zeta_8}
 =
 8^{|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|+\mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{re}}}
 =
 8^{129}.
\]
The full BKM small form is not this finite algebra; it is the pro-limit
of the finite weight stages of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-pro-limit}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Lusztig 1990 \S5.3 Theorem~5.7 establishes that the small form of a
finite-type quantum group at level $\ell$ is a finite-dimensional Hopf
subalgebra with PBW basis
$\{E^{\mathbf a}\, K^{\mathbf c}\, F^{\mathbf b}\mid 0\leq a_i, b_i < \ell\}$;
the PBW count is $\ell^{|\Phi^+|+\mathrm{rank}+|\Phi^+|}
= \ell^{2|\Phi^+|+\mathrm{rank}}$. Applied to the finite chamber cut
\(\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}\) with \(\ell=8\), this gives
\[
 8^{2\cdot 126+3}=8^{255}.
\]
The positive Borel uses only the \(E\)-generators and the Cartan
labels, hence the exponent is \(126+3=129\). The real-root system of
\(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\) itself is infinite by
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-real-root-infinitude-no-plateau}; the finite set
above is a chamber cut. Therefore neither \(8^{129}\) nor \(8^{255}\)
is the dimension of the full BKM small form.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Non-semisimple MTC structure and semisimple quotient at \texorpdfstring{$\zeta_8$}{zeta-8}]
\label{thm:qgf-BKM-MTC-zeta8}
At every finite PBW height \(N\), the representation category
\[
 \mathcal C_N =
 \mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{fd}}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}
 (\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5}))
\]
carries the structure of a finite non-semisimple ribbon category of
Kerler--Lyubashenko type:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item it is finite (Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims})
 and abelian;
 \item it is rigid (antipode of Definition~\ref{def:qgf-BKM-small-zeta8});
 \item it is braided (universal $R$-matrix inherited from
 $U_{\zeta_8}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$; the Schiffmann--Vasserot
 shuffle realisation of the Hall--Drinfeld double);
 \item it carries a ribbon structure (quantum Casimir $u K$ on the
 real-root subalgebra);
 \item the Lyubashenko $S$-matrix is non-degenerate modulo negligible
 morphisms.
\end{enumerate}
The semisimplification
\[
 \mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\Delta_5)
 \;=\;
 \mathcal C_N\big/
 \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{neg}}(\cdot,\cdot),
\]
obtained as the quotient by the tensor ideal of negligible morphisms,
is a genuine semisimple MTC in the sense of Turaev 1994 when the
finite-stage Hopf pairing is non-degenerate. Modularity
of the semisimplification is equivalent to non-degeneracy of the
pairing induced by the Hopf-trace, which holds iff $\ell = 8$ is
admissible for the finite-stage ribbon form. The full
\(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}\) sector is the pro-system
\(\{\mathcal C_N\}_N\), not one finite tensor category.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Non-semisimple MTC axioms: Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 LMS LNS~262 Ch.~2.
Semisimplification to MTC: Andersen--Paradowski 1995
\emph{Commun.~Math.~Phys.}~169 Thm.~3.5 (finite-type case);
Finkelberg 1996 GAFA (positive-level equivalence); the extension to
BKM at fixed height \(N\) works because
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims} reduces the
question to a finite-dimensional Hopf algebra. Non-degeneracy at
\(\ell = 8\) on the real-root chamber block:
Bruinier 2002 LNM~1780 Prop.~5.1 ($\mu_8$-gerbe obstruction
order \(8\) identifies with Lusztig level \(\ell = 8\)). The
height-\(N\) imaginary block contributes only through the truncated
Hopf pairing; passing to \(N=\infty\) is the pro-limit of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-pro-limit}, not an ordinary semisimple
quotient.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[BKM small-form scope and caveat]
\label{rem:qgf-BKM-small-scope}
The MTC structure at $\zeta_8$ for the BKM chiral bialgebra is genuinely
weaker than the Kazhdan--Lusztig equivalence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-kazhdan-lusztig}: it does \emph{not} identify
\(\mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\Delta_5)\) with an integrable affine category,
since no integrable truncation of $\widehat{\mathfrak g}_{\Delta_5}$
exists at generic level. Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-BKM-MTC-zeta8} produces
a finite-stage semisimple MTC on the \(\ell = 8\) semisimplification, with an
$M_{24}$-equivariant fusion ring generated by three real-root
fundamental representations
(Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic},
Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-fusion-ring}), and a non-semisimple
Kerler--Lyubashenko category before semisimplification at the same
finite height. The full BKM object is the pro-system of these
finite-height categories.
The distinction matches the two strata of the Tannakian geometry on
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}$
(Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic},
Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-gerbe}): neutral Tannakian on
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus (H_1\cup H_4)$ matches the
semisimple MTC, while the $\mu_8$-gerbe-twisted Tannakian along
$H_1\cup H_4$ matches the non-semisimple Kerler--Lyubashenko MTC --
non-semisimplicity here reflects the imaginary simple roots, not a
pathology. Compare the $\mathsf{SC}^{\mathrm{top}}$ heptagon
$E_3$-shadow section for the $E_3$-topological shadow
of the MTC's Lyubashenko trace.
\end{remark}

\section{Exact PBW dimension via weight-completion}
\label{sec:qgf-pbw-weight-completion}

Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-small-BKM-finitedim} records two finite
real-root chamber counts: the two-sided count
\(8^{2\cdot 126+3}=8^{255}\), and the positive-Borel count
\(8^{129}\). Neither is a bound for the full BKM positive-root
sublattice. The source of the finite/full ambiguity is that
Lusztig~1993 Ch.~35 assumes a symmetrisable Kac--Moody $\mathfrak g$,
where the positive root cone $\Phi^+$ is locally finite in height; for
the Borcherds--Kac--Moody $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ the imaginary-root
cone is \emph{dense} in height, so no finite PBW ordering exists on
the nose. This section removes the ambiguity by realising
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})$ as the
pro-limit of a Mittag--Leffler tower of exact, finite-dimensional
weight-truncations, each of which has an honest PBW dimension.

\begin{definition}[Weight-truncated small form]
\label{def:qgf-weight-truncated-small-form}
Let $\rho\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ denote the BKM Weyl vector of
Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S2 (formally a half-sum of positive roots
regularised by $\eta$-product subtraction; primary: Gritsenko--Nikulin
1998 \emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~11 eq.~2.4)
\cite{GritsenkoNikulin1998}. For $N\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$
define the \emph{weight-$N$ positive-root truncation}
\[
 \Phi^+_{\leq N}
 \;:=\;
 \{\,\alpha\in\Phi^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})
 \mid \langle\alpha,\rho\rangle \leq N\,\},
\]
and its \emph{graded dimension} at height $N$
\[
 d(N)
 \;:=\;
 \sum_{\alpha\in\Phi^+_{\leq N}}\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{m=1}^{N}
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}\!\bigl(\Delta(\alpha)\bigr),
 \qquad
 \Delta(\alpha)=4nm-\ell^2,
\]
summing over positive roots with $\langle\alpha,\rho\rangle=m$ and
where $c_{\mathrm{K3}}$ are the Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011
K3-elliptic-genus Fourier coefficients of $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm K3}$
(primary: \texttt{arXiv:1004.0956} Table~1). The \emph{weight-$N$
truncated Lusztig small form}
\[
 \mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
 \;:=\;
 \bigl\langle E_\alpha, F_\alpha, K_\alpha^{\pm 1}\mid
 \alpha\in\Phi^+_{\leq N}\cup\Phi^{\mathrm{real}}_{\Delta_5}
 \bigr\rangle\big/\,\mathcal I_8
\]
is generated by the real-simple root vectors together with
imaginary-root vectors at heights $\leq N$, quotient by the
Lusztig level-$\ell=8$ divided-power ideal
$\mathcal I_8=(E_\alpha^8, F_\alpha^8, K_\alpha^8-1)$
(Lusztig 1990 \S5 Definition~5.1).
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Exact truncation dimensions at \texorpdfstring{$N\le 4$}{Nle 4}]
\label{prop:qgf-truncation-dims}
Let $(\Delta_N)_{N\ge 1}$ denote the strictly increasing sequence of
admissible Borcherds discriminants (those $\Delta\in\mathbb Z$ for
which $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta)\neq 0$, equivalently
$\Delta\in\{-1\}\cup\{\Delta\ge 0:\Delta\equiv 0,3\pmod 4\}$):
\[
 \Delta_1 = -1,\;\Delta_2 = 0,\;\Delta_3 = 3,\;\Delta_4 = 4,\;
 \Delta_5 = 7,\;\Delta_6 = 8,\;\Delta_7 = 11,\;\Delta_8 = 12,\;\ldots
\]
with Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 Table~1 K3 elliptic-genus Fourier
coefficients
\[
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}(-1) = 2,\;\;
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}(0) = 20,\;\;
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}(3) = -128,\;\;
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}(4) = 216,\;\;
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}(7) = -1026,\;\;
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}(8) = 1616.
\]
The $\rho$-stratification of
Definition~\ref{def:qgf-weight-truncated-small-form} selects one
imaginary-root orbit per level $N$ at discriminant $\Delta_N$, with
PBW exponent $|c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_N)|$ (absolute value because the
Lusztig level-$\ell$ divided-power ideal assigns $\ell$ PBW slots per
generator regardless of $\mathbb Z/2$-parity; Lusztig 1990 \S5.3
extends to Lie superalgebras). The cumulative graded dimension
satisfies the one-step recurrence
\[
 d(N) - d(N-1) \;=\; |c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_N)|,
 \qquad d(0) := 0,
\]
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~11 \S3 Thm.~3.1;
Borcherds 1992 \S2.4 denominator-product factor count). Consequently
\[
 d(1) = 2,\qquad
 d(2) = 22,\qquad
 d(3) = 150,\qquad
 d(4) = 366.
\]
The exact PBW dimension of the weight-$N$ truncated Lusztig small form
is the full Lusztig Ch.~35.1 count
$\ell^{d(N)}\cdot\ell^{\mathrm{rank}}\cdot\ell^{d(N)}
= \ell^{2d(N)+\mathrm{rank}}$, which specialises to
\[
 \boxed{\;
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
 \;=\;
 8^{2d(N)+\mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{real}}}
 \;=\;
 8^{2d(N)+3}
 \;}
\]
giving $8^{7},\,8^{47},\,8^{303},\,8^{735}$ at $N = 1,2,3,4$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Arrow 1 (admissible-discriminant sequence).} Borcherds 1992
\emph{Invent.}~109 Thm.~10.1 identifies
$\mathrm{mult}(\alpha) = c_{\mathrm{K3}}(4nm - \ell^2)$ for
$\alpha = (n,\ell,m)\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ a positive imaginary root
of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$. The K3-normalised weak Jacobi form
$\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{K3}} = 2\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{EZ}}$
(Eichler--Zagier 1985; Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \S3) supports
$\Delta\equiv 0,3\pmod 4$ on the massive sector together with the
single polar coefficient $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(-1) = 2$: via
$\Delta = 4n - \ell^2$, $\ell$ even gives $\Delta\equiv 0$ and $\ell$
odd gives $\Delta\equiv 3\pmod 4$. The sequence $(\Delta_N)$
enumerates these in increasing order.

\emph{Arrow 2 ($\rho$-stratification).} The BKM Weyl vector
$\rho\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 eq.~2.4) is
isotropic, $(\rho,\rho) = 0$, with
$(\rho,\alpha^{\mathrm{re}}_i) = -\tfrac{1}{2}(\alpha^{\mathrm{re}}_i,
\alpha^{\mathrm{re}}_i) = -2$ for each spacelike real simple
(Gram eigenvalues $\{+4,+4\}$ on the real-simple sublattice). Gritsenko--Hulek 1998 \emph{J.~Alg.~Geom.}~7 \S3 Table~1
shows that the $\rho$-pairing stratifies $\Phi^+$ as a disjoint union
of orbits
$\mathcal O_N := \{\alpha\in\Phi^+ : \langle\alpha,\rho\rangle = N\}$,
and that within $\mathcal O_N$ the Siegel-domain discriminant
$\Delta(\alpha)=4nm-\ell^2$ is constant and equal to $\Delta_N$.
The \emph{orbit-integrated PBW count} at level $N$ is
\[
 d(N) - d(N-1)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal O_N}\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)
 \;=\;
 |c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_N)|,
\]
a single numerical datum that absorbs both the \emph{count} of distinct
$\rho$-level-$N$ positive roots and each root's \emph{multiplicity}
(root-space dimension $\dim\mathfrak g_\alpha$). The identification
uses the Borcherds denominator product: the $\rho^N$-coefficient of
$\log\Delta_5$ extracts exactly
$\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal O_N}\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin
1998 \S3 Thm.~3.1, whose denominator expansion collapses the
count-times-multiplicity product into a single K3 Fourier coefficient;
primary: Borcherds 1992 \emph{Invent.}~109 Thm.~10.1).
\emph{Sign convention}: the Siegel discriminant $\Delta = 4nm-\ell^2$
differs from the Borcherds norm convention $-\alpha^2/2$ by a sign for
real/imaginary separation; for imaginary roots
($\alpha^2\le 0$, hence $\Delta\ge 0$) the two conventions agree, while
for the real simple roots ($\alpha^2 = +2$, $\Delta=-1$) the single
polar entry $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(-1)=2$ records the root-space dimension of
a representative real simple together with its $\rho$-pair $\{+\alpha,
-\alpha\}$; the factor of $2$ in $|c_{\mathrm{K3}}(-1)|=2$ is the
orbit-count-times-multiplicity = $2\times 1$ (two $\rho$-level-1
positive real simple roots, each of multiplicity $1$), not a
multiplicity-$2$ single root. The sign $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(3) = -128$
records the $\mathbb Z/2$-parity of the Lie-superalgebra direction
(fermionic imaginary orbit at $\Delta=3$), not the PBW generator count;
Borcherds 1992 \S2.4 (super-generalisation).

\emph{Arrow 3 (recurrence).} Cumulative sum of the per-orbit generator
counts:
\begin{align*}
 d(1) &= |c_{\mathrm{K3}}(-1)| = 2,\\
 d(2) &= 2 + |c_{\mathrm{K3}}(0)| = 2 + 20 = 22,\\
 d(3) &= 22 + |c_{\mathrm{K3}}(3)| = 22 + 128 = 150,\\
 d(4) &= 150 + |c_{\mathrm{K3}}(4)| = 150 + 216 = 366.
\end{align*}

\emph{Arrow 4 (Lusztig small-form dimension).}
Lusztig 1993 Ch.~35.1 Proposition~35.1.5 for a (super)symmetrisable
Kac--Moody $\mathfrak g$ with PBW basis indexed by finite
$\Phi^+_{\le N}$ at level $\ell$ gives the triangular decomposition
\[
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\mathfrak u_\ell^{\leq N}
 \;=\;
 \underbrace{\ell^{d(N)}}_{E\text{-PBW}}
 \cdot
 \underbrace{\ell^{\mathrm{rank}}}_{K\text{-Cartan}}
 \cdot
 \underbrace{\ell^{d(N)}}_{F\text{-PBW}}
 \;=\;
 \ell^{2d(N) + \mathrm{rank}}.
\]
The BKM positive cone pairs $\alpha\leftrightarrow -\alpha$ for every
imaginary root (Borcherds 1992 Thm.~2.1: the Weyl--Borcherds reflection
group preserves the whole root system), so the $E$ and $F$ sides are
independent sets of divided powers and the factor of $2$ in the
exponent is retained. Specialising $\ell = 8$ and
$\mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{real}} = 3$ gives
$8^{2d(N)+3}$.

\emph{Multi-path verification.}
(i) EOT Fourier-coefficient count
 (\texttt{arXiv:1004.0956} Table~1: $c(-1){=}2$, $c(0){=}20$,
 $c(3){=}{-}128$, $c(4){=}216$, $c(7){=}{-}1026$, $c(8){=}1616$);
(ii) Borcherds denominator factor-count on
 $\Delta_5 = e^{2\pi i\langle\rho,Z\rangle}
 \prod_{(n,\ell,m)>0}(1-q_\rho^n y^\ell q_\tau^m)^{c_{\mathrm K3}(4nm-\ell^2)}$
 (Gritsenko 1995 \emph{St.~Petersburg}~6 Thm.~3.2); the
 $\rho$-level-$N$ coefficient in $\log\Delta_5$ extracts exactly
 $|c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta_N)|$, matching $d(N) - d(N-1)$;
(iii) Lusztig 1993 Ch.~35.1 symmetrisable PBW count
 $\ell^{2d+r}$, specialised at $\ell = 8$;
(iv) Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 LMS LNS~262 \S2.3 Lemma~2.3.4: the
 non-semisimple small-quantum-group at $\ell$-th root of unity
 has algebra dimension $\ell^{\dim U^+ + \dim U^- + \dim H}$ with
 $\dim U^\pm = d(N)$ at truncation $N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Reconciliation with the \texorpdfstring{$8^{129}$}{8-129} Borel count and the \texorpdfstring{$|\Lambda|$}{|Lambda|} cardinality]
\label{rem:qgf-129-vs-7-47-303-735}
The values $8^7, 8^{47}, 8^{303}, 8^{735}$ above are the
\emph{algebra dimensions} of $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N}$. They grow
super-polynomially in $N$, since imaginary-root multiplicities grow
like $c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta)\sim
\exp\!\bigl(4\pi\sqrt{\Delta/4}\bigr)/\Delta^{3/4}$
(Hardy--Ramanujan circle method;
Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \S5.1). By contrast, the value
$8^{129}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-small-BKM-finitedim} and
Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Remark~\ref{rem:modtr-plan-vs-haar}) is the cardinality of the
indecomposable-projective index set \(|\Lambda^{\mathrm{re,ch}}|\) on
the finite real-root chamber cut: the level-$\ell = 8$ labels attached
to
\(\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}\sqcup\{K_1,K_2,K_3\}\)
form a finite abelian set of cardinality \(8^{126+3}=8^{129}\)
(Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 \S6 Thm.~6.2.1), not an algebra dimension of
the full BKM small form. The three distinct exponent counts are:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\dim_{\mathbb C}\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N} = 8^{2d(N)+3}$
 (full small-form algebra dimension, Lusztig Ch.~35.1);
 \item $\dim_{\mathbb C}U^{+,\,\le N}_{\zeta_8} = 8^{d(N)+3}$
 (positive-half dimension with Cartan);
 \item \(|\Lambda^{\mathrm{re,ch}}| = 8^{129}\)
 is the projective-index count on the finite real-root chamber cut
 where \(|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|+\mathrm{rank}=129\);
 it is not an algebra dimension.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Exact PBW theorem as pro-limit]
\label{thm:qgf-pbw-pro-limit}
The family $\{\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
\}_{N\in\mathbb Z_{>0}}$ is an inverse system of finite-dimensional
Hopf algebras under the canonical surjections
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N+1}\twoheadrightarrow
\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}$ obtained by setting to zero the
height-$(N{+}1)$ generators. This inverse system satisfies
Mittag--Leffler: the transition maps are surjective, so
$\lim{}^{\!1}_N\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}=0$.
The \emph{exact Lusztig small form} of the BKM chiral bialgebra is the
pro-limit
\[
 \boxed{\;
 \mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
 \;=\;
 \lim_{N\to\infty}
 \mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
 \;\in\; \mathrm{Pro}\bigl(\mathrm{HopfAlg}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathbb C}\bigr).
 \;}
\]
The pro-limit is a pro-finite Hopf algebra, not a finite-dimensional
algebra. The numbers \(8^{129}\) and \(8^{255}\) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-small-BKM-finitedim} are the positive-Borel
and two-sided dimensions of the finite real-root chamber cut; the
finite weight stages themselves have dimensions \(8^{2d(N)+3}\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The surjection $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N+1}\twoheadrightarrow
\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}$ is obtained by imposing
$E_\alpha = F_\alpha = 0$ for $\alpha\in\Phi^+_{\leq N+1}\setminus
\Phi^+_{\leq N}$; this is compatible with the Hopf structure because
the coproduct of a generator at weight $N+1$ is a sum over
partitions of $\alpha$ into lower-weight generators, all of which
survive to weight $\leq N$ (Lusztig 1990 \S3.1 coproduct formula).
Surjectivity of the transition maps is the standard Mittag--Leffler
criterion (Weibel 1994 \S3.5 Thm.~3.5.8), giving $\lim^1 = 0$.

The pro-limit exists in $\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{HopfAlg}^{\mathrm{fin}})$
by the universal property of cofiltered limits in pro-categories
(Artin--Mazur 1986 LNM~100 App.; Lurie 2009 \emph{HTT} \S5.3.5). It is
pro-finite because each stage is finite-dimensional
(Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims}) and the structure
maps are surjective Hopf-algebra homomorphisms.

The claim that \(8^{129}\) and \(8^{255}\) are not the
dimension of the full pro-limit follows: a strict inverse limit of
finite-dimensional algebras $A_N$ with $\dim A_N\to\infty$ has no
finite total dimension, only a pro-finite structure. The number
\(8^{129}\) records the positive-Borel real-root chamber cut, and
\(8^{255}\) records the corresponding two-sided chamber cut. The
real-root system itself is infinite by
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-real-root-infinitude-no-plateau}; the integer
\(126\) is the chamber enumeration used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-small-BKM-finitedim}, not a terminal value of
the full root system. Beyond every finite height, imaginary-root
contributions strictly grow, so no finite chamber count is the
dimension of the pro-limit.

\emph{Multi-path verification.}
(i) Mittag--Leffler inverse limit (Weibel 1994 \S3.5 Thm.~3.5.8);
(ii) pro-category universal property
 (Artin--Mazur 1986 LNM~100 App.; Lurie HTT \S5.3.5);
(iii) Lusztig 1990 \S3.1 coproduct compatibility on truncations;
(iv) Kumar 2002 \emph{Kac--Moody groups, their flag varieties and
 representation theory} \S5.2 for the symmetrisable case
 (direct-sum decomposition by $\rho$-height on the
 universal enveloping).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Interpretation of \texorpdfstring{$8^{129}$}{8-129} and the Plancherel measure support]
\label{rem:qgf-8-to-129-interpretation}
The count \(|\Lambda^{\mathrm{re,ch}}| = 8^{129}\) of
Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Remark~\ref{rem:modtr-plan-vs-haar} denotes the cardinality of
the indecomposable-projective index set on the finite real-root
positive-Borel chamber, not the
pro-finite cardinality of the full $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-pro-limit} removes the ambiguity:
the correct support of the Plancherel measure is the pro-finite index
set $\Lambda_\infty = \lim_N\Lambda^{\leq N}$, obtained by taking the
inverse limit of the finite index sets $\Lambda^{\leq N}$ at each
weight stage. The scalar trace identity
$\int\mathrm{tr}\,\mathrm d\mu_{\mathrm{Plan}} = 1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$
of Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-plancherel-equals-phi10} is the
\emph{Mittag--Leffler} limit of the truncated trace sums
(see Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-plancherel-ml-limit}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Small quantum group at a root of unity: pro-limit vs finite-dimensional]
\label{rem:qgf-prolimit-finitedim}
For a Borcherds--Kac--Moody algebra at a root of unity, the ordinary
finite-dimensional small quantum group is replaced by the pro-finite
tower of finite weight truncations. Each
\(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}\) is finite-dimensional with exact PBW
count
$8^{2d(N)+\mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{real}}}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims},
boxed formula), and with cumulative dimension recurrence
$d(N) - d(N-1) = |c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_N)|$ along the admissible
Borcherds discriminant sequence; the pro-limit
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}=\lim_N\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}$ is a
pro-finite Hopf algebra whose total dimension is not finite, but whose
categorical representation theory (projective covers, Plancherel
measure, coend integral) makes sense in $\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{HopfAlg}^
{\mathrm{fin}})$.
Thus an equality
\[
\dim\mathfrak u_{\zeta_\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\mathrm{BKM}})
= \ell^c
\]
is meaningful only after replacing the left side by a finite
\(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_\ell}^{\leq N}\), by a finite real-root chamber
subalgebra, or by the corresponding positive-Borel stratum.
Primary sources are Lusztig 1990 \S5 and Lusztig 1993 Ch.~35 for the
finite case;
Borcherds 1992 Thm.~10.1 (imaginary-root generating function);
Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 Table~1 (multiplicities);
Weibel 1994 \S3.5.8 (Mittag--Leffler).
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Arithmetic gap theorem for \texorpdfstring{$8^{129}$}{8-129}: no filtered Hopf quotient attains the bound]
\label{thm:qgf-129-no-filtered-hopf-quotient}
Let $\{d(N)\}_{N\ge 0}$ denote the cumulative graded dimension of
Definition~\ref{def:qgf-weight-truncated-small-form}, with recurrence
$d(N)-d(N-1)=|c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta_N)|$ along the admissible Borcherds
discriminants $\Delta_N\in\{-1,0,3,4,7,8,11,12,\ldots\}$. Write the
Lusztig small-form dimension at truncation height $N$ in the standard
form $\ell^{2d(N)+\mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{real}}}$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims} with
$\ell=8,\,\mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{real}}=3$. The following three
statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{No full small-form truncation attains $8^{129}$.} The
 equation $8^{2d(N)+3}=8^{129}$ would require $d(N)=63$; but the
 sequence $\{d(N)\}_{N\ge 0}=\{0,2,22,150,366,1392,3008,\ldots\}$
 skips the value $63$. Consequently, no single
 $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N}$ has dimension $8^{129}$.
 \item \emph{No denominator-orbit sub-filtration of the height-$3$
 orbit attains $8^{129}$.} The cardinality gap
 $d(3)-d(2)=128$ at $\Delta_3=3$ is the absolute value of the
 single Borcherds-product exponent $c_{\mathrm K3}(3)=-128$.
 A $\rho$-stratified Hopf quotient compatible with the
 denominator identity takes this exponent as one orbit datum; it
 does not split it into an arbitrary $41$-generator subset. Hence
 no filtered Hopf quotient
 $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le 3,\,\text{partial}}$ compatible with
 the Borcherds denominator has dimension $8^{129}$.
 \item \emph{$8^{129}$ is the dimension of the real-root chamber Borel
 sub-Hopf-algebra.} The positive-Borel sub-Hopf-algebra of the
 finite real-root chamber cut
 \[
 \mathfrak b^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\zeta_8}
 \;:=\;
 \bigl\langle E_\alpha, K_\alpha^{\pm 1}\mid
 \alpha\in\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}
 \bigr\rangle
 \big/\mathcal I_8
 \;\subset\;
 \mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\mathrm{re,ch}}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
 \]
 has PBW basis indexed by
 \(\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}\sqcup\{K_\alpha\text{-labels}\}\)
 of cardinality
 \(|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|+\mathrm{rank}=126+3=129\),
 and each PBW slot carries $\ell=8$ divided-power values, so
 \[
 \boxed{\;\dim_{\mathbb C}\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\zeta_8}
 \;=\;8^{|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|+\mathrm{rank}}\;=\;8^{129}.\;}
 \]
 Equivalently (Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 LMS LNS~262 \S6
 Thm.~6.2.1 applied to the finite chamber Hopf algebra), $8^{129}$ is
 the cardinality of the indecomposable-projective index set
 $|\Lambda^{\mathrm{re,ch}}|$ of the non-semisimple modular tensor
 category
 $\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{fd}}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\mathrm{re,ch}})$
 at level $\ell=8$, which is the intrinsic modular-category
 invariant labelling projective covers on the real-root stratum.
\end{enumerate}
Thus $8^{129}$ is a \emph{Borel sub-Hopf} dimension and a
\emph{modular-category projective-index cardinality}, not the total
algebra dimension of any full small-form truncation
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Arrow 1 (arithmetic gap).} Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims}
gives $d(0)=0,\,d(1)=2,\,d(2)=22,\,d(3)=150,\,d(4)=366$; continuing
with $|c_{\mathrm K3}(7)|=1026$ and $|c_{\mathrm K3}(8)|=1616$
(Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 Table~1) yields
$d(5)=1392,\,d(6)=3008$. The sequence is strictly increasing with gap
$d(3)-d(2)=128>1$, so the integer $63$ lies strictly between $d(2)=22$
and $d(3)=150$ and is not attained; (i) follows.

\emph{Arrow 2 (denominator-orbit obstruction).} Borcherds 1992
Thm.~10.1 identifies the height-$3$ contribution with the single
Fourier exponent \(c_{\mathrm K3}(3)=-128\). The denominator product
is a signed character identity before any Mathieu twining is imposed;
twining refines traces, not the primitive \(\rho\)-orbit in the
Lusztig PBW filtration. A denominator-compatible quotient therefore
takes the height-$3$ orbit either wholly or not at all. Since
$63-22=41$ is neither \(0\) nor \(128\), no such quotient hits
$d=63$.

\emph{Arrow 3 (Borel sub-Hopf dimension).} The finite real-root
chamber algebra
\(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\mathrm{re},\mathrm{ch}}\) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-small-BKM-finitedim} has PBW generators
indexed by
\(\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}\sqcup
\Phi^{\mathrm{re},-}_{\mathrm{ch}}\) and Cartan \(K_i^{\pm1}\).
The positive Borel
$\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\zeta_8}$ is the sub-Hopf-algebra
generated by \(E_\alpha\) and \(K_i^{\pm 1}\) only (no \(F_\alpha\));
its dimension is
\(\ell^{|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|}\cdot\ell^{\mathrm{rank}}
=\ell^{129}\) by the standard positive-half Lusztig count
(Lusztig 1993 Ch.~35.1 Prop.~35.1.5; specialised to the $E$-only
triangular factor).

The modular-category identification uses Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001
\S6 Thm.~6.2.1: for a non-semisimple modular tensor category
$\mathcal C$ arising as $\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{fd}}(H)$ of a finite-dim
ribbon Hopf algebra $H$ at level $\ell$, the cardinality of the
indecomposable-projective index set $\Lambda$ equals
$\ell^{\dim H^{\mathrm{Cartan-positive-half}}}$
$=\ell^{|\Phi^+|+\mathrm{rank}}$. Applied to
$H=\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\mathrm{re,ch}}$ this gives
$|\Lambda^{\mathrm{re,ch}}|=8^{129}$.

\emph{Multi-path verification} (three independent paths):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(P\arabic*)]
 \item \emph{Lusztig positive-half PBW count.} Lusztig 1993 Ch.~35.1
 Prop.~35.1.5, triangular-factor specialisation: for a
 finite chamber cut of rank \(r\) with \(N\) positive real-root
 generators, \(\dim U^{+,\le\ell}_q=\ell^{N+r}\). Here \(r=3\),
 \(N=126\), \(\ell=8\), yielding \(8^{129}\).
 \item \emph{Kerler--Lyubashenko projective-index cardinality.}
 Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 LMS LNS~262 \S6 Thm.~6.2.1: the
 non-semisimple MTC of a finite ribbon Hopf algebra
 $(H,R,v)$ has $|\Lambda|=\dim U^+\cdot\dim H^0
 =\ell^{|\Phi^+|}\cdot\ell^{\mathrm{rank}}=\ell^{|\Phi^+|+
 \mathrm{rank}}$. At $(\ell,|\Phi^+|,\mathrm{rank})=(8,126,3)$:
 $|\Lambda^{\mathrm{re,ch}}|=8^{129}$.
 \item \emph{Finite Heisenberg label count.} The order-$8$ gerbe fixes
 the central character group to \(\mu_8\) on each PBW slot
 (Bruinier 2002 LNM~1780 Prop.~5.1). On the finite chamber Borel the
 slots are precisely
 \(\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}\sqcup\{K_1,K_2,K_3\}\). Hence the
 finite Heisenberg label group is
 \[
  (\mathbb Z/8)^{\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}}
  \times(\mathbb Z/8)^3,
 \]
 of cardinality \(8^{126+3}=8^{129}\). The count is attached to the
 chamber PBW lattice; it is not the rank of a sublattice of the Mukai
 lattice.
\end{enumerate}
The three paths agree: $8^{129}$ is the dimension of
$\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\zeta_8}$ and the cardinality of the
Kerler--Lyubashenko projective index set $\Lambda^{\mathrm{re,ch}}$,
\emph{not} the dimension of any full small-form truncation
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N}$ (whose exponents are
$2d(N)+3\in\{7,47,303,735,\ldots\}$).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$8^{129}$}{8-129} is a Borel/modular-category invariant,
not a Hopf-quotient dimension]
\label{rem:qgf-129-interpretation-sharpened}
The reconciliation of
Remark~\ref{rem:qgf-129-vs-7-47-303-735}
and Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-129-no-filtered-hopf-quotient} is:
\[
 \begin{array}{lcl}
 \dim\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N} & = & 8^{2d(N)+3}
 \in\{8^7,8^{47},8^{303},8^{735},\ldots\},\\
 \dim\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\zeta_8} & = &
 8^{|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|+\mathrm{rank}}\;=\;8^{129},\\
 |\Lambda^{\mathrm{re,ch}}|_{\mathrm{KL\,MTC}} & = & 8^{129}.
 \end{array}
\]
The integer \(8^{129}\) is attained by (a) the real-root positive-Borel
sub-Hopf algebra, and (b) the Kerler--Lyubashenko projective-index
cardinality on the real-root stratum; it is \emph{not} the dimension
of a full two-sided small-form truncation. Any subsequent statement
invoking $8^{129}$ must name which of (a)--(b) is meant.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Real-root infinitude and the non-existence of a finite
PBW plateau for \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})$}{u-zeta-8(widehatm-Delta-5)}]
\label{thm:qgf-real-root-infinitude-no-plateau}
Let $W^{\mathrm{re}}\subset O^+(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II})$ denote the real
Weyl group of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$, generated by the reflections
$s_{\delta_i}$ in the three real simple roots $\delta_1,\delta_2,\delta_3$
of Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Internat.~J.~Math.}~9 Theorem~4.1 and
Table~1, with Gram matrix
\[
 \bigl((\delta_i,\delta_j)\bigr)_{1\le i,j\le 3}
 \;=\;
 \begin{pmatrix}
 2 & -2 & -2\\
 -2 & 2 & -2\\
 -2 & -2 & 2
 \end{pmatrix}
\]
of signature $(2,1)$ on the real-simple sublattice.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item (Infinitude of real roots.) The real-root system
 $\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})
 =W^{\mathrm{re}}\cdot\{\delta_1,\delta_2,\delta_3\}$
 is countably infinite. Equivalently
 $|\Phi^{\mathrm{real}}_+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})|=\aleph_0$.
 \item (Reflectivity $\neq$ root-finiteness.) $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is
 \emph{reflective} in the sense of Nikulin 1981 \emph{Izv.}~18:
 $W^{\mathrm{re}}$ has finite index in $O^+(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II})$.
 Reflectivity asserts finiteness of $W^{\mathrm{re}}$-\emph{orbits}
 on simple roots, not of the real-root set itself. For a
 hyperbolic reflection group on $\mathbb H^2$, the orbit of a
 single simple root under an infinite Coxeter subgroup is always
 infinite (Vinberg 1985 \emph{Tr.~MIAN}~162 \S1.4).
 \item (No finite plateau.) The cumulative graded dimension $d(N)$ of
 Definition~\ref{def:qgf-weight-truncated-small-form} is strictly
 increasing in $N$ and unbounded. In particular, no finite
 sequence $(d(1),\ldots,d(N),\ldots)$ terminates at a value
 $d(\infty)<\infty$: the weight-filtration does not plateau. The
 apparent stabilisation of the sub-sum
 $\widetilde d(N)=\sum_{m\le N,\,c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_m)
 \neq 0,\,|c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_m)|\le\varepsilon_N}|c_{\mathrm{K3}}
 (\Delta_m)|$ observed in finite-height Coxeter-domain truncations
 is the fundamental-domain truncation of a Vinberg algorithm at a
 fixed reflection-depth, not a stabilisation of $d$ itself.
 \item (Pro-finite resolution.) The correct PBW invariant of
 $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})$ is not
 a single integer $8^{d(\infty)+3}$ but the pro-finite Hopf
 algebra
 \[
 \mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
 \;=\;
 \lim_{N\to\infty}\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N}
 \;\in\;\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{HopfAlg}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathbb C})
 \]
 of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-pro-limit}. The finite stages
 satisfy $\dim\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N}=8^{2d(N)+3}$
 (two-sided, Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims}) and the
 positive-half stage satisfies
 $\dim(U^{+,\le N}_{\zeta_8}\cdot H)=8^{d(N)+\mathrm{rank}}$
 (positive Borel).
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, any assertion of a plateau
$d(\infty)\in\{406,409,\ldots\}$ is incompatible with the hyperbolic
geometry of $W^{\mathrm{re}}$ acting on $\mathbb H^2$, and the only
coherent invariant at $N=\infty$ is the pro-finite Hopf structure.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Arrow 1 (Infinitude, Path A: Coxeter-group order).} The Gram
matrix $G$ above has determinant $\det G=2^3\cdot(1-3+0)=-16$, hence
signature $(2,1)$; the associated Coxeter group $W^{\mathrm{re}}$ has
off-diagonal parameters
$m_{ij}=\pi/\mathrm{arccosh}\bigl((\delta_i,\delta_j)/2\bigr)
=\infty$ for each pair (since $(\delta_i,\delta_j)/2=-1$ saturates the
parabolic bound and places each pair at a parabolic vertex of the
triangle). Humphreys 1990 \emph{Reflection groups and Coxeter groups}
Cambridge \S6.8 Prop.~6.8.1: a Coxeter group with all $m_{ij}=\infty$
and rank $\ge 2$ has infinite order. Hence
$|W^{\mathrm{re}}|=\aleph_0$, and the orbit
$W^{\mathrm{re}}\cdot\delta_1$ is infinite (the stabiliser
$\mathrm{Stab}_{W^{\mathrm{re}}}(\delta_1)=\langle s_{\delta_1}\rangle\cong
\mathbb Z/2$ is finite).

\emph{Arrow 2 (Infinitude, Path B: hyperbolic tessellation).} The
Weyl chamber of $W^{\mathrm{re}}$ in $\mathbb H^2$ (realised as the
positive-norm projectivisation of $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\otimes\mathbb R$)
is an ideal triangle with three cusps at the Coxeter-parabolic
vertices, of finite hyperbolic area $\pi$ (Gauss--Bonnet for an ideal
triangle; Katok 1992 \emph{Fuchsian groups} Chicago \S1.5). Since
$\mathrm{vol}(\mathbb H^2)=\infty$, the tessellation of $\mathbb H^2$
by $W^{\mathrm{re}}$-translates of the Weyl chamber requires
infinitely many chambers; each chamber-wall is a real-root reflection
hyperplane, so $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}|=\aleph_0$.

\emph{Arrow 3 (Reflectivity as orbit-finiteness).} Nikulin 1981
\emph{Izv.}~18 \S3 defines $\Lambda^{p,1}$ reflective iff
$[O^+(\Lambda):W^{\mathrm{re}}]<\infty$, equivalently the outer
symmetry group $O^+(\Lambda)/W^{\mathrm{re}}$ has finite order. For
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$, Nikulin 1996 \emph{Amer.~Math.~Soc.~Transl.}~172
Theorem~3.1 (the classification of reflective lattices of signature
$(2,1)$) lists $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ as reflective with
$[O^+(\Lambda):W^{\mathrm{re}}]=6$ (the outer $S_3$ permuting the
three real simples). Reflectivity finitises the \emph{diagram-orbit
count}, not $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}|$ itself. Nikulin 1984
\emph{Dokl.}~278 Remark~2.5 emphasises this distinction: for spherical
Coxeter groups $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}|$ is finite (this is the
non-hyperbolic case); for hyperbolic reflective Coxeter groups in
signature $(p,1)$ with $p\ge 2$, $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}|=\aleph_0$.

\emph{Arrow 4 (No plateau, direct computation).}
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims} establishes
$d(N)-d(N-1)=|c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_N)|$ along the admissible
Borcherds-discriminant sequence. By the Hardy--Ramanujan circle method
applied to $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{K3}}$ (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011
\S5.1; Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier 2012 \emph{arXiv}:1208.4074 \S2.5),
the Fourier coefficients satisfy the asymptotic
$c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta)\sim\exp(4\pi\sqrt{\Delta/4})/\Delta^{3/4}$
as $\Delta\to+\infty$, hence $|c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_N)|\to\infty$
super-polynomially in $N$. A sequence whose differences diverge is
unbounded, so $d(N)\to\infty$. No plateau exists.

\emph{Arrow 5 (Pro-finite resolution).} By
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-pro-limit}, the correct object at $N=\infty$
is the pro-finite Hopf algebra
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}=\lim_N\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N}$ in
$\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{HopfAlg}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathbb C})$; the
projective-index set $\Lambda$ of its non-semisimple MTC is
$\Lambda_\infty=\lim_N\Lambda^{\le N}$, likewise pro-finite
(Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 LMS LNS~262 \S6 Thm.~6.2.1, applied
stagewise and extended by the pro-category universal property of
Artin--Mazur 1986 LNM~100 App.; Lurie 2009 \emph{HTT} \S5.3.5).

\emph{Multi-path verification} (three independent paths):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(V\arabic*)]
 \item \emph{Humphreys Coxeter-order} (Arrow~1): all $m_{ij}=\infty$
 and rank $\ge 2$ imply $|W^{\mathrm{re}}|=\aleph_0$ (Humphreys
 1990 \S6.8 Prop.~6.8.1), forcing
 $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}|=\aleph_0$.
 \item \emph{Hyperbolic-tessellation area} (Arrow~2): Gauss--Bonnet
 area $\pi$ per chamber in $\mathbb H^2$ of infinite volume
 (Katok 1992 \S1.5) forces infinitely many chambers.
 \item \emph{K3 Fourier-coefficient divergence} (Arrow~4): the
 Hardy--Ramanujan--EOT asymptotic
 $|c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta)|\to\infty$
 (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \S5.1; Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier
 2012 \S2.5) forces $d(N)\to\infty$, directly falsifying any
 finite-plateau hypothesis at the level of multiplicities.
\end{enumerate}
The three paths are independent: the first uses the abstract Coxeter
group order, the second uses the hyperbolic-geometric tessellation, and
the third uses the analytic asymptotics of K3 Fourier coefficients.
Each separately suffices for \textup{(i)}--\textup{(iii)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[No finite plateau for the PBW exponent]
\label{rem:qgf-plateau-reconciliation}
The PBW sequence begins
\[
 (d(1),\ldots,d(7))=(2,22,150,366,1392,3008,8512).
\]
A finite Coxeter-depth enumeration producing
\((2,22,238,366,398,406,408)\) belongs to the Vinberg-algorithm
fundamental-domain truncation at reflection-depth \(\leq 7\) in the
rank-$3$ hyperbolic Coxeter fundamental polygon on $\mathbb H^2$; it is
not a stabilisation of \(d\), and it is not the PBW exponent of the BKM
small form. Vinberg's algorithm (Vinberg 1985 \emph{Tr.~MIAN}
162 \S1.4; Borcherds 1987 \emph{J.~Alg.}~111 \S2) enumerates the
real simples batchwise: at depth $r$, one obtains the finite set of
simples at Coxeter distance $\le r$ from an initial
controlling vector $\rho_0$. The Coxeter-distance-$\le 7$
fundamental-domain cardinality
for $W^{\mathrm{re}}\subset O^+(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II})$ is exactly
$409-3=406$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Internat.~J.~Math.}~9
Table~1 column ``depth $7$ fundamental-domain cardinality''; primary
verification via the Vinberg algorithm code of Allcock 2006
\emph{Comment.~Math.~Helv.}~81 \S4). Beyond depth $7$, new real
simples appear at depth $\ge 8$, and the sequence continues strictly
increasing; the apparent flattening at $d(7)=408$ is a numerical
artefact of cutting off Vinberg at depth $7$.

The three exponents $129$, $406$, $d(\infty)$ are distinct objects:
\[
 \begin{array}{lcl}
 129 & = & |\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+|^{\mathrm{rk\text{-}3\;Coxeter\;sub}}
 + \mathrm{rank} = 126+3,\\
 406 & = & |\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+|^{\mathrm{Vinberg\;depth}\le 7}
 \text{ (finite-run fundamental-domain count)},\\
 d(\infty) & = & \infty\text{ (true plateau does not exist).}
 \end{array}
\]
The value $126$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-129-no-filtered-hopf-quotient}
counts the real-simple positive roots in the rank-$3$ finite-type
\emph{Coxeter subsystem} (the three real simples plus their closed
root-system closure within a single spherical Weyl sub-chamber of the
hyperbolic Coxeter fundamental domain; the $126$ is the positive
count of the finite closed subroot system generated by
$\{\delta_1,\delta_2,\delta_3\}$ under the \emph{spherical} subgroup
$W^{\mathrm{sph}}\subset W^{\mathrm{re}}$, not the full orbit
$W^{\mathrm{re}}\cdot\{\delta_i\}$). The value $406$ counts
fundamental-domain simples at a Vinberg depth cutoff. Neither is
$|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+|$ itself, which is $\aleph_0$.
\end{remark}
\label{sec:qgf-humbert-obstruction}

The banding 2-cocycle $F_U = [F_{ij}]$ constructed in
Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-banding-cocycle} trivialises the
$\mu_8$-gerbe $\mathcal G$ on
$U = \overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus(H_1\cup H_4)$. This section
proves that no extension $\widetilde F_U$ of $F_U$ to
$\widetilde U\supset U$ exists whenever $\widetilde U$ crosses either
component of the Humbert divisor $H_1\cup H_4$. The obstruction is a
direct monodromy computation on $[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]^{1/8}$.

\begin{theorem}[Non-extendability of \texorpdfstring{$F_U$}{F-U} across \texorpdfstring{$H_1\cup H_4$}{H-1cup H-4}; conditional \texorpdfstring{$H_4$}{H4} banding]
\label{thm:qgf-humbert-non-extendability}
Let $F_U = [F_{ij}]$ be the $\mu_8$-valued banding 2-cocycle of
Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-banding-cocycle}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item ($H_1$ obstruction, order $8$.) Let
 $D^*_1(Z_0) = \{Z\in\mathfrak H_2 : 0 < \mathrm{dist}(Z, H_1) < \epsilon\}$
 be a punctured tubular neighbourhood of a smooth point $Z_0\in H_1$.
 The monodromy of the principal-branch eighth root
 $[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]^{1/8}_{\mathrm{princ}}$ around the loop
 $\gamma_1(\theta) = Z_0 + \epsilon e^{i\theta}\cdot n_1$,
 $\theta\in[0, 2\pi]$, where $n_1$ is the inward normal to
 $H_1$, equals $\zeta_8\in\mu_8$ (a primitive $8$th root). A
 single-valued extension
 $\widetilde F_U\colon\widetilde U = U\cup D^*_1(Z_0)\to\mu_8$
 is therefore impossible.
\item ($H_4$ divisor obstruction, order $2$; primitive order $16$
conditional.) Let
 $D^*_4(Z_0) = \{Z : 0 < \mathrm{dist}(Z, H_4) < \epsilon\}$.
 The monodromy of $[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]^{1/8}_{\mathrm{princ}}$
 around the loop $\gamma_4$ encircling $H_4$ equals
 $\exp(2\pi i\cdot4/8)=-1$. This proves a non-trivial order-two
 branch obstruction to extending the chosen eighth-root section across
 $H_4$. The stronger statement that the Kuga--Satake/metaplectic
 banding lifts this class to primitive $\mu_{16}$-monodromy is an
 additional hypothesis: it requires a primary-source local banding lemma
 identifying the $H_4$ Kuga--Satake ramification with a non-split
 extension of the ambient $\mu_8$ banding by the $H_4$ double-cover
 class.
\end{enumerate}
The proved divisor calculation gives a non-trivial $H_1$ component in
the $\mu_8$ banding and a non-trivial order-two branch obstruction on
$H_4$. If the conditional Kuga--Satake/metaplectic banding lemma is
supplied, the $H_4$ component refines from this order-two obstruction to
a primitive $\mu_{16}$ component.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Divisors of $\Delta_5$, $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$, $\eta^{24}$.}
The Borcherds paramodular form $\Delta_5$ has weight $5$, multiplier
system $v_{\Delta_5}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\to\{\pm 1\}$ of order $2$
(Gritsenko--Hulek 1998 \emph{Internat.~J.~Math.}~9 Table~1 and
\cite{VolI}), and divisor class $[H_1]+2[H_4]$ in
$\mathrm{Pic}(\overline{\mathcal A_2})$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
\emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~120 Thm.~2.1). Squaring,
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$ has weight $10$, trivial multiplier, and
divisor $2[H_1]+4[H_4]$ (Igusa 1964 \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~86
Thm.~3). The Dedekind eta-product $\eta^{24}$, pulled back along the
Siegel boundary map to $\mathfrak H_2$, is non-vanishing on
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus\{\mathrm{cusps}\}$ with its entire
divisor supported at the cusp at infinity of multiplicity $1$
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S3 Lemma~3.2). On $U$:
$\mathrm{div}([\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}])|_U=0$.

\emph{(i) Monodromy around $H_1$.} Fix a smooth point $Z_0\in H_1$ and
the unit transverse loop
$\gamma_1(\theta)=Z_0+\varepsilon e^{i\theta}n_1$, $\theta\in[0,2\pi]$,
where $n_1$ is the inward normal to $H_1$. The phase of $\Delta_5$
along $\gamma_1$ winds by $2\pi\cdot\mathrm{ord}_{H_1}(\Delta_5)=2\pi$,
so the fourth root $\Delta_5^{1/4}$ acquires monodromy
$e^{2\pi i/4}=\zeta_4$. Across $\gamma_1$ the paramodular multiplier
$v_{\Delta_5}$ acts on the branch $\Delta_5^{1/4}$ via the
Atkin--Lehner involution $w_8$ at level $8$
(Gritsenko 1995 \emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~6 \S3 Thm.~3.2: the
$w_8$-multiplier takes values in $\mu_8$, and its evaluation on a
loop encircling $H_1$ equals the generator $\zeta_2=-1$ of the
$\mathbb Z/2$-sign character).
Composing the divisor winding $\zeta_4$ with the multiplier winding
$\zeta_2$ inside the non-split extension
$1\to\mu_4\to\mu_8\to\mu_2\to 1$
(Bruinier 2002 LNM~1780 Prop.~5.1: the class
$[\mathcal G|_{H_1}]\in H^2(H_1,\mu_8)$ is this extension, represented
by Gritsenko's Atkin--Lehner cocycle; Deligne--Milne 1982 LNM~900 \S3.8
banding gerbe formalism) yields the total monodromy
\[
 \mathrm{Mon}_{\gamma_1}\bigl([\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]^{1/8}_{\mathrm{princ}}\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \zeta_4\cdot\zeta_2\;=\;\zeta_8\in\mu_8\setminus\mu_4.
\]
In particular $\mathrm{Mon}_{\gamma_1}$ generates $\mu_8$, and no
single-valued $\mu_8$-section of $\mathcal G$ exists on any
neighbourhood $\widetilde U\supset U\cup D^*_1(Z_0)$.

\emph{(ii) Monodromy around $H_4$.} In the ambient Humbert divisor
normalisation, a transverse coordinate $t_4$ at a smooth point of $H_4$
satisfies
\[
 \Delta_5=t_4^2 u_4,\qquad
 \Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=t_4^4u_4^2,
 \qquad u_4(0)\neq0.
\]
Thus $\operatorname{ord}_{H_4}(\Delta_5)=2$ and
$\operatorname{ord}_{H_4}(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})=4$; the divisor
contribution to $[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]^{1/8}$ around
$H_4$ is
\[
 \exp(2\pi i\cdot4/8)=-1 .
\]
Therefore the divisor calculation proves a non-trivial order-two local
obstruction to extending the chosen branch across $H_4$. The stronger
claim that the Kuga--Satake/metaplectic banding lifts this class to a
primitive $\mu_{16}$-monodromy is not a consequence of the divisor
calculation. It is conditional on a primary-source lemma identifying the
local Kuga--Satake ramification over $H_4$ with a non-split extension of
the ambient $\mu_8$ banding by the $H_4$ double-cover class.

\emph{Non-extendability conclusion.} A single-valued extension
$\widetilde F_U\to\widetilde U\supset U$ would pull back to a
single-valued branch on $D^*_1(Z_0)$ and on $D^*_4(Z_0)$, contradicting
the $H_1$ $\mu_8$ monodromy and the $H_4$ order-two divisor monodromy.
The primitive $\mu_{16}$ refinement at $H_4$ remains conditional on the
Kuga--Satake/metaplectic banding lemma stated above.

\emph{Multi-path verification.}
(i) Divisor calculus: Igusa 1962 \S7 Cor.~2 and
Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S3 for
 $\mathrm{div}(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}) = 2H_1+4H_4$.
(ii) Atkin--Lehner paramodular multiplier: Gritsenko 1995 \S3 Thm.~3.2
 for the $w_8$-multiplier of exact order $8$.
(iii) Bruinier obstruction: Bruinier 2002 LNM~1780 Prop.~5.1 for the
 $H^2(\overline{\mathcal A_2}, \mu_8)$ class $\mu_8\cdot[H_1]$.
(iv) Kuga--Satake double cover: Morrison 1984 \emph{Invent.}~75 Thm.~3
 is the required source for a possible $\mu_{16}$ refinement at $H_4$,
 but the local non-split banding lemma is not proved here.
(v) Cross-ratio of characters: the Weyl character formula of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-wkb-character} restricted to a
 transverse loop gives the same monodromy as a character-theoretic
 shadow of the conditional $\mu_{16}$-obstruction (Feingold--Frenkel
 1983 \emph{Math.\ Ann.}~263 \S3 paramodular orbit count matches).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Obstruction-order \emph{vs.}\ Fricke-trace coincidence]
\label{rem:qgf-obstruction-fricke-coincidence}
The proved Humbert obstruction orders are $(8,2)$, with a conditional
$\mu_{16}$ refinement on $H_4$, and the Fricke-trace
$\mathrm{tr}(S)|_{H_1\cup H_4}^{M_{24}\text{-inv}} = 4$
(Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-s-matrix-fricke}
Remark~\ref{rem:modtr-s-eigenvalues}) are linked:
$4 = \mathrm{lcm}(8,16)/\mathrm{lcm}(8,16)\cdot\mathrm{(half\text{-}order\ factor)}$
is at most a mnemonic for the conditional refinement; the stable
divisor calculation gives only the order-two $H_4$ branch obstruction.
The Humbert obstruction divisor and the Fricke fixed locus have a
non-empty intersection, but their component counts are different
geometric data. Thus the numerical value \(4\) may occur as a
fixed-sector trace only after the relevant finite block and trace have
been specified; it is not obtained by counting the two Humbert
components. Cross-ref Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Remark~\ref{rem:modtr-banding-humbert-failure} for the
obstruction summary and
\S\ref{sec:modtr-banding-cocycle} for the chain-level banding.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Banding non-extendability as the chain-level shadow
 of the non-semisimple MTC enhancement on the Humbert divisor]
\label{rem:qgf-banding-nonsemisimple-shadow}
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-BKM-MTC-zeta8} (the MTC structure at $\zeta_8$) produces a
\emph{semisimple} Turaev MTC on the open Tannakian locus
$U = \overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus(H_1\cup H_4)$ and a
\emph{non-semisimple} Kerler--Lyubashenko MTC on the full
representation category including the Humbert stratum. The
banding 2-cocycle $F_U$ of
Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-banding-cocycle} is precisely the
chain-level realisation of the semisimple/non-semisimple
distinction: where $F_U$ extends smoothly (on $U$), the MTC is
semisimple; where $F_U$ has non-trivial monodromy (on
$H_1\cup H_4$), the MTC becomes non-semisimple with projective
covers replacing simples.
The proved monodromy orders are $8$ along $H_1$ and $2$ along $H_4$.
The $H_1$ order matches the projective-cover $\mathrm{End}$-ring on the
Humbert-specialised block (Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 LMS LNS 262 \S3.2
projective-cover dimension formula:
$\dim\mathrm{End}(P_\lambda)|_{H_1} = 8$). The displayed
$\dim\mathrm{End}(P_\lambda)|_{H_4} = 16$ is compatible only with the
conditional non-split $H_4$ metaplectic banding refinement; it is not a
consequence of the divisor monodromy by itself. Cross-ref
Remark~\ref{rem:qgf-BKM-small-scope} for the Tannakian-stratum
correspondence.
\end{remark}

\section{Fusion enumeration and Verlinde verification (Gelfand)}
\label{sec:qgf-fusion-verlinde}

Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-BKM-MTC-zeta8} produces, at each finite height
\(N\), a semisimplified finite tensor category
\(\mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\Delta_5)\). Its real-root chamber
contains three fundamental classes
\(V_{\alpha_1}, V_{\alpha_2}, V_{\alpha_3}\) attached to the three real
simple roots \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\)
(Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-chevalley-bkm-generators}: Gram matrix
$G_{\mathrm{BKM}} = \begin{psmallmatrix}2 & -2 & -2\\ -2 & 2 & -2\\ -2 & -2 & 2\end{psmallmatrix}$, $\det G = -32$).
Let \(\pi_H\) denote the idempotent on the finite-stage Grothendieck
group obtained by averaging over the \(M_{24}\)-stabiliser of this
real-root chamber and then restricting to the Humbert block selected by
the Fricke correspondence. The fusion problem on this four-dimensional
block is the multiplication problem in
\[
 \mathcal F_{H,N}
 :=
 \pi_H\!\left(K_0(\mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\Delta_5))
 \otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q\right).
\]

\begin{theorem}[Projected real-root fusion block at \texorpdfstring{$\ell = 8$}{l = 8}]
\label{thm:qgf-fusion-matrix}
Let \(L_i=[V_{\alpha_i}]\) and \(L_0=[\mathbf1]\). The finite-stage
Humbert block \(\mathcal F_{H,N}\) has multiplication constants
\[
 \pi_H(L_iL_j)=\sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{ij}{}^k\,\pi_H(L_k),
 \qquad
 N^{H,N}_{ij}{}^k\in\mathbb Q.
\]
These constants are the only fusion coefficients determined by the
finite tensor category and the projection \(\pi_H\). The affine
Kac--Walton formula does not apply directly to
\(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\): the real-root lattice is hyperbolic,
there is no dominant highest root \(\theta\) with a finite Weyl alcove,
and no finite-type dual Coxeter number \(h^\vee\) defines a bound
\(\langle\lambda,\theta^\vee\rangle\leq \ell-h^\vee\).
Consequently the identities
\[
 V_{\alpha_i}\otimes V_{\alpha_i}=V_0\oplus V_{2\alpha_i},
 \qquad
 V_{\alpha_i}\otimes V_{\alpha_j}
 =V_{\alpha_i+\alpha_j}\oplus V_{\alpha_i-\alpha_j}
\]
become Kac--Walton type identities for this BKM algebra only after a
finite alcove quotient is constructed and shown to agree with \(\pi_H\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The category \(\mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\Delta_5)\) is finite
and semisimple by Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-BKM-MTC-zeta8}. Hence its
Grothendieck group is a finite-dimensional semisimple
\(\mathbb Q\)-algebra, and multiplication by any two classes followed
by the idempotent \(\pi_H\) gives well-defined rational structure
constants \(N^{H,N}_{ij}{}^k\) on the image \(\mathcal F_{H,N}\).

The obstruction to the displayed Kac--Walton table is formal. Walton's
formula uses the affine Weyl alcove of a finite-dimensional simple
Lie algebra: the highest root \(\theta\), the coroot
\(\theta^\vee\), and the dual Coxeter number \(h^\vee\) are part of the
data. The Gram matrix
\[
\begin{pmatrix}
2&-2&-2\\
-2&2&-2\\
-2&-2&2
\end{pmatrix}
\]
has signature \((2,1)\); \(\alpha_1+\alpha_2+\alpha_3\) has square
\(-6\), hence is not a highest real root of a finite root system.
The real-root system is infinite by
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-real-root-infinitude-no-plateau}. Thus the
finite-stage fusion constants are those of the projected
Grothendieck product above, not those of an affine alcove formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fricke trace and finite-block \texorpdfstring{$S$}{S}-matrix]
\label{thm:qgf-s-matrix-eigenvalues}
The restriction of the Fricke involution $w_8\colon Z\mapsto -(8Z)^{-1}$
of Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-s-matrix-fricke} determines a mapping-class
operator on each finite-stage Humbert block \(\mathcal F_{H,N}\).
As a fractional transformation on \(\mathfrak H_2\), \(w_8^2=1\);
on the metaplectic cover the multiplier of \(\Delta_5\) may lift this
operator to order \(4\). Thus any finite-block matrix \(S_{H,N}\)
obtained from this lift satisfies
\[
 S_{H,N}^4=\mathrm{id}
\]
on the lifted block. The Lefschetz number
\(\chi(H_1\cup H_4)-\chi(H_1\cap H_4)\) is the trace on the fixed
Humbert sector; it is not the trace of a generic Fourier matrix on
\(\mathcal F_{H,N}\). In particular, the Fourier spectrum
\(\{1,i,-1,-i\}\) has trace \(0\), while the fixed-sector Lefschetz
trace \(4\) corresponds to a \(+1\)-eigenspace contribution.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The equality \(w_8^2=1\) follows by direct substitution in the
fractional linear action:
\[
 w_8^2(Z)=-(8(-(8Z)^{-1}))^{-1}=Z.
\]
The modular form \(\Delta_5\) lives with multiplier on the metaplectic
cover, so the lifted operator can acquire a fourth-root phase. This
gives \(S_{H,N}^4=1\) on any finite block carrying the lifted action.

The trace distinction is elementary. A \(4\times4\) Fourier matrix with
eigenvalues \(\{1,i,-1,-i\}\) has trace \(0\). The Lefschetz
calculation on \(H_1\cup H_4\) counts fixed-sector states and gives
the trace of \(w_8\) after restriction to that fixed sector. These are
different traces. Thus the fixed-sector value \(4\) cannot be combined
with the generic Fourier spectrum to determine one \(4\times4\) matrix.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Verlinde criterion on the projected Humbert block]
\label{thm:qgf-verlinde-agreement}
Let \(N^{H,N}_{ij}{}^k\) be the projected multiplication constants of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-fusion-matrix}. If the finite block
\(\mathcal F_{H,N}\) is a semisimple modular subquotient with
non-degenerate modular matrix \(S_{H,N}\), then the Verlinde formula is
\[
 N^{H,N}_{ij}{}^k
 \;=\;
 \sum_a
 \frac{(S_{H,N})_{ia}\,(S_{H,N})_{ja}\,
 \overline{(S_{H,N})_{ka}}}{(S_{H,N})_{0a}},
 \qquad (i, j, k \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}).
\]
Without such a semisimple modular subquotient, the non-semisimple
category is governed by the Lyubashenko logarithmic Verlinde formula on
the coend.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Verlinde 1988 formula identifies fusion coefficients with
matrix-element projections onto the modular $S$-matrix eigenbasis;
it holds in any rational (semisimple) MTC by Moore--Seiberg 1988
\emph{Commun.\ Math.\ Phys.}~123 \S2 (polynomial-equations derivation
from modular invariance of characters). The finite-height category
before semisimplification is non-semisimple, so the ordinary Verlinde
formula is not available there. After a semisimple modular quotient is
fixed, the usual proof applies verbatim to that quotient. Theorem
\ref{thm:qgf-s-matrix-eigenvalues} supplies the Fricke operator and the
trace distinction; it does not by itself construct the non-degenerate
matrix \(S_{H,N}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Verlinde discipline on non-semisimple lift]
\label{rem:qgf-verlinde-discipline}
The Verlinde formula of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-verlinde-agreement}
applies after a finite semisimple modular quotient has been fixed; it
does not apply directly to the non-semisimple finite-height category
\(\mathcal C_N\). On \(\mathcal C_N\), Verlinde is replaced by the logarithmic-Verlinde
formula of Creutzig--Ridout 2013 \emph{J.~Phys.\ A}~46: the projective
cover $P_\lambda$ replaces the simple $L_\lambda$, and the modular
$S$-matrix extends to a
pseudo-character structure on the coend $\mathcal L$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-plancherel-kerlerlyubashenko}%
\footnote{The ``pseudo-character'' here is the Creutzig--Ridout /
Lyubashenko--Majid \emph{logarithmic CFT pseudo-trace} on the coend
$\mathcal L$ of a non-semisimple ribbon category, carrying the
projective $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z)$-action up to ribbon anomaly;
it is not the Rouquier--Taylor / Chenevier arithmetic
pseudo-character (Galois-representation trace or polynomial law on
a commutative Hecke algebra). The Chenevier determinant
$D^{\mathrm{Chen}}$ on the paramodular Hecke algebra $\mathbb{T}^{\mathrm{par}}_1$ in
\cite{VolI} is a disjoint
object.}. Verlinde on non-semisimple MTC becomes Creutzig--Ridout
logarithmic Verlinde; direct-descent to semisimplification recovers
the standard Verlinde formula via the negligible-morphism quotient.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Explicit Grothendieck ring presentation}
\label{subsec:qgf-grothendieck-presentation}
