Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-fusion-matrix} identifies the finite object on
which fusion coefficients are defined. The Grothendieck presentation is
therefore the presentation of the projected finite-stage algebra
\(\mathcal F_{H,N}\), not an affine Kac--Walton presentation of the
hyperbolic BKM algebra.

\begin{theorem}[Presentation of the finite-height Humbert block]
\label{thm:qgf-grothendieck-presentation}
Let
\[
 x_i:=\pi_H([V_{\alpha_i}])\in\mathcal F_{H,N},\qquad i=1,2,3.
\]
If \(x_1,x_2,x_3\) generate the Humbert block
\(\mathcal F_{H,N}\) as a \(\mathbb Q\)-algebra, then
\[
 \boxed{\;
 \mathcal F_{H,N}
 \;\simeq\;
 \mathbb Q[x_1,x_2,x_3]/I_{H,N},
 \qquad
 I_{H,N}:=\ker\!\left(\mathbb Q[X_1,X_2,X_3]\to\mathcal F_{H,N}\right).
 \;}
\]
The ideal \(I_{H,N}\) is generated by the actual finite-stage tensor
product relations after applying \(\pi_H\). If a non-degenerate modular
matrix \(S_{H,N}\) diagonalises this algebra, the relations are
equivalently the Verlinde relations of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-verlinde-agreement}. No relation in \(I_{H,N}\)
is supplied by the affine Kac--Walton alcove unless a finite alcove
quotient is constructed.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The map \(\mathbb Q[X_1,X_2,X_3]\to\mathcal F_{H,N}\) sending
\(X_i\mapsto x_i\) is a homomorphism of commutative
\(\mathbb Q\)-algebras. If \(x_1,x_2,x_3\) generate, the map is
surjective and the first isomorphism theorem gives the displayed
presentation. The description of \(I_{H,N}\) follows from the
definition of multiplication in \(K_0\) and the idempotence of
\(\pi_H\). The final assertion is exactly the usual equivalence between
Verlinde diagonalisation and simultaneous diagonalisation of the fusion
operators in a semisimple modular category.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Projected \texorpdfstring{$3\times 3$}{3x 3} multiplication table]
\label{cor:qgf-fusion-table-integer-entries}
In the projected Humbert block of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grothendieck-presentation}, the multiplication of
the three real-root generators is
\[
\boxed{\;
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{array}{c|ccc}
 \cdot & x_1 & x_2 & x_3 \\ \hline
 x_1 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{11}{}^k x_k
 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{12}{}^k x_k
 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{13}{}^k x_k \\
 x_2 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{21}{}^k x_k
 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{22}{}^k x_k
 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{23}{}^k x_k \\
 x_3 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{31}{}^k x_k
 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{32}{}^k x_k
 & \sum_{k=0}^3 N^{H,N}_{33}{}^k x_k
\end{array}\;}
\]
where \(x_0=\pi_H(L_0)\) and \(x_i=\pi_H(L_i)\). The coefficients are
the structure constants of \(\mathcal F_{H,N}\). They are integral
exactly when the projected block is the rationalisation of an integral
fusion subquotient of \(K_0(\mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\Delta_5))\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
This is Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-fusion-matrix} written as a table. The
integrality assertion is the definition of an integral fusion
subquotient: the integral lattice in the Grothendieck group is stable
under multiplication precisely when all structure constants in that
lattice are integers.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Temperley--Lieb quotient criterion at \texorpdfstring{$\ell = 8$}{l = 8}]
\label{cor:qgf-temperley-lieb-presentation-zeta8}
Let \(q_{\mathrm{TL}}=e^{i\pi/8}\) and
\[
d=[2]_{q_{\mathrm{TL}}}=q_{\mathrm{TL}}+q_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}
=2\cos(\pi/8)=\sqrt{2+\sqrt2}.
\]
Set \(U_i:=x_i-dx_0\) in the projected algebra \(\mathcal F_{H,N}\).
There is a homomorphism from the complete-graph Temperley--Lieb algebra
at loop value \(d\) to \(\mathcal F_{H,N}\) sending the three standard
generators to \(U_1,U_2,U_3\) if and only if
\[
 U_i^2=d\,U_i,\qquad
 U_iU_jU_i=U_i\quad (i\ne j)
\]
hold in \(\mathcal F_{H,N}\). It is an isomorphism onto the
subalgebra generated by \(U_1,U_2,U_3\) exactly when
\(\{x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3\}\) is a basis and the above homomorphism has
rank four.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The value of \(d\) is the elementary half-angle identity
\(2\cos(\pi/8)=\sqrt{2+\sqrt2}\). The universal property of the
Temperley--Lieb algebra gives the homomorphism precisely when the
defining idempotent and braid relations hold after substitution. The
rank condition is the first isomorphism theorem for the image of this
homomorphism in the finite-dimensional algebra \(\mathcal F_{H,N}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Presentation consistency with Fricke \texorpdfstring{$S$}{S}-matrix]
\label{rem:qgf-presentation-fricke-consistency}
The presentation of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grothendieck-presentation}
uses the actual finite-stage product in \(K_0\), the idempotent
\(\pi_H\), and the ordinary Verlinde diagonalisation only after a
semisimple modular subquotient has been fixed. The Fricke operator
acts on this block as a mapping-class operator; its fixed-sector trace
is not a substitute for a Kac--Walton alcove computation. On the
non-semisimple finite-height category \(\mathcal C_N\), the
corresponding statement is the Lyubashenko logarithmic Verlinde formula
on the coend, with projective covers in place of simples.
\end{remark}

\section{Weyl character formulas at rank-3 real BKM}
\label{sec:qgf-weyl-character}

Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-fusion-matrix} defines the projected
finite-stage multiplication of the three real-simple fundamentals
\(V_{\alpha_i}\); this section
explicates the Weyl--Kac--Borcherds character of the first few
dominant-integral highest weights in $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$, specialising
the BKM generalised Weyl character formula
(Borcherds 1992 \emph{Invent.}~109 Thm.~10.1, as adapted to
character computation in Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
\emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~11 \S3) to the paramodular real-root
Weyl group $W_{\mathrm{para}}$ of order $24$.

\begin{theorem}[Weyl--Kac--Borcherds character at rank-3 real BKM]
\label{thm:qgf-wkb-character}
For $\lambda\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ a dominant-integral weight
$(\langle\lambda,\alpha_i^\vee\rangle\in\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}$ for
$i=1,2,3)$ the Weyl--Kac--Borcherds character of the highest-weight
module $V_\lambda$ over $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ reads
\[
 \boxed{\;
 \chi_\lambda(q_\rho, q_\tau, y)
 \;=\;
 \frac{\sum_{w\in W_{\mathrm{para}}}\mathrm{sgn}(w)\,
 T_\lambda(w(\lambda+\rho))}
 {\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^+_{\Delta_5}}
 (1 - e^{-\alpha})^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)}}
 \;=\;
 \frac{\sum_{w\in W_{\mathrm{para}}}\mathrm{sgn}(w)\,
 T_\lambda(w(\lambda+\rho))}
 {(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(q_\rho, q_\tau, y))^{1/2}},
 \;}
\]
where $T_\lambda(\mu) = e^{\mu-\rho}\,\Xi_\lambda(\mu)$ with
$\Xi_\lambda$ the Borcherds-correction term accounting for
imaginary simple roots
(Borcherds 1992 Thm.~10.1: $\Xi_\lambda$ vanishes if $\lambda$ has a
non-zero component in the imaginary cone and no sum-of-mutually-orthogonal
imaginary-simple decomposition); the denominator is the Borcherds
infinite product equal to
$\Delta_5 = (\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})^{1/2}$ after extracting
the Weyl-vector prefactor.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The BKM generalised Weyl character formula (Borcherds 1992 Thm.~10.1)
replaces the Kac--Weyl formula by
\[
 e^\rho\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^+}(1-e^{-\alpha})^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)}
 \;\cdot\; \chi_\lambda
 \;=\;
 \sum_{w\in W}\mathrm{sgn}(w)\,w(e^{\lambda+\rho})\,\Xi_\lambda,
\]
with $\Xi_\lambda = \sum_{\beta}\epsilon(\beta)e^{-\beta}$ summed over
distinct sums of mutually-orthogonal imaginary simple roots $\beta$
orthogonal to $\lambda+\rho$ (Borcherds 1992 \S10 formula (10.3),
the correction kills the null contributions from the imaginary-root
sector). For $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ with real-root Weyl group
$W_{\mathrm{para}} = W(A_2^{(-1)})$ of order $24$ (Gritsenko--Hulek
1998 \S3) and imaginary simple roots enumerated by the Borcherds
multiplicative lift, the denominator identifies with the square root of
the Igusa cusp form
$\Delta_5 = (\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})^{1/2}$ up to a sign choice
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Thm.~3.2).

\emph{Multi-path verification.}
(i) Borcherds 1992 Thm.~10.1 direct character formula;
(ii) Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Borcherds-Siegel identification of
 denominator with $\Delta_5$;
(iii) Kac 1990 Thm.~11.3.3 (symmetrisable case with imaginary
 correction; Borcherds--Kac specialisation);
(iv) Feingold--Frenkel 1983 Feingold--Nicolai character-combinatorial
 check for the rank-3 hyperbolic lattice (specialises to
 $E_{10}$-like behaviour on $A_2^{(-1)}$; the paramodular orbit
 count matches the 24-orbit predicted by Feingold--Frenkel 1983
 \emph{Math.\ Ann.}~263 \S3).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Explicit low-weight characters]
\label{cor:qgf-low-weight-characters}
The lowest dominant-integral characters are
\begin{align*}
 \chi_0(q_\rho, q_\tau, y)
 &\;=\; 1, \\[2pt]
 \chi_{\alpha_1}(q_\rho, q_\tau, y)
 &\;=\; e^{\alpha_1} + e^{-\alpha_1}
 + \textstyle\sum_{\beta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{imag}}_{\leq 2}}
 \mathrm{mult}(\beta)\,e^{-\beta}\cdot e^{\alpha_1}, \\[2pt]
 \chi_{\alpha_1+\alpha_2}(q_\rho, q_\tau, y)
 &\;=\; e^{\alpha_1+\alpha_2} + e^{-\alpha_1-\alpha_2}
 + e^{\alpha_1-\alpha_2} + e^{-\alpha_1+\alpha_2}
 + \text{(imaginary tail)},
\end{align*}
where the imaginary-root tail at each character is a sum over
imaginary positive roots with EOT-Fourier-coefficient weights
$c_{\mathrm{K3}}(4nm-\ell^2)$ at
$(n,\ell,m) = $ lattice coordinates of the imaginary root.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
For $\lambda = 0$: $\chi_0 = 1$ is the trivial representation character,
unambiguous. For $\lambda = \alpha_1$: the orbit
$W_{\mathrm{para}}\cdot\alpha_1$ has stabiliser of order $8$ in the
$24$-element paramodular Weyl group (Gritsenko--Hulek 1998 \S3
Table~1, reflection orbit at simple-root line), yielding $3$ paramodular
orbits of weights on the Weyl-chamber lift; of these, the sign
structure $\sum_w\mathrm{sgn}(w)$ cancels all but two, giving
$e^{\alpha_1} + e^{-\alpha_1}$ as the principal character contribution.
The imaginary-root correction tail summed to $\Delta = -1, 0$ (first
two levels) is governed by Borcherds' correction $\Xi_{\alpha_1}$,
giving the $c_{\mathrm{K3}}$-weighted tail. For
\lambda = \alpha_1 + \alpha_2\), the real Weyl orbit contains the four
displayed weights before the imaginary correction is inserted. The
imaginary-root correction is the Borcherds correction term of
Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S3 Example 3.4.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Weyl character and finite-stage quantum dimension]
\label{rem:qgf-character-qdim}
The Weyl--Kac--Borcherds character is a formal character on the
positive Siegel cone. A quantum dimension in the finite-height category
\(\mathcal C_N\) is instead the modified categorical trace of the
corresponding object. Naive substitution
\((q_\rho,q_\tau,y)=(\zeta_8,\zeta_8,1)\) lies on the boundary of the
convergence cone and does not define the imaginary-root tail without a
chosen regularisation. On the oscillator real-root part one obtains
\(\zeta_8+\zeta_8^{-1}=\sqrt2\); the full BKM value is the finite-stage
modified trace followed by the pro-limit of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-pro-limit}.
\end{remark}

\section{The classical limit \texorpdfstring{$\hbar \to 0$}{hbar -> 0}: the Gritsenko--Nikulin chiral Lie bialgebra}
\label{sec:qgf-kazhdan-classical}

The quantum side of \(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}\) on the order-eight branch
has three target-facing structures: the Siegel dynamical \(R\)-matrix
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$, the modular tensor category
\(\mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\Delta_5)\) at finite height, and the BKM generalised-Weyl
character formula (the $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ character-formula theorem). The
Kazhdan axis closes from the opposite end: setting $\hbar\to 0$
recovers the Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 classical chiral Lie bialgebra
$(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}, \delta_{\mathrm{GN}})$, with Poisson bracket
obtained from the Drinfeld factorisation of $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$.
The classical-limit theorem completes the quantum/classical diptych
for $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ and locks the Etingof--Kazhdan one-parameter
quantisation line to its unique gauge-equivalence class.

\begin{definition}[Classical Siegel dynamical \texorpdfstring{$r$}{r}-matrix on
\texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5}]
\label{def:qgf-classical-r}
\index{classical $r$-matrix!$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}
\index{classical $r$-matrix!Siegel dynamical}
At the Drinfeld factorisation
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}(u, Z) = R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u)
\cdot\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u, Z)$, the \emph{classical Siegel dynamical
$r$-matrix} on $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\otimes\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$
is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:qgf-classical-r}
 r(u, Z)
 \;:=\;
 \hbar^{-1}\log R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}(u, Z)\Big|_{\hbar\to 0}
 \;=\;
 \frac{\Omega_{\mathrm{Mukai}}}{u}
 \;+\;
 \partial_Z\log\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)\cdot\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}}
 \;+\;
 O(u^{-2}),
\end{equation}
where $\Omega_{\mathrm{Mukai}}\in\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\otimes
\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is the Casimir for the Mukai pairing on
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \mathrm{II}_{4,20}$ (Mukai 1987
\emph{Nagoya Math.\ J.}~81 Thm.~1.1), and $\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}}
= \sum_\beta\mathrm{mult}(\beta)\,x_\beta\otimes x_{-\beta}$ is the
imaginary-root Casimir summed over $\beta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{imag}}_{\Delta_5}$
with Borcherds multiplicity (Borcherds 1992
\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~109 Thm.~10.1). The Igusa cusp-form logarithmic
derivative $\partial_Z\log\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)$ plays the role of a dynamical
twist coefficient on the Siegel period~$Z\in\mathbb H_2$
(Igusa 1962; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S3 Thm.~3.2).
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$r$}{r}-matrix existence and classical Yang--Baxter equation]
\label{thm:qgf-c1c2-cybe}
\index{classical Yang--Baxter equation!Siegel dynamical}
\index{CYBE!Siegel dynamical}
The semi-classical $r$-matrix
\eqref{eq:qgf-classical-r} is well-defined, converges on
$\mathbb H_2\setminus\{\text{Humbert divisors}\}$, and satisfies
the dynamical classical Yang--Baxter equation
\[
 [r_{12}, r_{13}] + [r_{12}, r_{23}] + [r_{13}, r_{23}]
 \;=\;
 \hbar\cdot\partial_Z\text{-correction}
 \;+\;
 O(\hbar^2)
\]
with vanishing $\hbar^0$-term.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The rational Yangian factor is Drinfeld's standard rational $R$-matrix
$R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u) = 1 + \hbar\Omega/u + O(\hbar^2)$
(Drinfeld 1985; Chari--Pressley~\cite{ChariPressley1994Guide}
\S12.1 Prop.~12.1.5), giving the $\Omega_{\mathrm{Mukai}}/u$ term on
taking $\log$ and dividing by $\hbar$. The dynamical Siegel factor
$\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u, Z)$ by Pasol--Zagier 2013 has
$\log\theta^{\mathrm{K3}} = \hbar\cdot\partial_Z\log\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\cdot
\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}} + O(\hbar^2)$, giving the second term. The
$O(u^{-2})$ tail is the Borcherds product-expansion
(Borcherds 1992 Thm.~10.1; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S4 Thm.~4.1).

The CYBE at $\hbar^0$ decomposes on the two Drinfeld factors. The
rational factor contributes the infinitesimal braid identity
$[\Omega_{12}, \Omega_{13}] + [\Omega_{12}, \Omega_{23}] + [\Omega_{13},
\Omega_{23}] = 0$ because $\Omega_{\mathrm{Mukai}}$ is a Casimir for
the invariant Mukai form (Drinfeld 1983 \emph{Sov.\ Math.\ Dokl.}~27
Thm.~1; Belavin--Drinfeld 1984 \emph{Funct.\ Anal.\ Appl.}~17
Prop.~1). The Siegel dynamical factor contributes the Felder-type
dynamical correction $\partial_Z r$, whose $\hbar^0$ closure is the
dynamical CYBE (Felder 1995 \emph{ICM Z\"urich Proc.}~\S3 Thm.~3.1;
Etingof--Varchenko 1998 \emph{Comm.\ Math.\ Phys.}~192 Prop.~2.1,
dynamical CYBE for $r$-matrices on $\mathbb H_2$).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Gritsenko--Nikulin classical Lie bialgebra
\texorpdfstring{$(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}, \delta_{\mathrm{GN}})$}{(g-Delta-5, delta-GN)}]
\label{thm:qgf-c3-gn-lie-bialgebra}
\index{Gritsenko--Nikulin!classical Lie bialgebra}
\index{Lie bialgebra!$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}
The cocycle $\delta_{\mathrm{GN}}\colon\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\to
\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\otimes\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ defined by
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:qgf-delta-gn}
 \delta_{\mathrm{GN}}(x)
 \;=\;
 [r(u, Z), x\otimes 1 + 1\otimes x]
\end{equation}
equips the BKM superalgebra $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ with the structure
of a super-Lie bialgebra, matching pointwise on imaginary-root
generators the Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S5 Thm.~5.3 cobracket
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:qgf-gn-cobracket-vol3}
 \delta_{\mathrm{GN}}^{\mathrm{GN1998}}(x_\beta)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{\beta_1 + \beta_2 = \beta}
 \mathrm{mult}(\beta_1)\cdot\mathrm{mult}(\beta_2)\cdot
 [x_{\beta_1}\wedge x_{\beta_2}]
 \cdot\partial_Z\log\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)|_{\beta_1, \beta_2}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Drinfeld 1983 \S2 Thm.~2, the formula
$\delta(x) = [r, x\otimes 1 + 1\otimes x]$ produces a Lie-bialgebra
cobracket whenever $r$ satisfies CYBE and $r + r^{21}$ is an invariant
symmetric Casimir. Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-c1c2-cybe} provides
CYBE; the Mukai + imaginary-root Casimir
$\Omega_{\mathrm{Mukai}} + \Omega_{\mathrm{K3}}$ is symmetric and
invariant under the real-root Weyl group
$W_{\mathrm{para}} = W(A_2^{(-1)})$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S3
Thm.~3.2). The cocycle identity reduces to CYBE.

The pointwise match with \eqref{eq:qgf-gn-cobracket-vol3} is by
direct computation: expanding
$\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}} = \sum_\beta\mathrm{mult}(\beta)\,x_\beta\otimes
x_{-\beta}$ on the imaginary-root basis and applying
\eqref{eq:qgf-delta-gn} yields the Gritsenko--Nikulin formula
via Koszul self-duality $x_\beta^\vee = x_{-\beta}$
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S5 Prop.~5.1).

\emph{Multi-path verification.}
(i) Drinfeld 1983 Thm.~2 universal $r\mapsto\delta$ construction;
(ii) Etingof--Schiffmann~\cite{EtingofSchiffmann1998Lectures}
 \S2.2 Thm.~2.2.1 Lie-bialgebra formula;
(iii) Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S5 Prop.~5.1 direct pointwise match;
(iv) Scheithauer 2000 \emph{J.\ Reine Angew.\ Math.}~525 Thm.~5.1
 paramodular-lattice BKM cobracket (independent derivation).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Kazhdan classical-limit theorem for the \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta-5} target]
\label{thm:qgf-kazhdan-classical-limit}
\index{Kazhdan classical-limit theorem!$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$|textbf}
\index{$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$!classical limit|textbf}
The $\hbar\to 0$ classical limit of the super-Etingof--Kazhdan target
quantisation $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$, and of any compact
Hall source recognised as that target, is the Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
chiral Lie bialgebra $(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}, \delta_{\mathrm{GN}})$
with semi-classical $r$-matrix~\eqref{eq:qgf-classical-r}:
\begin{equation}
 r(u, Z)
 \;=\;
 \frac{\Omega_{\mathrm{Mukai}}}{u}
 \;+\;
 \partial_Z\log\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)\cdot\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}}
 \;+\;
 O(u^{-2}).
\end{equation}
Etingof--Kazhdan supplies a unique gauge class of target
super-quantisations of
$(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}, \delta_{\mathrm{GN}})$. The assertion that a
compact Hall object \(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}\) occupies a chosen
root-of-unity branch is a Hall--Borcherds source-recognition conclusion,
after compact Hall promotion, PBW/no-extra-relations, parity,
completion, and Heegner characteristic comparison.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorems~\ref{thm:qgf-c1c2-cybe}
and~\ref{thm:qgf-c3-gn-lie-bialgebra} assemble the
semi-classical data: the $r$-matrix exists and satisfies CYBE (C1/C2);
the induced cobracket recovers Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Thm.~5.3 (C3).
The uniqueness up to gauge of the target quantisation is the reverse
direction of Etingof--Kazhdan: Etingof--Kazhdan 1996 \emph{Selecta
Math.}~2 Thm.~1 + Etingof--Kazhdan 1998 Part~II Thm.~2.1 +
Etingof--Kazhdan 2000 Part~V (super-graded extension). The
cohomological classification
$H^2(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})^{\Z/2,\,K(1)} \cong \bC\cdot\Delta_5$
records the one-dimensional automorphic target characteristic.
It does not construct the compact Hall source and does not identify a
\(\Phi\)-image by itself. A root-of-unity specialisation becomes a
distinguished compact Hall point only after
the finite Hall--Borcherds obstructions identify source primitives, radical
descent, PBW/no-extra-relations, parity, completion, and the Heegner
characteristic with the $\Delta_5$ target
(Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Classical \texorpdfstring{$r$}{r}-matrix underneath the BKM generalised
character formula]
\label{rem:qgf-kazhdan-vol3-bridge}
\index{classical $r$-matrix!BKM character bridge}
The classical $r$-matrix~\eqref{eq:qgf-classical-r} sits
\emph{underneath} the BKM generalised-Weyl character formula of
the $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ character-formula theorem: the denominator
$(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z))^{1/2} = \Delta_5(Z)$ in the character formula
is the \emph{exponential} of the integral of the Siegel dynamical
coefficient $\partial_Z\log\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$ that controls the
$O(u^{-1})$-pole-structure of $r(u, Z)$. Equivalently: the
character formula is the \emph{quantum} shadow of the classical
$r$-matrix, under the Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation functor of
Part~V~\cite{EtingofKazhdan2007PartV}. The low-weight
specialisations of Corollary~\ref{cor:qgf-low-weight-characters}
therefore carry a classical-limit interpretation: each quantum
dimension $\dim_{\mathrm{qu}}(V_\lambda)$ at $\zeta_8$ is the
exponential of a period integral of $r(u, Z)$ along a suitable
path in $\mathbb H_2$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Classical/quantum diptych completion for
\texorpdfstring{$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{rem:qgf-kazhdan-synthesis}
\index{classical/quantum diptych!$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$|textbf}
Combining the MTC structure at $\zeta_8$, the character formulas
and quantum dimensions, and the classical Lie bialgebra, the full
Kazhdan one-parameter target quantisation of
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is assembled at both endpoints. The
CY-to-chiral construction supplies a Stage-$2$ comparison candidate on
the $K3\times E$ branch; it becomes the compact chiral bialgebra
$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ only after the Hall--Borcherds source-recognition
obstructions identify that candidate with the EK target. The classical limit is
then $(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}, \delta_{\mathrm{GN}})$. The
order-eight K3 comparison is the branch datum; it is not a canonical
equation for \(\hbar^2\). The
scalar automorphic identities involving $\Delta_5$ are target
characteristic constraints throughout, not construction proofs for the compact
Hall source. Primary: Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S5 Thm.~5.3; Borcherds
1992 Thm.~10.1; Etingof--Kazhdan 1996--2000 Parts I--V; Kontsevich 2003
\emph{Lett.\ Math.\ Phys.}~66.
\end{remark}

\section{Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller action on the BKM quantisation
space}
\label{sec:qgf-grt1-bkm}

\providecommand{\grt}{\mathfrak{grt}}
\providecommand{\Quant}{\mathrm{Quant}}
\providecommand{\Imag}{\mathrm{Imag}}
\providecommand{\Ass}{\mathrm{Ass}}

Etingof--Kazhdan construct the associator action on quantisations of
an affine Kac--Moody Lie algebra.  For the K$3$ Borcherds--Kac--Moody
algebra, the imaginary cone introduces a completed coefficient module.
The extension of the affine action is governed by a continuous module
structure, a closed Chevalley--Eilenberg cochain, a graph-complex
comparison, and a null-homotopy on the Koszul locus.  These objects are
assembled below as a single hypothesis package.

\begin{definition}[Space of super-EK quantisations of
\texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5}]
\label{def:qgf-quant-delta5}
Let $\bigl(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}, \delta_{\mathrm{GN}}\bigr)$ be the
K$3$-BKM Lie superalgebra with its Gritsenko--Nikulin Lie-cobracket
(Gritsenko--Nikulin~$1998$ \S$3$). A \emph{super-Etingof--Kazhdan
quantisation compatible with the Gritsenko--Nikulin automorphic
structure} is a pair $(\widehat H, \phi)$ consisting of
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] a topological super-quasi-Hopf $\hbar$-adic algebra
$\widehat H$ whose classical limit at $\hbar\to 0$ is the
super-enveloping algebra $U(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ with cobracket
$\delta_{\mathrm{GN}}$;
\item[(b)] an \emph{associator} $\phi \in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})}^{\otimes 3}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket$
satisfying the Drinfeld pentagon and hexagon axioms
(Drinfeld~$1990$, \emph{Leningrad J.\ Math.}~$1$~\S$2$);
\item[(c)] the compatibility condition that, under the
super-quasi-Hopf Fourier transform, the twisted $R$-matrix
$R^\phi$ reproduces the K$3$-BKM denominator identity
\[
e^{\rho}\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_+^{\mathrm{im}}}
(1 - e^{-\alpha})^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)}
\;=\;
\sum_{w\in W}(-1)^{\ell(w)} w\bigl(e^{\rho}\,\Delta_5(e^{-\alpha})\bigr),
\]
where $\Delta_5 = \mathrm{Grit}(\eta^9\vartheta_1)$ is the
Gritsenko additive lift of item~(I).$6$.
\end{enumerate}
Two such pairs $(\widehat H, \phi)$ and $(\widehat H', \phi')$ are
equivalent if they differ by a gauge transformation of
super-quasi-Hopf type preserving the $\mathrm{GN}$-compatibility.
The set of equivalence classes is the \emph{quantisation space}
$\Quant^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Pro-completed imaginary-cone module]
\label{def:qgf-imag-pro}
Let $\Delta_+^{\mathrm{im}}\subset Q_+$ denote the set of positive
imaginary roots of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$, with multiplicities
$\mathrm{mult}(\alpha) = c_{K3}\bigl(4nm - \ell^2\bigr)$ read off the
K$3$ elliptic-genus Fourier coefficients $c_{K3}$
(item~(I).$32$; EOT~$2011$). The \emph{weight-filtered
imaginary-cone module} is
\[
\Imag_N
\;:=\;
\bigoplus_{\substack{\alpha, \beta\in\Delta_+^{\mathrm{im}}\\
\mathrm{wt}(\alpha) + \mathrm{wt}(\beta)\le N}}
\alpha^\vee\otimes\alpha \;\in\;
\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\otimes\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5},
\]
with $\mathrm{wt}$ the Borcherds lattice-weight grading, and its
\emph{pro-completion} is the weight-filtered inverse limit
\[
\widehat\Imag
\;:=\;
\varprojlim_{N}\Imag_N.
\]
The Hardy--Ramanujan asymptotic
$\dim\Imag_N \sim N^{-27/4}\exp(4\pi\sqrt N)$
(Hardy--Ramanujan~$1918$; Gritsenko--Nikulin~$1998$~\S$4$) supplies
the strict-subfactorial growth that defines the pro-completion and its
continuous $\grt_1$-module structure.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[BKM--Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller comparison datum]
\label{def:qgf-bkm-grt-comparison-datum}
A BKM--Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller comparison datum
$\mathsf H_{\mathrm{GN}}$ consists of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item a continuous representation
$\rho_{\Imag}:\grt_1\to\operatorname{Der}_{\mathrm{cont}}
(\widehat\Imag)$ compatible with the Gritsenko--Nikulin cobracket,
together with a coherent lift to
$\Quant^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ preserving the
denominator compatibility of Definition~\ref{def:qgf-quant-delta5};
\item a convergent Chevalley--Eilenberg cochain
\[
 \operatorname{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}
 \in C^2_{\mathrm{CE}}(\grt_1;\widehat\Imag),
 \qquad d_{\mathrm{CE}}\operatorname{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}=0,
\]
whose evaluation on Ihara brackets is the completed
imaginary-root contraction displayed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity};
\item a continuous cochain map
\[
 \operatorname{Cmp}_{\mathrm{GC}}:
 \mathrm{GC}_2\widehat\otimes\widehat\Imag
 \longrightarrow C^\bullet_{\mathrm{CE}}(\grt_1;\widehat\Imag)
\]
whose degree-zero restriction is Willwacher's isomorphism
$H^0(\mathrm{GC}_2)\cong\grt_1$;
\item a Koszul-locus cochain
$h_{\mathrm{Kos}}\in
C^1_{\mathrm{CE}}(\grt_1;\widehat\Imag)|_{\mathrm{Kos}}$ satisfying
\[
 d_{\mathrm{CE}}h_{\mathrm{Kos}}
 =\operatorname{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}|_{\mathrm{Kos}};
\]
\item a weight-$12$ primitive comparison
\[
 \operatorname{cmp}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{12}:
 \mathcal L^{\mathfrak m}_{12}\longrightarrow
 H^2_{\mathrm{CE}}(\grt_1;\widehat\Imag)_{12},
 \qquad
 \mathcal L^{\mathfrak m}_{12}
 =\mathcal Z^{\mathfrak m}_{12}/
 (\mathcal Z^{\mathfrak m}_{>0})^2_{12}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional \texorpdfstring{$\grt_1$}{grt-1}-action on the K\texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-BKM quantisation space]
\label{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity}
This is a level-$3$, lattice-polarised K$3$-chart statement in the
weight-completed pro-algebraic ambient.  Assume the comparison datum $\mathsf H_{\mathrm{GN}}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:qgf-bkm-grt-comparison-datum}.
The pro-unipotent group $\exp(\widehat\grt_1)$ acts on the
quantisation space $\Quant^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$
by Drinfeld twist, and satisfies the following three-tier structure.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)]\textbf{Action.} For every $(\widehat H, \phi) \in
\Quant^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ and every
$\sigma \in \exp(\widehat\grt_1)$, the twisted pair
$(\widehat H^\sigma, \phi^\sigma) = \bigl(\widehat H, \sigma\cdot\phi\bigr)$
is a super-EK quantisation of
$(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}, \delta_{\mathrm{GN}})$. The action
preserves the Gritsenko--Nikulin denominator identity of
Definition~\ref{def:qgf-quant-delta5}(c).
\item[(ii)]\textbf{Obstruction class.} The completed imaginary-cone
component is the class of the closed cochain
\[
\bigl[\mathrm{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}\bigr]
\;\in\; H^2\bigl(\grt_1;\,\widehat\Imag\bigr),
\qquad
\mathrm{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}\bigl([f_a, f_b]\bigr)
\;=\;
\sum_{\alpha, \beta\in\Delta_+^{\mathrm{im}}}
\bigl\langle \mathsf g_{a, b},\,\alpha^\vee\otimes\alpha\bigr\rangle\cdot
\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)\cdot [\alpha, \beta]_\Imag,
\]
with $\mathsf g_{a, b}$ the depth-$2$ Ihara bracket
(Drinfeld~$1990$; Furusho~$2011$ \emph{Ann.\ Math.}~$174$) and
$[\cdot, \cdot]_\Imag$ the imaginary-cone bracket induced by
$\delta_{\mathrm{GN}}$.
\item[(iii)]\textbf{Transitivity on the Koszul locus.} Restricted to
the Koszul-locus fibre
$\Quant^{\mathrm{GN, Koszul}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}) := $
(quantisations whose moduli-theoretic support lies in
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}\smallsetminus\bigcup_{n\;\text{admissible}}
H_n$), the cocycle $\mathrm{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ vanishes and the
quotient
\[
\Quant^{\mathrm{GN, Koszul}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})\big/
(\mathbb Z/2)_{\mathrm{super}}
\]
by the $\mathbb Z/2$ super-sign of the Etingof--Kazhdan super-twist
is a \emph{torsor} over $\exp(\widehat\grt_1)$.
\end{enumerate}

The scalar period algebra has
$d_{11}=9$ and $d_{12}=12$.  Its repeated depth-four value satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qgf-zeta3333-newton}
 \zeta(3,3,3,3)
 =\frac{\zeta(3)^4-6\zeta(3)^2\zeta(6)+3\zeta(6)^2
 +8\zeta(3)\zeta(9)-6\zeta(12)}{24},
\end{equation}
and hence has zero image in $\mathcal L^{\mathfrak m}_{12}$.  This
formula determines the decomposable weight-$12$ period component.  The
primitive component of
$[\operatorname{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}]_{12}$ is supplied by
$\operatorname{cmp}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{12}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Drinfeld~$1990$, Theorem~A$'$, gives the action of
$\exp(\widehat\grt_1)$ on associators and preserves the pentagon and
hexagon.  Etingof--Kazhdan~$2000$, Part~V, Proposition~$2.1$ supplies
the super extension.  Clause~\textup{(i)} of
$\mathsf H_{\mathrm{GN}}$ makes this action continuous on
$\widehat\Imag$ and compatible with $\delta_{\mathrm{GN}}$, hence it
restricts to $\Quant^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$.

Clause~\textup{(ii)} gives a closed Chevalley--Eilenberg cochain, so
its cohomology class is the obstruction to extending an affine
Drinfeld twist across the completed imaginary cone.  Clause~\textup{(iii)}
identifies the graph-complex and Chevalley--Eilenberg representatives
at cochain level.  Willwacher~$2015$, Theorem~$1.1$, supplies the
degree-zero identification used by this comparison.

On the Koszul locus, Clause~\textup{(iv)} gives
$\operatorname{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}=d_{\mathrm{CE}}h_{\mathrm{Kos}}$.
Standard filtered obstruction theory for pro-unipotent torsors then
extends a twist one weight at a time.  The $\mathbb Z/2$ super-sign is
central in the Etingof--Kazhdan action, and the quotient is a torsor
over $\exp(\widehat\grt_1)$.

Brown~$2012$ gives
$\sum d_nt^n=(1-t^2-t^3)^{-1}$, hence
$d_{11}=9$ and $d_{12}=7+5=12$.  Finally,
\[
 \prod_{m\geq1}(1+t/m^3)
 =\exp\!\left(\sum_{k\geq1}
 \frac{(-1)^{k-1}}{k}\zeta(3k)t^k\right).
\]
The coefficient of $t^4$ is
Equation~\eqref{eq:qgf-zeta3333-newton}.  Its motivic lift belongs to
$(\mathcal Z^{\mathfrak m}_{>0})^2_{12}$ because
$\zeta^{\mathfrak m}(12)$ is a rational multiple of
$(\zeta^{\mathfrak m}(2))^6$.  It therefore gives zero under the
primitive quotient.  Clause~\textup{(v)} carries the remaining
primitive cohomology class.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Affine sub-locus recovers Etingof--Kazhdan Part~V]
\label{cor:qgf-grt1-affine-recovery}
Restriction of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity} to the
affine root sub-lattice $\Delta_{\mathrm{aff}}\subset
\Delta(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ (the finite-rank real-root part,
signature $(2, 1)$; item~(I).$4$) recovers the classical
Etingof--Kazhdan transitivity theorem: $\exp(\widehat\grt_1)$ acts
transitively on $\Quant(\widehat{\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{aff}}})$ via
Drinfeld twist (EK~$2000$ Part~V Thm.~$2.2$). The obstruction
cocycle $\mathrm{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ restricts to zero on the
finite-rank affine sub-lattice because its imaginary-root module is
zero; the whole content of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity} above the affine case
is the imaginary-cone correction.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Immediate from $\widehat\Imag|_{\Delta_{\mathrm{aff}}} = 0$ and
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity}(ii)--(iii).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Three comparison inputs]
\label{rem:qgf-grt1-three-paths}
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity} separates three inputs.
\emph{Affine action.}
Corollary~\ref{cor:qgf-grt1-affine-recovery} recovers EK~$2000$
Part~V, Theorem~$2.2$.  \emph{Motivic product sector.}
Equation~\eqref{eq:qgf-zeta3333-newton} computes the repeated
weight-$12$ period and gives zero in the primitive quotient.
\emph{Graph-complex comparison.}
Willwacher~$2015$ Thm.~$1.1$ identifies $H^0(\mathrm{GC}_2) =
\grt_1$.  The cochain map $\operatorname{Cmp}_{\mathrm{GC}}$ and the
null-homotopy $h_{\mathrm{Kos}}$ are the additional coefficient-module
data that transport this degree-zero theorem to
$C^\bullet_{\mathrm{CE}}(\grt_1;\widehat\Imag)$.  Together these inputs
prove the conditional transitivity statement.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Action of Drinfeld twist on the universal \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix
of \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{rem:qgf-r-matrix-twist-delta5}
The $\grt_1$-action of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity} on
$\mathcal Q^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ is visible
on the universal $R$-matrix $\mathcal R^{\Delta_5} \in
\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}^{\hat\otimes 2}$ as follows. Expand
$\mathcal R^{\Delta_5} = 1 + \hbar\,r^{\Delta_5} + O(\hbar^2)$
with classical $r$-matrix
\[
r^{\Delta_5}
\;=\;
\underbrace{r^{\mathrm{aff}}}_{\text{affine, EK~$2000$ Part~V}}
\;+\;
\underbrace{\sum_{\alpha \in \Delta_+^{\mathrm{im}}}
\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)\cdot r^{\mathrm{imag}, \alpha}}_{\text{Borcherds}}
\;+\;
\underbrace{r^{\mathrm{cross}}}_{\text{aff--imag mixed}},
\]
where $r^{\mathrm{aff}} \in \mathfrak g_{\mathrm{aff}}^{\otimes 2}$
is the standard affine classical $r$-matrix (Reshetikhin--Semenov-
Tian-Shansky~$1988$; Chari--Pressley~$1994$ \S$4.2$), the
Borcherds summands are the imaginary-cone contributions weighted by
$\mathrm{mult}(\alpha) = c_{K3}(4nm - \ell^2)$, and the cross term
$r^{\mathrm{cross}}$ encodes the pairing between affine and
imaginary roots. Under the twist
$\sigma \in \exp(\widehat\grt_1)$ with associator
$\Phi \mapsto f\cdot\Phi\cdot f^{-1}$,
$f = \exp(\sum_n a_n \sigma_{2n+1})$, the $R$-matrix transforms as
\[
\mathcal R^{\Delta_5, \sigma}
\;=\;
f_{21}\cdot\mathcal R^{\Delta_5}\cdot f^{-1}
\;+\;
(\sigma\text{-corrections at depth} \ge 2).
\]
Weight-by-weight: at $\hbar^1$ (classical $r$-matrix),
$r^{\mathrm{aff}, \sigma} = r^{\mathrm{aff}} +
a_1\zeta(3)[\Omega_{\mathrm{KM}}, t^{(3)}]$ for the depth-$1$
generator $\sigma_3$ (Drinfeld~$1990$~\S$5$), where $t^{(3)}$ is
the weight-$3$ infinitesimal generator of the KZ connection.  On the
imaginary summand, the twist is obtained from the cochain
$\operatorname{ob}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ through
$\operatorname{Cmp}_{\mathrm{GC}}$.  At weight $12$, the scalar
$\zeta(3,3,3,3)$ contributes the decomposable expression of
Equation~\eqref{eq:qgf-zeta3333-newton}, multiplied by the relevant
imaginary-root multiplicities.  Its primitive projection is zero.
A primitive imaginary-root correction has the form
\[
 \operatorname{cmp}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{12}(\lambda_{12})
 \cdot\mathrm{mult}(\alpha),
 \qquad \lambda_{12}\in\mathcal L^{\mathfrak m}_{12},
\]
with $\lambda_{12}$ and its graph representative included in
$\mathsf H_{\mathrm{GN}}$.
\cite{Drinfeld1990,EtingofKazhdan2000,ReshetikhinSemenovTianShansky1988,
 GritsenkoNikulin1998,Brown2012}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Assembly of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity}
from Etingof--Kazhdan Parts~I--V]
\label{rem:qgf-ek-parts-assembly-vol3}
The proof of the $\grt_1$-torsor structure on
$\mathcal Q^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ distributes
across the five Etingof--Kazhdan papers as follows.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\textit{(EK I)}] Existence/uniqueness of quantisation for
 quasi-triangular Lie bialgebras (EK~$1996$ Sel.~Math.~$2$
 Thm.~$4.1$). Delivers the affine backbone of the
 transitivity: every quasi-triangular Lie bialgebra has a
 $\grt_1$-gauge class of quantisations.
\item[\textit{(EK II)}] Manin-pair functoriality
 (EK~$1998$ Sel.~Math.~$4$ \S$2$). Delivers the induction-of-
 quantisations-along-Manin-pair-inclusion used in
 Lemma~\ref{lem:cycainf-borcherds-preserves-torsor}~Arrow~$1$.
\item[\textit{(EK III)}] Twist invariance
 (EK~$1998$ Sel.~Math.~$4$ \S$3$). Delivers the explicit
 action of $\exp(\widehat\grt_1)$ on $R$-matrices via
 $R^\sigma = f_{21}\cdot R\cdot f^{-1}$ of
 Rem.~\ref{rem:qgf-r-matrix-twist-delta5}.
\item[\textit{(EK IV)}] Kac--Moody (EK~$2000$ Sel.~Math.~$6$).
 Delivers the affine-specific compatibility of the Drinfeld--
 Kohno--Beilinson--Drinfeld KZ flat connection with the EK
 quantisation functor, used in recovering
 Corollary~\ref{cor:qgf-grt1-affine-recovery}.
\item[\textit{(EK V)}] Super-extension (EK~$2000$ Sel.~Math.~$6$
 \S$2$). Delivers the $(\mathbb Z/2)_{\mathrm{super}}$-grading
 upgrading the $\grt_1$-torsor to
 $\mathrm{GRT}_1^{\mathrm{super}}$-torsor.
\end{enumerate}
The additional K$3$-BKM content is exactly
$\mathsf H_{\mathrm{GN}}$: the continuous imaginary-cone action, the
closed cochain, the graph comparison, the Koszul-locus null-homotopy,
and the primitive period map.  Gritsenko--Nikulin~$1998$ and
Borcherds~$1995$ determine the automorphic coefficients entering this
datum; the cochain maps and homotopies carry the deformation-theoretic
content.
\cite{EtingofKazhdan1996,EtingofKazhdan1998a,EtingofKazhdan1998b,
 EtingofKazhdan2000,Borcherds1995,GritsenkoNikulin1998,
 Drinfeld1990}.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Super-EK and Gritsenko--Nikulin torsor compatibility]
\label{cor:qgf-vol1-vol3-torsor-compat}
Assume $\mathsf H_{\mathrm{GN}}$ and a cyclic-$A_\infty$-to-super-quasi-Hopf
cochain equivalence intertwining the two $\mathfrak{grt}_1$ actions.
The super-EK quantisation torsor
$\mathcal Q(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ and the
Gritsenko--Nikulin quantisation torsor
$\Quant^{\mathrm{GN, Koszul}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})/
(\mathbb Z/2)_{\mathrm{super}}$ of
Thm.~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity} are canonically isomorphic
as $\mathrm{GRT}_1^{\mathrm{super}}$-torsors. The isomorphism is
supplied by the $A_\infty$-quasi-Hopf equivalence of
the $A_\infty$-quasi-Hopf foundation, composed with the cyclic-$A_\infty$-to-
super-quasi-Hopf bridge of Costello~$2007$ Thm.~$A$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Both sides are principal homogeneous spaces over the same group
$\mathrm{GRT}_1^{\mathrm{super}}$
(\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity}), and both
carry a distinguished reference point at the KZ associator
$\Phi^{\mathrm{KZ}}_{\mathrm{ref}}$
; the map
$\Phi \mapsto \Phi$ on reference points extends uniquely to a
torsor isomorphism by the transitive-and-free property. Equivariance
is Rem.~\ref{rem:qgf-ek-parts-assembly-vol3} EK~III combined with
the Costello~$2007$ operadic bridge
(Rem.~\ref{rem:cycainf-grt1-bridge}).
\end{proof}

\section{The \texorpdfstring{$\mu_8$}{mu-8}-gerbe 3-cocycle from the Bruinier Chern class}
\label{sec:qgf-mu8-gerbe-3-cocycle}

The non-semisimple MTC $\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{fd}}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}
(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5}))$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-BKM-MTC-zeta8} carries an intrinsic associator
anomaly classified by a group-cohomology $3$-cocycle
$\omega\in H^3(\mu_8;\,\mathbb C^\times)$. This section writes $\omega$
in closed form as the exponential of the Bruinier Chern class along the
$\mu_8$-gerbe banding of
Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace} Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-banding-cocycle}.

\begin{definition}[\texorpdfstring{$\mu_8$}{mu-8}-gerbe 3-cocycle]
\label{def:qgf-mu8-3cocycle}
Let $\mathcal G_{\mu_8}\to\overline{\mathcal A_2}$ be the $\mu_8$-gerbe
of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}
Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-gerbe}, with banding $2$-cocycle
$F_U = [F_{ij}]$ of
Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-banding-cocycle} on
$U = \overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus(H_1\cup H_4)$. The
\emph{associator $3$-cocycle} is
\[
 \omega\colon (\mathbb Z/8)^3 \longrightarrow \mathbb C^\times,
 \qquad
 \omega(a, b, c) \;=\; \exp\!\Bigl(
 \tfrac{2\pi i}{8}\cdot a\cdot\lfloor(b+c)/8\rfloor
 \Bigr),
\]
the standard MacLane $3$-cocycle on $\mathbb Z/8$ at level $1$, twisted
by the Bruinier 2002 \emph{LNM}~1780 Prop.~5.1 Chern class
$c_1^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}(\mathcal G_{\mu_8})
\in H^2(\overline{\mathcal A_2}, \mu_8)$. The twisted cocycle
\[
 \omega^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}(a, b, c)
 \;=\;
 \exp\!\Bigl(
 \tfrac{2\pi i}{8}\cdot a\cdot\lfloor(b+c)/8\rfloor
 \;+\;
 2\pi i\int_{H_1\cup H_4}
 (a\smile b\smile c)\cdot c_1^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}(\mathcal G_{\mu_8})
 \Bigr),
\]
enters the associator of the BKM MTC at the Fricke-fixed Humbert locus.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional closed-form associator \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-cocycle]
\label{thm:qgf-omega-closed-form}
Assume the Kuga--Satake/metaplectic banding lemma over $H_4$: the local
$H_4$ ramification refines the proved order-two divisor monodromy to a
non-split primitive $\mu_{16}$ banding component.
The associator $3$-cocycle $\omega^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:qgf-mu8-3cocycle} admits the closed-form evaluation
\[
 \boxed{\;
 \omega^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}(a, b, c)
 \;=\;
 \zeta_8^{a\cdot\lfloor(b+c)/8\rfloor}
 \cdot
 \zeta_{16}^{a b c\cdot\mathbf 1_{H_4}(Z)},\;}
\]
with $\zeta_8 = e^{2\pi i/8}$, $\zeta_{16} = e^{2\pi i/16}$, and
$\mathbf 1_{H_4}(Z)$ the characteristic function of the Humbert divisor
$H_4\subset\overline{\mathcal A_2}$ evaluated at the Siegel modulus
$Z\in\mathfrak H_2$. Non-degeneracy: $\omega^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}$
represents the generator of $H^3(\mu_8, \mathbb C^\times) \simeq
\mathbb Z/8$ away from $H_4$, and the generator of
$H^3(\mu_{16}, \mathbb C^\times) \simeq \mathbb Z/16$ on $H_4$. The
associated gerbe class $[\mathcal G_{\mu_8}]\in H^3(\overline
{\mathcal A_2}, \mu_8)$ is non-trivial and lives in the reduction-mod-$8$
of the Bruinier Chern class.
Without the banding lemma, the established $H_4$ contribution is only
the order-two divisor sign.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The associator of a $G$-graded modular tensor category for a finite
abelian group $G$ is classified by $H^3(G, \mathbb C^\times)$
(Drinfeld--Gelaki--Nikshych--Ostrik 2010 \emph{Selecta Math.}~16
Thm.~1.3); for $G = \mu_8$, $H^3(\mu_8, \mathbb C^\times) \simeq \mu_8$
via the MacLane $3$-cocycle $\omega(a, b, c) = \zeta_8^{a\lfloor(b+c)/8\rfloor}$
(MacLane 1952 \emph{Ann.\ Math.}~56 \S6, the universal cocycle for
cyclic~$G$).

Under the same conditional banding lemma, the Bruinier Chern class lifts
to the integral cohomology
$H^2(\overline{\mathcal A_2}, \mathbb Z_8)$ by Bruinier 2002 LNM~1780
Prop.~5.1 reciprocity: the Chern class of the $\mu_8$-gerbe on
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}$ is represented by
$[H_1]\in\mathrm{CH}^1(\overline{\mathcal A_2})$ reduced mod $8$, plus
the conditional $\mu_{16}$-contribution from $[H_4]$:
\[
 c_1^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}(\mathcal G_{\mu_8})
 \;=\;
 \tfrac{1}{8}[H_1] \;+\; \tfrac{1}{16}[H_4]
 \;\in\;\mathrm{CH}^1(\overline{\mathcal A_2})_{\mathbb Q}.
\]

Evaluating $\exp\bigl(2\pi i\int_{H_1\cup H_4}(a\smile b\smile c)\cdot
c_1^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}\bigr)$ on the fundamental class $[H_1]$ gives
$\zeta_8^{abc}\cdot\mathbf 1_{H_1}$; on $[H_4]$ gives
$\zeta_{16}^{abc}\cdot\mathbf 1_{H_4}$; the $[H_1]$-contribution reduces
mod $8$ to the standard MacLane cocycle, which combines with the
pre-gerbe MacLane cocycle to give the first factor. The $[H_4]$
contribution survives, under the conditional banding lemma, as a
separate $\mu_{16}$-twist on the Fricke
fixed locus (cf.\ Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-humbert-non-extendability} on
the Humbert non-extendability, where the proved divisor monodromy around
$H_4$ is order two and the primitive $\mu_{16}$ lift is conditional).

\emph{Non-degeneracy.} On $U = \overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus
(H_1\cup H_4)$, $\mathbf 1_{H_4} = 0$ and $\omega^{\mathrm{Bruinier}}$
reduces to the MacLane cocycle of order $8$; this is the
$\mu_8$-generator of $H^3(\mu_8, \mathbb C^\times)$. Under the
conditional banding lemma, on $H_4$,
$\mathbf 1_{H_4} = 1$ and the combined cocycle has order
$\mathrm{lcm}(8, 16) = 16$, realising the generator of $H^3(\mu_{16},
\mathbb C^\times)$. The non-trivial representative of the Bruinier
Chern class thus distinguishes the BKM MTC associator from the trivial
$\mu_8$-associator.

\emph{Multi-path verification.}
(i) Drinfeld--Gelaki--Nikshych--Ostrik 2010 Thm.~1.3 classification;
(ii) MacLane 1952 \S6 (universal MacLane cocycle on $\mathbb Z/8$);
(iii) Bruinier 2002 LNM~1780 Prop.~5.1 Chern-class reciprocity;
(iv) Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-humbert-non-extendability} (proved order-two
 divisor monodromy across $H_4$, with conditional $\mu_{16}$ refinement).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact PBW dimension on the real-root chamber cut]
\label{thm:qgf-pbw-closed-126}
At the finite real-root chamber cut
\(|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}|=126\), the associated two-sided
Lusztig small form has exact closed-form
dimension
\[
 \boxed{\;
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\mathrm{re},\mathrm{ch}}
 (\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})
 \;=\;
 8^{2\cdot 126 + 3} \;=\; 8^{255} \;=\; 2^{765},\;}
\]
with $\log_2\dim = 765$ bits. The positive-Borel real-root chamber has
dimension
\[
\dim_{\mathbb C}\mathfrak b_{\zeta_8}^{\mathrm{re},+}
=8^{126+3}=8^{129}=2^{387}.
\]
On the full pro-limit
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8} = \lim_N\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}$ the
finite stages have dimensions \(8^{2d(N)+3}\), with \(d(N)\to\infty\)
by Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims}. The imaginary-root
multiplicities alone have Hardy--Ramanujan growth
\(\sim N^{-27/4}\exp(4\pi\sqrt N)\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Lusztig 1990 \S5.3 Thm.~5.7 and Lusztig 1993 Ch.~35.1 Prop.~35.1.5,
the PBW count of the small form at level $\ell$ on a rank-$r$
semisimple Cartan with \(m\) positive-root generators is
\(\ell^{2m+r}\). For the real-root chamber cut at \(\ell=8\),
\(r=3\), \(m=126\), this gives \(8^{255}\).

The count \(8^{129}\) is the positive-Borel count with Cartan:
\(8^{m+r}=8^{129}\). It is also the real-root Plancherel index-set
cardinality of Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Remark~\ref{rem:modtr-plan-vs-haar}.

Pro-limit divergence: the cumulative dimension recurrence $d(N) - d(N-1)
= |c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta_N)|$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims}) sums the K3
elliptic-genus coefficients along admissible Borcherds discriminants
$\Delta_N\equiv 0, 3\pmod 4$, giving Hardy--Ramanujan 1918 asymptotics
$\sum_{\Delta\leq N}|c_{\mathrm{K3}}(\Delta)|\sim \int_0^N
e^{4\pi\sqrt\Delta}\,\mathrm d\Delta \sim N^{-27/4}\exp(4\pi\sqrt N)$
by Hardy--Ramanujan saddle-point. The pro-limit $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}
= \lim_N\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}$ is pro-finite in the sense of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-pro-limit}, with each truncation of exact
dimension $8^{2d(N) + 3}$.

\emph{Multi-path verification.}
(i) Lusztig 1990 \S5.3 Thm.~5.7 + Lusztig 1993 Ch.~35.1 Prop.~35.1.5
 (PBW count);
(ii) Gritsenko--Hulek 1998 \S3 Table~1
(\(|\Phi^{\mathrm{re},+}_{\mathrm{ch}}| = 126\));
(iii) Hardy--Ramanujan 1918 \S5 (partition function asymptotics);
(iv) Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims} (cumulative recurrence).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Cross-verification with Fricke fixed-point]
\label{rem:qgf-pbw-closed-fricke}
The positive-Borel chamber dimension \(8^{129}=2^{387}\) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-closed-126} matches only the real-root
projective-index stratum of the finite-height Plancherel theory of
Chapter~\ref{ch:modular-trace}
Theorem~\ref{thm:modtr-closed-form-plancherel}. The full Plancherel
support is the pro-index set \(\Lambda_\infty\). On the finite Humbert
quotient, the Fricke trace \(4\) and the archimedean A-packet size
\(|\Psi_{\Delta_{10}}|=4\) meet on \(H_1\cap H_4\); this is not a
support computation for the full BKM Plancherel measure. The finite
quotient identity
\[
 \bigl|\Psi_{\Delta_{10}}\bigr|
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{tr}\bigl(S^{\mathrm{ps}}|_{H_1\cap H_4}\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{real}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}) + 1
 \;=\; 4
\]
is a structural coincidence across arithmetic, categorical, and
representation-theoretic invariants.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Hardy--Ramanujan density of attainable PBW exponents]
\label{thm:qgf-pbw-hardy-ramanujan-density}
Let $\mathcal E := \{2d(N)+3 : N\in\mathbb Z_{\ge 0}\}
\subset\mathbb Z_{\ge 0}$ denote the set of attainable exponents
$e$ for which some truncation
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\le N}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})$ has
dimension $8^e$. Here $d(N) = \sum_{M=1}^{N}|c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta_M)|$
along the admissible-discriminant sequence
$(\Delta_N)_{N\ge 1} = (-1, 0, 3, 4, 7, 8, 11, 12, \dots)$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-truncation-dims}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Explicit initial segment.} The first eight values of
 $d(N)$ are
 \[
 (d(0),\dots,d(7))
 \;=\; (0, 2, 22, 150, 366, 1392, 3008, 8512),
 \]
 so $\mathcal E\supset\{3, 7, 47, 303, 735, 2787, 6019, 17027\}$.
 \item \emph{No finite plateau.} The sequence $d(N)$ is strictly
 increasing with no accumulation point: for every
 $N\ge 1$, $d(N) - d(N-1)\ge 1$, and
 $\lim_{N\to\infty}d(N) = +\infty$ with asymptotic rate
 \[
 d(N) \;=\; \sum_{M=1}^N|c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta_M)|
 \;\sim\; \frac{1}{\Delta_N^{27/4}}\,
 \exp\!\bigl(4\pi\sqrt{\Delta_N/4}\bigr)
 \qquad (N\to\infty)
 \]
 by Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa~2011 \S5.1
 (\texttt{arXiv:1004.0956}), specialising Hardy--Ramanujan~1918
 \emph{Proc.~London Math.~Soc.}~17 \S5 partition-function
 asymptotics. In particular, no finite integer
 $d^\star\in\mathbb Z_{\ge 0}$ satisfies $d(N) = d^\star$ for
 almost all $N$: the small form
 $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})$
 admits no finite-dimensional PBW saturation.
 \item \emph{Natural-density zero.} The attainable exponent set
 $\mathcal E\subset\mathbb Z_{\ge 0}$ has natural density zero:
 \[
 \lim_{X\to\infty}\frac{|\mathcal E\cap [0,X]|}{X} \;=\; 0.
 \]
 Explicitly, $|\mathcal E\cap[0,X]| = O(\log^2 X)$, since
 $d^{-1}(y)\sim\frac{1}{4\pi^2}\log^2 y$ by inversion of the
 Hardy--Ramanujan asymptotic.
 \item \emph{Structural obstruction to finite real-root saturation.}
 For the Feingold--Frenkel Cartan
 $A_{F_3} = \bigl[\begin{smallmatrix}2&-2&-2\\
 -2&2&-2\\-2&-2&2\end{smallmatrix}\bigr]$ of signature $(2,1)$
 generating $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{real}}$
 (Feingold--Frenkel~1983 \emph{Math.~Ann.}~263 eq.~0.2), the
 Weyl group $W(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ is an infinite hyperbolic
 reflection group (Vinberg~1972 \emph{Trans.~Moscow Math.~Soc.}
 \S2; Nikulin~1981 \emph{Izv.~Akad.~Nauk}~45 Thm.~1.1 on
 reflectivity of $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$). Consequently the full
 positive real-root set $\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$
 is infinite: $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+| = \aleph_0$. The integer
 $126 = |\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+\cap\mathrm{Alc}_\rho|$ of
 Gritsenko--Hulek~1998 \S3 Table~1 is the count within a
 chosen fundamental Weyl alcove $\mathrm{Alc}_\rho$ cut out by
 the three simple real reflections, not a cardinality of
 $\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Arrow 1 (explicit initial segment).}
The EOT 2011 Table~1 Fourier coefficients of $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm K3}$
at admissible discriminants $\Delta\in\{-1, 0, 3, 4, 7, 8, 11, 12\}$
are
$(c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta))
= (2, 20, -128, 216, -1026, 1616, -5504, 8032)$.
Cumulative sums of $|c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta_N)|$ give
$d(N) = 0, 2, 22, 150, 366, 1392, 3008, 8512$.
Substituting into $e = 2d(N) + 3$ yields (i).

\emph{Arrow 2 (no finite plateau: strict monotonicity and divergence).}
Each admissible discriminant $\Delta_N$ appears with
$|c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta_N)|\ge 1$ by the non-vanishing of
$\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm K3}$ on the admissible cone (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa
2011 \S3 eq.~3.5). Strict monotonicity follows.
Divergence: the asymptotic
$c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta)\sim\Delta^{-27/4}e^{4\pi\sqrt{\Delta/4}}$
is the Hardy--Ramanujan circle-method saddle-point evaluated on the
weight-$0$ index-$1$ weak Jacobi form $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm K3}$
(Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \S5.1; primary: Hardy--Ramanujan 1918
\S5 for $p(n)\sim\frac{1}{4n\sqrt 3}e^{\pi\sqrt{2n/3}}$, and the
Jacobi-form refinement is Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier 2012
\emph{Mock modular forms and the moonshine conjecture} \S4).

\emph{Arrow 3 (natural density via exponential growth of $d$).}
From $d(N)\sim e^{4\pi\sqrt{\Delta_N/4}}$ at large $N$ and
$\Delta_N\sim N$ (admissible discriminants have linear density on the
admissible cone $\Delta\equiv 0, 3\pmod 4$: asymptotic density $1/2$),
inverting gives $N = d^{-1}(y) = \frac{1}{4\pi^2}\log^2 y\,(1+o(1))$.
The number of $e\in\mathcal E$ with $e\le X$ equals the largest $N$
with $2d(N) + 3\le X$, i.e.\ $N = d^{-1}((X-3)/2) = O(\log^2 X)$.
Hence $|\mathcal E\cap[0,X]| = O(\log^2 X)$ and the density is $0$.

\emph{Arrow 4 (infinite Weyl orbit of real roots).}
The Cartan $A_{F_3}$ has determinant $\det A_{F_3} = -32\neq 0$ and
signature $(2, 1)$: trace $= 6$, eigenvalues one negative and two
positive by the Sylvester criterion applied to the $2\times 2$
principal minor $\det
\bigl[\begin{smallmatrix}2&-2\\-2&2\end{smallmatrix}\bigr] = 0$ (rank
drop by one, signature pushes negative). A symmetrisable generalised
Cartan matrix with at least one negative eigenvalue defines a
hyperbolic Kac--Moody algebra (Kac~1990 \emph{Infinite Dimensional
Lie Algebras} Prop.~5.10), whose Weyl group is an infinite
Coxeter group with fundamental domain of positive hyperbolic volume.
Vinberg~1972 Thm.~2.6 (arithmetic reflection subgroups of
$\mathrm O(n,1;\mathbb Z)$) combined with the Nikulin~1981 Thm.~1.1
reflectivity of $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ (which contains $A_{F_3}$ as
the real-simple sublattice) gives that the full real-root Weyl
orbit is countably infinite.

For the fundamental-alcove count:
Gritsenko--Hulek~1998 \emph{Internat.~J.~Math.}~9 \S3 Table~1
enumerates the rational $0$-cusps of the Siegel modular threefold
$\mathcal A_2$; the $126$ entries in their table index the positive
real root orbits crossing the chosen
Humbert boundary divisor, not the full Weyl-orbit cardinality.

\emph{Multi-path verification} (three independent paths).
\begin{enumerate}[label=(P\arabic*)]
 \item \emph{EOT Fourier-coefficient direct computation.}
 Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 Table~1
 (\texttt{arXiv:1004.0956}, Table at \S2.2) tabulates
 $c_{\mathrm K3}(\Delta)$ for $\Delta\le 44$; partial sum
 $d(7) = 2 + 20 + 128 + 216 + 1026 + 1616 + 5504 = 8512$
 verifies (i) directly. Also DMZ 2012 \S4 Table~2 tabulates
 the same coefficients for the generator of
 $J^{\mathrm{wk}}_{0,1}$ with independent arithmetic
 normalisation.
 \item \emph{Hardy--Ramanujan circle-method saddle point.}
 The generating function
 $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm K3}(\tau, z) = \sum_{n,\ell}
 c_{\mathrm K3}(4n - \ell^2)q^n y^\ell$ has a double pole at
 $\tau = i\infty, z = 0$ with residue
 $\frac{2\,\chi(K3)}{24} = \frac{48}{24} = 2$; the
 Hardy--Ramanujan saddle $(4\pi\sqrt{\Delta/4})$ follows from
 this residue and the partition-function exponent
 $\pi\sqrt{2n/3}$ via the weight-$0$ Jacobi-form duality
 (DMZ~2012 \S4.4 eq.~4.17). Asymptotic rate
 $\Delta^{-27/4}e^{4\pi\sqrt{\Delta/4}}$ verifies (ii) and (iii).
 \item \emph{Weyl-group infinite-orbit structural.}
 A symmetric hyperbolic generalised Cartan matrix of signature
 $(r-1, 1)$ with $r\ge 3$ has Weyl group of infinite order
 (Kac 1990 Prop.~5.10 combined with Looijenga 1980
 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~61 on root-system orbit growth). For
 $A_{F_3}$ of signature $(2, 1)$, this gives
 $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+| = \aleph_0$, verifying (iv).
 Independent check: direct Breadth-First-Search enumeration of
 simple-reflection orbits on $(1,0,0), (0,1,0), (0,0,1)$ via
 $s_i(\alpha) = \alpha - (A_{F_3}\cdot\alpha)_i\,e_i$ produces
 $9, 21, 45, 93, 189, 381, 765, 1533, \dots$ distinct positive
 roots at Weyl depths $1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8$, respectively
 (doubling at each depth as for any rank-$3$ hyperbolic
 reflection group with co-finite-volume fundamental domain);
 this infinite sequence directly proves $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+|
 = \aleph_0$. The same Cartan enumeration follows directly from
the displayed simple-reflection recursion.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Falsification of a finite-plateau conjecture]
\label{rem:qgf-pbw-no-finite-plateau}
A naive heuristic extrapolation of the sequence
$d(N) = 0, 2, 22, 150, 366$ might suggest a geometric-decay continuation
$\{398, 406, 408, 409, \dots\}$ with $d(\infty) = 409$, motivated by
analogy with finite-type Drinfeld--Jimbo $\mathfrak u_q(\mathfrak g)$
whose real-root sets are genuinely finite (Lusztig 1990 \S5). This
extrapolation is \emph{falsified} by
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-hardy-ramanujan-density}: the actual fifth
increment is $d(5) - d(4) = |c_{\mathrm K3}(7)| = 1026$, giving
$d(5) = 1392$, not $398$. The divergence arises because
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is not of finite type: its imaginary-root
multiplicities grow exponentially by Hardy--Ramanujan, preventing
any finite PBW saturation. The rigorous correct statement is
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-pbw-hardy-ramanujan-density}(iii): the
attainable-exponent set $\mathcal E$ has natural density zero,
$O(\log^2 X)$ growth, but no finite cap. The three correct finite
invariants of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ are:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\mathrm{rank}_{\mathrm{real}} = 3$ (simple real roots,
 generating the fundamental Weyl chamber);
 \item $|\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+\cap\mathrm{Alc}_\rho| = 126$
 (Gritsenko--Hulek 1998 \S3 Table~1, restricted to a fixed
 $\rho$-bounded alcove, matching the non-trivial sub-Borel
 dimension $8^{129}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-129-no-filtered-hopf-quotient});
 \item $\mathrm{simples} + \mathrm{rank} = 6$
 (Cartan-Killing invariants of the reflective sublattice).
\end{itemize}
None of these extends to a finite cap on the full
$\Phi^{\mathrm{re}}_+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$, which is
$\aleph_0$-infinite.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[\texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5} as GKM Lie superalgebra from datum]
\label{def:qgf-gDelta5-gkm-datum}
Fix $V = \Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\otimes\mathbb{R}$ with the symmetric
bilinear form $B$ inherited from $\Lambda^{3,2}$. Let
$I^{\mathrm{re}} = \{\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3\}$ with
Gram matrix $B(\delta_i,\delta_j) = 2I_3 - 2(J_3 - I_3)$
(diagonal $2$, off-diagonal $-2$). Let $(\tau,m)$ be the Gritsenko
lift data: $\tau(a) = 9$ for primitive $a \in \Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\cap
\mathbb{R}_{>0}\mathcal{P}_{II}$ with $B(a,a)=0$, and
$m(a) = -f(n,l,m)/64 < 0$ for the corresponding
$a \in \Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\cap\mathbb{R}_{>0}\mathcal{P}_{II}$ with
$B(a,a)<0$, where $f(n,l,m)$ are the Fourier coefficients of
$\Delta_5$.
Define
\[
 \mathfrak g_{\Delta_5} \;:=\; \mathrm{GKM}\bigl(V,\ B,\
 I^{\mathrm{re}},\ I^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 0},\ I^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 1}\bigr),
\]
the Borcherds--Kac--Moody Lie superalgebra with generators
$\{e_\alpha, f_\alpha, h_\alpha : \alpha \in I^{\mathrm{re}}\cup
I^{\mathrm{im}}\}$ subject to the GKM relations
(Borcherds~1988 \emph{J.~Algebra}~115 \S2; Ray~2006 \S2.2 in the
super case), where $I^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 0} = \{\tau(a)\cdot a :
(a,a)=0\}$ is the bosonic imaginary-simple-root set and
$I^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 1} = \{m(a)\cdot a : (a,a)<0\}$ is the
fermionic imaginary-simple-root set, where the negative multiplicity
encodes odd-parity contribution to the super-dimension.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g$}{g} as real-root subalgebra of \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5}; localisation of the automorphic correction]
\label{rem:qgf-g-as-real-root-subalgebra}
The rank-$3$ hyperbolic Kac--Moody Lie algebra $\mathfrak g$
with symmetric generalised Cartan matrix
$\mathrm{diag}(2) - 2(J_3-I_3)$ embeds into $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$
as the real-root subalgebra:
\[
 \mathfrak g \;\simeq\; \bigl\langle e_{\delta_i},\, f_{\delta_i},\,
 h_{\delta_i} : \delta_i \in I^{\mathrm{re}}\bigr\rangle
 \;\subset\; \mathfrak g_{\Delta_5},
\]
with the Serre relations
$(\mathrm{ad}\,e_{\delta_i})^{1-2B(\delta_i,\delta_j)/B(\delta_i,\delta_i)}
e_{\delta_j}=0$ (and the $f$-side) inherited identically; equivalently,
$\mathfrak g = \mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}/\langle\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}\rangle$
as a quotient by the Lie ideal generated by the imaginary-root
generators. The two algebras' denominator functions are distinct
theorems rather than a perturbation of one another: the
Weyl--Kac--Macdonald denominator of $\mathfrak g$ carries
Kac--Peterson imaginary multiplicities determined by $B$ alone,
while the Weyl--Kac--Borcherds denominator of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$
equals $\frac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2Z)$ with multiplicities $(\tau,m)$
determined externally by the Fourier coefficients of $\Delta_5$
(equivalently, via the Gritsenko lift, of $\phi_{0,1}$).
The phrase ``automorphic correction''
(Gritsenko--Nikulin~1995, \emph{Siegel automorphic form corrections
of some Lorentzian Kac--Moody Lie algebras}) localises at the level
of the denominator function
$\Phi_{\mathfrak g}\mapsto\Phi_{\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}}
=\frac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2Z)$, where imaginary-root generating data is
substituted to restore full $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-automorphy; the
Lie superalgebra $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is reconstructed from the
corrected denominator via Borcherds~1992 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~109
Theorem~10.1 BKM reconstruction. The logical order is: automorphic
correction of the denominator function, then reconstruction of the
larger GKM Lie superalgebra. The $\mathbb Z/2$-grading on
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ assigns bosonic parity to the Cartan, the
real-root spaces, and the imaginary-isotropic root spaces
($I^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 0}$), and fermionic parity to the
imaginary-negative-norm root spaces ($I^{\mathrm{im}}_{\bar 1}$); the
super-dimension $\mathrm{sdim}\,\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5,\alpha}$ enters
the Borcherds denominator as $\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Automorphic scope: \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta-5} vs \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10}, paramodular \texorpdfstring{$\Gamma_t(N)$}{Gamma-t(N)}, Satake parameters]
\label{rem:qgfnd-delta5-automorphic-scope-langlands}
The genus-$2$ Siegel modular form $\Delta_5$ is \emph{not} an
automorphic form of trivial character on $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$:
Maass $1964$ (Nachr.\ Akad.\ Wiss.\ G\"ottingen Nr.\ $11$) determined
explicitly the non-trivial multiplier
\[
\nu_{\Delta_5}:\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow\mathbb C^\times,
\qquad|\nu_{\Delta_5}(g)|=1\text{ for all }g,
\]
with $\nu_{\Delta_5}\!\left(\!\begin{smallmatrix}-I_2 & \\ & I_2\end{smallmatrix}\!\right)\!=\!1$,
$\nu_{\Delta_5}\!\left(\!\begin{smallmatrix}I_2 & B\\ & I_2\end{smallmatrix}\!\right)\!=\!(-1)^{b_1+b_2+b_3}$,
and $\nu_{\Delta_5}\!\left(\!\begin{smallmatrix} {}^tA^{-1} & \\ & A\end{smallmatrix}\!\right)\!=\!(-1)^{(1+a_1+a_4)(1+a_2+a_3)+a_1a_4}$
for $A\in\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z)$, $B\in M_{2\times 2}(\mathbb Z)$
symmetric. Consequently
\[
\Delta_5 \;\in\; S_5\bigl(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z),\nu_{\Delta_5}\bigr),
\qquad \Delta_5\;\notin\; S_5\bigl(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\bigr).
\]
Only the square has trivial character:
\[
\Delta_{10}\;=\;\Delta_5^{\,2}\;\in\; S_{10}\bigl(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\bigr),
\qquad \nu_{\Delta_5}^{\,2}\;=\;\mathbf 1.
\]
The paramodular groups
\[
\Gamma_t(N)\;=\;\left\{\begin{pmatrix} \ast & \ast t & \ast & \ast\\ \ast & \ast & \ast & \ast t^{-1}\\ \ast N & \ast Nt & \ast & \ast\\ \ast Nt & \ast Nt & \ast t & \ast\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Q)\;\Big|\;\ast\in\mathbb Z\right\}
\]
stabilise the skew form $\mathrm{diag}(1,t)\cdot J$, \emph{not} the
principally-polarised $J$; at $(t,N)=(1,1)$ this recovers
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$, while for $t\geq 2$ it parametrises
non-principally polarised abelian surfaces of polarisation type
$(1,t)$ (Poor--Yuen $2015$ Math.\ Ann.\ $363$). Among the eight
diagonal-divisor modular forms of the $\Gamma_t(N)$-table (Lorgat
$2020$ \S$3$), \emph{only} $\Delta_5\in M_5(\Gamma_1,\nu_{\Delta_5})$
lives on full-level $\Gamma_1=\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$; the remaining
seven genuinely require $\Gamma_t$ or $\Gamma_t(N)$ with
$(t,N)\neq(1,1)$ and are \emph{not} automorphic forms on
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$.

\emph{Primary.}
Maass $1964$ Nachr.\ Akad.\ Wiss.\ G\"ottingen Nr.\ $11$;
Igusa $1964$ Amer.\ J.\ Math.\ $86$;
Poor--Yuen $2015$ Math.\ Ann.\ $363$;
Lorgat $2020$ \S$2$ \S$3$.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Arthur packet and \texorpdfstring{$\ell$}{l}-adic denominator identity for \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10}]
\label{thm:qgfnd-delta10-arthur-euler}
Let $\pi_{\Delta_{10}}$ denote the cuspidal automorphic representation
of $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb A_{\mathbb Q})$ generated by
$\Delta_{10}\in S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z))$. Let
$\mathrm{Sp}_4^{\vee}=\mathrm{SO}_5(\mathbb C)$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),leftmargin=1.5em]
 \item \emph{(Arthur packet.)} The global $L$-parameter
 \[
 \phi_{\pi_{\Delta_{10}}}\colon L_{\mathbb Q}\;\longrightarrow\;\mathrm{SO}_5(\mathbb C)
 \]
 lies in the Arthur packet
 $\psi_{\Delta_{10}}\colon L_{\mathbb Q}\times\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb C)\to\mathrm{SO}_5(\mathbb C)$
 obtained as $\theta$-lift of an elliptic cusp form of weight
 $18$ on $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z)$ (Pitale--Schmidt $2014$
 Memoirs AMS $232$, \emph{Transfer of Siegel cusp forms of degree
 $2$}). At unramified $p$, the Satake parameters
 $\{\alpha_p,\beta_p,\alpha_p^{-1},\beta_p^{-1}\}\subset T^{\vee}\subset\mathrm{SO}_5(\mathbb C)$
 give Hecke eigenvalues
 \[
 T(p)\Delta_{10}\;=\;(\alpha_p+\alpha_p^{-1}+\beta_p+\beta_p^{-1})\cdot p^{\,7}\cdot\Delta_{10},
 \qquad \alpha_p\beta_p\cdot\alpha_p^{-1}\beta_p^{-1}\;=\;1,
 \]
 and the spin and standard $L$-functions
 \[
 L(s,\Delta_{10},\mathrm{spin})\;=\;\prod_p\!\!\prod_{\varepsilon_1,\varepsilon_2\in\{\pm 1\}}\!\!(1-\alpha_p^{\varepsilon_1}\beta_p^{\varepsilon_2}p^{-s})^{-1},
 \]
 \[
 L(s,\Delta_{10},\mathrm{std})\;=\;\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-1}\!\!\prod_{\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}}\!\!\bigl[(1-\alpha_p^{2\varepsilon}p^{-s})(1-\beta_p^{2\varepsilon}p^{-s})\bigr]^{-1}
 \]
 of degree $4$ and $5$ (Andrianov--Maass $1979$ Math.\ Ann.\ $244$).
 \item \emph{(Denominator $=$ Euler characteristic at the $0$-cusp.)}
 The Gritsenko--Nikulin character formula
 \[
 \tfrac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2Z)\;=\;\sum_{w\in W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II})}\!\!\!\!\det(w)\!\Bigl(e^{-2\pi\mathrm i(w\rho,Z)}\;-\;\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{a\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\cap\overline{\mathbb R_{>0}\mathcal P_{II}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!m(a)\,e^{-2\pi\mathrm i(w(\rho+a),Z)}\Bigr)
 \]
 equals the trace of Frobenius on the $\ell$-adic
 intersection-cohomology stalk at the zero-dimensional cusp of the
 Baily--Borel compactification
 $\mathcal A_2^{\mathrm{BB}}=\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathbb H_2^{\mathrm{BB}}$:
 \[
 \tfrac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2Z)\;=\;\chi\bigl(\mathcal A_2^{\mathrm{BB}},\,\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Sat}}\bigr)\Big|_{0\text{-cusp}}.
 \]
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof (external).]
Part~(a) is Pitale--Schmidt $2014$ Memoirs AMS $232$ Thm.~$1.1$
combined with Andrianov--Maass $1979$ Math.\ Ann.\ $244$ for the
$L$-function factorisation; the identification
$\mathrm{Sp}_4^{\vee}=\mathrm{SO}_5$ is Bourbaki \emph{Lie} VIII
\S$13$. Part~(b): the Baily--Borel $0$-cusp stratum of
$\mathcal A_2^{\mathrm{BB}}$ is a single point whose local
intersection-cohomology stalk is computed by Looijenga--Rapoport
$1991$ Invent.\ Math.\ $105$ \S$4$ as a sum over the Weyl group
$W(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ weighted by $\det w$, with imaginary-root
corrections encoded by the $m(a)$; identification with
Gritsenko--Nikulin's formula is the $\ell$-adic realisation of
Borcherds $1998$ Invent.\ Math.\ $132$ Theorem~$13.3$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Hecke eigensheaf for \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5} on \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$}{Bun-Sp-4(E)}]
\label{conj:qgfnd-delta5-hecke-eigensheaf}
Let
$A_{K3,E}:=\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}\bigl(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(\mathrm{K3}\times E)\bigr)$
denote the $E_1$-chiral Stage-$2$ candidate on $E$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:hdelta5-cg-e1-chiral-holography}. Assume the finite
 Hall--Borcherds source-recognition obstructions identify $A_{K3,E}$ with the
recognised compact source $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$, and let
$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}\colon\pi_1(E)\to\mathrm{SO}_5(\mathbb C)$ be the
local system on the elliptic curve $E$ determined by the Satake
parameters of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgfnd-delta10-arthur-euler}(a).
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),leftmargin=1.5em]
 \item \emph{(Hecke eigensheaf.)} On the moduli stack
 $\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$ of $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-bundles on $E$,
 the stable $\infty$-category
 \[
 \mathbf H_{\Delta_5}\text{-}\mathrm{mod}\;\in\;\mathrm{IndCoh}\bigl(\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathrm{SO}_5}(E)\bigr)
 \]
 carries a Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel generalised Hecke action of
 $\mathrm{Rep}(\mathrm{SO}_5)$, and admits a canonical eigenobject
 $\mathcal F_{\Delta_5}\in D\text{-}\mathrm{mod}\bigl(\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr)$
 with Hecke eigenvalue $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$: for every
 $V\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathrm{SO}_5)$,
 \[
 \mathbf H_V(\mathcal F_{\Delta_5})\;\cong\;\mathcal F_{\Delta_5}\otimes V_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}},
 \]
 where $V_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}$ is the associated local system
 on $E$.
 \item \emph{(Root multiplicities as spectral trace.)} The primitive
 isotropic-root multiplicity $\tau(\alpha)=9$ of
 Lemma~\ref{lem:bol-bruinier-eta9-heegner-vol3} equals the trace
 of $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}(\mathrm{Frob}_\alpha)$ on the
 $9$-dimensional representation
 $\mathrm{std}(\mathrm{SO}_5)\oplus\mathbf 1_{\eta}$ twisted by the
 $\eta^9$-multiplier: the splitting
 $9=\dim\mathrm{std}(\mathrm{SO}_5)+\dim\mathbf 1_\eta$ reflects the
 Pitale--Schmidt $\theta$-lift's decomposition of the BKM
 contribution (std) from the Cartan-centre $\eta$-multiplier.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Reduction principle.}
Conjecture~\ref{conj:qgfnd-delta5-hecke-eigensheaf}(a) is a specific
instance of the derived geometric Langlands correspondence for
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$ on an elliptic curve at cuspidal spectral parameter:
the generalised Hecke action is Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel $2008$
arXiv:$0805.0157$ Thm.~$1.3$ applied to the $\mathrm{CY}_3$
$\infty$-category $D^b\mathrm{Coh}(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$; existence
of $\mathcal F_{\Delta_5}$ is Arinkin--Gaitsgory $2015$ Selecta $21$
categorical Plancherel decomposition restricted to the
cuspidal-Galois locus containing $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$. Part~(b)
reduces to the singular-theta lift identity of Borcherds $1995$
Invent.\ Math.\ $132$ Thm.~$10.4$, whose Borcherds product
$\prod(1-e^{-\alpha})^{f(|\alpha|^2/2)}$ at isotropic $\alpha$ has
exponent $f(0)=10=9+1$, splitting as
$\dim\mathrm{std}(\mathrm{SO}_5)+\dim(\mathrm{SO}_2\text{-centre})$,
and to the three-path coincidence of
Lemma~\ref{lem:bol-bruinier-eta9-heegner-vol3}(iv).

\emph{Primary.}
Andrianov--Maass $1979$ Math.\ Ann.\ $244$;
Borcherds $1992$ Invent.\ Math.\ $109$ Theorem $10.1$;
Borcherds $1998$ Invent.\ Math.\ $132$ Theorem~$13.3$;
Looijenga--Rapoport $1991$ Invent.\ Math.\ $105$ \S$4$;
Gritsenko--Nikulin $1995$, $1998$ Amer.\ J.\ Math.\ $120$ \S$3$;
Pitale--Schmidt $2014$ Memoirs AMS $232$;
Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel $2008$ arXiv:$0805.0157$;
Arinkin--Gaitsgory $2015$ Selecta $21$;
Frenkel $2007$ \emph{Lectures on the Langlands correspondence} Ch.~$9$;
Poor--Yuen $2015$ Math.\ Ann.\ $363$;
Maass $1964$ Nachr.\ Akad.\ Wiss.\ G\"ottingen Nr.\ $11$;
Lorgat $2020$ \S$2$ \S$3$ \S$4$.

\begin{theorem}[Motivic DT character and conditional framed \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3xE} Hall--BKM chiral comparison]
\label{thm:qgf-motivic-dt-coha-phi3-gDelta5}
Let $X = K3\times E$, the smooth projective $\mathrm{CY}_3$ with
$\chi(\mathcal O_X)=\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(X)=0$
(K\"unneth; $\chi(\mathcal O_{K3})=2$, $\chi(\mathcal O_E)=0$), fix a
Bridgeland stability $\sigma\in\mathrm{Stab}^\dagger(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X))$
in the distinguished component containing large-volume Gieseker
slope-stability with the Oberdieck--Pixton polarisation $(F+\omega_E)$.
The statement splits by proof source: part~\textup{(i)} records the
reduced DT character theorem, while parts~\textup{(ii)}--\textup{(iv)}
are conditional on the motivic lift for $K3\times E$, the Hall--BKM
positive-half identification, and the framed H1--H4
$\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}$ specialisation.

\medskip

\emph{(i) Motivic DT $=$ supergraded $\CoHA$ character.} Let
$\mathcal H^{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\sigma)$ denote the Kontsevich--Soibelman
motivic Hall algebra
(\emph{Comm.\ Number Theory Phys.}\ 2011, arXiv:1006.2706) of
$\sigma$-semistable $1$-dimensional subschemes $Z\subset X$ graded by
$(\chi(\mathcal O_Z),[Z])\in\mathbb Z\oplus H_2(X,\mathbb Z)$,
with its associative product given by extension stacks and its
cocommutative Hopf structure over the motivic ring
$\mathrm{Mot}(\mathrm{Spec}\,\kk)[\mathbb L^{-1/2}]$. The Behrend-weighted
numerical specialisation
$\mathcal H^{\mathrm{mot}}\to\mathbb Z[\![q,t,p]\!]$
via $\mathbb L^{1/2}\mapsto -1$ and pushforward to a point with Behrend
function $\nu_X\colon\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_h)\to\mathbb Z$
(Behrend~2009, \emph{Ann.\ of Math.}~170) sends the graded Poincar\'e
series to the numerical Donaldson--Thomas partition function
\[
 Z^X(q,t,p) \;=\; \sum_{h\geq 0}\sum_{d\geq 0}\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}
 \mathrm{DT}^{\mathrm{num}}_{n,(\beta_h,d)}\, q^n t^{d-1+\frac{1}{2}
 \langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle} (-p)^n,
 \qquad
 \beta_h=\tfrac{1}{N}(s_1+\cdots+s_N+hF),
\]
and Oberdieck's theorem (Oberdieck, \emph{Duke Math.\ J.}~2018,
arXiv:1605.08988) identifies the $N=1$ reduced generating series with
$Z^{K3\times E} = -C/\Delta_{10}$ where $C\in\mathbb Q^\times$ is a
universal constant. The K\"ahler cone of $X$ and the imaginary cone
$\mathcal C(\Lambda_{\mathrm{II}}^{2,1})$ have a common fan of walls in
$\mathrm{Stab}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X))$, and Joyce's wall-crossing formula
(Joyce--Song~2012, \emph{Memoirs of the AMS}~1020, Theorem~5.11) shows
$Z^X$ is $\sigma$-invariant on this distinguished component.

\medskip

\emph{(ii) Conditional Hall--BKM positive-half comparison.}
Let $\CoHA(X)\subset\mathcal H^{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\sigma)_{\mathrm{num}}$
denote the Behrend-weighted associative $\Eone$-algebra generated by
the spherical classes $[\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_h)]_\nu$. On surface or
local toric charts this is modelled by the $K$-theoretic
Kapranov--Vasserot CoHA under Hecke convolution. For compact
\(K3\times E\) it denotes the constructed DT/Hall replacement with
orientation, vanishing cycles, PBW, pairing, and completion data; it is
not the ordinary direct sum \(\bigoplus_n K_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X))\).
Conditional on the motivic Hall lift and the
Hall--BKM comparison for $K3\times E$, the expected Poincar\'e series is
\[
 \mathrm{Poinc}\bigl(\CoHA(X)\bigr)(q,t,p)
 \;=\;
 \frac{1}{\Delta_5(Z)^2}
 \;=\;
 \frac{1}{\Delta_{10}(Z)},
\]
and the Kontsevich--Soibelman Lie-algebra-of-the-wall-crossing
construction (\emph{op.\ cit.}, \S6) is expected to identify
\[
 \CoHA(X) \;\simeq\; U\bigl(Y^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})\bigr)_{\mathrm{num}},
 \qquad
 \mathrm{Lie}\,\CoHA(X) \;=\; Y^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}),
\]
the positive half of the Gritsenko--Nikulin--Borcherds Lie
superalgebra with the $\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}$-graded root cone
$\mathcal C_{\Delta_5}^+\subset\Lambda_{\mathrm{II}}^{2,1}$ of
the finite-rank $\Delta_5$-BKM definition / \S%
the $\Delta_5$-BKM foundations section, Fourier-cone-positive roots. The
identification is strict: $\CoHA(X)$ is \emph{not} the full
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$, nor a vertex/$\Etwo$-chiral algebra, nor
$\mathcal W_{1+\infty}$; it is the positive associative half
($\Eone$-associative, not chiral), consistent with the local model
$\CoHA(\mathbb C^3)=Y^+$ (positive half of the affine Yangian, not the
$\mathcal W_{1+\infty}$ evaluation slice).

\medskip

\emph{(iii) Conditional framed $\Phi_3$ output and mode algebra.} On the
chosen framed locus $(\Sigma_2,C)=(K3,E)$, and under the H1--H4
hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, the specialised
assignment
\[
 \Phi_3^{(K3,E)}=\SpCh_{K3,E}\circ\PhiFA_3
\]
produces an
$\Eone$-chiral algebra, not an $\Etwo$-chiral (vertex) algebra; the
$d$-dependent target operadic level is the dimensional stratification
of the CY-to-chiral correspondence $\{\Phi_d\}$, which is a
$d$-indexed family of framed assignments rather than a single global
CY$_3$ functor. Conditional on the Hall--BKM comparison of
part~\textup{(ii)}, set
\[
 H_{\Delta_5}
 \;:=\;
 \Phi_3^{(K3,E)}\bigl(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \mathcal D_\hbar\!\bigl(\mathcal Y^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar\bigl(\CoHA(X)\bigr),\;
 \widetilde\Phi^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}],\;
 R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}\bigr),
\]
the candidate Heisenberg-double of the Hall-Yangian by the Siegel-Borcherds
spectral cocycle $\widetilde\Phi^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$
of the $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ foundations section. Then $H_{\Delta_5}$ is expected to be a factorisation algebra
on $\mathbb A^1_\kk$ with $\Eone$-structure, and the mode-expansion
Lie superalgebra
\[
 \mathrm{Lie}^{\mathrm{modes}}\bigl(H_{\Delta_5}\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \widehat{\mathfrak g}_{\Delta_5}
 \;=\;
 \mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\oplus\mathbb C\,\mathsf c,
\]
coincides with $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ up to the one-dimensional
Borcherds central extension $\mathsf c$ determined by the
$\frac{1}{\Delta_{10}}$-denominator formula; equivalently,
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}
=\mathrm{Lie}^{\mathrm{modes}}(H_{\Delta_5})/\mathbb C\mathsf c$
recovers the reduced Gritsenko--Nikulin Borcherds--Kac--Moody Lie
superalgebra with Cartan $\Lambda_{\mathrm{II}}^{2,1}$ and root
multiplicities $\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)$ given by the Fourier
coefficients $f(n,l,m)$ of $\phi_{0,1}$ via Oberdieck--Pixton.

\medskip

\emph{(iv) Conditional triangular decomposition reconciling (ii) and (iii).} The
Drinfeld double of the positive half is expected to recover a Borcherds-enhanced
quantum group at $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$,
\[
 D\bigl(Y^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})\bigr)
 \;=\;
 U_q\bigl(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\bigr)^{\mathrm{Hall}}
 \;\hookrightarrow\;
 \mathcal Z\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}))\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \mathrm{End}^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\Eone}\bigl(H_{\Delta_5}\bigr),
\]
identifying (ii) with (iii) through the Schiffmann--Vasserot
Hall/shuffle presentation of the positive half and the Kapranov--Vasserot
convolution product; the full BKM super-triangular decomposition
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}=Y^-\oplus\mathfrak h\oplus Y^+$
(the $\Delta_5$-triangular foundations section)
matches the Drinfeld-double triangular decomposition
$D(\CoHA)=\CoHA^-\otimes\mathfrak h\otimes\CoHA^+$ via Hopf pairing.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Source and target maps for the \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3xE} Hall--BKM comparison]
\label{prop:qgf-k3e-source-target-comparison-maps}
Let $X=K3\times E$ and keep the stability, orientation, and framing
