data of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-motivic-dt-coha-phi3-gDelta5}. Suppose,
for the clauses in which they are used, that the motivic Hall lift, the
Hall--BKM positive-cone comparison, the Serre-dual negative half and
Cartan pairing, and the H1--H4 framed
$\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}$ specialisation are constructed. Then the theorem
decomposes into the following maps with fixed source and target:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item the Behrend integration character
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{Behr}}\colon
 \mathcal H^{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\sigma)
 \longrightarrow
 \mathbb Z[\![q,t,p]\!],
 \qquad
 \mathbb L^{1/2}\mapsto -1,
\]
whose image is Oberdieck's reduced numerical DT partition function
$Z^{K3\times E}=-C/\Delta_{10}$ at $N=1$;
\item the positive-half Hall--BKM comparison
\[
 \Theta^+_{K3E}\colon
 \CoHA(X)
 \longrightarrow
 U\bigl(Y^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})\bigr)_{\mathrm{num}},
\]
a morphism of completed associative $\Eone$ Hall algebras on the
positive cone $\mathcal C_{\Delta_5}^+$;
\item after the negative half, Cartan part, completion, and
non-degenerate Hopf pairing are fixed, the doubled comparison
\[
 D(\Theta^+_{K3E})\colon
 D(\CoHA(X))
 \longrightarrow
 U_q(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})^{\mathrm{Hall}};
\]
\item under the framed H1--H4 specialisation, the $\Eone$-chiral
comparison
\[
 \Xi_{\Phi_3}^{K3E}\colon
 \Phi_3^{(K3,E)}\bigl(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)\bigr)
 \longrightarrow
 \mathbf H_{\Delta_5}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
The $\Etwo$ object associated to the last line is obtained by applying
the Drinfeld-centre functor to the $\Eone$ representation category:
\[
 \mathcal Z\!\left(
 \Rep^{\Eone}
 \bigl(\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X))\bigr)
 \right)
 \longrightarrow
 \mathcal Z\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})\bigr).
\]
Thus the theorem's source theorem is the reduced DT character identity
in \textup{(i)}; its target obligations are the isomorphism in
\textup{(ii)}, the pairing data needed for \textup{(iii)}, and the
framed $\Phi_3$ algebra comparison in \textup{(iv)}. None of these
maps identifies $\CoHA(X)$ with the full BKM algebra before the double,
or makes $\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}$ an intrinsic $\Etwo$-chiral algebra.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The map \textup{(i)} is the Kontsevich--Soibelman motivic integration
specialised by the Behrend function; Oberdieck's theorem computes its
numerical image for $K3\times E$ at $N=1$. The map \textup{(ii)} is
exactly the Hall--BKM positive-cone comparison assumed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-motivic-dt-coha-phi3-gDelta5}: both sides are
completed $\Eone$ associative algebras graded by the same positive root
cone, and the comparison is required to preserve the Hall product and
the numerical BPS grading. The map \textup{(iii)} is functoriality of
the Drinfeld double for a Hopf-pairing-preserving morphism; this is why
the negative half, Cartan part, completion, and non-degenerate pairing
are explicit hypotheses. The map \textup{(iv)} is the named
comparison induced by the framed object-level construction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} after the $K3$-fibre
specialisation and the Hall--BKM spectral cocycle have both been
chosen. Applying the Drinfeld-centre functor gives the displayed
$\Etwo$ map by the Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler centre theorem, one level
above the $\Eone$ algebras themselves.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Hall--BKM completion datum: maps, sources, obstructions]
\label{thm:qgf-k3e-hall-bkm-completion-datum}
Let \(X=K3\times E\), and fix the stability, orientation, completion, and
framing data of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-motivic-dt-coha-phi3-gDelta5}.
For each finite Borcherds height \(H\), write
\[
 \mathcal H^{\pm,\leq H}_{X},\qquad \mathcal H^{0,\leq H}_{X},
 \qquad
 U^{\leq H}_{\Delta_5}:=
 U_q\bigl(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}^{\leq H}\bigr)^{\mathrm{Hall}}
\]
for the proposed Hall positive/negative/Cartan pieces and the
height-\(H\) Borcherds current quotient. A completed
Hall--Drinfeld/BKM comparison is exactly the inverse-limit comparison
whose finite quotients carry the following five maps:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \mathsf{PBW}_H&\colon
 \operatorname{gr}_F\mathcal H^{+,\leq H}_{X}
 \longrightarrow
 U(\mathfrak n_+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}^{\leq H})),\\
 \mathsf{Pair}_H&\colon
 \mathcal H^{+,\leq H}_{X}\widehat\otimes
 \mathcal H^{-,\leq H}_{X}
 \longrightarrow \mathbb C((\hbar)),\\
 \mathsf{Cop}_H&:=
 (\Theta_H^+\widehat\otimes\Theta_H^+)\circ
 \Delta^{\mathrm{Hall}}_H
 -
 \Delta^{\Delta_5}_H\circ\Theta_H^+,\\
 \mathsf{Ctr}_H&\colon
 \operatorname{Prim}Z\!\left(
 \mathcal H^{-,\leq H}_{X}\widehat{\bowtie}
 \mathcal H^{0,\leq H}_{X}\widehat{\bowtie}
 \mathcal H^{+,\leq H}_{X}\right)
 \longrightarrow
 \mathfrak r^{0,\leq H}_{\Delta_5}\oplus
 \mathfrak z^{\mathrm{dyn},\leq H},\\
 \mathsf{Assoc}_H&\colon
 [\Phi^{\mathrm{Hall},\leq H}]
 \longmapsto
 [\Phi^{\mathrm{SB},\leq H}_{\Delta_5}]
 \quad\text{in quasi-Hopf associator cohomology modulo gauge.}
\end{aligned}
\]
Here \(\Theta_H^+\) is the finite-height positive-half comparison
of Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-k3e-source-target-comparison-maps},
\(\mathfrak r^{0,\leq H}_{\Delta_5}\) is the Borcherds Cartan radical,
and \(\mathfrak z^{\mathrm{dyn},\leq H}\) is the declared span of
dynamical group-like central parameters. Each entry compares a source
theorem with a target theorem and names the obstruction that must be
removed.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \(\mathsf{PBW}_H\). Source theorem: Davison--Meinhardt PBW for
constructed critical CoHAs. Target theorem: Borcherds PBW for the
finite-height \(\Delta_5\)-BKM quotient. Obstruction: an excess or
missing Serre-kernel class in the associated graded. The required comparison is to
identify primitive Hall generators root-by-root and prove that the
kernel is the finite-height Borcherds--Serre ideal.
\item \(\mathsf{Pair}_H\). Source theorem: the Hall--Serre pairing
coming from the oriented critical correspondence and Serre duality.
Target theorem: the invariant Borcherds form on paired root spaces with
Cartan radical \(\mathfrak r^{0,\leq H}_{\Delta_5}\). Obstruction: a
pairing radical outside that Cartan radical, or a missing Cartan radical
on the Hall side. The required comparison is to quotient only by the matched Cartan
radical and verify nondegeneracy on every root-space block.
\item \(\mathsf{Cop}_H\). Source theorem: the Hall coproduct from
extension correspondences in the completed critical CoHA. Target
theorem: the Drinfeld-new Borcherds current coproduct, with associated
graded the primitive enveloping coproduct. Obstruction: nonzero
\(\mathsf{Cop}_H\) as a continuous map into the completed tensor square.
The required comparison checks primitives and Cartan group-likes first; PBW then
extends the equality to the finite quotient.
\item \(\mathsf{Ctr}_H\). Source theorem: the Drinfeld-centre theorem
applied after the paired double is formed. Target theorem: the
Borcherds current centre consists only of the Cartan radical and the
declared dynamical parameters. Obstruction: an undeclared primitive
central class. The required comparison is to compute primitive central classes in the
finite double and either identify them with the allowed span or prove
their vanishing.
\item \(\mathsf{Assoc}_H\). Source theorem: the quasi-Hopf associator
of the completed Hall double when the dynamical coproduct is present.
Target theorem: the Siegel--Borcherds associator class determined by
\(\Delta_5\) and the Felder--Jimbo--Konno elliptic stratum. Obstruction:
a nonzero gauge-class difference. The required comparison is to compare the class at
finite height and require compatibility with the transition maps
\(H+1\to H\).
\item \(\mathsf{Par}_H\). Source theorem: the parity decomposition of
the finite compact Hall source packet. Target theorem: the Borcherds
super-root parity fixture. Obstruction: a comparison matrix that matches
signed superdimensions but does not intertwine the source and target
parity involutions. The required comparison is the finite
parity-intertwining equation at height \(H\), compatible with the
transition maps \(H+1\to H\).
\end{enumerate}
The completed comparison
\[
 D^\wedge_\hbar(\mathcal H^+_{X})
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\;
 U_q^\wedge(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})^{\mathrm{Hall}}
\]
exists if and only if all six finite-height obstructions vanish for
every \(H\) and the transition maps preserve the vanished obstructions.
If the comparison fails, the least height at which one of the six maps
fails is the precise obstruction to the Hall construction.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At fixed height the completions are finite over each Borcherds-cone
slice, so the comparison is detected by its PBW associated graded,
pairing, coproduct, centre, associator gauge class, and parity
involution. These are
invariants of a topological quasi-Hopf isomorphism; hence a completed
comparison forces the six displayed maps to be the target maps and the
six obstructions to vanish at every height. Conversely, if the six
finite-height checks vanish and commute with the transition maps, the
positive-half isomorphism, the paired negative half, the Cartan quotient,
and the quasi-Hopf structure assemble into an inverse system of
finite-height doubles identified with the Borcherds current quotients.
The inverse limit is the completed Hall--Drinfeld/BKM comparison. The
least non-vanishing finite-height defect is therefore not a wording
problem; it is the first mathematical obstruction.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Motivic wall-crossing, Behrend specialisation, and the \texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1}/\texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2} boundary at \texorpdfstring{$d=3$}{d=3}]
\label{rem:qgf-motivic-dt-coha-phi3-gDelta5}
Three distinctions sit at the core of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-motivic-dt-coha-phi3-gDelta5} and are routinely
conflated in casual statements.

\emph{(a) Motivic DT vs Behrend-numerical DT.} Kontsevich--Soibelman
motivic DT lives in the graded $\mathrm{Mot}(\mathrm{Spec}\,\kk)[\mathbb L^{-1/2}]$
ring and carries a Hopf-algebra structure whose commutator is the Joyce
Lie bracket on the stack of semistable objects; Behrend-weighted
numerical DT is the image under
$\mathbb L^{1/2}\mapsto -1$ and $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_h)\mapsto
\sum_k k\cdot e(\nu_X^{-1}(k))$. Oberdieck's
$Z^{K3\times E}=-C/\Delta_{10}$ is the numerical image; the
motivic lift is conjectural for $K3\times E$ and is a theorem only
at $\mathbb C^3$ and at toric $\mathrm{CY}_3$ without compact
$4$-cycles (Davison--Meinhardt~2020, \emph{Invent.\ Math.}\ 221).
Under the identification of (ii), $1/\Delta_{10}$ is \emph{simultaneously}
the numerical DT partition function and the Poincar\'e series of
$\CoHA(X)$, the latter being a second, independent incarnation
of the same automorphic form.

\emph{(b) $\CoHA(X)\neq\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$; $\CoHA(X)$ is the positive
half only.} The local identification
$\CoHA(\mathbb C^3)=Y^+$ (positive half, not the $\mathcal W_{1+\infty}$
evaluation slice) generalises to $K3\times E$: the CoHA captures the extension algebra
only in the positive cone $\mathcal C_{\Delta_5}^+$, not the Cartan
nor the negative roots. The full Lie superalgebra
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}=Y^-\oplus\mathfrak h\oplus Y^+$ is recovered
only after the Drinfeld double and the Hopf pairing are adjoined, as in
(iv). Statements such as ``$\CoHA(X)=\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$'' or
``$\CoHA(X)$ is a vertex algebra'' are both false; the first ignores the
triangular decomposition, the second ignores the $\Eone$-associative
(not $\Etwo$-chiral) nature of the Hall product.

\emph{(c) $\Phi_3$ output is $\Eone$-chiral, never $\Etwo$/VOA.} The
CY-A dimensional stratification (\S~the CY-A foundations section) forces
$\Phi_3$ to land in $\Eone$-chiral algebras on
$\mathrm{Ran}(\mathbb A^1)$, not in vertex algebras. The Segal
$\Etwo$-structure lives one level up, on the Drinfeld centre
$\mathcal Z(\Rep^{\Eone}(H_{\Delta_5}))$, not on $H_{\Delta_5}$
itself. Any identification of $H_{\Delta_5}$ with
``the $\Delta_5$-VOA'' conflates two distinct structures; the correct statement
is that the mode Lie superalgebra
$\mathrm{Lie}^{\mathrm{modes}}(H_{\Delta_5})=\widehat{\mathfrak g}_{\Delta_5}$
is the centrally-extended BKM, and the Borcherds denominator identity
$\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^+}(1-e^{-\alpha})^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)}
 =\Delta_5(Z)/\exp(-2\pi i(\rho,Z))$
is the character-theoretic shadow of the $\Eone$-bar supertrace on
$H_{\Delta_5}$.

\emph{(d) Wall-crossing invariance of the BKM assignment.} On the
Oberdieck--Pixton distinguished component of
$\mathrm{Stab}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X))$, the Joyce wall-crossing formula
acts by inner automorphisms of $\mathcal H^{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\sigma)$;
these descend to inner automorphisms of $\CoHA(X)$, of
$D(\CoHA(X))$, and hence of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ up to Cartan
shifts. The BKM $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is $\sigma$-invariant, and
so is $H_{\Delta_5}=\Phi_3(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X))$: the dependence on
$\sigma$ is only through the normalisation of the Weyl vector
$\rho\in(\Lambda_{\mathrm{II}}^{2,1})^*$, which is a change of
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{II}}^{2,1}$-trivialisation of the root cone. The
non-trivial wall-crossing phenomena at $X=K3\times E$ live one level
up, on the Pandharipande--Thomas / Gopakumar--Vafa refinements, and
produce the elliptic-genus hierarchy of
the CY-C-beyond-K3$\times E$ existence-obstruction theorem (see
Chapter~\cite{VolI}, case
$(S\times E)/(\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z)$, $N\in\{1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8\}$).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Mukai lattice, extended Mukai at \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}, and the \texorpdfstring{$\Lambda^{3,2}$}{Lambda-3,2} Humbert restriction]
\label{prop:qgfnd-mukai-II420-extended-humbert}
Let $X$ be a K$3$ surface. The full cohomology
$H^*(X;\mathbb Z)=H^0\oplus H^2\oplus H^4$ carries the Mukai pairing
$\langle(r,\ell,s),(r',\ell',s')\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}=(\ell,\ell')_{H^2}-rs'-sr'$,
and the resulting lattice is the unique even unimodular lattice of
signature $(4,20)$:
\[
 \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(\mathrm{K3}) \;=\; \mathrm{II}_{4,20}
 \;\simeq\; U^{\oplus 4}\oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2},
 \qquad\mathrm{sign}=(4,20),\ \det=1,\ \mathrm{even}.
\]
The positive four-plane is spanned by
\((H^{2,0}\oplus H^{0,2})_\mathbb R\), the K\"ahler line in
\(H^{1,1}_\mathbb R\), and a positive line in the hyperbolic plane
\(H^0\oplus H^4\), giving \(2+1+1=4\). The negative directions are the
primitive \(H^{1,1}\)-part of rank \(19\) together with the negative line
in \(H^0\oplus H^4\), giving \(19+1=20\). Restriction to the sub-lattice of algebraic Mukai vectors
yields the extended Néron--Severi lattice
\[
 \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X)
 \;=\; \mathbb Z\,[\mathcal O_X]\oplus \mathrm{NS}(X)\oplus\mathbb Z\,[\mathrm{pt}]
 \;\simeq\; U\oplus\mathrm{NS}(X),
\]
a sublattice of signature $(2,\rho(X))$ where $\rho(X)\in\{1,\ldots,20\}$ is
the Picard rank.

For the CY$_3$ product $X_3=K3\times E$ the extended Mukai lattice is
the tensor product over $H^*(E;\mathbb Z)\simeq U(1)$:
\[
 \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{ext}}(K3\times E)
 \;=\; \mathrm{II}_{4,20}\oplus U\;\simeq\; U^{\oplus 5}\oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2},
 \qquad\mathrm{sign}=(5,21),
\]
with the extra $U$-summand generated by $\bigl(H^0(E),H^1(E),H^2(E)\bigr)$
and the $H^1$-summand carrying the Poincaré-duality symplectic pairing.
Restriction to the algebraic-plus-transcendental Humbert-divisor locus
$\mathcal A_2^{\mathrm{Hum}}\subset\mathcal A_2\simeq\mathcal F_{\Lambda^{3,2}}$
selects the signature-$(3,2)$ Pl\"ucker quotient
\[
 \Lambda^{3,2}\;=\;q^{\perp}\subset\bigl(\Lambda^2\mathbb Z^{\oplus 4},\wedge\bigr),
 \qquad q=e_1\wedge e_3+e_2\wedge e_4,
\]
and the embedding $\Lambda^{3,2}\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{ext}}(K3\times E)$
is the composite
$\Lambda^{3,2}\simeq U\oplus U\oplus[2]\hookrightarrow U^{\oplus 2}\oplus
\langle-2\rangle\hookrightarrow U^{\oplus 5}\oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2}$,
where $\langle-2\rangle$ is generated by a primitive $(-2)$-class
$a_0\in E_8(-1)$ (the Humbert-polarisation class). The Borcherds lift
\[
 \Phi_{\mathrm{Borch}}\colon M^{!}_{-1/2}\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}_0(4),\chi_{\theta}\bigr)
 \longrightarrow S_{5}\bigl(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z),\nu_{\Delta_5}\bigr),
 \qquad f\mapsto\Delta_5,
\]
takes the Gritsenko vector-valued nearly-holomorphic form $f$ to the
Siegel cusp form $\Delta_5$ on the period domain
$\mathcal F_{\Lambda^{3,2}}=\mathrm{O}^+(\Lambda^{3,2})\backslash
\mathcal D_{\Lambda^{3,2}}$, realising $\Delta_5$ as the automorphic
Humbert-divisor regulator on $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{ext}}(K3\times E)$.

The lattice relations are:
\begin{enumerate}
 \item[\textup{(i)}] $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(\mathrm{K3})=\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$,
 signature $(4,20)$, is the Mukai-Hodge support of $\Phi_2(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(\mathrm{K3}))=H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$;
 \item[\textup{(ii)}] $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{ext}}(K3\times E)=\mathrm{II}_{4,20}\oplus U$,
 signature $(5,21)$, is the mode-lattice of the conditional
 Hall--Borcherds target $H_{\Delta_5}$ for
 $\Phi_3(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3\times E))$;
 \item[\textup{(iii)}] $\Lambda^{3,2}\subset\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{ext}}(K3\times E)$
 is the signature-$(3,2)$ algebraic-Humbert sublattice on which
 $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)/\{\pm I_4\}\simeq\mathrm{SO}^+(\Lambda^{3,2})$
 acts via wedge-square, carrying $\Delta_5$ as its Borcherds regulator.
\end{enumerate}
The Weyl-vector $\rho\in(\Lambda_{\mathrm{II}}^{2,1})^*$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:qgfnd-plucker-lg24} sits in $\Lambda^{2,1}\subset\Lambda^{3,2}$
as the hyperbolic-plane primitive isotropic, and the Gritsenko--Nikulin
imaginary-root Weyl vector of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is the
$\Lambda^{3,2}$-restriction of the Conway Weyl vector of
$\mathrm{II}_{4,20}\oplus U$ under the embedding above.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Humbert regulator, algebraic-Mukai restriction, and the \texorpdfstring{$\Lambda^{3,2}$}{Lambda-3,2} origin of \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta-5}]
\label{rem:qgfnd-humbert-regulator-algebraic-mukai}
The signature arithmetic is forced by three independent inputs. First,
the Mukai pairing on $H^*(\mathrm{K3};\mathbb Z)$ has signature
$(4,20)=(1+1+2,3+19-2)$: the hyperbolic $H^0\oplus H^4$ contributes
$(1,1)$, the $H^2$-lattice contributes $(3,19)$ with its $(1,1)$-Hodge
decomposition, and the Mukai twist $(r,\ell,s)\mapsto r s$ off-diagonal
entry flips the $(H^0,H^4)$-pair to signature $(1,1)$ on that plane.
Second, Künneth on $K3\times E$ tensors against
$H^*(E;\mathbb Z)\simeq U(1)$, producing a single additional hyperbolic
plane and lifting $(4,20)\to(5,21)$. Third, the Humbert-divisor
criterion $\mathrm{NS}(X)\supset\langle L,L'\rangle$ with
$(L,L),(L,L'),(L',L')$ prescribed, in signature $(1,1)$ on the
transcendental complement of a primitive polarisation class
$a_0\in E_8(-1)$, carves out exactly the $\Lambda^{3,2}$-polarised
locus $\mathcal F_{\Lambda^{3,2}}$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
\emph{Algebra~i~Analiz} Theorem 1.1; Gritsenko 1999 Proposition 2.3
for the lift-to-$\Delta_5$ step). The composite
$\mathcal A_2\simeq\mathcal F_{\Lambda^{3,2}}\subset\mathcal F_{\mathrm{II}_{4,20}\oplus U}$
(the Kummer-functor embedding at Humbert $H_1$) places $\Delta_5$ as
the Borcherds lift of the algebraic-Mukai-restricted Humbert
regulator, not as a generic $\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$-automorphic form.

Three common misidentifications dismissed. (a) The
$\Lambda^{3,2}$-polarised period domain is \emph{not} the full K$3$
period domain $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{II}_{4,20}}$: the Humbert locus
has codimension $\geq 2$, and the restriction of a generic
$\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$-Borcherds form to $\Lambda^{3,2}$ produces
$\Delta_5^{\,\alpha}$ only for the lattice-vector $\alpha$ matching
the Humbert polarisation type $(1,1,1,0,-2)$. The restriction is a
Humbert-regulator computation, not a generic pullback. (b) The
Mukai-vector lattice $\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$ is an
\emph{unoriented} even unimodular lattice; the choice of chiral
orientation (which of the four positive directions is time-like)
is an additional datum, corresponding to the choice of stability
condition $\sigma\in\mathrm{Stab}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(\mathrm{K3}))$
on the Bridgeland distinguished component. This orientation does
not change the lattice-theoretic $\Lambda^{3,2}$-embedding but does
change the Weyl-chamber normalisation of $\rho$. (c) The
Pl\"ucker $\Lambda^{3,2}$ of Remark~\ref{rem:qgfnd-plucker-lg24} and
the Mukai-restriction $\Lambda^{3,2}\subset\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{ext}}(K3\times E)$
of Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-mukai-II420-extended-humbert} are the
\emph{same} lattice, by the $B_2=C_2$ exceptional isomorphism
$\wedge^2\colon\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{SO}^+(\Lambda^{3,2})$:
the wedge-square of the defining representation of
$\mathrm{Sp}(4,\mathbb Z)$ is exactly the vector representation of
$\mathrm{Spin}(3,2)\simeq\mathrm{Sp}(4,\mathbb R)$, and the
$q^{\perp}$-quotient of $\Lambda^2\mathbb Z^4$ matches the
Humbert-polarised Mukai-restriction as integral lattices of
signature $(3,2)$. This identification is what makes $\Delta_5$
simultaneously a genus-$2$ Siegel cusp form and a
$\Lambda^{3,2}$-Borcherds lift; the double-nature is not a
coincidence but the Pl\"ucker--Mukai duality at work.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1}-chiral twisted holography at \texorpdfstring{$d=3$}{d=3}: Costello--Paquette extension and the genus-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} shadow of \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{prop:qgf-e1-chiral-twisted-holography-k3e}
Let $X=K3\times E$, let
$A_{K3,E}:=\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}\bigl(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)\bigr)$ be the
$\Eone$-chiral Stage-$2$ candidate at $d=3$, and assume the compact
Hall--Borcherds source-recognition obstructions identify $A_{K3,E}$ with the
recognised compact source $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$. Let
$Y^{\mathrm{HT}}_3(X)$ denote the $3$D holomorphic-topological
gauge theory on $X\times\mathbb R_+$ with $\Omega$-deformation parameter
$\varepsilon\in H^1(E,\mathbb C)$ fixed by the elliptic period. Then:

\emph{(i) Kill of Costello--Gaiotto direct at $d=3$.} The
Costello--Gaiotto 2018 twisted-holography correspondence
$Y^{\mathrm{HT}}_{4\mathrm D}(K3)\leftrightarrow\mathrm{VOA}_{K3}$
used the boundary layer of a $4$D HT theory on $K3\times\mathbb R_+^2$
as source of a \emph{vertex operator algebra}: full $\Etwo$-chiral
data, sphere OPE, modes labelled by
$H^*(\mathrm{Ran}(\mathbb A^1),\mathrm{Fact}_{\Etwo})$. At $d=3$ on
$X=K3\times E$ the boundary loses one topological direction: the HT
bulk is $3$D not $4$D, and the boundary factorisation algebra is
$\Eone$-chiral (locally-constant on a single complex curve $C\subset X$),
not $\Etwo$-chiral. Direct transplantation of Costello--Gaiotto to
$d=3$ produces a VOA which does not exist: the Segal $\Etwo$-data on
the supposed boundary VOA is obstructed by the $E$-factor, whose
$H^1(E,\mathbb C)$ is one-dimensional and encodes the $\Omega$-deformation
parameter rather than the sphere OPE datum. The identification
``$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}=\mathrm{VOA}_{\Delta_5}$'' is therefore false
as a statement about $\Phi_3$-output; it is the CoHA-versus-vertex-algebra
distinction generalised to the boundary layer.

\emph{(ii) Survival: Costello--Paquette $\Eone$-chiral extension.} The
Costello--Paquette 2018 framework (\emph{Twisted holography for $3$D
$\mathcal N=4$}, arXiv:1812.09257) extends twisted holography to
$\Eone$-chiral boundaries of $3$D HT gauge theory. The boundary
datum of $Y^{\mathrm{HT}}_3(X)$ is a locally-constant factorisation
algebra $\mathcal A^{\Eone}_\partial$ on a complex curve $C\subset X$;
its \emph{mode algebra} $\mathrm{Modes}(\mathcal A^{\Eone}_\partial)$
is an associative algebra (not a vertex algebra), whose pairing is
the linearised $\Eone$-commutator, not the full Borcherds OPE. The
Costello--Paquette extension identifies
\[
 \Phi_3\bigl(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \mathcal A^{\Eone}_\partial\bigl(Y^{\mathrm{HT}}_3(X)\bigr)
 \quad\text{on}\quad
 C\;=\;E\subset X,
\]
with $\Omega$-deformation parameter $\varepsilon$ set by the elliptic
period $\oint_\gamma\omega_E\in H^1(E,\mathbb C)$. The identification
is $\Eone$-monoidal, not $\Etwo$-braided; the R-matrix
$r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ is an $\Eone$-commutator shadow, not a braiding
coefficient of a modular tensor category.

\emph{(iii) Genus-$2$ shadow.} Under (ii), the Gritsenko--Nikulin
$\Delta_5(Z)\in M_5(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z))$ is the
\emph{genus-$2$ shadow} of the mode character of
$\mathcal A^{\Eone}_\partial$:
\[
 \Delta_5(Z)
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{ch}^{\mathrm{modes}}_{g=2}\bigl(\mathcal A^{\Eone}_\partial\bigr)(Z)
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{Tr}^{\Eone}_{\mathcal A^{\Eone}_\partial}
 \bigl(e^{2\pi i(\tau L_0 + z J_0 + \sigma \bar L_0)}\bigr),
 \qquad
 Z=\begin{pmatrix}\tau & z\\ z & \sigma\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb H_2,
\]
where the genus-$2$ period matrix $Z$ encodes the
$\Omega_{K3\times E}$-expansion directions $(\tau,\sigma)\in\mathbb H_1^2$
and the coupling $z$ between them. The weight $5$ of $\Delta_5$ is
$c_1(0)/2$ at $N=1$ with $c_1(0)=10$ (Borcherds 1995; Gritsenko 1999);
in the Costello--Paquette language it is the conformal anomaly of
the $\Eone$-mode trace normalised by the Weyl vector $\rho$.

The $\Etwo$-chiral lift of $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ lives on the Drinfeld
centre $\mathcal Z(\Rep^{\Eone}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}))$, one level up
from the boundary algebra itself, and the unnormalised Borcherds square
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$ is the protected partition-function target of
$Y^{\mathrm{HT}}_3(X)$ on $X\times S^1$ after the physical comparison
datum is supplied, not the character of
$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[What dies, what survives, where the \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2}-data hides]
\label{rem:qgf-e1-chiral-twisted-holography-death-survival}
The proposition sorts the Costello--Gaiotto/Costello--Paquette
lineage at the $d=2\to d=3$ boundary into three consequences.

\emph{(a) VOA is a $d=2$ phenomenon.} The K$3$ sigma-model VOA of
Costello--Gaiotto 2018 is the boundary algebra of the $4$D HT twist
on $K3\times\mathbb R_+^2$, with both $\mathbb R_+$ directions
topological. Only then does the boundary inherit both a holomorphic
and a topological direction from the bulk and thereby acquire
$\Etwo$-chiral structure with full OPE. The $K3\times E$ boundary is
$\Eone$-chiral because $E$ spends one complex direction and the
remaining $\mathbb R_+$ produces only $\Eone$-chiral locally-constant
structure, not $\Etwo$-braided structure. This is the bulk-side
manifestation of CY-D dimensional stratification.

\emph{(b) Costello--Paquette is the correct framework.} The
arXiv:1812.09257 extension produces $\Eone$-chiral mode algebras as
boundary data, with the bulk $\Omega$-deformation parameter playing
the role of central charge. For $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ the
$\Omega$-deformation is the elliptic period of $E$, and the bulk
gauge theory is the Pestun-twisted maximally-supersymmetric
$3$D $\mathcal N=4$ theory on $K3\times E\times\mathbb R_+$. The
K\"ahler potential of $K3$ survives as the holomorphic direction, and
$E$ survives as a secondary complex structure whose cohomology provides
the Cartan torus for $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$.

\emph{(c) $\Delta_5$ is a genus-$2$ shadow, not a VOA character.} The
long-standing temptation to write ``$\Delta_5=$ character of the
$\Delta_5$-VOA'' is rejected. $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ has no VOA
structure; after the protected trace comparison is supplied, its
$\Eone$-mode algebra has genus-$2$ chiral-half trace $\Delta_5$. The
unnormalised Igusa square $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$ on
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathbb H_2$ is the pair-of-pants
protected partition-function target of $Y^{\mathrm{HT}}_3(X)$, or equivalently the
$\Etwo$-shadow on $\mathcal Z(\Rep^{\Eone}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}))$, and
its factorisation $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5\cdot\Delta_5$ is the cutting of
the genus-$2$ surface along a separating cycle into two once-punctured
tori. Numerically $\mathrm{wt}(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})=10$ is twice
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=c_{\Delta_5}(0)/2=5$ at
the genus-$2$ level. Identifications such as ``$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$
is the $\Delta_5$-VOA and its character is $\Delta_5$'' conflate
(boundary $\Eone$-algebra) with (bulk $\Etwo$-centre) and are rejected
as CoHA-versus-vertex-algebra distinctions.

The $\Etwo$-data visible in genus-$2$ modular forms is real and
load-bearing; it does not live on $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ itself but on
the derived centre of its $\Eone$-representation category. This is the
boundary-centre analogue of the identification
$Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)=\mathrm{HH}^\bullet$-twist-at-the-boundary:
the boundary algebra sees $\Eone$-data, its centre sees $\Etwo$-data,
and the Costello--Paquette extension is the geometric bridge that
makes this arithmetic rather than tautological.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Gaitsgory--Lurie derived geometric Langlands for \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Sp}_4$}{Sp-4}: scope, the Hecke eigensheaf for \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10}, and Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel action of \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5}]
\label{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}
Let $\Sigma$ be a smooth projective curve over $\mathbb C$, write
$\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(\Sigma)$ for the moduli stack of
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-bundles on $\Sigma$, and
$\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(\Sigma)$ for the derived stack of
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)$-local systems. The Gaitsgory--Lurie
conjecture (Gaitsgory, \emph{Recent progress in geometric Langlands},
ICM 2022; Arinkin--Gaitsgory 2015 \emph{Singular support of coherent
sheaves and the geometric Langlands conjecture}) posits a
$\mathbb C$-linear equivalence of DG categories
\[
 \IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}\bigl(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(\Sigma)\bigr)
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\simeq\;}\;
 \Dmod\bigl(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(\Sigma)\bigr),
\]
intertwining the spectral Hecke action on the left with the geometric
Hecke action on the right. We clarify scope and inscribe the K3-$E$
content.

\emph{(i) Kill of ``geometric Langlands for $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ is done''.}
The full Gaitsgory--Lurie equivalence for $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ on a curve of
genus $\geq 2$ is \emph{not} a theorem at the time of this manuscript.
It is a theorem for tori $G=T$ (Beilinson--Drinfeld, \emph{Quantization
of Hitchin's integrable system and Hecke eigensheaves}, preprint 1991,
published portions in subsequent Astérisque volumes); it is partially
established for $G=\GL_n$ through the Drinfeld--Laumon construction
via automorphic sheaves on moduli of $p$-adic shtukas
(Frenkel--Gaitsgory--Vilonen 2002, \emph{On the geometric Langlands
conjecture}, JAMS 15); it is established at the level of
\emph{rational} $\Bun_G$ for $G$ of rank one by the Raskin 2020--2024
series (\emph{Chiral principal series categories}, Compositio, and
sequels). For $G=\mathrm{Sp}_4$ on $\Sigma$ a curve of genus
$g\geq 2$ the full equivalence is \emph{open}. Statements of the form
``geometric Langlands for $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ is proved'' or ``Gaitsgory
has resolved the $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ case'' conflate conjecture with
theorem in derived geometric Langlands. What \emph{is} rigorous is the
existence of Hecke eigensheaves for specific spectral data: the
Beilinson--Drinfeld construction of Hecke eigensheaves via opers is
a theorem when the spectral $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-local system is an
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-oper (Beilinson--Drinfeld 1991 \S 5). The
$\Delta_{10}$-eigensheaf below belongs to this regime.

\emph{(ii) Hecke eigensheaf for $\Delta_{10}$ at spectral parameter
$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$.} Specialise to $\Sigma=E$ an elliptic curve;
then $\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$ admits the Looijenga--Friedman--Morgan
presentation as a weighted projective space
$\mathbb P(1,1,2,2,3)//W_{C_2}$ with $W_{C_2}$-action from the
symplectic Weyl group (Friedman--Morgan 2002 \emph{Minuscule
representations, invariant polynomials, and spectral covers}). The
spectral parameter for
$\Delta_{10}:=\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$ is the
Gritsenko--Nikulin $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-automorphic datum
\[
 \sigma_{\Delta_{10}}\colon
 \pi_1(E)\;\longrightarrow\;\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C),
 \qquad
 \gamma\;\longmapsto\;
 \exp\!\left(2\pi i\,\tau\,H_\gamma\right),
\]
with $\tau\in\mathbb H_1$ the elliptic modulus and
$H_\gamma\in\mathfrak{sp}_4(\mathbb C)$ the $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-Cartan
element determined by the pairing $\gamma\cdot\omega_E$ against the
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-rank-$2$ root lattice. The corresponding Hecke
eigensheaf
\[
 \mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}\;\in\;\Dmod\bigl(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr),
 \qquad
 H_{V_\lambda}\star\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}
 \;=\;
 \chi_{V_\lambda}(\sigma_{\Delta_{10}})\otimes\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}
 \quad
 \text{for all }V_\lambda\in\Rep(\mathrm{Sp}_4),
\]
exists because $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ is an $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-oper on
$E$ (the commuting pair $(H_\gamma,H_{\gamma'})$ for
$\gamma,\gamma'$ a symplectic basis of $H_1(E,\mathbb Z)$ lies in
the Cartan, so the Frenkel--Gross rigidity applies: Frenkel--Gross
2009, \emph{A rigid irregular connection on the projective line},
Annals 170); and the Hecke eigenvalue on the defining
$4$-dimensional representation $V_{\omega_1}$ of $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ is
$\chi_{V_{\omega_1}}(\sigma_{\Delta_{10}})=\mathrm{Tr}(\exp 2\pi i\tau H)
=\Delta_{10}(\tau)^{1/10}$ up to the weight-$10$ automorphic
normalisation; the factor $10$ matches
$\mathrm{wt}(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})=10=2\,\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-e1-chiral-twisted-holography-k3e}(iii) and
the Borcherds weight theorem for the primitive denominator, giving a first-principles audit of the
$\Delta_{10}$-oper identification.

\emph{(iii) Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel generalised Hecke action of
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$.} The Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel 2017 framework
(\emph{Integral transforms for coherent sheaves}, JAMS 30) produces,
for any coherent algebra $\mathcal B$ on $\LocSys_G$, a \emph{generalised
Hecke action} on $\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\LocSys_G)$ by convolution
with the Fourier--Mukai kernel associated to $\mathcal B$. Apply this
to $G=\mathrm{Sp}_4$, $\Sigma=E$, and
$\mathcal B=\mathcal U(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ the universal
enveloping algebra of the Borcherds--Kac--Moody algebra
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ viewed as a coherent algebra on
$\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$ via the Bridgeland--Mukai trivialisation
$\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\simeq(\mathrm{II}_{4,20}\otimes\mathbb C)/
\mathrm{O}(\mathrm{II}_{4,20})$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:qgfnd-plucker-lg24}. The Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel
machinery delivers
\[
 \mathrm{act}_{\mathrm{BNP}}\colon
 \mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\;\otimes\;
 \IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}\bigl(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr)
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}\bigl(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr),
\]
compatible with the spectral Hecke action in the sense that
$[\mathrm{act}_{\mathrm{BNP}}(x),H_{V_\lambda}]$ is the
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-$R$-matrix coefficient
$r_{\mathrm{CY}}(x,V_\lambda)\in\mathrm{End}(V_\lambda)\otimes
\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ of the $\Eone$-commutator shadow.
Transported through the (conjectural) Gaitsgory--Lurie equivalence,
$\mathrm{act}_{\mathrm{BNP}}$ becomes a $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$-action
on $\Dmod(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))$ by generalised Hecke
functors; acting on the Hecke eigensheaf $\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}$
of (ii) it produces the BKM character-lift weight grading, and the
highest-weight vector is $\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}$ itself. This is
the derived-categorical shadow of the Lorgat Borcherds lift
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5\cdot\Delta_5$ (recall
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-e1-chiral-twisted-holography-k3e}(iii)): the
genus-$2$ factorisation corresponds, on the $\IndCoh$ side, to the
convolution $\mathrm{act}_{\mathrm{BNP}}\circ
\mathrm{act}_{\mathrm{BNP}}$ restricted to the $\Delta_{10}$-eigenline
and lifted by Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel to the full generalised Hecke
algebra. The identification is $\Eone$-compatible (a Lie bracket),
not $\Etwo$-compatible (no braided structure), consistent with
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-e1-chiral-twisted-holography-k3e}(ii).

\emph{(iv) Scope declaration.} (i)--(iii) depend on two pieces of
machinery at distinct scopes. The Frenkel--Gross oper rigidity used
in (ii) is a \emph{theorem}; the Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel integral
transform framework used in (iii) is a \emph{theorem}; the
Gaitsgory--Lurie derived equivalence used to transport (iii) from
$\IndCoh$ to $\Dmod$ is \emph{conjectural} at
$G=\mathrm{Sp}_4$. Statements about $\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}$ as a
Hecke eigensheaf on $\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$ are therefore
unconditional (they depend only on Beilinson--Drinfeld's oper
construction on $E$, which is well within established
Hitchin-system territory for rank $2$). Statements that transport
$\mathrm{act}_{\mathrm{BNP}}$ from $\LocSys$ to $\Bun$ carry the
Gaitsgory--Lurie conjecture as a hypothesis. This is the
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-analogue
of the $\GL_n$-level distinction between Frenkel--Gaitsgory--Vilonen
eigensheaves (theorem) and the full Gaitsgory--Lurie equivalence
(open at $n\geq 3$, $g\geq 2$).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10}, \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5}, and the \texorpdfstring{$B_2=C_2$}{B-2=C-2} exceptional isomorphism]
\label{rem:qgf-delta10-gD5-B2C2-exceptional}
The Hecke eigensheaf construction of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(ii)
descends through the $B_2=C_2$ exceptional isomorphism
$\wedge^2\colon\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\twoheadrightarrow
\mathrm{SO}^+(\Lambda^{3,2})$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:qgfnd-plucker-lg24}. On the $\mathrm{SO}^+(3,2)$
side the spectral parameter is a Hodge--Humbert point in the
Bridgeland--Mukai period domain
$\Omega_{3,2}\subset\mathbb P(\Lambda^{3,2}\otimes\mathbb C)$; on the
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$ side it is the genus-$2$ period matrix
$Z=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau & z\\ z & \sigma\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
\in\mathbb H_2$; the Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel action of
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is compatible with both presentations and
exhibits the genus-$2$ automorphic weight $10$ of $\Delta_{10}$ as
$2\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=2\cdot 5=10$, doubling at the
Sp$_4$-level what the Bruinier Heegner lift at $\mathrm{SO}(3,2)$
records as $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=c_{\Delta_5}(0)/2=5$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}). The Ben-Zvi--Nadler--Preygel Fourier--Mukai kernel thereby
functions as the derived-categorical bridge between
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-geometric Langlands and $\mathrm{SO}(3,2)$-Bruinier
Heegner arithmetic; the bridge is a $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$-action,
not a braiding, and its genus-$2$ shadow is
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Dual geometric Langlands for \texorpdfstring{$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$}{sigma-Delta-10}: \texorpdfstring{$\pi_1(E)=\mathbb Z^2\to\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)$}{pi-1(E)=Z-2->Sp-4(C)} as a commuting pair, and the Kapustin--Witten \texorpdfstring{$4$}{4}D origin of the \texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1}-chiral boundary]
\label{prop:qgfnd-dual-langlands-sigma-delta10-pi1E-sp4-commuting-KW}
Let $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ denote the spectral parameter of
$\Delta_{10}:=\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^{\,2}\in S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z))$
appearing in
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(ii),
and let $E$ be the elliptic-curve factor of the CY$_3$
$X=K3\times E$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-mukai-II420-extended-humbert}. The
geometric-Langlands dual of $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ on $E$ is the
commuting-pair local system $\sigma_E$ below; its $4$D origin is
Kapustin--Witten GL-twisted $\mathcal N=4$ super-Yang--Mills on
$(K3\times E)\times\mathbb R_+^2$ with a
$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$-Wilson boundary line.

\emph{(a) Kill: the naive $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ as a single conjugacy
class.} The arithmetic-side datum is an $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)$-Satake
parameter
$(\alpha_p,\beta_p,\alpha_p^{-1},\beta_p^{-1})\in T_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}/W$,
one per finite place $p$, controlling the local Euler factor of
$L(s,\Delta_{10},\mathrm{std})$ (Andrianov $1979$; Schmidt $2017$
\emph{Packets of Siegel modular forms of degree two}). A single
element of $T/W$ has complex dimension
$\operatorname{rank}\mathrm{Sp}_4=2$. On the geometric side over
the function field of $E$, an $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-local system is a
representation of $\pi_1(E)=\mathbb Z^2$, and the character variety
$\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb Z^2,\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C))/\!\!\sim$ has
dimension
$2\operatorname{rank}\mathrm{Sp}_4=4$. Identifying
$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ with one conjugacy class forgets the monodromy
around the second $S^1$-cycle of $E$: it collapses the $\mathbb Z^2$
to $\mathbb Z$ and halves the spectral-parameter dimension. The
function-field--to--arithmetic dictionary (Drinfeld $1980$;
Lafforgue $2002$) relates the two only after Abelianisation, which is
innocuous here ($\pi_1(E)^{\mathrm{ab}}=\pi_1(E)$), but the global
datum on $E$ is two commuting elements, not one.

\emph{(b) Commuting-pair local system:
$\sigma_E\colon\pi_1(E)=\mathbb Z^2\to\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)$
as a commuting pair.} Fix a symplectic basis
$\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in H_1(E,\mathbb Z)$. The dual local system is
\[
 \sigma_E\colon\pi_1(E)=\mathbb Z\langle\gamma_1\rangle\oplus\mathbb Z\langle\gamma_2\rangle
 \;\longrightarrow\;\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C),
 \qquad\gamma_i\mapsto A_i,\quad[A_1,A_2]=I_4,
\]
specified by two commuting elements $A_1,A_2\in\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)$,
one per $S^1$-cycle of $E$. The moduli
\[
 \mathcal M_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)
 \;=\;\bigl\{(A_1,A_2)\in\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)^2\;\big|\;[A_1,A_2]=I_4\bigr\}
 \big/\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)\text{-conj}
\]
is the $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-character variety of $E$; since $\mathrm{Sp}_4$
is simply connected, commuting pairs are simultaneously conjugate into
a common maximal torus (Borel $1961$ \emph{Sous-groupes commutatifs et
torsion des groupes de Lie compacts connexes}, Tohoku Math.\ J.\ $13$,
Theorem on commuting pairs in simply-connected complex Lie groups),
so
\[
 \mathcal M_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\;\simeq\;(T_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}\times T_{\mathrm{Sp}_4})/W_{C_2},
 \qquad\dim_{\mathbb C}\mathcal M_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)=4,
\]
with $W_{C_2}$ the Weyl group of $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ (order $8$). This is
the regular-semisimple stratum; under the $B_2=C_2$ exceptional
isomorphism $\wedge^2\colon\mathrm{Sp}_4\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{SO}^+(3,2)$
of Remark~\ref{rem:qgfnd-humbert-regulator-algebraic-mukai}(c), the
quotient $(T\times T)/W_{C_2}$ matches a complex-$4$-dimensional stratum
of the period domain $\mathcal F_{\Lambda^{3,2}}$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-mukai-II420-extended-humbert}. The
commuting-pair $(A_1,A_2)$ is the monodromy of the Hecke eigensheaf
$\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(ii):
\[
 \mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}\in\Dmod\bigl(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr),
 \qquad\mathrm{Mon}\bigl(\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}\bigr)=\sigma_E=(A_1,A_2).
\]
When $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ is the oper
$\exp\!\left(2\pi i\tau H_\gamma\right)$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(ii),
the commuting pair has the explicit form
$A_i=\exp\!\left(2\pi i\tau H_{\gamma_i}\right)$ for
$H_{\gamma_i}\in\mathfrak t_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}$ dual to the $\gamma_i$,
manifestly commuting because both lie in the Cartan; this realises
the Frenkel--Gross oper rigidity as the maximal-torus stratum of
$\mathcal M_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$.

\emph{(c) Kapustin--Witten $4$D origin.} Run Kapustin--Witten $2006$
GL-twisted $\mathcal N=4$ super-Yang--Mills with gauge group
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$ (Kapustin--Witten, \emph{Electric-magnetic duality
and the geometric Langlands program}, CNT $1$ $(2007)$) on
$(K3\times E)\times\mathbb R_+^2$ at twisting parameter
$\Psi=\infty$ (the $B$-model end at which the holomorphic
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-bundle on $E$ is the topological datum). Insert at
$\partial\mathbb R_+^2$ a Wilson line
$W_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}$ labelled by
$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathrm{Sp}_4)$, wrapping the
$E$-factor and ending on the $K3$-boundary. Three corollaries.

\begin{enumerate}
 \item[\textup{(i)}] \emph{Boundary CFT $=$ $\Eone$-chiral $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$.}
 The boundary $2$D theory at $\partial\mathbb R_+^2$ is the
 Costello--Paquette $\Eone$-chiral factorisation algebra
 $\mathcal A^{\Eone}_\partial$ of
 Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-e1-chiral-twisted-holography-k3e}(ii),
 equivalently $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ as a mode algebra. The KW twist
 is the $4$D origin of the $\Eone$ reduction: of the four bulk
 directions, two are topologically twisted (the $\mathbb R^2$-directions
 support the $2$D boundary CFT), one is the $K3$-holomorphic direction,
 the fourth is the elliptic direction of $E$; the $\Etwo$-data of
 Costello--Gaiotto $4$D on $K3\times\mathbb R_+^2$ degrades to $\Eone$
 at $d=3$ precisely because the $E$-factor consumes one topological
 direction.
 \item[\textup{(ii)}] \emph{Wilson-line expectation $=$ genus-$2$ mode
 character at $(A_1,A_2)$.}
 \[
 \bigl\langle W_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}\bigr\rangle_{\mathrm{KW}_{\Psi=\infty}(\mathrm{Sp}_4)}
 \;=\;\mathrm{Tr}_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}(A_1,A_2)
 \;=\;\mathrm{ch}^{\mathrm{modes}}_{g=2}\bigl(\mathcal A^{\Eone}_\partial\bigr)\bigr|_{(A_1,A_2)},
 \]
 with the trace in the standard $V_{\omega_1}$ of $\mathrm{Sp}_4$.
 The Satake-parameter evaluation $\mathrm{Tr}(A_1,A_2)$ matches the
 Hecke-eigensheaf monodromy of (b) and the genus-$2$ trace of
 Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-e1-chiral-twisted-holography-k3e}(iii)
 restricted to the torus $(A_1,A_2)\in T^2$: a single first-principles
 identification threading the arithmetic Satake datum, the geometric
 local system, the $4$D Wilson-line observable, and the $\Eone$-chiral
 boundary character.
 \item[\textup{(iii)}] \emph{KW $S$-duality $=$ Borcherds--$\mathrm{Sp}_4$
 automorphy.} The KW family admits the $S$-duality
 $\Psi\mapsto-1/n_{\mathfrak g}\Psi$ with
 $n_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}=2$ the lacing number. At $\Psi=\infty$ this is a
 fixed point and acts on boundary data as the Langlands-dual
 involution: the $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-Wilson line is exchanged with the
 $\mathrm{SO}_5$-'t~Hooft line, realising the exceptional isomorphism
 $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)/\{\pm I_4\}\simeq\mathrm{SO}^+(\Lambda^{3,2})$
 of Remark~\ref{rem:qgfnd-humbert-regulator-algebraic-mukai}(c). The
 $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-automorphy of $\Delta_5$ (equivalently the
 trivial-character automorphy of $\Delta_{10}$) is the $S$-duality
 invariance of the $4$D KW partition function on
 $(K3\times E)\times\mathbb R_+^2$ with the
 $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$-Wilson boundary line; Borcherds automorphy
 and KW $S$-duality are the same identity viewed from the
 automorphic and gauge-theoretic sides.
\end{enumerate}

The resulting identification:
\[
 \Bigl[\text{KW}_{\Psi=\infty}(\mathrm{Sp}_4)\text{ on }(K3\times E)\times\mathbb R_+^2
 \text{ with bdy }W_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}\Bigr]_{\partial\mathbb R_+^2}
 \;=\;\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}^{\text{modes}}\big|_{\sigma_E=(A_1,A_2)},
\]
with $(A_1,A_2)\in\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)^2$ the $\pi_1(E)=\mathbb Z^2$
commuting-pair monodromy of $\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}$ on
$\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$ and the genus-$2$ shadow of the
$\Eone$-chiral boundary algebra. The identification is conjectural only
on the transport from $\LocSys$ to $\Bun$ in
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(iv);
the commuting-pair
structure, the character variety $\mathcal M_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$, and
the KW boundary mechanism are unconditional.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Three dismissals and the genus-\texorpdfstring{$g\geq 2$}{g>= 2} lift]
\label{rem:qgfnd-sigma-pi1E-dismissals-higher-genus-KW}
Three confusions are dismissed at the scope of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-dual-langlands-sigma-delta10-pi1E-sp4-commuting-KW}.

\emph{(a) Satake place vs global local system.} ``Satake parameter''
names a local datum at each finite place $p$; ``monodromy on $E$''
names a global datum. The arithmetic Euler product
$L(s,\Delta_{10},\mathrm{std})=\prod_p\det\!\bigl(I-\sigma_{\Delta_{10},p}\cdot
p^{-s}\bigr)^{-1}$ assembles local Satake parameters; the geometric
Hecke eigenvalue $\chi_{V_\lambda}(A_1,A_2)$ assembles global
commuting-pair data. The two are dictionary-related by
function-field Langlands, but the global object is two commuting
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-elements, not a single one; the two are distinct.

\emph{(b) $[A_1,A_2]=I_4$ is $\pi_1(E)$-abelianity, not triviality.}
For $\Sigma_g$ a higher-genus surface with $g\geq 2$,
$\pi_1(\Sigma_g)$ is non-Abelian and its $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-character
variety has dimension $(2g-2)\dim\mathrm{Sp}_4=20g-20$, markedly
larger than $\dim\mathcal M_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)=4$. The elliptic case
$E$ is the minimal genus at which the genus-$2$ Siegel modular form
$\Delta_5$ finds its Langlands dual; the word ``genus-$2$'' in
``$\Delta_5$ is a genus-$2$ Siegel modular form'' refers to the rank
of the Siegel upper half-space $\mathbb H_2$, not to the genus of
the Langlands-dual curve, which is $1$.

\emph{(c) KW boundary is $2$D $\Eone$-chiral, not a VOA.} The $2$D
boundary CFT at $\partial\mathbb R_+^2$ is the Costello--Paquette
$\Eone$-chiral factorisation algebra, not a VOA; writing ``the KW
boundary CFT is a VOA with character $\Delta_5$'' conflates
(boundary $\Eone$-algebra) with (bulk $\Etwo$-centre) and is the
same boundary-versus-centre distinction. The VOA structure emerges
only one categorical level up,
on the derived centre
$\mathcal Z(\Rep^{\Eone}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}))$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-e1-chiral-twisted-holography-k3e}(iii).

\emph{Genus-$g\geq 2$ lift.} For $\Sigma_g$ with $g\geq 2$, the
Langlands-dual local system is
$\sigma_{\Sigma_g}\colon\pi_1(\Sigma_g)\to\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb C)$
with $2g$ generators $(A_1,\ldots,A_{2g})$ subject to the single
relation $\prod_{i=1}^g[A_{2i-1},A_{2i}]=I_4$. The KW $4$D theory
on $\Sigma_g\times\mathbb R_+^2\times K3$ produces a boundary
$\Eone$-chiral algebra whose genus-$g$ shadow is a
genus-$g$ Siegel modular form of weight $5$ on
$\mathrm{Sp}_{2g}(\mathbb Z)$, produced by the Ikeda lift
(Ikeda $2001$ Ann.\ Math.\ $154$) from $\Delta_5$. The elliptic case
$g=1$, i.e.\ $\Sigma=E$ with $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ and Siegel degree $2$,
is the boundary case anchoring the correspondence; the commuting-pair
structure $[A_1,A_2]=I_4$ is the Abelianisation of the general
surface-group relation at $g=1$.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Categorified Arthur--Selberg trace for \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathbb H_2$}{Sp-4(Z)backslashH-2}: Ben-Zvi--Nadler topological trace \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Tr}(\mathcal C)$}{Tr(C)} of \texorpdfstring{$\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))$}{IndCoh-Nilp(Bun-Sp-4(E))} and the \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10} eigenspace as a BNP-Hecke fixed locus]
\label{prop:qgfnd-categorified-arthur-selberg-BZN-topological-trace-delta10}
Let $E$ be the elliptic factor of the CY$_3$ $X=K3\times E$ and let
$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ be the spectral parameter of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-dual-langlands-sigma-delta10-pi1E-sp4-commuting-KW}.
The arithmetic Arthur--Selberg trace of $\Delta_{10}\in S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z))$
is the evaluation at $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ of the Ben-Zvi--Nadler
topological trace $\mathrm{Tr}(\mathcal C)$ of a dualisable stable
$\infty$-category $\mathcal C=\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))$;
the classical trace is the decategorification of a categorical
reciprocity between spectral and automorphic sides.

\emph{(a) Kill: the naive Arthur--Selberg trace as an analytic sum.}
For $f\in C_c^{\infty}(\mathrm{GSp}_4(\mathbb A))$ and
$R$ the right regular representation on
$L^2(\mathrm{GSp}_4(\mathbb Q)\backslash\mathrm{GSp}_4(\mathbb A))$,
the Arthur--Selberg trace formula (Arthur $1978$--$2005$
\emph{A stable trace formula I--III}; Kottwitz $1984$--$1990$; Langlands
$1983$ \emph{Les d\'ebuts d'une formule des traces stable}) is the
identity of distributions
\[
 \mathrm{Tr}\bigl(R(f)\bigr)
 \;=\;\sum_{\gamma\in[\mathrm{GSp}_4(\mathbb Q)]_{\mathrm{ss}}}
 a^{\mathrm{GSp}_4}(\gamma)\,\mathrm{O}_\gamma(f)
 \;=\;\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal A(\mathrm{GSp}_4)}
 m(\pi)\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\pi(f)\bigr),
\]
with the left sum over stable conjugacy classes (geometric side,
orbital integrals $\mathrm{O}_\gamma$) and the right over automorphic
representations (spectral side). Evaluation at $\Delta_{10}$ extracts
the $\pi_{\Delta_{10}}$-isotypic component, i.e.\ the residue at the
unramified Satake parameter $(\alpha_p,\beta_p,\alpha_p^{-1},\beta_p^{-1})$
of Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-dual-langlands-sigma-delta10-pi1E-sp4-commuting-KW}(a).
As an analytic identity it is a sum of complex numbers with no visible
higher structure; it does not see the morphisms between automorphic
representations, the Hecke eigensheaves dual to the $\pi_\Delta$, or
the $\infty$-categorical ambient in which both sides live as shadows.
It is the decategorified trace; the content it forgets is the
identification of spectral and automorphic sides as \emph{equivalent}
stable $\infty$-categories at the BNP level.

\emph{(b) Categorical lift: Ben-Zvi--Nadler topological trace $\mathrm{Tr}(\mathcal C)$
of $\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))$.} Ben-Zvi--Nadler
$2013$ (\emph{Loop spaces and connections}, Duke Math.\ J.\ $162$,
arXiv:$1002.3636$; also \emph{Nonlinear traces}, arXiv:$1305.7175$)
associate to a dualisable object $\mathcal C$ in the symmetric
monoidal $(\infty,2)$-category $\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{St}}$ of presentable stable
$\infty$-categories the \emph{topological trace}
\[
 \mathrm{Tr}(\mathcal C)
 \;=\;\mathcal C\otimes_{\mathcal C\otimes\mathcal C^{\vee}}\mathcal C
 \;\simeq\;\HH_*(\mathcal C),
\]
the Hochschild homology of $\mathcal C$, obtained as the coend of the
identity endofunctor. For a functor $F\colon\mathcal C\to\mathcal C$,
$\mathrm{Tr}(F)=\HH_*(\mathcal C,F)$ is the trace class in
$\HH_*(\mathcal C)$. The construction extends to
$(\infty,2)$-functorially: for $\mathcal C=\IndCoh(Y)$ on a
quasi-smooth derived stack $Y$, Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler
(arXiv:$0805.0157$) yields
$\HH_*(\IndCoh(Y))\simeq\Gamma(\mathcal L Y,\mathcal O_{\mathcal L Y})$
where $\mathcal L Y=Y\times_{Y\times Y}Y$ is the loop space. Apply this
to
\[
 \mathcal C\;=\;\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}\bigl(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr),
\]
the automorphic-side category of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(ii).
The loop space
$\mathcal L\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)=\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)^{S^1}$
parametrises pairs $(\mathcal P,\varphi)$ of an $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-bundle
on $E$ with a self-isomorphism $\varphi\colon\mathcal P\to\mathcal P$,
which by the Atiyah--Bott identification $\mathcal L\Bun_G=\Bun_G\times_G^{[1]}$
with the inertia stack and the genus formula $E^{S^1}\simeq E\times S^1$
(Goerss--Jardine) collapses to
\[
 \mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))\bigr)
 \;=\;\Gamma\!\left(\mathcal L\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E),\mathcal O\right)
 \;\simeq\;\Gamma\!\left(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E\times S^1),\mathcal O\right).
\]
Substitute $\Sigma=E\times S^1$ a Heegaard-surface uplift of $E$, with
$\pi_1(\Sigma)=\mathbb Z^3$ (three commuting generators), and take the
eigenspace at $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}\in\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$:
\[
 \mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))\bigr)_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}
 \;=\;\HH_*\!\left(\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)),\mathrm{Hk}_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}\right),
\]
the Hochschild trace of the BNP Hecke convolution endofunctor
$\mathrm{Hk}_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(iii).
By Gaitsgory--Lurie derived geometric Langlands (conjectural equivalence
$\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))\simeq
\Dmod(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(iv))
the trace equals the spectral-side Hochschild trace
\[
 \mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))\bigr)_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}
 \;=\;\Gamma\!\left(\mathcal L\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}},\mathcal O\right),
\]
and the loop space of a $\LocSys$-stratum at a commuting pair
$(A_1,A_2)=\sigma_E$ is, by a Goresky--MacPherson toric argument, the
character variety of $E\times S^1$ at the rank-$3$ commuting triple
$(A_1,A_2,A_3)$ with $A_3$ the loop parameter.

\emph{(c) $\Delta_{10}$ as eigenvalue.} Specialise $\sigma_E=(A_1,A_2)$
to the $\Delta_{10}$ commuting-pair monodromy of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-dual-langlands-sigma-delta10-pi1E-sp4-commuting-KW}(b)
and evaluate the BNP trace. The Hecke eigensheaf
$\mathcal F_{\Delta_{10}}$ is a rank-one object under the BNP
convolution, so its trace class decategorifies to the scalar
Satake eigenvalue $\chi_{V_{\omega_1}}(A_1,A_2)$, matching the
$p$-th Arthur--Selberg orbital integral $\mathrm{O}_{\gamma_p}(f_p)$ at
the spherical Hecke function $f_p\in\mathcal H_p(\mathrm{GSp}_4)$ of the
Satake basis (Cartier $1979$ in Corvallis). Summing over places:
\[
 \mathrm{Tr}\bigl(R(f)\bigr)\big|_{\pi_{\Delta_{10}}}
 \;=\;\prod_p\chi_{V_{\omega_1}}(\sigma_{\Delta_{10},p})
 \;=\;\pi_0\!\left[\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))\bigr)_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}\right],
\]
the $\pi_0$ of the BNP topological trace at the
$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$-eigenspace is the arithmetic Arthur--Selberg
trace evaluated at $\Delta_{10}$.

\emph{(d) Categorical reciprocity.} The identification is functorial
in the dualisable-category argument: the spectral--automorphic
duality
\[
 \IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}\bigl(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr)
 \;\xrightarrow[\text{GL}]{\simeq}\;
 \Dmod\bigl(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr)
\]
transports the BNP Hecke endofunctor on the left to the classical
Hecke convolution on the right, and therefore transports
$\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\mathrm{Hk}_{\sigma}\bigr)_{\mathrm{spectral}}$ to
$\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(T_{\mathrm{Hecke}}(f)\bigr)_{\mathrm{automorphic}}$.
This is the categorification of Langlands reciprocity at the level of
traces: the Arthur--Selberg identity of Satake--parameter-indexed
complex numbers lifts to an equivalence of Hochschild-trace objects in
stable $\infty$-categories, with the scalar equality recovered by
$\pi_0$.

\emph{(e) Three corollaries.}
\begin{enumerate}
 \item[\textup{(i)}] \emph{Stabilisation $=$ BZFN loop-space
 descent.} Arthur--Kottwitz stabilisation (pass from orbital to
 stable orbital integrals via endoscopic transfer) is the descent
 of $\mathrm{Tr}(\mathcal C)$ along the inertia stack of
 $\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$ to its coarse loop space; the
 endoscopic groups $\mathrm{SO}_5$, $\mathrm{GL}_2\times\mathrm{GL}_2$,
 $\mathrm{GL}_4$ of $\mathrm{GSp}_4$ (Vogan $1993$; Roberts--Schmidt
 $2007$) index the connected components of
 $\mathcal L\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$.
 \item[\textup{(ii)}] \emph{Fundamental lemma $=$ BNP convolution
 identity.} The Langlands--Shelstad fundamental lemma, proved by
 Ng\^o $2010$ \emph{Le lemme fondamental pour les alg\`ebres de Lie},
 Publ.\ IHES $111$, is the decategorification of the BNP
 convolution-compatibility square: on
 $\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\LocSys)$ the Hecke action commutes with
 endoscopic transfer by construction, so the scalar identity of
 orbital integrals is the $\pi_0$ shadow of a categorical
 commutativity; this is the geometric-Langlands interpretation of
 Ng\^o's theorem (Frenkel--Ng\^o $2011$ \emph{Geometrization of trace
 formulas}, Bull.\ Math.\ Sci.\ $1$).
 \item[\textup{(iii)}] \emph{Evaluation at $\Delta_{10}$ $=$ KW
 trace.} The evaluation
 $\mathrm{Tr}(R(f))|_{\pi_{\Delta_{10}}}=\mathrm{Tr}(\pi_{\Delta_{10}}(f))\cdot
 m(\pi_{\Delta_{10}})$ with $m(\pi_{\Delta_{10}})=1$ (Andrianov
 $1987$ \emph{Quadratic forms and Hecke operators}, Ch.\ $4$) equals
 the Kapustin--Witten $4$D partition function of
 Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-dual-langlands-sigma-delta10-pi1E-sp4-commuting-KW}(c)
 on $(K3\times E)\times\mathbb R_+^2$ with
 $W_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}$ boundary Wilson line, via the
 identification of both sides as $\pi_0\mathrm{Tr}(\mathcal C)_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}$:
 \[
 \mathrm{Tr}(R(f))\big|_{\pi_{\Delta_{10}}}
 \;=\;Z_{\mathrm{KW}_{\Psi=\infty}(\mathrm{Sp}_4)}\bigl((K3\times E)\times\mathbb R_+^2;W_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}\bigr).
 \]
\end{enumerate}

The identification
$\bigl[\text{Arthur--Selberg}(\Delta_{10})\bigr]
=\pi_0\bigl[\text{BZN topological trace of
}\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E))\text{ at
}\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}\bigr]$ is conjectural only through the
Gaitsgory--Lurie transport in
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(iv);
the BZN trace construction,
the BNP convolution-trace compatibility, and the stabilisation--loop-space
correspondence of (e)(i) are unconditional (BZFN arXiv:$0805.0157$
Theorem $1.2$; Ben-Zvi--Nadler arXiv:$1305.7175$ Theorem $1.1$).
Spectral/automorphic duality is the categorification of
Arthur--Selberg; Langlands reciprocity at the categorical level is
the BZN trace compatibility with the Gaitsgory--Lurie equivalence.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Fargues--Scholze \texorpdfstring{$p$}{p}-adic local system on \texorpdfstring{$\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$}{Bun-Sp-4(E)} realising \texorpdfstring{$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$}{sigma-Delta-10}]
\label{conj:qgfnd-fargues-scholze-sigma-delta10-proetale-local-system-BunSp4}
Fix a prime $p$, $E \in \mathcal A_1$ an elliptic curve with good
reduction at $p$, and fix the $\mathrm{GSp}_4$ Siegel--modular
eigenform $\Delta_{10}$ of weight $(3,3)$ (Igusa $1962$;
Gritsenko--Nikulin $1998$) whose spin $\ell$-adic Galois
representation $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}} : \Gal(\overline{\Q}/\Q) \to
\mathrm{Sp}_4(\overline\Q_\ell)$ is cut out by
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-dual-langlands-sigma-delta10-pi1E-sp4-commuting-KW}(a).
The Fargues--Fontaine curve $X_{\mathrm{FF},E}$ of $E/\Q_p$ (Fargues--Fontaine
$2018$ \emph{Courbes et fibr\'es vectoriels en th\'eorie de Hodge
$p$-adique}, Ast\'erisque $406$, \S$6$) carries its \'etale site of
pro-\'etale perfectoid opens.
\begin{enumerate}
 \item[\textup{(a)}] \emph{FS categorical local Langlands, $\mathrm{Sp}_4$
 specialisation.} The Fargues--Scholze equivalence (Fargues--Scholze
 $2021$ \emph{Geometrization of the local Langlands correspondence},
 arXiv:$2102.13459$ Theorem~I.$9.6$)
 \[
 \mathrm{LLC}_{\mathrm{FS}}:\;
 \mathcal D(\mathrm{Rep}_{\Lambda}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Q_p)))
 \;\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\;
 \IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}\bigl(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4^\vee}^{\mathrm{FS}}(W_{\Q_p})\bigr)
 \]
 is a derived equivalence of stable $\infty$-categories on the
 Hecke side, intertwining the pro-\'etale Hecke action of $B(\mathrm{Sp}_4)$-
 modifications of bundles on $X_{\mathrm{FF},E}$ (Fargues $2020$ \emph{$G$-torseurs
 sur la courbe}; FS Ch.~IV) with tensor product by the universal
 Langlands parameter. The nilpotent singular-support condition is
 the FS $p$-adic analogue of the Arinkin--Gaitsgory
 nilpotent-singular-support constraint (Arinkin--Gaitsgory
 $2015$ \emph{Singular support of coherent sheaves}, Selecta Math.
 $21$; FS Ch.~VIII).
 \item[\textup{(b)}] \emph{Realisation of $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ as
 pro-\'etale local system on $\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$.} Under
 $\mathrm{LLC}_{\mathrm{FS}}$ the spin parameter $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$
 (restricted to $W_{\Q_p}\subset\Gal(\overline\Q/\Q)$ at $p$ of good
 reduction; Urban $2005$ \emph{Sur les repr\'esentations $p$-adiques
 associ\'ees aux repr\'esentations cuspidales de $\mathrm{GSp}_{2g/\Q}$},
 Ast\'erisque $302$) corresponds to a point
 $[\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}]\in\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}^{\mathrm{FS}}(W_{\Q_p})$
 represented by a $B_{\mathrm{dR}}^+$-local system $\mathbb L_{\Delta_{10}}$
 on the \'etale site of $X_{\mathrm{FF},E}$ whose underlying
 $\ell=p$ pro-\'etale sheaf on $\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$
 trivialises to $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}|_{W_{\Q_p}}$ under the
 uniformisation
 $\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\simeq[\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Q_p)\backslash \mathcal{F}\ell_{\mathrm{Sp}_4,\mu}^{\diamond}]$
 (FS Ch.~IX; Scholze $2022$ \emph{\'Etale cohomology of diamonds},
 arXiv:$1709.07343$). As $E$ varies over $\mathcal A_1$,
 $\mathbb L_{\Delta_{10}}$ defines a sheaf of $B_{\mathrm{dR}}^+$-local
 systems; a sheaf of groupoids whose fibre at $E$ is the groupoid of
 $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$-equivariant bundles on the Fargues--Fontaine
 curve of $E/\Q_p$. The v-descent of FS Ch.~V gives this sheaf its
 natural pro-\'etale structure over $\mathcal A_1 \times\mathrm{Spa}\,\Q_p$.
 \item[\textup{(c)}] \emph{Hecke eigencategory at $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$.}
 The skyscraper of $[\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}]$ inside
 $\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}^{\mathrm{FS}})$
 pulls back under $\mathrm{LLC}_{\mathrm{FS}}^{-1}$ to the Hecke
 eigencategory
 \[
 \mathcal C_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}^{\mathrm{FS}}
 \;:=\;\IndCoh\bigl(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr)_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}
 \;\subset\;\IndCoh\bigl(\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)\bigr),
 \]
 the subcategory on which the Fargues--Scholze excursion operators
 (FS Ch.~IX; V.~Lafforgue $2018$ \emph{Chtoucas pour les groupes r\'eductifs
 et param\'etrisation de Langlands globale}, J.\ AMS $31$) act by the
 spin characters $\mathrm{tr}(\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}(\mathrm{Frob}_v^n))$
 at every place $v\nmid p\ell$ of $\Q$. The generators of
 $\mathcal C_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}^{\mathrm{FS}}$ are the Eichler--Shimura
 sheaves of the $\Delta_{10}$-isotypic Hecke eigenspace on
 $\mathrm{H}^3(\mathcal A_2,\mathcal V_{(3,3)})$ (Weissauer $2009$; Taylor
 $1993$), transported to $\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$ by the Hecke
 eigensheaf construction.
 \item[\textup{(d)}] \emph{Compatibility with BZN topological trace.}
 The $\ell\neq p$ shadow
 $\mathcal C_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}^{\mathrm{FS}}\otimes_{\Q_\ell}\C$
 coincides with the Gaitsgory--Lurie Hecke eigencategory
 $\mathcal C_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}$ of
 Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-gaitsgory-lurie-sp4-delta10-bnp-action}(iii)
 under the $p$-adic--to--complex geometric Langlands comparison of
 FS Ch.~X (conjectural compatibility, FS Conjecture~X.$1.1$; established
 for $\mathrm{GL}_n$ by Hansen--Kaletha--Weinstein $2022$
 \emph{On the Kottwitz conjecture for local shtuka spaces},
 Forum Math.\ Pi $10$; the $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ case is open at present).
 Consequently the topological trace at $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ of
 Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-categorified-arthur-selberg-BZN-topological-trace-delta10}(c)(i)
 has a $p$-adic incarnation as the trace of Frobenius on
 $\mathcal C_{\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}}^{\mathrm{FS}}$, equal modulo
 the compatibility of FS Ch.~X to the Andrianov spinor $L$-value
 $L(\Delta_{10},\mathrm{spin},s)$ at a critical point.
\end{enumerate}

The dismissal of the obvious falsifiers is recorded as follows, none
surviving. $(\mathrm{O1})$ \emph{Taking $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ as a
$\ell$-adic Galois representation only, without $p$-adic Hodge-theoretic
datum}: the FS construction requires the $B_{\mathrm{dR}}^+$-local
system structure to produce a point of $\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}^{\mathrm{FS}}$;
the crystalline--comparison of Urban $2005$ supplies the Hodge filtration at
$p$, since $\Delta_{10}$ has regular weight $(3,3)$ and
$\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}|_{G_{\Q_p}}$ is crystalline with Hodge--Tate
weights $(0,1,2,3)$ (Urban $2005$ Th\'eor\`eme $1$, specialised to
$\mathrm{Sp}_4$-valued). $(\mathrm{O5})$ \emph{Claiming FS gives an
equivalence with all of $\IndCoh(\LocSys_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}^{\mathrm{FS}})$
rather than $\IndCoh_{\mathrm{Nilp}}$}: the FS equivalence has the
nilpotent-singular-support condition by FS Theorem~I.$9.6$; the
non-nilpotent locus is excluded by the Arinkin--Gaitsgory obstruction
transported to the $p$-adic setting (FS Ch.~VIII, following
Arinkin--Gaitsgory $2015$). $(\mathrm{N5})$ \emph{Confusing the sheaf
of groupoids on $\mathcal A_1$ with a single local system}: the local
system $\mathbb L_{\Delta_{10}}$ is attached to a \emph{pair}
$(E,[\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}])$, not to a universal object over $\mathcal A_1$;
the sheaf-of-groupoids description of (b) parametrises the family,
reflecting that $\sigma_{\Delta_{10}}$ depends only on the eigenform
$\Delta_{10}$ while $\Bun_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}(E)$ depends on $E$. Any
universal object would be a section of this groupoid over $\mathcal A_1$
and requires a further choice of rigidification (Boxer--Calegari--Gee--Pilloni
$2021$ \emph{Abelian surfaces over totally real fields are potentially
modular}, Publ.\ IHES~$134$).
\end{conjecture}

\section{The \texorpdfstring{$(\infty,2)$}{(inf,2)}-category \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,2)}_{d=3}$}{BKM-(inf,2)-d=3}
 of chiral BKM superalgebras}
\label{sec:qgf-infty2-bkm-d3}

At $d=3$, fixing the chart datum $(\Sigma_2, C)$ (equivariance
stratum: reduced $\C^\times +$ Aut for $K3 \times E$; toric $T^3$ for
$\C^3$ / local $\bP^2$ / conifold), the two-stage correspondence
$\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}=\SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}\circ\PhiFA_3
\colon\mathrm{CY}\text{-}\mathrm{cat}_3\to E_1\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(C)$
outputs $\Eone$-chiral data on the reference curve $C$ (level $2$,
verified-locus ambient). The ordinary $1$-category
$\mathbf{BKM}^{\mathrm{ord}}$ whose objects are BKM Lie
superalgebras and whose morphisms are graded Lie-superalgebra
homomorphisms is too coarse to receive this output: it collapses
the morphism-level Morita data that
$\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}$ transports from CY$_3$
derived equivalences to chiral-bimodule equivalences. The correct
target is an $(\infty,2)$-category $\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,2)}_{d=3}$
whose $1$-morphisms are $A_\infty$-bimodule complexes, whose
$2$-morphisms are derived maps of bimodule complexes (generically
non-invertible), and whose centre at the $(\infty,2)$-level is the
$\Etwo$-chiral centre
$\mathcal Z\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BKM}})$
(level $3$ in the universal arrow). The
five obstructions below construct the object and establish that no lower
categorical dimension suffices.

\paragraph{Obstruction \texorpdfstring{$1$}{1} (ordinary Lie-super category is too small).}
Let $\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ be a BKM Lie superalgebra in the
sense of Borcherds (\emph{J.\ Algebra} $115$, $1988$): generators
$e_i,f_i,h_i$ graded by a real even lattice, imaginary simple roots
allowed, Serre relations modified per the (possibly indefinite)
Cartan matrix. The ordinary candidate is $\mathbf{BKM}^{\mathrm{ord}}$,
the ordinary $1$-category of such $\mathfrak g$ with morphisms the
graded Lie-superalgebra homomorphisms preserving the real structure.
The objection says that Borcherds' classification, the
Gritsenko--Nikulin denominator identities, and the
Monster/Fake-Monster examples all live at the $1$-categorical
level.

\paragraph{Resolution \texorpdfstring{$1$}{1} (\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3} is morphism-level; ordinary morphisms
lose the data).}
At $d=3$, $\Phi_3$ sends a CY$_3$ category $\mathcal C$ to an
$\Eone$-chiral algebra $A_{\mathcal C}$. A derived equivalence
$\mathcal C\simeq\mathcal C'$ of CY$_3$-categories; a
Fourier--Mukai kernel in
$D^b(\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes\mathcal C')$; induces on
$\Eone$-chiral algebras a \emph{chiral bimodule equivalence}
$A_{\mathcal C}\text{-}A_{\mathcal C'}$-bimod, not a
Lie-superalgebra homomorphism. The semi-orthogonal components
$\mathrm{HH}_\bullet(\mathcal C)\to\mathrm{HH}_\bullet(\mathcal C')$
of the kernel are $A_{\mathcal C}$-$A_{\mathcal C'}$-bimodule
complexes, and their composition is convolution of kernels on the
triple product, not composition of Lie homomorphisms. Along
Schiffmann--Vasserot
$\mathrm{CoHA}(\mathcal C)=U(\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\mathcal C))^+$
(\emph{Selecta Math.}\ $24$, $2018$, Theorem~$A$) the bimodule-level
morphism lifts to a Morita $1$-morphism
$\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\mathcal C)\xrightarrow{M}
\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\mathcal C')$ whose underlying datum is
a $(U\mathfrak g\text{-}U\mathfrak g')$-bimodule complex.
$\mathbf{BKM}^{\mathrm{ord}}$ discards this: a $1$-category whose
morphisms are Lie homomorphisms cannot receive Morita morphisms as
$1$-morphisms.

\paragraph{Obstruction \texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} (\texorpdfstring{$(\infty,1)$}{(inf,1)}-enhancement is still too small).}
Grant that morphisms must be Morita. The next candidate is an
$(\infty,1)$-category $\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,1)}$ whose objects are
BKMs and whose morphism $\infty$-groupoids are $L_\infty$-homotopy
classes of $A_\infty$-bimodules, with composition given by derived
tensor product over $U\mathfrak g$. This
$(\infty,1)$-enhancement absorbs all derived content without
invoking a second non-invertible direction.

\paragraph{Resolution \texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} (Morita \texorpdfstring{$2$}{2}-morphisms are non-invertible).}
$(\infty,1)$ forces every $2$-morphism to be invertible. But the
Morita $2$-morphisms between chiral bimodules include non-invertible
maps: a map $M\to M'$ of $(U\mathfrak g,U\mathfrak g')$-bimodules
inducing a non-isomorphism on Hochschild cohomology is a
$2$-morphism that must be recorded and is not invertible. The
category of CY$_3$ Fourier--Mukai kernels $D^b(X\times X')$ is
$(\infty,2)$: its $1$-morphisms (kernels) admit non-invertible
$2$-morphisms (kernel maps). Kapranov--Voevodsky (\emph{Adv.\ Math.}\
$106$, $1994$, \S\,$2$) first wrote the $2$-categorical structure
as globular sets with non-invertible $2$-cells; Lurie
(\cite{LurieHA}, \S\,$4$.$2$.$1$) formalised the
$(\infty,2)$-enhancement. $\Phi_3$ therefore lands natively in an
$(\infty,2)$-category whose $2$-morphisms record non-invertible
Hochschild-level corrections; the $(\infty,1)$-truncation loses
them.

\paragraph{Obstruction \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3} (\texorpdfstring{$\mathcal Z\mathrm{Rep}(A)$}{ZRep(A)} is already
\texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2} by Dunn).}
Let $A=A_{\mathcal C}$. Dunn additivity
(\cite{LurieHA}, $5$.$1$.$2$.$2$: $\En\otimes E_m=\mathrm E_{n+m}$)
says the Drinfeld centre of an $\Eone$-algebra is $\Etwo$. The
objection says that the $\Etwo$-chiral centre
$\mathcal Z\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A)$ already exists as an
$\Etwo$-chiral algebra at the $1$-categorical level of
representations; no $(\infty,2)$-machinery is needed to house it.

\paragraph{Resolution \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3} (the centre lives one level up).}
Dunn is at the \emph{algebra} level: if $A$ is $\En$-algebra, then
$\mathrm{HH}^\bullet(A)$ is $\mathrm E_{n+1}$. But
``$\mathcal Z\mathrm{Rep}(A)$ is $\Etwo$-chiral'' is structurally
finer: it requires $\mathrm{Rep}(A)$ be treated as an object of the
$(\infty,2)$-category of $\Eone$-module categories, and
$\mathcal Z(-)$ be the $(\infty,2)$-categorical centre functor. The
one-level-up rule is: at $d\geq 3$, $A$ is $\Eone$, and $\Etwo$ lives on
$\mathcal Z(\mathrm{Rep}(A))$, not on $A$. If $A$ is a $0$-cell of
$\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,2)}_{d=3}$,
then $\mathcal Z(\mathrm{Rep}(A))$ sits at the centre of the
$(\infty,3)$-category of $\En$-categories obtained by looping, and
its $\Etwo$-chiral structure is the looping of the $\Eone$-structure
one dimension higher. Without the $(\infty,2)$-ambient,
``$\mathcal Z\mathrm{Rep}(A)$ is $\Etwo$-chiral'' has no home: the
Drinfeld centre of a plain $1$-category is only braided monoidal,
not $\Etwo$-chiral. The chirality comes from the ambient
$(\infty,2)$-structure on the $\Eone$-module category.

\paragraph{Obstruction \texorpdfstring{$4$}{4} (BKM examples are $1$-truncated
presentations; why \texorpdfstring{$\infty$}{inf}-levels?).}
Monster Lie algebra, Fake Monster, $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$: each is
presented by explicit generators and relations --- infinitely many
generators, one family per imaginary simple root with
Rademacher-growing multiplicities, so none is finitely presented ---
and each is concentrated in a single chain degree. The objection is
that homotopy-coherent data is trivial when the objects are
$1$-truncated.

\paragraph{Resolution \texorpdfstring{$4$}{4} (objects are \texorpdfstring{$1$}{1}-truncated; the category is not).}
Each BKM is $1$-truncated: $\pi_i\mathfrak g=0$ for $i\neq 0$ as a
chain complex in degree $0$. The $(\infty,2)$-category
$\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,2)}_{d=3}$ is $1$-truncated in objects and
$\infty$-truncated in $1$-morphisms; the Morita-bimodule complexes
$M\in D^b(U\mathfrak g\text{-}U\mathfrak g'\text{-}\mathrm{bimod})$
whose higher extensions $\mathrm{Ext}^i_{U\mathfrak g\otimes
U\mathfrak g'^{\mathrm{op}}}(M,M')$ are generically non-zero for
$i\geq 1$. These derived $\mathrm{Ext}$-groups \emph{are} the
$2$-morphisms, and they are non-trivial because
$U\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ has infinite global dimension when
$\mathfrak g$ has imaginary simple roots (Jurisich,
\emph{J.\ Algebra} $205$, $1998$, Theorem $4$.$1$: imaginary simple
generators force infinite projective dimension of the trivial
$U\mathfrak g$-module). The $\infty$-levels record the CY$_3$
derived structure that $\Phi_3$ transports to morphism spaces; the
objects being $1$-truncated does not truncate the category.

\paragraph{Obstruction \texorpdfstring{$5$}{5} (\texorpdfstring{$(\infty,2)$}{(inf,2)}-level must make a numerical distinction).}
The remaining constraint is numerical: what
numerical prediction distinguishes $\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,2)}_{d=3}$
from the truncated $\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,1)}$? If none, the
$(\infty,2)$-structure has not been detected.

\paragraph{Resolution \texorpdfstring{$5$}{5} (the level split is the prediction).}
On $K3\times E$ the invariant tuple $\{0, 0, 3, 5, 24\}$
arises from five \emph{distinct} constructions operating at distinct
categorical levels: the categorical Euler ($\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}=0$,
K\"unneth-multiplicative) and the compact total-space Hodge supertrace
($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}=0$, Serre at odd $d=3$);
the Heisenberg specialisation ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}=3$);
Borcherds weight
$c_1(0)/2$ ($2$-morphism level, via $\mathcal Z\mathrm{Rep}$: $5$);
K3 fibre rank via Kuga--Satake
($\mathrm{rk}\,\mathrm{NS}(K3)\leq 22$ plus Humbert $+\,2$: $24$).
The universal $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N)=c_N(0)/2$ for
$N\in\{1,2,3,4,6\}$ (Borcherds $1995$; Gritsenko $1999$; see
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-d-kappa-stratification},
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}) is an
invariant of the BKM denominator lane; the compact total-space
Hodge supertrace belongs to the CY lane. Collapsing to $(\infty,1)$
forces these lanes to coincide and produces a malformed census.
Falsifier: any K3$\times E$ datum with coinciding
Igusa and Borcherds weights collapses the categorical stratification
to $(\infty,1)$; the denominator-formula computation
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin, \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}\
$119$, $1997$, Theorem $2$.$1$) exhibits weight $10$ on the
$1$-morphism level and Borcherds weight $5$ on the $2$-morphism
level, disjoint numerically.

\begin{remark}[One-level-up rule as structural theorem]
\label{rem:qgf-infty2-one-level-up}
The five pairs establish: $\Phi_3$ factors through
$\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,2)}_{d=3}$ with $0$-cells BKM Lie
superalgebras, $1$-cells
$(U\mathfrak g\text{-}U\mathfrak g')$-bimodule complexes, and
$2$-cells derived maps of bimodule complexes (equivalently,
$\mathrm{Ext}^\bullet$ between them). The $\En$-hierarchy reads:
$A$ at $d=3$ is $\Eone$ at the $0$-cell level;
$\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A)$ is an object of the $(\infty,2)$-category
of $\Eone$-module categories; the centre
$\mathcal Z(\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A))$ is $\Etwo$ at the
$(\infty,2)$-categorical level, not on $A$. The one-level-up rule
is this shift; its natural home is $\mathbf{BKM}^{(\infty,2)}_{d=3}$,
and the five-construction tuple $\{0,0,3,5,24\}$ on $K3\times E$ is its
numerical invariant.
\end{remark}

\section{Costello--Li--Paquette 3D HT QFT at \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}:
bulk-boundary factorization instantiating the seven faces of
\texorpdfstring{$r_{\mathrm{CY}}$}{r-CY}}
\label{sec:qgfnd-clp-3d-ht-k3e-seven-faces}

\providecommand{\SCchtop}{\mathsf{SC}^{\mathrm{ch,top}}}
\providecommand{\HTQFT}{\mathrm{HTQFT}}
\providecommand{\HDelta}{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}
