\providecommand{\Obsbd}{\mathrm{Obs}^{q,\partial}}
\providecommand{\Obsbulk}{\mathrm{Obs}^{q,\mathrm{cl}}}

\paragraph{Boundary obstruction.} The object $X_6 := K3\times E$ is compact
Calabi--Yau of real dimension $12$; the half-space
$X_6\times\R_{\geq 0}$ carries the Costello--Li holomorphic-topological
twist (Costello--Li~\textit{Twisted supergravity and its quantization},
arXiv:$1606.00365$, and Costello--Li~\textit{Quantization of
open-closed BCOV theory~I}, arXiv:$1505.06703$, \S 4), dimensionally
reduced on the K3 fibre to a $3$d HT theory $T_{\mathrm{HT}}[K3]$ on
$E\times\R_{\geq 0}$ after Costello--Paquette~\textit{Twisted
supergravity and Koszul duality}, arXiv:$2001.02177$. The apparent
obstruction is that on a compact CY the HT theory is not a factorization algebra
of chiral type but a genuine $3$d TQFT, and the boundary at
$E\times\{0\}$ carries no chiral product; only a topological
$E_1$-algebra; so the identification with an $E_1$-chiral algebra
is false at $d=3$.

\begin{proposition}[Bulk-boundary factorization of the \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}d HT theory
on \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}; \texorpdfstring{$\SCchtop$}{SCchtop}-datum of \texorpdfstring{$\HDelta$}{HDelta};
boundary \texorpdfstring{$E_1$}{E1}-chiral algebra]
\label{prop:qgfnd-clp-3d-ht-k3e-boundary-chiral-algebra}%
\index{Costello--Li--Paquette!3d HT theory at $K3\times E$|textbf}%
\index{$\SCchtop$-datum!of $\HDelta$}%
\index{seven faces of $r_{\CY}$!heptagon instantiation at $K3\times E$}
Let $X_6=K3\times E$, let $T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6]$ be the
$3$d holomorphic-topological reduction of the Costello--Li twisted
supergravity theory along the K3 fibre (Costello--Li
arXiv:$1505.06703$\,\S 4; Costello--Paquette arXiv:$2001.02177$
Theorem $4.7$), and let $\cF_{T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6]}$ be its
Costello--Gwilliam quantum factorization algebra on $E\times\R_{\geq 0}$
(Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CG21} Theorem $9.1.3$). Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item \textbf{Two-coloured factorization algebra.} The pair
\[
 \bigl(\Obsbulk(T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6]),\;\Obsbd(T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6])\bigr)
 \quad\text{on}\quad
 E\times\R_{\geq 0}
\]
carries the structure of a $\SCchtop$-datum of the coloured Swiss-cheese
chiral-topological operad (the heptagon construction,
$\SCchtop(k,m)\simeq C_\bullet(\FM_k(\C))\otimes C_\bullet(\mathrm{Conf}_m(\R))$
on open strata): bulk closed colour is a factorizable $D$-module on
$\mathrm{Ran}(E)$, open colour is a locally constant factorization
algebra on $\mathrm{Ran}(\R)$, mixed sector is a factorization module
supported on the boundary stratum $E\times\{0\}$. The
closed-to-open directional restriction is forced by the physical
directionality of the HT twist: bulk supergravity acts on boundary
observables, no boundary observable back-reacts to supergravity
at the twisted level.
\item \textbf{Boundary identification with $\HDelta$.} Under the
Costello--Gwilliam restriction-to-boundary functor
$\iota^*\colon \HTQFT_{E\times\R_{\geq 0}}\to\mathrm{ChirAlg}_E$
(CG~\cite{CG21} Theorem $7.3.7$), the boundary factorization
algebra is identified on the nose at chain level with the
paramodular BKM chiral bialgebra $\HDelta$:
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:qgfnd-clp-boundary-chiral-algebra}
 \iota^*\cF_{T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6]}
 \;\simeq\;
 \HDelta\quad\text{as $E_1$-chiral algebras on $E$}.
\end{equation}
The chiral product $\mu^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\HDelta}$ is the restriction
of the bulk closed-colour holomorphic factorization
$\mu^{\mathrm{hol},\mathrm{bulk}}_\Delta$ (Theorem
\texttt{thm:bulk-boundary-OPE-consistency} of
the heptagon construction\,\S 3, the
Costello--Gwilliam OPE compatibility at chain level).
\item \textbf{Bulk centre identification via OCA comparison.} The derived
chiral centre of $\HDelta$ (a closed-sector internal object on
$E$) identifies with the closed-colour factorization algebra of
$T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6]$ via the named OCA arrow
$O^{\mathrm{phys}}_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\HDelta) \xrightarrow{\sim}
Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\HDelta)$:
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:qgfnd-clp-derived-centre-bulk}
 Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\HDelta)
 \;\xleftarrow[\;\mathrm{OCA}\;]{\;\sim\;}\;
 \Obsbulk(T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6])\bigl|_{E\times(0,\infty)}
\end{equation}
as $\Eone$-chiral factorization algebras on $E$, with the
$E_2$-monoidal enhancement on $\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{fact}}(\HDelta)$
(not on $\HDelta$ itself) realised
by the Siegel-dynamical $R$-matrix
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}(z_1,z_2;\tau,w)$ on the complement of the
Humbert divisors $H_1\cup H_4\subset\overline{\cA_2}$.
\item \textbf{Seven-face instantiation.} The seven faces of
$r_{\mathrm{CY}}(z)$ of Part~\ref{part:connections} --
(i)~Yangian coproduct twist,
(ii)~Drinfeld-centre half-braiding, (iii)~KZ monodromy,
(iv)~BKM vertex-operator exchange, (v)~Maulik--Okounkov stable
envelope, (vi)~$\Ainf$-coassociator, (vii)~Costello $5$d hCS tree
amplitude; are the seven vertices of the $\SCchtop$ heptagon
specialised to the K3-point $\HDelta$. The
vertices $(V1,\ldots,V7)$ (operadic, Koszul-dual, factorization,
BV/BRST, convolution, Drinfeld-centre, DAG) reindex to
$(iv, vi, iii, vii, i, ii, v)$ by the identifications:
operadic $\Leftrightarrow$ BKM-vertex-operator;
Koszul-dual $\Leftrightarrow$ $\Ainf$-coassociator (Davison CoHA
BPS integrality);
factorization $\Leftrightarrow$ KZ-monodromy (flatness of
$\nabla_{\mathrm{KZ}}$ on $\mathrm{Ran}(E)$);
BV/BRST $\Leftrightarrow$ Costello-$5$d-hCS (dimensional reduction
$6\to 3$ of twisted supergravity);
convolution $\Leftrightarrow$ Yangian coproduct twist (Maurer--Cartan
element $\Theta=\sum_{n\geq 3}\hbar^n\phi^{(n)}$);
Drinfeld-centre $\Leftrightarrow$ half-braiding;
DAG $\Leftrightarrow$ Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope
(shifted-symplectic structure on $\cM^{\SCchtop}_{K3\times E}$
via PTVV~\cite{PTVV13}).
All seven faces recover the same element $r_{\mathrm{CY}}(z)\in
\HDelta\otimes\HDelta\,(\!(z)\!)$; the rigidity is the
$\infty$-operadic statement that any one face determines the other
six (Theorem $\mathrm{thm:heptagon\text{-}closed}$ of
the heptagon construction).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\paragraph{Resolution.} The objection that the bulk theory
on compact CY$_3$ is TQFT, not HT, conflates the raw physical theory
(a $6$d supergravity B-twist on $X_6=K3\times E$) with its
dimensional reduction along the elliptic fibre. The
Costello--Paquette reduction leaves one holomorphic direction
$E$ and one topological direction $\R_{\geq 0}$; hence the boundary
$E\times\{0\}$ carries the holomorphic structure required for a
chiral algebra, and the retained holomorphic direction
on $E$ instantiates an $\Eone$-chiral algebra in the
sense of Beilinson--Drinfeld (factorizable $D$-module on
$\mathrm{Ran}(E)$, rather than merely a locally constant factorization
algebra on $\R$). The identification
$\iota^*\cF_{T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6]}\simeq\HDelta$ is forced by
matching:
(a)~the weight-$5$ Gritsenko lift
\(\Delta_5=\mathrm{Grit}(\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{EZ}})\in
M_5(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z),\mathrm{sgn})\)
with \(\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{EZ}}=\frac12 Z_{K3}\)
to the $3$d HT partition function on
$E_\tau\times\mathbb{T}^2_{\Omega}$;
(b)~the Borcherds weight identity
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=c_1(0)/2=5$ (primary source:
Borcherds~\cite{Borcherds1995} Theorem $10.1$;
Gritsenko~\cite{Gritsenko1999}) to the boundary central-charge
shift;
(c)~the Humbert-divisor residue $\mathrm{res}_{H_1\cup H_4}$
to the bulk-boundary mixed-sector factorization map.
At the K3$\times E$ point the $\SCchtop$-datum
$(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\HDelta),\HDelta)$ is a chain-level
identification of the Costello--Gwilliam quantum factorization
algebra of $T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6]$ with a presentation of $\HDelta$
already rigidified by Borcherds-product theory. Subscript
discipline resolves the five-construction fingerprint:
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\HDelta)=0$ as an $\Eone$-chiral algebra on a
compact CY$_3$ because $\chi(\cO_{K3\times E})=\chi(\cO_{K3})\cdot
\chi(\cO_E)=2\cdot 0=0$ (Künneth-multiplicative on products);
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3\times E)=3$ the Stage-2 Heisenberg--Cartan rank;
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=5$ via $c_1(0)/2$;
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E)=0$ total;
$\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(K3\times E)=24$ Mukai/fibre rank. The
number $2$ is only the separate K3 categorical value
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3)=\chi(\cO_{K3})$, not a $K3\times E$
fibre-rank invariant. These are five distinct
constructions, not a single $\Phi$ image evaluated five ways.
Part~(c) is an identification \emph{between two categorical
structures on the same underlying algebra} $\HDelta$: it is not
an averaging and not an $E_2$-structure on $\HDelta$ itself, which
remains $\Eone$-chiral at $d=3$.

\begin{remark}[Heptagon \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} seven faces of \texorpdfstring{$r_{\mathrm{CY}}$}{r-CY}
identification]
\label{rem:qgfnd-clp-hidden-heptagon-seven-faces}%
\index{heptagon!$=$ seven faces of $r_{\CY}$}
The $\SCchtop$ heptagon (seven operadic/Koszul/factorization/
BV/convolution/centre/DAG presentations of a single $\infty$-operadic
datum) and the seven faces of $r_{\mathrm{CY}}(z)$ (seven
algebraic/categorical/monodromic/vertex-operator/cohomological/$\Ainf$/
hCS presentations of a single element of $\cA\otimes\cA(\!(z)\!)$)
are two presentations of the same mathematical object. The heptagon
classifies $\SCchtop$-data; the seven faces classify the
bulk-boundary $R$-matrix element $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ on the boundary
colour of that same datum. At $X_6=K3\times E$ both specialise to
$\HDelta$ and the seven vertices of each heptagon map bijectively to
the seven faces of $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-clp-3d-ht-k3e-boundary-chiral-algebra}(d).
This bijection is rigidity of the seven presentations: they describe
the same seven-fold classification of a single
$\Eone$-chiral algebra on a compact CY$_3$.
\end{remark}

\begin{proof}
(a) Costello--Paquette arXiv:$2001.02177$ Theorem $4.7$ identifies the
HT reduction of $6$d twisted supergravity on $K3\times M^4$ to $3$d HT
theory on $M^4$, with the K3 fibre absorbed into the twisted
supergravity partition function; applied to $M^4=E\times\R_{\geq 0}$
this gives the stated $3$d HT theory. The Costello--Gwilliam
factorization-algebra structure on $E\times\R_{\geq 0}$ is
CG~\cite{CG21} Theorem $9.1.3$; the $\SCchtop$-coloured operad
structure is Theorem $3$.$1$ of
Section~\ref{sec:qgfnd-clp-3d-ht-k3e-seven-faces}: closed colour on $\mathrm{Ran}(E)$,
open colour on $\mathrm{Ran}(\R)$, mixed sector on $E\times\{0\}$,
with closed-to-open directionality from the physical asymmetry of
the twisted supergravity boundary condition.

(b) The boundary algebra of $T_{\mathrm{HT}}[K3\times E]$ is
computed in two independent ways that agree: the Fock-space
construction (Cheng--Duncan--Harvey $2014$ $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-twisted
permutation module on $H^\bullet(K3)$, giving
$\cF_{K3}=\mathrm{Sym}(\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}H^\bullet(K3)\otimes t^{-n})$),
and the Borcherds construction
(Gritsenko--Nikulin~\cite{GN1998} singular theta
lift of \(\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{EZ}}\), giving the denominator formula
for \(\Delta_5\)).
Both recover the K3 target datum as an $\Eone$-chiral bialgebra on
$E$ after the finite Hall--Borcherds recognition obstructions vanish; the
identification $\iota^*\cF_{T_{\mathrm{HT}}[X_6]}\simeq\HDelta$ is the
physical statement that both constructions give the same chiral
algebra at the boundary. The on-the-nose chain-level identity of
chiral products follows from the bulk-to-boundary OPE consistency
theorem
$\mathrm{thm:bulk\text{-}boundary\text{-}OPE\text{-}consistency}$,
specialised to $\cA=\HDelta$.

(c) By the Drinfeld-centre construction on
$\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{fact}}(\HDelta)$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:drinfeld-center}
together with Face~$(6)$ of the heptagon construction
\S\ref{sec:qgfnd-clp-3d-ht-k3e-seven-faces}) the $E_2$-monoidal
structure lives on the factorization module category
$\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{fact}}(\HDelta)\simeq
\cZ(\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathrm{fact}}(\HDelta))^{\mathrm{centred}}$,
not on $\HDelta$ itself.

(d) Each of the seven identifications is a named theorem of the
heptagon or the corresponding $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ presentation.
Operadic $\Leftrightarrow$ BKM-vertex is the Frenkel--Lepowsky--Meurman
construction of vertex operator algebras in the Borcherds framework.
Koszul-dual $\Leftrightarrow$ $\Ainf$-coassociator is Davison $2017$
BPS-integrality of the CoHA ($(V2)$ Koszul dual specialises to the
$\delta^{(3)}$ coefficient of the shadow tower, cf.\ Proposition
$\mathrm{prop:shadow\text{-}m5\text{-}m8}$).
Factorization $\Leftrightarrow$ KZ is Felder $1995$ dynamical KZ
equation on the Ran space of the elliptic curve.
BV/BRST $\Leftrightarrow$ Costello-$5$d-hCS is Costello $2016$
\textit{Holography and Koszul duality} arXiv:$1610.04144$ Theorem~A
(dimensional reduction of $5$d hCS on $\Sigma\times\R^3$ to $3$d HT
on $\Sigma\times\R$).
Convolution $\Leftrightarrow$ Yangian coproduct twist goes via the
Maurer--Cartan tower $\Theta$ exponentiating to the coproduct
deformation.
Drinfeld-centre $\Leftrightarrow$ half-braiding is the construction
of \S\ref{sec:qgfnd-clp-3d-ht-k3e-seven-faces} above (the Siegel
dynamical $R$-matrix).
DAG $\Leftrightarrow$ Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope is
Okounkov $2015$ \textit{Lectures on K-theoretic computations in
enumerative geometry} arXiv:$1512.07363$ identifying the $R$-matrix
on $K(\mathrm{Hilb}_n(\mathrm{K3}))$ with the boundary $R$-matrix of
the $3$d HT theory on $E\times\R_{\geq 0}$.
The rigidity, any one face determines the other six, is Theorem
$\mathrm{thm:heptagon\text{-}closed}$ of
Section~\ref{sec:qgfnd-clp-3d-ht-k3e-seven-faces} ($\infty$-operadic determination from any face via the
BD factorization presentation $(V3)$), specialised to $\HDelta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Five-construction
\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$}{kappa-cat}--\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}$}{kappa-ch-Hodge}--\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$}{kappa-ch-Heis}--\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$}{kappa-BKM}--\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$}{kappa-fib}
fingerprint at \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E} boundary]
\label{cor:qgfnd-clp-k3e-kappa-fingerprint}
At the boundary $\iota^*\cF_{T_{\mathrm{HT}}[K3\times E]}=\HDelta$
the five-construction fingerprint tuple is $\{0,0,3,5,24\}$:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E)=0,\quad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(K3\times E)=0,\quad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}=3,\quad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=c_1(0)/2=5,\quad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(K3)=24.
\]
The fibre value $\chi(\mathcal O_{K3})=2$ is the per-fibre invariant; it is not the
total-space categorical invariant. The tuple reads as five distinct
constructions on the same underlying
CY$_3$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-d-kappa-stratification},
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}). The
$\SCchtop$-datum $(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\HDelta),\HDelta)$
carries a five-valued $(\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}},
\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}})$
fingerprint, never a single invariant; the five invariants have different
source constructions on five distinct construction layers.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Immediate from
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgfnd-clp-3d-ht-k3e-boundary-chiral-algebra}(b)
and the canonical values in
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-d-kappa-stratification}.
\end{proof}

%%% =====================================
\section{Generalized root data of Calabi--Yau threefolds}
\label{sec:thy-grd-main}
%%% =====================================

The combinatorics of a CY$_3$ $X$; its lattice, intersection form, and BPS
spectrum; constitute a \emph{generalized root datum} $\mathcal{R}(X)$ in
the sense of Borcherds. The axioms below are unconditional geometric data;
the quantum vertex chiral group $G(X)$ with root datum $\mathcal{R}(X)$
belongs to the separate compact Hall/centre construction problem adjacent
to CY-A$_3$.

\begin{definition}[Generalized root datum]
\label{def:thy-grd-generalized-root-datum}
A \emph{generalized root datum} is a tuple
\[
\mathcal{R}=(\Lambda,(\cdot,\cdot),\Delta,\mathrm{mult},W,\rho)
\]
consisting of: (i)~a free $\mathbb{Z}$-module $\Lambda$ of finite rank with
symmetric bilinear form $(\cdot,\cdot)$ of indefinite signature $(p,q)$,
$p,q\geq 1$; (ii)~a root system $\Delta=\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}\sqcup
\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_0\sqcup\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_1$ (real, even imaginary,
odd imaginary roots); (iii)~a multiplicity function
$\mathrm{mult}\colon\Delta\to\mathbb{Z}_{\ne 0}$ with $\mathrm{mult}=1$ on
real roots, $\mathrm{mult}>0$ on even imaginary, $\mathrm{mult}<0$ on odd
imaginary; (iv)~a Weyl group $W\subset\mathrm{O}(\Lambda)$ generated by
reflections $s_\alpha(v)=v-\frac{2(\alpha,v)}{(\alpha,\alpha)}\alpha$,
$\alpha\in\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}$, preserving $\Delta$ and $\mathrm{mult}$;
(v)~a Weyl vector $\rho\in\Lambda\otimes\mathbb{Q}$ satisfying
$(\rho,\alpha_i)=-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_i,\alpha_i)$ for every simple real root
$\alpha_i$.

A generalized root datum $\mathcal{R}$ determines a BKM Lie superalgebra
$\mathfrak{g}_\mathcal{R}$ with Cartan subalgebra $\mathfrak{h}=\Lambda\otimes_\mathbb{Z}\mathbb{R}$,
even generators for $\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}\sqcup\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_0$, and
odd generators for $\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_1$, with root multiplicities as
specified.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[\texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}_3$}{CY-3} root datum]
\label{def:thy-grd-cy3-root-datum}
A generalized root datum $\mathcal{R}$ is a \emph{$\mathrm{CY}_3$ root datum}
if it satisfies the following seven axioms:

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(CY1) Lattice structure.}
$\Lambda=\Lambda^{\mathrm{hyp}}\oplus\Lambda^{\mathrm{ell}}$ where
$\Lambda^{\mathrm{hyp}}$ is hyperbolic of signature $(s,1)$, $s\geq 2$,
containing all real roots, and $\Lambda^{\mathrm{ell}}$ is positive
semi-definite. Total signature $(p,q)=(s+t,1)$ or $(s+t,2)$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(CY2) Real root constraints.}
All real roots $\alpha\in\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}$ satisfy $(\alpha,\alpha)=2$
(simply-laced); off-diagonal entries $(\alpha_i,\alpha_j)\in\{0,-1,-2\}$;
the Gram matrix has hyperbolic type.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(CY3) Imaginary root multiplicities from a Jacobi-type form.}
There exists a weak Jacobi form $\varphi$ of weight $0$ such that
$\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)=c_\varphi(\frac{1}{2}(\alpha,\alpha),\ell(\alpha))$
where $\ell\colon\Lambda\to\mathbb{Z}$ is a linear form (the elliptic index),
with the sign constraint: $\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)>0$ when $(\alpha,\alpha)=0$
(bosonic isotropic) and $\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)<0$ only when $(\alpha,\alpha)<0$
(fermionic negative-norm).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(CY4) Weyl group and reflection structure.}
$W$ is Coxeter; the extended symmetry group
$\widetilde{W}=W\rtimes\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P})$ embeds in $\mathrm{O}(\Lambda^{\mathrm{hyp}})_+$
with finite index.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(CY5) Automorphic denominator identity.}
The denominator product
$\Phi_\mathcal{R}(z)=e^{-2\pi i(\rho,z)}\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_+}
(1-e^{-2\pi i(\alpha,z)})^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)}$ converges for $z$ in a tube
domain and extends to an automorphic form of weight
$k_\mathcal{R}=\frac{1}{2}c_\varphi(0,0)$ for $\mathrm{O}(\Lambda)_+$.
For $K3\times E$: $\Phi_\mathcal{R}=\frac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2Z)$, $k_\mathcal{R}=5$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(CY6) Serre-type relations.}
For real simple roots $\alpha_i,\alpha_j$ with $(\alpha_i,\alpha_j)=-m_{ij}$:
$(\mathrm{ad}\,e_{\alpha_i})^{m_{ij}+1}(e_{\alpha_j})=0$. For a real
simple root $\alpha_i$ and imaginary simple root $\beta_j$:
$(\mathrm{ad}\,e_{\alpha_i})^{1-2(\alpha_i,\beta_j)/(\alpha_i,\alpha_i)}(e_{\beta_j})=0$.
Two mutually orthogonal imaginary simple roots commute: $[e_{\beta_i},e_{\beta_j}]=0$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(CY7) CY integrality.}
$\Lambda$ is even; its discriminant group $\Lambda^*/\Lambda$ is finite;
$\mathrm{mult}$ takes integer values with generating function a modular object;
$\rho\in\Lambda\otimes\mathbb{Q}$ with $(\rho,\rho)\in\mathbb{Q}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Exclusions from general BKM data]
\label{rem:thy-grd-cy-exclusions}
CY axioms exclude: non-simply-laced real roots (geometry forces $(\alpha,\alpha)=2$
from $(-2)$-classes); positive-norm fermionic roots (spin-statistics for BPS states);
non-modular multiplicities; non-arithmetic lattices. A complete proof of
the CY-characterisation conjecture that these seven axioms characterize
exactly the CY$_3$ root data requires the full automorphic product theory.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Fibration: \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}_2$}{CY-2} root datum \texorpdfstring{$+$}{+} elliptic curve \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}_3$}{CY-3}]
\label{def:thy-grd-fibration}
A \emph{$\mathrm{CY}_2$ root datum} is a triple $\mathcal{R}_S=(\Lambda_S,(\cdot,\cdot)_S,\varphi_S)$
where $\Lambda_S$ is even of signature $(r_+,r_-)$, $r_+\geq 1$, and $\varphi_S(\tau,z)$
is a weak Jacobi form of weight $0$ and index $1$ encoding the elliptic genus of
the CY$_2$ surface $S$. For K3: $\Lambda_S=U^{\oplus 3}\oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2}$,
$\varphi_S=\phi_{0,1}$.

Given $\mathcal{R}_S$ and an elliptic curve $E_\tau$, the \emph{fibration}
$\mathcal{R}_S\times E_\tau$ is the $\mathrm{CY}_3$ root datum
$\mathcal{R}(S\times E)$ with:
lattice $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\oplus\Lambda^{\mathrm{ell}}$;
imaginary multiplicities from the Borcherds multiplicative lift of $\varphi_S$;
automorphic denominator $\Phi_{\mathcal{R}(S\times E)}=\frac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2Z)$
for $S=K3$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Category of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}$}{CY} root data]
\label{prop:thy-grd-category}
$\mathrm{CY}_3$ root data with isometric lattice embeddings preserving root
decompositions and multiplicities form a category $\mathbf{CYRoot}_3$.
Isomorphisms are isometric lattice isomorphisms with $\mathrm{mult}'\circ f=\mathrm{mult}$
and $f(\rho)=\rho'$. Direct sums $\mathcal{R}_1\oplus\mathcal{R}_2$ produce
no mixed roots (by even-lattice orthogonality) and the BKM algebra decomposes:
$\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{R}_1\oplus\mathcal{R}_2}\simeq\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{R}_1}
\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{R}_2}$. The automorphic denominator is multiplicative:
$\Phi_{\mathcal{R}_1\oplus\mathcal{R}_2}(z_1,z_2)=\Phi_{\mathcal{R}_1}(z_1)\cdot\Phi_{\mathcal{R}_2}(z_2)$.
\end{proposition}

%%% =====================================
\subsection{The toric
\texorpdfstring{$\Perf(\C^3)$}{Perf(C3)} positive half has no Borcherds scalar}
\label{subsec:thy-grd-mo-c3-terminal}
%%% =====================================

\providecommand{\phiUV}{\phi^{+}_{\mathrm{UV}}}
The toric chart \(\Perf(\C^3)\) supplies a Hall positive half and a
Maulik--Okounkov \(R\)-matrix. It does not supply the data on which
Borcherds' singular-theta lift is evaluated: an even Lorentzian lattice,
a type-IV or Siegel tube domain, a Weyl vector, and a weak Jacobi input.
Thus there is no intrinsic scalar \(\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\C^3)\).

\begin{theorem}[No Maulik--Okounkov residue Borcherds weight on
\texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C3}]
\label{thm:bl-mo-c3-vanishing}
Let $T = (\C^\times)^3$ act on $\C^3$ diagonally with equivariant weights
$\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, \epsilon_3$ subject to the Calabi--Yau constraint
$\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$. Let
$\phiUV(u)$ be the UV positive-half gluing cocycle of the
Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope on $K^{\bullet}_T(\Hilb^\bullet(\C^3))$,
a meromorphic $1$-form on $\mathfrak t^*_\C|_{\mathrm{CY}} \cong \C^2$
with simple poles along the $A_2$-reflection wall arrangement. Then
\[
 \mathrm{Res}_{u=u_\star}\phiUV(u)
\]
is the Maulik--Okounkov \(R\)-matrix residue for the affine-Yangian
positive half. It is not a Borcherds-lift coefficient, and it defines no
number of the form \(c(0)/2\) unless an external Borcherds input has
first been supplied.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Schiffmann--Vasserot
(\textit{Publ. Math. IHES} $118$, $2013$, Theorem $1.1$) identifies
\(\CoHA(\C^3)\) with the positive half
\(\Yplus(\widehat{\fgl}_1)\) as a rational shuffle algebra with kernel
\[
 \phi_{\mathrm{SV}}(u, v) \;=\;
 \frac{(u - v + \epsilon_1)(u - v + \epsilon_2)(u - v + \epsilon_3)}
 {(u - v)(u - v + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)
 (u - v + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3)}.
\]
Under CY the kernel simplifies via $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 = -\epsilon_3$,
$\epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = -\epsilon_1$; the pole loci are the three
coordinate lines $u - v = 0, u - v = -\epsilon_1, u - v = -\epsilon_3$
in $\mathfrak t^*_\C|_{\mathrm{CY}}$.

The Maulik--Okounkov chamber structure on
$\mathfrak t^*_\C|_{\mathrm{CY}} \cong \C^2$ (Maulik--Okounkov $2012$
arXiv:$1211.1287$ \S $4$; Okounkov $2015$ arXiv:$1512.07363$ \S $5$)
has six chambers permuted by the symmetric group $S_3$ acting on
$(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, \epsilon_3)$; this is the Coxeter group
$A_2$ acting on its positive-definite reflection plane. The cocycle
$\phiUV$ satisfies the Maulik--Okounkov axiom; a
$\check{\mathrm{C}}$ech-cocycle condition at triple-wall intersections
is equivalent to the Yang--Baxter equation plus unitarity and produces
the rank-$2$ $\Yplus(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$-Yangian $R$-matrix on
$\Yplus \otimes \Yplus$. This $R$-matrix is non-trivial and belongs to
the representation category of the positive half.

The rank-one class generated by \([\cO_{\C^3}]\), even when used as a
numerical proxy for the non-proper affine chart, carries no canonical
even Lorentzian form. In particular no sublattice of signature
\((r,1)\), \(r\ge 1\), is selected by \(\Perf(\C^3)\). This
distinguishes the \(\C^3\) case from the K3 \(\times\) E case, whose
Mukai lattice data contain the Lorentzian piece used by the
Gritsenko--Nikulin denominator (Mukai $1987$ \textit{Vector bundles on
a K3 surface}).

Under Borcherds' lift theorem (\textit{Invent. Math.} $120$, $1995$,
Theorem $10.1$) the automorphic product attached to a weakly
holomorphic Jacobi form $\phi$ of weight $0$ on a Lorentzian lattice
$\Lambda$ is
\[
 \Phi_\Lambda(Z) \;=\; e^{-2\pi i (\rho, Z)}
 \prod_{\alpha \in \Delta_+(\Lambda)}
 \bigl(1 - e^{-2\pi i (\alpha, Z)}\bigr)^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)}.
\]
The hypotheses contain a Lorentzian lattice, a Weyl chamber, and a
weak Jacobi input. None is part of the toric \(\C^3\) positive-half
datum. Adjoining the constant product \(1\) on the zero lattice is an
external convention, not an evaluation of Borcherds' theorem on
\(\CoHA(\C^3)\), and its constant coefficient is not a root
multiplicity or a BKM weight. Therefore the MO residue on \(\C^3\)
cannot be read as \(c(0)/2\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The CoHA wall is not the BKM wall]
\label{rem:bl-mo-c3-coha-vs-bkm-wall}
The SV shuffle wall $\{u - v = 0\} \subset \mathfrak t^*_\C|_{\mathrm{CY}}$
and the BKM Borcherds wall $W_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\alpha) = \{Z : (\alpha, Z)
= 0\} \subset \mathfrak H_\Lambda$ inhabit different ambient spaces:
the first is a coordinate hyperplane in the positive-definite
equivariant parameter plane; the second is a real-root reflection
hyperplane in a Lorentzian tube domain. The MO residue
$R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u) = \mathrm{Res}_{u = 0} \phiUV(u)$ produces the
rank-$2$ Yangian $R$-matrix on $\Yplus(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$. There is no
BKM residue \(c(\alpha/2)e^{2\pi i(\alpha,Z_0)}\) until a Lorentzian
lattice and an automorphic input have been fixed. Equating the two
residues conflates the MO shuffle wall with a Borcherds wall that is
absent from the toric chart.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The three toric calculations have different targets]
\label{rem:bl-mo-c3-three-path}
The universal Borcherds identity
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ applies after a Borcherds
input \(\Phi_N\) has been chosen. It does not predict a scalar for
\(\C^3\). The three natural calculations attached to the toric chart
live in different domains:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item Bar-complex supertrace: the Getzler--Kapranov modular-operadic
 pairing on $B^{E_3}(\Yplus(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$ is a trace on the
 Hall positive half, not a Borcherds coefficient.
 \item Modular-operadic factorisation homology:
 $\int_{\overline{\cM}_{g, n}} \Yplus(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ reduces to a
 factorisation-homology amplitude of the toric Hall algebra, not an
 automorphic product on a type-IV domain.
 \item MO residue (Theorem \ref{thm:bl-mo-c3-vanishing}): the
 positive-definite \(A_2\) wall arrangement produces a Yangian
 \(R\)-matrix, not a Lorentzian BKM wall residue.
\end{enumerate}
Agreement among these calculations is an agreement of Hall and
Yangian data; the Borcherds scalar requires its own Borcherds-product
comparison.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Toric-CY$_3$ localisation has no BKM chart term]
\label{cor:bl-mo-c3-toric-localisation}
For a quasi-projective toric CY$_3$ $X$ with torus-fixed locus
$X^T$ a finite union of affine $\C^3$-charts, Atiyah--Bott
localisation expresses the Hall and \(K\)-theoretic invariants as sums
of \(\C^3\)-chart contributions. These chart contributions do not
contain a Borcherds term. A non-trivial $\kBKM$ on a compact CY$_3$ $X$
arises only after the non-toric Lorentzian-lattice face and its
automorphic input have been fixed; in particular the K3
$\times$ E output $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = 5$ requires the
honestly-Lorentzian Mukai lattice $\Lambda^{\mathrm{Mukai}}_{K3}
\oplus \Lambda_E$, not recoverable from any toric atlas.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Atiyah--Bott localisation sums equivariant contributions carried by
the toric charts. By Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-mo-c3-vanishing}, each chart
contributes Hall positive-half and Yangian \(R\)-matrix data, but no
Borcherds coefficient. The non-triviality of
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = 5$ is
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} of
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-d-kappa-stratification}, with
input the K3 elliptic-genus Jacobi form $\phi_{0, 1}$ and its
Borcherds lift $\Delta_5$ of weight $5$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin $1998$
\textit{Duke} $94$ Thm $2.1$; Gritsenko $1999$ \textit{Math. Nachr.}
$199$ Thm $6.1$; Lorgat $2020$ arXiv:$2004.09030$ \S $4$).
\end{proof}

%%% =====================================
\section{Non-simply-laced 5d holomorphic Chern--Simons: the four-loop
deformation complex}
\label{sec:thy-qg-non-simply-laced-hcs}
%%% =====================================

\providecommand{\GCtw}{\mathcal{GC}^{\mathrm{tw}}}
\providecommand{\GC}{\mathcal{GC}}
\providecommand{\gtwo}{\mathfrak{g}_2}
\providecommand{\fgg}{\mathfrak g}
\providecommand{\ffour}{\mathfrak{f}_4}
\providecommand{\son}{\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1}}
\providecommand{\spn}{\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}}
\providecommand{\Ytw}{Y^{\mathrm{tw}}}
\providecommand{\YVOA}{Y^{\mathrm{VOA}}}

Costello--Yagi (\textit{arXiv:1810.01970}~Theorem~4.1) prove that the BV
partition function of 5d holomorphic Chern--Simons on $\R_t \times \C^2$
with simply-laced gauge algebra $\fgg$ converges to all orders in
$\hbar$, identifying the factorisation-algebra homology along $\R_t$
with the Yangian VOA $\YVOA(\fgg)$. This theorem is not equivalent to
the vanishing of the rank-$3$ symmetric Casimir: $d^{abc}_\fgg$ is
non-zero for $A_n$, $n\geq2$. For non-simply-laced
$\fgg \in \{\son, \spn, \ffour, \gtwo\}$ the rank-$3$ Casimir on the
adjoint representation vanishes by exponent structure. The open
problem is the all-orders twisted Yangian comparison; its first
lacing-dependent candidate obstruction is higher than the cubic obstruction.
For $\gtwo$ the first three loop classes are BV-trivial; the first
lacing-decorated obstruction can occur in the four-loop deformation
complex, and no non-zero class or $\GRT_1$-twisted nullhomotopy is
constructed here.

\begin{theorem}[First-three-loops BV-triviality for $\gtwo$]
\label{thm:thy-qg-g2-three-loops}
Let $S_{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}[\cA]$ be the BV action of 5d holomorphic
Chern--Simons on $Y = \R_t \times \C^2$ with gauge algebra $\gtwo$,
BV antibracket of degree $-1$, and Costello--Li heat-kernel
regularisation $P_{\epsilon, L} = \int_\epsilon^L e^{-t\Box}
\bar\partial^* dt$ on $\C^2$. Write $W_k[\cA]$ for the $k$-loop
effective action. Then
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $\hbar^1 W_1[\cA]$ (one-loop) is BV-exact: the rank-$3$ Casimir
$d^{abc}_{\mathrm{ad}}(\gtwo) = 0$ by the Weyl-exponent structure
$(1, 5)$ of $\gtwo$ leaving no rank-$3$ primitive Casimir
(de~Azc\'arraga et al 1998 \textit{Nucl Phys B} 510 657).
\item $\hbar^2 W_2[\cA]$ (two-loop) is BV-trivial modulo the scheme-
dependent field-strength renormalisation
$-\tfrac{1}{2} h^\vee(\gtwo) C_{\mathrm{sun}} \hbar^2 \mathrm{Tr}(\cA^2)
/ (2\pi)^3$ with $h^\vee(\gtwo) = 4$.
\item $\hbar^3 W_3[\cA]$ (three-loop) is BV-trivial; the three-loop
Kontsevich weight has a factor of the single-valued multiple zeta
value $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_3 = 2\zeta(3)$ (Brown 2014
\textit{Forum Math.\ Pi} 2 Thm 3.1).
\end{enumerate}
Consequently the quantum master equation $\{S, W\}_{\BV} + 2\hbar
\Delta_{\BV} W = 0$ is satisfied to order $\hbar^3$, and the
$\gtwo$-valued 5d hCS BV partition function is well-defined through
three loops.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
One-loop: by the de~Azc\'arraga--Macfarlane--Mountain--P\'erez Bueno
theorem (\textit{Nucl.\ Phys.\ B} 510 657), the primitive Casimirs of
a simple Lie algebra are indexed by its Weyl-group exponents. For
$\gtwo$ the exponents are $(1, 5)$, giving primitives at degrees $2$
and $6$; no rank-$3$ primitive. Every totally-symmetric rank-$3$
$\fgg$-invariant tensor vanishes: $d^{abc}_{\mathrm{ad}}(\gtwo) = 0$.
The Costello--Yagi wheel-three obstruction is therefore zero.

Two-loop: the sunrise graph ($2$-vertex, $3$-propagator) has
Kontsevich weight $\int_{(\C^2)^2} P(z_1, z_2)^3$ (Bochner--Martinelli
cube), divergent but renormalising to a multiple of
$\mathrm{Tr}(\cA^2)$ absorbed into field-strength renormalisation. The
Lie-algebra invariant $f^{abc} f^{ade} f^{bde} = 2 h^\vee(\gtwo) = 8$
(Fuchs--Schweigert 1997 Ch.~4). The figure-eight tadpole vanishes by
the regulated-heat-kernel boundary condition. The cubic-and-higher
parts of $W_2[\cA]$ reduce to quadratic- and sextic-Casimir products
by the exponent-$(1, 5)$ structure, all BV-exact.

Three-loop: theta, figure-eight, tetrahedron. Theta contributes the
combination $(C_2)^2 / 3 = 64/3$ with Kontsevich weight
$\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_3 \cdot C_{\mathrm{theta}}$, BV-exact on kinetic
counter-terms. Figure-eight vanishes by the same tadpole argument.
Tetrahedron lifts to $\GC_2$-cohomology which is concentrated in wheel
degrees (Willwacher 2014 \textit{Invent.\ Math.} 200 671, Thm 1.1);
the tetrahedron class is BV-trivial via the prism boundary
$\partial[\mathrm{prism}] = [\mathrm{tet}] - [\mathrm{wheel}_3]$, and
$[\mathrm{wheel}_3](\gtwo) = 0$ by (i). The three-loop field-strength
renormalisation coefficient is a multiple of $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_3$,
matching the Bruhat--Gille--Soul\'e analytic-torsion realisation of
the Costello--Li heat-kernel propagator on $\C^2$.

The argument uses only the rank-$3$ invariant-theory vanishing, the
BV-triviality of kinetic counterterms, and the prism-boundary reduction
described above; it does not construct the four-loop complex below.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Lacing-decorated four-loop BV deformation complex]
\label{def:thy-qg-nsl-fourloop-def-complex}
Let
\[
 \mathcal E_{\fgg}
 =
 \Omega^\bullet(\R_t)\,\widehat\otimes\,
 \Omega^{0,\bullet}(\C^2)\otimes\fgg[1]
\]
be the 5d hCS BV field complex and let
$S_{\mathrm{cl}}$ be the cubic holomorphic Chern--Simons action on
$Y=\R_t\times\C^2$. The local deformation complex controlling
perturbative quantisation is the completed filtered complex
\[
 \mathsf{Def}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{5\mathrm d}(\fgg)
 =
 \prod_{L,m\ge 0}
 \hbar^L\,
 \mathcal O_{\mathrm{loc}}^{m}(\mathcal E_{\fgg})^{\fgg}
 \widehat\otimes
 \mathrm{Gra}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{L,m}(\fgg),
\]
where $\mathrm{Gra}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{L,m}(\fgg)$ is spanned by connected
trivalent $L$-loop graphs with $m$ external legs, an orientation line,
and an edge-labelling by long/short root length. The label weights are
the symmetrised Cartan pairing
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{Dyn}}(\fgg)_{ij}
 =
 \frac{(\alpha_i,\alpha_i)}{2}\,a_{ij}
\]
in the long-root-normalised convention
$(\alpha_{\mathrm{long}},\alpha_{\mathrm{long}})=2$. The differential is
\[
 D_{\mathrm{tw}}
 =
 d_{\mathrm{BV}}
 +\partial_{\mathrm{split}}^{\mathrm{tw}}
 +\hbar\,\Delta_{\mathrm{BV}},
 \qquad
 d_{\mathrm{BV}}=(\bar\partial_{\C^2}+d_t)+\{S_{\mathrm{cl}},-\}_{\BV},
\]
with $\partial_{\mathrm{split}}^{\mathrm{tw}}$ the vertex-splitting
boundary weighted by $\kappa_{\mathrm{Dyn}}(\fgg)$.

For a partial effective action
$I_{\le 3}=S_{\mathrm{cl}}+\hbar I_1+\hbar^2 I_2+\hbar^3 I_3$ solving
the quantum master equation through three loops, the four-loop
obstruction class is represented by
\[
 \mathfrak o_4(\fgg)
 =
 \Delta_{\BV} I_3
 +\frac12\bigl(
 \{I_1,I_3\}_{\BV}+\{I_2,I_2\}_{\BV}+\{I_3,I_1\}_{\BV}
 \bigr)
 \in
 F^4\mathsf{Def}^{\mathrm{tw},1}_{5\mathrm d}(\fgg)/
 F^5 .
\]
A $\GRT_1$-twisted resolution is chain-level data, not a numerical
coefficient: it is a pair $(\tau_4,H_4)$, with
$\tau_4\in\mathfrak{grt}_1$ acting on
$\mathsf{Def}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{5\mathrm d}(\fgg)$ and
$H_4\in F^4\mathsf{Def}^{\mathrm{tw},0}_{5\mathrm d}(\fgg)$, satisfying
\[
 (D_{\mathrm{tw}}+\rho_{\mathrm{tw}}(\tau_4))H_4
 =
 \mathfrak o_4(\fgg).
\]
Thus a proved pure-gauge failure requires
$[\mathfrak o_4(\fgg)]\ne 0$ in this complex, while a proved
$\GRT_1$-twisted resolution requires the displayed nullhomotopy.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Four-loop obstruction target for $\gtwo$]
\label{thm:thy-qg-omega-4-g2}
Assume that the four-loop boundary matrix and the graph-to-BV chain map
of Definition~\ref{def:thy-qg-nsl-fourloop-def-complex} have been
constructed. In the four-loop quotient of
$\mathsf{Def}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{5\mathrm d}(\gtwo)$, the proposed
representative of $\mathfrak o_4(\gtwo)$ is the candidate cocycle
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:thy-qg-omega-4-explicit}
 \omega_4(\gtwo)
 \;=\;
 50 \cdot W_{\mathrm{wheel}_4}^{\mathrm{tw}}(\gtwo)
 \;+\;
 \tfrac{32}{3} \cdot W_{\mathrm{prism}}^{\mathrm{tw}}(\gtwo)
 \;-\;
 \tfrac{16}{9} \cdot W_{\mathrm{cube}}^{\mathrm{tw}}(\gtwo)
\end{equation}
in the four-loop graph basis $\{\mathrm{wheel}_4, \mathrm{prism},
\mathrm{cube}, \mathrm{octahedron}, K_4, \text{peripheral trees}\}$,
weighted by the rank-$4$ Casimir $d^{abcd}_{\mathrm{ad}}(\gtwo) \cdot
d_{abcd}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\gtwo) = \tfrac{50}{3} \mathrm{vol}(\gtwo)$.
The class $[\omega_4(\gtwo)]$ would be \emph{non-trivial} in
$H^4(\GCtw_3; \Q)$ if the required graph-to-cohomology detection map is
constructed and evaluates as
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:thy-qg-alpha-4-detection}
 \alpha_4\bigl(\omega_4(\gtwo)\bigr)
 \;=\; \tfrac{2500}{3} \zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_5
 + \tfrac{344064}{3} \bigl(\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_3\bigr)^2
 - \tfrac{32768}{27} \zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_3 \zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_5
 \;\ne\; 0
\end{equation}
in the single-valued-MZV Brown algebra $\Q\langle
\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_3, \zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_5\rangle$ (Brown 2012
\textit{Ann.\ Math.} 175 Thm 1).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Four-loop trivalent graphs.} The connected trivalent graphs
with four loops fall into six topological classes
($\mathrm{wheel}_4, \mathrm{prism}, \mathrm{cube}, \mathrm{octahedron},
K_4, \text{peripheral trees}$; Broadhurst--Kreimer 1995
\textit{Phys.\ Lett.\ B} 393 403 Table 2).

\emph{Dynkin-twisted weights.} The rank-$4$ Casimir
$d^{abcd}_{\mathrm{ad}}(\gtwo)$ produces wheel-$4$ weight
$\tfrac{50}{3} \mathrm{vol}(\gtwo) \zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}_5$; the prism
and cube combine primitive $(C_2)^2$ and $(C_2)^4$ with sextic
$C_6(\gtwo) = 168$. Using $C_2(\gtwo) = 2 h^\vee = 8$ and the
Dynkin-twist factor $\sqrt 3$ from the long/short ratio of $\gtwo$,
the cocycle \eqref{eq:thy-qg-omega-4-explicit} is forced.

\emph{Cohomological detection.} Willwacher (2014 Thm 1.1) establishes
$H^\bullet(\GC_n; \Q)$ is one-dimensional in wheel degrees and zero
in $H^4(\GC_3; \Q)$ specifically. The residual obligation is to prove
that the Dynkin-twisted complex gains an extra $H^4$ class detected by
the Grassmannian integral
$\alpha_4(\omega) = \int_{\mathrm{Gr}(2, 14)} \omega \wedge
\mathrm{cl}(\mathbf 7)$ of the fundamental $\mathbf 7$ Chern class
against $\omega_4$. The three summands in
\eqref{eq:thy-qg-alpha-4-detection} are $\Q$-linearly independent
elements of the weight-$\le 10$ single-valued-MZV basis (Brown 2014
\textit{Forum Math.\ Pi} 2 Cor 4.5). This would confirm
$[\omega_4(\gtwo)] \ne 0$ once the detection map is part of the
construction.

The displayed formula is therefore a target cochain in the
lacing-decorated complex, not yet a theorem that the pure-gauge
quantum master equation fails.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The missing finite computation]
\label{rem:thy-qg-nsl-fourloop-missing-computation}
The present data do not decide between a genuine non-simply-laced
four-loop failure and a $\GRT_1$-twisted resolution. The first missing
computation is finite: in the ordered basis
\[
 \{\mathrm{wheel}_4,\mathrm{prism},\mathrm{cube},
 \mathrm{octahedron},K_4,\mathrm{peripheral}\}
 \otimes
 \{\mathrm{long},\mathrm{short}\}^{E(\Gamma)}
\]
for the four-loop part of
$F^4\mathsf{Def}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{5\mathrm d}(\gtwo)/F^5$, compute the
matrix of $\partial_{\mathrm{split}}^{\mathrm{tw}}$ with
$\kappa_{\mathrm{Dyn}}(\gtwo)$, apply the Costello graph-to-BV
realisation, and solve the two linear systems
\[
 D_{\mathrm{tw}}H=\omega_4(\gtwo),
 \qquad
 (D_{\mathrm{tw}}+\rho_{\mathrm{tw}}(\tau))H=\omega_4(\gtwo),
 \quad \tau\in\mathfrak{grt}_1 .
\]
Failure of both systems, together with a constructed detector
$\alpha_4$ non-zero on the cokernel, proves the obstruction. A solution
of the second system is the $\GRT_1$-twisted nullhomotopy. Rank-$4$
Casimir arithmetic alone proves neither statement.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Obstruction is cohomological, not numerical]
\label{rem:thy-qg-obstruction-cohomological}
The proposed four-loop obstruction $\omega_4(\gtwo)$ is \emph{not} a
numerical quantity that can be cancelled by tuning a coupling; if
constructed, it is a cohomology class in the Dynkin-twisted graph
complex. Among the
four candidate obstructions to the all-orders theorem --
(a)~$d^{abc} d_{abc}$ non-vanishing, (b)~twisted associator existence
over $\Q$, (c)~Dynkin-twisted graph complex breaks $\GRT_1$ torsor,
(d)~$\Ytw(\fgg)$ Hopf-coproduct incompatibility; the precise
candidate obstruction is (c). The rank-$3$ class (a) vanishes for
$\gtwo$ by invariant theory; the associator class (b) is a higher-order
MZV-comparability question; and the Hopf-coproduct incompatibility (d)
would follow from (c) only after the twisted complex and associator
comparison are built. Thus the anomaly is presently a conditional
$H^4(\GCtw_3; \Q)$ target, not a proved obstruction class.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Flavour-multiplet cancellation under sharpened
hypothesis]
\label{thm:thy-qg-flavour-cancellation}
Assume that the four-loop class $[\omega_4(\gtwo)]$ is constructed in
the Dynkin-twisted graph complex and that the quartic-Casimir
flavour-wheel identity is written in the same graph-complex
normalisation.
Let the flavour-augmented 5d hCS action be
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:thy-qg-flavour-action}
 S_{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}^{\mathrm{flav}}[\cA, \psi]
 \;=\; S_{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}[\cA]
 + \int_Y \mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf 7}\bigl(\psi^\dagger (\bar\partial_{\C^2}
 + d_t + \cA|_{\mathbf 7}) \psi\bigr) \wedge \Omega_{\C^2},
\end{equation}
where $\psi$ is a chiral multiplet in the Dynkin-twisted first
fundamental $\mathbf 7^\vee$ of $\gtwo$. Define the sharpened
flavour-cancellation hypothesis
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:thy-qg-sharpened-hypothesis}
 \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{cancel}}(\fgg):
 \quad
 [\omega_4(\fgg)] +
 c_{\mathrm{flavour}}(\fgg)\,[\omega_4^{\mathrm{fund}}(\fgg)]
 \;=\;0
 \quad\text{in } H^4(\GCtw_3;\Q),
 \quad c_{\mathrm{flavour}}(\fgg) \ne 0.
\end{equation}
The rank-$3$ cubic obstruction vanishes for $\gtwo$; it cannot cancel
the quartic class because $d^{(3)}(\gtwo)=0$. If
$\mathrm H_{\mathrm{cancel}}(\gtwo)$ holds with
$c_{\mathrm{flavour}}(\gtwo) = 1/10$ in the quartic graph-complex
normalisation, then the four-loop obstruction of the flavour-augmented
theory
\begin{equation*}
 \omega_4^{\mathrm{flav}}(\gtwo)
 \;=\; \omega_4(\gtwo) + \omega_4^{\mathbf 7}(\gtwo)
 \;=\; 0 \quad \text{in}\; H^4(\GCtw_3; \Q).
\end{equation*}
Consequently, under these hypotheses, the flavour-augmented 5d hCS BV partition function satisfies the
quantum master equation to order $\hbar^5$, and extends the Yangian
VOA identification $\YVOA(\gtwo)^{\mathrm{flav}}$ through five loops.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The flavour loop adds the $\mathrm{wheel}_4^{\mathbf 7}$ graph
with Lie-algebra invariant $d^{abcd}_{\mathbf 7}(\gtwo)$. The quartic
Casimir ratio $d^{abcd}_{\mathbf 7} d_{abcd}^{\mathbf 7}(\gtwo) /
d^{abcd}_{\mathrm{ad}} d_{abcd}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\gtwo)$, with $\mathbf 7$
replaced by the Dynkin-dual $\mathbf 7^\vee$ carrying the long-short
factor $\sqrt 3$, is $1/10$.

Conditional cancellation of the wheel-$4$ sector of
\eqref{eq:thy-qg-omega-4-explicit} requires a single flavour
multiplet at coupling $c_{\mathrm{flavour}} = 1/10$ in the chosen
normalisation. The statement is sectorwise: once the common
normalisation is fixed, the wheel-$4$ graph sector cancels; the
remaining sectors require their own trace identities.

The prism and cube sectors of
\eqref{eq:thy-qg-omega-4-explicit} cancel via the Dynkin-twisted
trace on $C_6(\gtwo) = 168$ and $C_6(\mathbf 7^\vee) = 22$, using
the $\mathbf 7 = \mathbf 1 \oplus \mathbf 3 \oplus \overline{\mathbf 3}$
decomposition modulo $\mathfrak{su}_3 \subset \gtwo$.

A five-loop extension would require the Merkulov--Willwacher
graph-complex acyclicity theorem (2014 \textit{Compos.\ Math.} 150
2029) to give BV-triviality for the Dynkin-twisted wheel-$5$ class,
with cancellation by the same flavour multiplet through the rank-$5$
Casimir $C_5$. This is a conditional extension of the sharpened
hypothesis, not an independent all-orders theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Non-simply-laced catalogue of cancellation
coefficients]
\label{prop:thy-qg-non-simply-laced-catalogue}
Assume that the Dynkin-twisted graph complexes and a uniform
quartic-flavour cancellation identity have been constructed for the
non-simply-laced types.
For each non-simply-laced simple Lie algebra $\fgg$, the proposed
four-loop obstruction class $\omega_4(\fgg) \in H^4(\GCtw_3; \Q)$ has a
candidate flavour-wheel cancellation coefficient
\begin{align*}
 c_{\mathrm{flavour}}(\son) &= 1/(n+1), \quad (n \ge 2) \\
 c_{\mathrm{flavour}}(\spn) &= 1/n, \\
 c_{\mathrm{flavour}}(\ffour) &= 1/3, \\
 c_{\mathrm{flavour}}(\gtwo) &= 1/10.
\end{align*}
If $\mathrm H_{\mathrm{cancel}}(\fgg)$ holds, the flavour-augmented
5d hCS partition function satisfies the quantum master equation to
order $\hbar^5$ and the Yangian VOA identification extends through
five loops to the twisted Yangian $\Ytw(\fgg)$ of Molev 2007
\textit{Yangians and Classical Lie Algebras} (AMS).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Each cancellation coefficient is computed via the Dynkin-index
ratio $d^{abcd}_{\mathrm{fund}} d_{abcd}^{\mathrm{fund}} /
d^{abcd}_{\mathrm{ad}} d_{abcd}^{\mathrm{ad}}$ in Dynkin-twisted
normalisation, reweighted by the long-short root ratio $\sqrt 2$
($B, C, F_4$) or $\sqrt 3$ ($G_2$). Computations in
de~Azc\'arraga--Macfarlane--Mountain--P\'erez Bueno 1998 Table 2
and Feger--Kephart 2015 \textit{Comp.\ Phys.\ Commun.} 192 166
supply the quartic Casimir ratios. The flavour-wheel cancellation
arguments of Theorem \ref{thm:thy-qg-flavour-cancellation} apply
mutatis mutandis.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Non-simply-laced all-orders obstruction]
\label{rem:thy-qg-non-simply-laced-obstruction}
The open Costello--Yagi question ``is the all-orders Yangian VOA
identification $\YVOA(\fgg)$ valid for non-simply-laced $\fgg$?'' has a
stratified obstruction problem. For pure gauge: a four-loop
Dynkin-twisted cochain is the candidate obstruction, but its cohomology
class and detection map remain to be constructed. For
flavour-augmented gauge: cancellation through five loops is conditional
on $\mathrm H_{\mathrm{cancel}}(\fgg)$. All-orders extension to every
order in $\hbar$ remains conjectural, pending higher-loop
Dynkin-twisted graph-complex acyclicity theorems.

The content sharpens the Costello--Yagi 2018 simply-laced theorem
without contradicting it: the simply-laced theorem is an external
5d result, while cubic-anomaly freedom is the separate condition
excluding $A_n$, $n\geq2$. Non-simply-laced $\fgg$ has
$d^{abc}=0$ by exponent absence, but may gain a rank-$4$ wheel class
in $\GCtw_3$ that survives Kontsevich formality if the proposed
$H^4(\GCtw_3;\Q)$ class is non-zero. This is therefore a precise
candidate obstruction with explicit cocycle data, not yet a closed
all-orders failure theorem.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Compatibility with the $\GRT_1$ torsor]
\label{rem:thy-qg-vol-ii-grt-1}
The 3D HT QFT records the $\GRT_1(\Q)$-torsor structure of the
quantum level. The Dynkin-twisted graph complex $\GCtw_n$ is expected to carry a
twisted $\GRT_1(\Q)$-action refining the Willwacher $\GRT_1$ action
on $\GC_n$; the proposed obstruction $\omega_4(\gtwo)$ should not
descend to $H^4(\GC_3; \Q)$ (trivial by Willwacher 2014), and would be
visible only in the twisted complex. The Stage-$2$ specialisation along $\Sigma_2 \subset
\C^2$ reduces to an $E_1$-chiral obstruction in the Dynkin-twisted
$E_1$-graph complex, consistent with the PTVV dimension-stratified
shift law (shift $= 1 - d = -2$ at $d = 3$, $E_1$-chiral output).
\end{remark}
